# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## How to talk to the owner (ALWAYS) — STRICT RULE (owner-directed 2026-07-27)

The owner is not a developer. **Every message to the owner must be in PLAIN, SHORT, everyday language a beginner can understand.** This is a STRICT, non-negotiable rule set by the owner — it applies to every single chat reply, always, no exceptions.
- **SHORT:** get to the point. A few clear sentences, not long stretched-out paragraphs. Say what happened, what it means, and what you did — then stop. No filler, no walls of text.
- **PLAIN, not developer language:** no jargon — no "endpoints", "payloads", "idempotent", "race conditions", "constraints", "migrations", "check violation", "dead-letter queues". If a technical word is truly unavoidable, explain it in the same sentence with a real-life comparison (a mailman, a bookmark, a security guard).
- **Talk BUSINESS, not code:** explain what things mean for the business (files, borrowers, ClickUp cards, emails), not what they mean in the code.
- Commit messages and code comments stay technical; chat with the owner never is.

## How to BUILD (ALWAYS) — HIGHEST-END, NEVER CHEAP — STRICT RULE (owner-directed 2026-08-16)

**Owner's words: *"everything that they build should be built how Microsoft, Google, Apple, and the
military would build something — which means highest end, not cheapy. Even if there's a cheaper, easier
way to do it, they should go the harder way. It should be professional and high-end, not cheapy."***

This is a standing rule over every change, in both products. It is not a licence to gold-plate a
one-line fix; it is a ban on the *cheap shape* — the spot-patch, the second copy, the hand-kept list,
the assertion in place of a proof. When the cheap way and the structural way both work, **take the
structural way.** Ten concrete tests of that, all of which this repo already applies somewhere:

1. **Prove it, don't assert it.** Rehearse against the real thing before proposing it — a real
   Postgres, a real payload, a real render — never a mock that agrees with you. ("Test like you fly":
   the aerospace practice that exists because systems met real conditions for the first time in flight.)
2. **A test must be proven to FAIL.** Break the production rule on purpose and confirm the suite goes
   red — with an unmutated control green on either side. A test nobody has seen fail is decoration.
   Watch for false confidence: a *crashing* test also "fails", and looks like proof.
3. **One definition, never a second copy.** Two copies of a rule drift, and the one that drifts is the
   one that leaks. Where a mirror is unavoidable (a browser twin of a server rule), a test must fail
   the moment they disagree.
4. **Generate, don't hand-maintain.** A list somebody has to remember to update is a list that goes
   stale silently. Derive it from the source of truth.
5. **Fail closed, and never silently.** Unreadable input, an unknown value, a vendor timeout — answer
   the safe way and SAY SO. No swallowed errors, no silent caps, no "probably fine".
6. **Never remove something until nothing depends on it.** Add the new beside the old, move every
   reader, then remove. Never a rename in place on a live system.
7. **Fix the root and every place it surfaces**, never only the instance you were shown. (The
   long-standing rule below; it is the same principle.)
8. **Pin your tools.** Same input, same version, same result. An unpinned dependency is an outage
   scheduled for a date you do not control.
9. **Write the acceptance evidence BEFORE the step**, and do not proceed on the feeling that it went
   fine. State what must be true; then check it.
10. **Stage anything that touches production**, and roll back before diagnosing. Recovery first,
    understanding second.

**And the honesty half, which is what actually makes the above worth anything:** report what was
measured, not what was hoped. If a number is estimated, say estimated. If a guard is redundant today,
write that down rather than implying it bites. If something was not done, name it. A confident wrong
answer is the most expensive thing that can be produced here.

## TWO PRODUCTS, TWO SYSTEMS — RTL and LONG-TERM never mix (owner-directed 2026-08-02) — STRICT, HARD RULE, NO EXCEPTIONS

PILOT now has **two loan products, and they are two separate systems**:

- **RTL — Residential Transition Loans** (bridge + ground-up construction + fix & flip). **Everything in this repository built before 2026-08-02 is RTL.** RTL stays the MAIN product and the main build.
- **LT — Long-Term Loans.** Brand new, starting from **ZERO** on 2026-08-02. For now it is a **side build for visibility only — it does not go live**, no borrowers, no production traffic. It may be promoted to a full system later.

Treat them like **two different companies' software that happen to share one repo** — the owner's own words: *"the same way if I'm telling you to build something you don't know if you should build this for Amazon or for eBay you ask."* The following is non-negotiable and applies to every session, every agent, and every change:

1. **NEVER ASSUME WHICH SIDE A REQUEST IS FOR.** If the owner asks for something and it is not stated, or not 100% obvious, which product it belongs to — **STOP AND ASK. Never guess, never "do both to be safe," never pick the likelier one.** A wrong guess here is a worse failure than doing nothing.
2. **THE ONE SHARED ZONE IS IDENTITY — and it is READ-ONLY to LT (owner-directed 2026-08-03: *"same login same borrower record, keep it separate everything else"*).** There are three zones, not two: **shared identity** (the login in `src/auth/index.js`, the `borrowers` person record, the `staff_users` roster), the **RTL product zone**, and the **LT product zone**. Identity is shared because the owner said so; **the two product zones never touch each other.** Consequences the owner asked for by name: **a borrower sees ALL their files in one place, long-term and short-term**, and **an officer sees ALL of their files, long-term and short-term** — so an LT file records its borrower and its officer by pointing at those shared records. LT **READS** identity; it does not rewrite it — *"keep borrower read only"* (2026-08-03). **An officer CAN change a borrower profile from a long-term file** — through the ONE shared editor (`app-v2/src/components/BorrowerProfilePanel.jsx`) and the existing `PATCH /api/staff/borrowers/:id`, never through LT write code, so the person record keeps a single writer (a dozen RTL modules already heal, enrich and de-duplicate it). For that editor to open at all, LT records the officer↔person link in `borrower_officers` (db/327 — the identity-zone table built for *"the client who has only ever done non-RTL business with them"*), because the existing visibility rule resolves an officer's relationship through `applications`, an RTL table, and would otherwise refuse the very officers this was meant for. See charter §4.1.1. Everything else is a brand-new LT build: *"the workflow will be different, the sets will be different, integrations will be different, it will be a brand new build."* The exact authorizations are the `authorized` block in `docs/LONG-TERM-AUTHORIZED-COPIES.md`, and that block is the whole list.
3. **NOTHING ELSE CROSSES WITHOUT THE OWNER'S EXPLICIT WRITTEN AUTHORIZATION — per item.** You may not copy, move, re-use, import, extend, generalize, or "share" ANY code, table, column, migration, condition, template, endpoint, screen, component, prompt, mapping, or integration from RTL into LT (or from LT into RTL). Wanting to re-use something is normal and expected — **ask, get it in writing, then record it in `docs/LONG-TERM-AUTHORIZED-COPIES.md` before you write the first line.** Authorization is per item and never blanket; "we approved X" is not approval for Y. An entry in that ledger is the ONLY thing that makes a crossing legal, and the CI gate reads that ledger.
4. **THE BACK END IS SEPARATE, ALWAYS AND FOREVER.** LT back-end code lives ONLY in `src/longterm/**` and LT front-end code ONLY in `app-v2/src/longterm/**`; LT HTTP lives ONLY under `/api/lt/*`; LT tables AND their trigger functions are ONLY named `lt_*`; LT schema changes are ONLY in their own `db/NNN_lt_*.sql` migrations; LT tests are ONLY `scripts/test-lt-*.js`. **No LT table may reference an RTL table. No RTL trigger may fire on an LT table (and the reverse). LT may not read or write an RTL table in raw SQL either — a crossing does not need a `require()`. No shared writer, no shared service module, no shared database pool, no shared queue row, no shared checklist/condition template.** The single permitted seam in the whole back end is `src/server.js` mounting the LT router — plus `scripts/test-lt-*.js`, which exist to test it.
5. **DO NOT TOUCH RTL IN ORDER TO BUILD LT.** No new column on `applications` (or any other RTL table), no new ClickUp field mapping, no new Encompass / SharePoint / DocuSign / Sitewire / Trustpoint wiring, no new checklist template, no new enum value — **unless the owner asked for that exact thing.** Owner's words: *"don't add any columns don't add any mapping unless we specifically ask you to."*
6. **LT IS EXPLICITLY NOT GETTING (for now): document underwriting, orders.** Out of scope until the owner says otherwise in writing. Do not build them, do not stub them, do not "leave room" for them by copying RTL shapes. **CONDITIONS ARE NOW IN SCOPE FOR LT — the owner said otherwise in writing (2026-08-14), which is exactly how this rule was always meant to be reopened**: *"you should set up your DSCR condition center — it should pull the conditions directly from Encompass … we should be uploading documents in our system which should be uploaded directly to the e-folder and linked to a certain condition"* and, again, *"We should build a condition center that should be linked on the condition for existing files that had conditions, with all the documents in there linked … The condition center should be built very enhanced with the documentation logic and all other sections."* **THIS CHANGES THE SCOPE, NOT THE SEPARATION.** The LT condition center is a BRAND-NEW build in `src/longterm/**` / `lt_*` / `/api/lt/*` — rule 3 still stands in full, so not one line, table, template or endpoint may be copied or generalized from RTL's conditions, checklists, `checklist_templates`, `checklist_items`, `conditions`, the Condition Center rules engine or the document/eFolder machinery without a per-item entry in `docs/LONG-TERM-AUTHORIZED-COPIES.md`. Reading Encompass conditions is a READ, so it is covered by the existing read-only integration; **an eFolder upload is a WRITE to Encompass and is governed by `docs/ENCOMPASS-WRITE-AUTHORIZATIONS.md`, which today authorizes it in principle but records that its request/response shape is UNVERIFIED — so nothing may write to the eFolder until that entry is completed and the write gate lists the module.** Document underwriting and orders remain out of scope.
7. **THE FRONT END MAY SHOW BOTH — THE BACK END MAY NOT.** One pipeline may list RTL and LT together, and it must offer **filters (Both / RTL only / Long-Term only)** and carry a **visible product stamp on every row and every file header** so anyone can tell at a glance which product a file is. That merge happens **only in the read/view layer** (each product answers for its own rows, the edge tags and concatenates) — **never a SQL join, a shared table, or a shared write path.**
8. **A FEATURE BUILT FOR ONE SIDE NEVER AUTOMATICALLY APPLIES TO THE OTHER.** The standing "every fix is all-sides" and "previous AND future files" rules mean *every surface of THAT product* — they never reach across to the other product. A bug fixed on RTL is not fixed on LT unless the owner asks for it on LT too.
9. **WHEN IN DOUBT — ASK. Silence is never permission.**
10. **THE INVESTOR NAME NEVER REACHES A CLIENT — HARD RULE (owner-directed 2026-08-14): *"The client should not be able to see the investor name. Never ever! Not borrowers, not TPOs, only internal staff."*** The investor / capital provider who buys a long-term loan is INTERNAL knowledge: not a field, not a label, not a document name, not an email, not a PDF, not an export, not a filter option, not a tooltip, not an error message. Their **contact details, their own loan number and the funding channel** are covered too — the channel names *how* a loan is funded, which implies *who*. **`src/longterm/audience.js` is the ONE definition**, built on the investor REGISTRY (117 recorded spellings) because a hand-typed name is spelled 151 ways and a `!== 'Deephaven'` check passes `Deepahven approval.pdf` straight to a borrower. Two defences, in order: **(a) don't send it** — build a client payload FOR the client (`maySeeField`, `stripInternalOnly`); **(b) scrub free text** a human typed (`scrubInvestorNames`) — a condition body, a comment, a filename, an email. It **fails closed**: any audience that is not exactly `internal` is a client. Guarded by `scripts/test-lt-investor-block.js`, which sweeps EVERY recorded spelling through five sentence shapes and fails if one survives. **Never re-implement the check** — a second copy is how the two drift, and the one that drifts is the one that leaks. This is the LT analogue of the standing RTL rule "never expose a note buyer / capital partner name on a borrower-facing surface", and it is STRICTER: RTL allows staff-only surfaces to name them, LT additionally blocks **TPOs**. Full reasoning + the judgement calls: `docs/longterm/AUDIENCE-RULES.md`.

Enforcement (the same rules, in every place a rule can live): CI gate `scripts/check-product-separation.js` (runs at the head of `npm test`, blocks the PR and the deploy) with `scripts/test-product-separation-gate.js` proving it still bites, PR checklist `.github/pull_request_template.md`, rule mirror for GitHub + other AI agents `.github/PRODUCT-SEPARATION.md` and `AGENTS.md` (git root), the authorization ledger `docs/LONG-TERM-AUTHORIZED-COPIES.md`, and the architecture/research write-up `docs/LONG-TERM-LOANS-SEPARATION-CHARTER.md`.

**What the gate can and cannot see — a green build is NOT proof that nothing crossed.** It catches *structural* crossings: an import either way, raw SQL against the other product's tables, a cross-product foreign key, an `lt_`/`long_term` column on an RTL table, a migration touching both sides, a trigger or function carrying one product's logic onto the other's table, an LT table or function not named `lt_*`, RTL back-end code calling `/api/lt`, and any attempt to delete the rules or unwire the gate itself. It CANNOT see RTL code **copied by value** into an LT folder, a plainly-named new column added to an RTL table for LT's benefit, a new ClickUp/Encompass/SharePoint mapping, or a new checklist template. Rules 1, 4, 5, 7 and 8 above rest on the person doing the work.

## THE DATABASE IS NOT RENAMED, AND THE SCHEMA WORK IS DOCUMENTATION ONLY — STRICT RULE (owner-directed 2026-08-16)

**Owner's words: *"we are not renaming anything. Please don't do the risky stuff."*** This closes
Phase 4 of `docs/SCHEMA-AS-SOURCE-OF-TRUTH-PLAN.md` permanently. It is not deferred and not pending a
later decision.

- **NEVER rename a table, a column, a relation or a constraint** for readability, tidiness, consistency,
  or as a side effect of any other work. If a name is wrong, it is wrong and it stays — `llcs` already
  holds corporations, partnerships and trusts under a name that no longer fits, and CLAUDE.md already
  records the decision not to rename it. That is the standard.
- **The `docs/schema/` files are DOCUMENTATION.** `beyond-prisma.json`, `BEYOND-PRISMA.md`,
  `PICTURE.html` and `schema.prisma` are generated, read by nothing at runtime, and every statement
  their scripts run is a `SELECT`. **Never rebuild a database from `schema.prisma`** — it cannot express
  749 objects this database has, and the generated SQL runs without a single error while omitting every
  one of them. The numbered `db/*.sql` migrations are the only thing that builds this database.
- **The two schema drift checks are ADVISORY on purpose** (`check-schema-snapshot.js`,
  `check-lt-schema-drift.js`). Making either blocking is one environment variable
  (`SCHEMA_SNAPSHOT_ENFORCE=1`, `LT_SCHEMA_DRIFT_ENFORCE=1`) and is **the owner's call, not an
  agent's** — it would start failing other people's pull requests.
- **Do not add anything to the backup system** (`scripts/backup-*.js`, `src/lib/backup/**`) without the
  owner asking for it by name — including read-only additions. It is the one thing here whose failure
  cannot be repaired after the fact.

### Adding a migration — the two commands, and the one thing you no longer have to remember

**Ask for the number, never pick one: `npm run migration:new -- "what it is for"`.** It writes
`db/NNN_what_it_is_for.sql` with the idempotent shape `migrate-boot` requires and a header self-labelled
with its own number. **Do not hand-pick a number by looking at `db/`** — that is how 033, 088 and 113
collided. The tool takes the next number after every one it can see across `db/` **and every git ref this
clone knows about**, because the collision is between two sessions on two BRANCHES and the working tree
shows you only one of them; it prints what it searched and says plainly that a branch nobody has pushed
is invisible to it. It never re-uses a gap: a gap exists because a number was abandoned, which usually
means another branch still holds a file with it. `check-migrations.js` remains the gate that CATCHES a
collision; this is what stops you creating one.

**The schema map now refreshes ITSELF on your pull request — do not regenerate it by hand.** When your
migration changes the database, CI rebuilds `docs/schema/` from the database your migrations actually
build and commits it back to your branch (job `schema-push` in `.github/workflows/test.yml`, via
`scripts/ci-schema-commit.js`). It commits ONLY those generated files, it **never force-pushes** — if you
pushed while it was working it does nothing at all and the refreshed copy is still attached to the run as
the `schema-map` artifact — and it carries `[skip ci]`. So `git pull` before your next commit, and expect
a commit from `github-actions[bot]` on your branch. **That job holds the only write permission in the
whole workflow, and it must stay the smallest job in it: never move its `contents: write` onto `test-db`
or up to the workflow level** (`scripts/test-ci-schema-commit-pure.js` fails the build if you do — it
counts). Nothing about this runs on main: there the check stays advisory and must never fail, because
`deploy` needs `test-db` and a documentation file must never block a release.

**If you do regenerate by hand, it is TWO commands, not one:** `npm run schema:snapshot` (needs
`DATABASE_URL` pointing at a database built from these migrations) **and then** `npm run schema:restamp`
(no database needed). Stopping after the first leaves the map's header quoting the previous database's
numbers and fails the header test — following the old one-command advice is what broke a build once, and
`check-schema-behind.js` now prints both. `npm run schema:picture` regenerates the readable page from the
inventory. All three honour `SCHEMA_OUT_DIR`, which is how CI rebuilds the whole set into a scratch
directory without touching your working tree.

Everything else about a schema change is unchanged and unaffected by any of the above: a new numbered
idempotent `db/NNN_*.sql`, applied by the existing runner. If a migration lands and the map is somehow
still not regenerated, `check-schema-behind.js` says so on the next test run, with no database needed.

**A LONG-TERM (`lt_*`) schema change has a THIRD step the bot does not do for you (learned 2026-08-23,
the red main behind PR #1325): declare the new column/table in `src/longterm/prisma/schema.prisma` in
the SAME commit as the migration.** `check-lt-schema-drift` (and its blocking test
`test-lt-schema-drift-pure`) compares that MODEL against the schema map, and the schema-push bot
refreshes ONLY `docs/schema/` — never the model. db/621 added two `lt_loans` columns, the bot's map
refresh landed as a `[skip ci]` commit seconds before the squash, and the map+model mismatch was first
exercised ON MAIN, holding the gated deploy. The check abstains on a stale map for "declared but no
column yet" (a model landing with its migration), but a column the map PHOTOGRAPHS that the model does
not declare is never excused — so the model edit cannot wait for a later pass.

## Repository layout gotcha

The entire project lives in the **`yscap-repo-root_8/`** subfolder of the git root — `package.json`, `src/`, `db/`, `web/`, `app/` are all there, not at the git top level. Run all `npm` commands from inside `yscap-repo-root_8/`. Render auto-detects this nested `package.json`; deploys run from that folder.

## Commands

Backend (run from `yscap-repo-root_8/`):
- `npm start` — run the server (`node src/server.js`). Serves the API + the static site in `web/`.
- `npm run dev` — same, but with `RUN_SYNC=1` so the ClickUp/Encompass sync worker also starts.
- `npm run migrate` — apply `db/*.sql` manually. **Usually unnecessary**: the server auto-migrates on boot (see below).
- `npm run create-admin -- <email> <password> [role] [full name]` — seed/reset a staff super-admin (also driven by `ADMIN_EMAIL`/`ADMIN_PASSWORD` env on boot).

Frontend SPA (run from `yscap-repo-root_8/app/`):
- `npm install && npm run build` — builds the React portal **into `../web/portal/`**. Render does NOT build the frontend, so **after any change under `app/src/`, rebuild and commit the regenerated `web/portal/` bundle** or the change won't deploy.
- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server for local portal work.

There is **no test runner configured**. Verification in this repo is done by running the server against a local Postgres and exercising endpoints (e.g. ad-hoc Node scripts using `fetch` against `app.listen(...)`, or `require('./src/server')` which does not auto-listen). A real Postgres is required — the code has no in-memory/mocked DB.

## Environment / config

All config comes from env vars, centralized in `src/config.js` (which also loads a bundled `.env` at the project root if present, never overriding real env). Key ones: `DATABASE_URL` (required — unset makes pg fall back to localhost and every request fails with `ECONNREFUSED`), `JWT_SECRET`, `SSN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (both refuse the dev default in production and generate an ephemeral value + warn), `EMAIL_PROVIDER` (`auto` infers from `RESEND_API_KEY`/`MS_*`), `NOTIFY_FROM`, `APP_URL`, `STORAGE_DIR` (point at a Render persistent disk in prod), `INTAKE_API_KEY`. `render.yaml` is the source of truth for the production service + Postgres + persistent disk.

## Architecture

**One Express process serves everything** (`src/server.js`): the JSON API under `/auth` and `/api/*`, plus the static marketing site and prebuilt SPA out of `web/`. On boot it runs `src/migrate-boot.js` → `ensureSchema()` (waits for the DB, applies `db/schema.sql` only if the base tables are missing, then the idempotent numbered migrations every time) and `bootstrapAdmin()`. `/api/health` probes the DB (200/503).

**Migrations** (`db/`): `schema.sql` defines base tables and is NOT idempotent (bare `CREATE TABLE`) — it runs only on an empty DB. `002_`…`NNN_*.sql` are all `IF NOT EXISTS` / `ON CONFLICT` and safe to re-run on every boot. Adding schema = a new numbered file that is idempotent.

**Two front-ends, one origin:**
- `web/` — the existing static marketing site + standalone tools in `web/tools/*.html` (rehab budget, track record, loan application, term sheet…). The site's pricing/guideline engines (`window.YSP`/`GSP`/`TitleCost`) are **frozen** and must never be modified except by an explicit owner-directed guideline change (see the frozen-baseline note under Session rules). Officer branding is `?lo=<code>`-driven (`web/brand.js`); the loan-application officer dropdown is now fed live from `GET /api/roster` with the static list as fallback.
- `app/` — the React (Vite + HashRouter) borrower/staff portal, built to `web/portal/` and served at **`/portal/`**. Because it's under `/portal/` with a HashRouter, all portal deep links (and email links) must be `/portal/#/<route>` — see `src/lib/email/catalog.js` `link()` and `cfg.portalPath`.

**Auth** (`src/auth/index.js` + `src/lib/crypto.js`): borrowers self-register; staff are admin-provisioned/invited. Custom HS256 JWT, scrypt password hashing, TOTP MFA, and AES-256-GCM SSN encryption — all on Node's built-in `crypto`, **no native deps** (only `express` + `pg` are installed, so Render builds cleanly). Session revocation via a `token_version` claim checked against the DB. A pending-MFA "challenge" JWT carries `mfa:true` and must never be accepted as an access token.

**Roles & authorization:** staff roles are `super_admin > admin > {loan_officer, processor, underwriter}`. `super_admin` satisfies every `requireRole` gate. `seesAll` (admin/super_admin/underwriter) see every application; loan_officer/processor are scoped to files they're assigned to (enforced by a path-scoped middleware on `/applications/:id` and explicit checks on borrower/SSN/document reads). Only a super_admin may modify another super_admin or grant that role.

**Data model** (`db/schema.sql` + migrations): `borrowers` (PII) is separate from `borrower_auth` (login) to reduce blast radius. A borrower has many `applications` (one per property); `llcs` and `track_records` hang off the borrower. `checklist_templates`/`checklist_items` drive the document/condition workflow (audience borrower/staff, RTL phase workflow in `005`). `documents` reference bytes stored via `src/lib/storage.js`. `staff_users` is the single source of truth for the team roster (`007`). `leads` (`008`) captures public marketing-tool submissions. `notifications` (in-app + email fan-out), `sync_queue` (ClickUp/Encompass, deferred), and `audit_log` (GLBA PII trail) round it out. SSNs are encrypted at rest and stripped from any `raw_intake`/`payload` jsonb via `src/lib/redact.js`.

**Notifications & email:** `src/lib/notify.js` always writes an in-app `notifications` row and best-effort fans out a branded email. Email provider is selected in `src/lib/email/index.js` (`resend`/`graph`/`noop`); messages are built by `src/lib/email/catalog.js` and rendered by `src/lib/email/template.js` (table-based HTML, LIGHT/white-first PILOT palette, with the site PILOT lockup image `web/(v2/)assets/brand/pilot-lockup-email.png` via `APP_URL` — see the light-email session rule below). A failed send never breaks the request. **One event, one copy (owner-directed 2026-08-09):** a notification that exists BECAUSE an inbound email arrived (order return, file-thread reply, closing chain, chat email reply) passes `opts.alreadyEmailed` — who that email already reached — and `notify.js` skips the duplicate EMAIL for anyone on it while still writing their in-app row. The shared vocabulary is `src/lib/looped-in.js`; the inbound families wire it in `src/lib/file-inbox.js` (which also has the terminal `on_chain` outcome when the whole team was on the email), `order-inbox.js` and `chat.js`. Any NEW inbound-email-driven notification must pass the same option or it reintroduces the double-notification bombardment.

**Storage** (`src/lib/storage.js`): pluggable provider (`local` default, `s3`/`sharepoint` stubs share the interface). `local` persists under `STORAGE_DIR` with path-traversal defense, atomic writes, sharded dirs, and streaming reads. It probes writability and falls back to a temp dir (logging loudly) if `STORAGE_DIR` can't be written, so uploads never hard-fail; `/api/health` reports `storageWritable`/`storagePersistent`/`storageBase`. On Render the default filesystem is ephemeral — `STORAGE_DIR` must point at a mounted persistent disk (currently `/var/data/uploads` on a mounted 5 GB disk). Downloads stream through `src/lib/serve-document.js` after an authorization check.

## Session rules

- **BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE: which product is this — RTL or Long-Term? (owner-directed 2026-08-02).** Every task starts by answering that question, and if the answer is not certain you **ask the owner and wait**. The full law is the *"TWO PRODUCTS, TWO SYSTEMS"* section at the top of this file — read it before you touch code, a table, or a migration. Nothing crosses between the two products without written authorization recorded in `docs/LONG-TERM-AUTHORIZED-COPIES.md`.
- **V2 / PILOT is the canonical brand for every build (owner-directed, 2026-07-14).** "PILOT by YS Capital" is the product name and V2 is the DEFAULT experience — `web/v2` serves at the root (`src/server.js`), V1 is frozen at `/v1` (kept, never deleted). All future work ships against the V2/PILOT brand system; never reintroduce V1 styling or the pre-PILOT wordmark into a new build. The V2 portal builds from **`app-v2/`** into **`web/v2/portal/`** (base `/portal/`), and the V2 marketing/tools live in **`web/v2/`** — frontend brand/copy changes go in `app-v2/src/` and must be rebuilt into `web/v2/portal/` to deploy (the old `app/`→`web/portal/` is V1). Canonical references: brand sheet `web/preview/pilot-brand.html`; palette + type tokens `app-v2/src/styles.css` (Ink `#141B22`, Gold `#AE8746`, Teal `#2F7F86`, Paper `#F6F3EC`; Fraunces + Hanken Grotesk); the CSS lockup `.pilot-lockup`/`.pilot-mark` + `Brand`/`BrandLockup` in `app-v2/src/components/Layout.jsx`; install identity in `app-v2/public/manifest.webmanifest` (`PILOT` / "PILOT by YS Capital").
  - **V2 IS THE ONLY VERSION WE USE — V1 IS FULLY PARKED (owner-directed 2026-08-04: "Build is only on Version 2. We can park Version 1 totally aside. We don't use Version 1 anymore.").** Every NEW build and every fix ships **only** on V2 — `app-v2/src` (front end, rebuilt into `web/v2/portal/`) and `web/v2/` (marketing/tools). V1 (`app/`, `web/portal/`, `web/tools/`) stays FROZEN at `/v1`, kept and never deleted, but it is **no longer a build target**: do NOT port a new feature into `app/src`, do NOT rebuild `web/portal/`, and the standing "every fix is all-sides" / "previous AND future" rules now mean every V2 SURFACE — NOT V1. Two exceptions remain frozen-by-value: the pricing/guideline engines (every copy, including the V1 ones under `web/tools` — the frozen source of truth, edited only by an owner-directed guideline change) and any explicitly owner-directed V1-and-V2 fix (e.g. the LO branding bar, where the two `brand.js` copies are byte-identical by design). When a rule below says "build on V2 (canonical); V1 frozen", that is now simply the default for everything.
- **Surface the PILOT name tastefully — a nice amount, not everywhere (owner-directed, 2026-07-14).** Put the name on DURABLE identity surfaces (logo lockup, page titles, install name, empty-state headlines, sign-in, the "Invite to PILOT" flow) and on the automation's own artifacts (SharePoint sync folders — "Synced by Pilot"). Do NOT brand transient/functional microcopy (upload spinners "Uploading…", drop hints, save toasts, generic buttons) or repeat the name twice on one screen. Tagline: "Navigate every deal."
  - **Email is LIGHT and leads with the PILOT lockup — matching the site (owner-directed, 2026-07-14).** Every notification email is rendered by the single shared template `src/lib/email/template.js` (all callers — `notify.js`, `catalog.js`, `chat.js` — go through `render()`), so brand changes happen there once and apply to all emails. The email uses the white-first PILOT palette 1:1 with the site portal tokens (Paper `#F6F3EC` canvas, white `#FFFFFF` card, Gold `#AE8746` hairline, Teal `#2F7F86` button, ink `#141B22` text, muted `#4B585C`; footer links deeper teal `#256168` for AA on white). The header is the EXACT site top-left lockup — gold navigation-chevron mark + "PILOT" in the real Fraunces face + a quiet "by YS Capital" — shipped as an IMAGE (`web/(v2/)assets/brand/pilot-lockup-email.png`, referenced via `cfg.emailLogoUrl`) because email clients strip web fonts; a bulletproof text lockup (ink "PILOT" on white) renders only if that image is unset/blocked. The regulated **YS Capital corporate identity is preserved in the FOOTER** (company name + NMLS #2609746 + address + the business-purpose disclosure) — that is where the corporate lockup now lives; the header co-brands PILOT-primary with the "by YS Capital" endorsement. Do NOT revert emails to the old dark ink canvas. Regenerate the lockup PNG from the exact `.pilot-lockup` markup + the real Fraunces/Hanken woff2 (fontsource via jsDelivr; Google Fonts is blocked in this env) if the mark/wordmark ever changes. Keep the email inline-styles + table-only (no web fonts, no `<style>` blocks) so it renders in Outlook/Gmail/Apple Mail.
  - **Every notification email says WHICH file and is genuinely repliable (owner-directed, 2026-07-20).** The notification system was upgraded from "vague" to detailed: (1) **file identity is centralized in `src/lib/notify.js`** — `enrichFileOpts(opts, audience)` runs inside `notifyStaff`/`notifyBorrower`/`notifyAppStaff`/`notifyAppBorrowers`/`notifyAdmins`, keyed on `opts.applicationId`, and auto-attaches a `subjectTag` (loan# · street → appended to the SUBJECT line only, never the in-body H1) plus a detail `meta` block, so ~every file email names the property/loan in the inbox WITHOUT each call site hand-building it. It never overwrites a caller's explicit `meta`/`subjectTag`/`link`. Fan-out helpers pre-fetch `fileContext` ONCE and pass `_fileCtx` to avoid an N-per-recipient query. The `_fileCtx`/`_enriched`/`subjectTag`/`kicker` opts are EMAIL-ONLY — never written to the `notifications` DB row. (2) **`fileContext()` returns `subjectTag` + a borrower-safe `borrowerMeta`** (File, Property, Program [scrubbed], Loan type, Loan amount — NO internal contact row, NO staff `extraMeta`); borrower emails use `borrowerMeta`, staff emails the full `meta`. The frozen note-buyer/capital-partner scrub in `notifyBorrower` still runs AFTER enrichment (enriched meta values join the scrub `protect` list so a real address like "12 Churchill Lane" is never rewritten). (3) **`template.js` renders `subjectTag` (subject suffix), `kicker` (a small upper-case eyebrow auto-mapped from the notification `type` via `KICKER_OF`), and a `replyable` footer line**. `render()` with none of these is byte-identical to before (back-compat). (4) **Emails are NOT no-reply**: `cfg.notifyFrom` defaults to a real monitored address (`notifications@yscapgroup.com`), and `cfg.replyToDefault` (`REPLY_TO`, default `sales@yscapgroup.com`) is the guaranteed Reply-To when no per-file `file+<id>@` address or officer inbox applies — wired in BOTH `notify._emailRow` and `catalog.deliver`. (5) **The borrower gets the SAME rich, borrower-safe term sheet as staff on registration** — the shared builder `product-registration.borrowerTermsEmail(...)` (full structure breakdown; the 3-month minimum-earned-interest line is now CONDITIONAL on `termOptions.minInterestEnabled` per the 2026-07-22 toggle rule below — never a "prepayment penalty", and never shown when it's off) is used on BOTH register paths (`staff.js` + `borrower.js`). Borrower stays gated to MAJOR-email events only (#88) — no per-document-upload bombardment. **The borrower term-sheet email is sent only when a HEADLINE number actually changed on (re-)registration (owner-directed 2026-07-20): `persistProductRegistration` returns `{id, economicsChanged}` — comparing the previous registration's `borrowerTermsKey` (loan amount · rate · cash-to-close · term · program · product · advance/holdback split) to the new one — and both register routes gate the borrower email on it. First registration always sends; an internal re-register for the SAME stuff sends no nudge; any real change re-notifies. The team's `product_registered` notice still fires on every (re-)register. Test `scripts/test-notification-reregister-quiet.js`.** Tests: `scripts/test-notification-email.js` (in `npm test`). Do NOT reintroduce a "no-reply" sender, and route new file notifications through the notify helpers (pass `applicationId`) rather than hand-building a subject.
  - **Notification wave 2/3 — officer identity, status explanations, new touchpoints + scheduled digests (owner-directed 2026-07-20).** Built on the chokepoint above: (1) **`fileContext` LEFT JOINs the assigned loan officer** and adds a "Your loan officer" contact row (name · title · NMLS · phone · email) to `borrowerMeta` + exposes `ctx.officer`/`ctx.officerRow`, so EVERY borrower email shows who's handling their loan (officer business contact only — never a note buyer). (2) **Borrower status-change emails carry a plain-language explanation** of what the status means + what's next (`BORROWER_STATUS_EXPLAIN` in `staff.js`). (3) **New event-driven borrower emails**, all borrower-safe + throttled: "Meet your loan officer" on assignment (`officer_assigned`, staff.js `/assign`, fires once per real officer change); "You're all caught up" when a signed-off/waived condition was the LAST open borrower item (`all_caught_up`, staff.js sign-off handler, once/file/~day via audit_log, uses `reminders.outstandingItems`); document-accepted confirmation on a plain accept — **REMOVED (owner-directed 2026-07-20 evening): accepting a document is an INTERNAL workflow step, so the borrower is NOT notified at all (no email, no in-app row); the borrower still sees the accepted status in their checklist. Only borrower ACTION items (`doc_rejected`/`doc_requested`) and real milestones reach them. Test `scripts/test-notification-doc-accept.js`. A tool submission (Scope of Work, track record, term sheet) saves the SAME logical document in several FORMATS — HTML + XML + PDF — as separate `documents` rows on ONE checklist item, so a reject/request-more verdict was firing the borrower email once PER FORMAT (owner-reported 2026-07-20: three identical "needs a new document" emails). `staff.js` `claimItemVerdictEmail(checklistItemId, action)` atomically claims a 5-minute slot keyed on the checklist item so the export set produces ONE email; the sibling formats update silently (Test `scripts/test-notification-doc-verdict-dedup.js`). Same pattern for any new per-document borrower verdict — key the throttle on the checklist item, not the document.** — appraisal-received milestone (`milestone`, appraisal.js import — NEVER exposes the value/findings); draw + retainage released milestones (`draw`, sitewire.js — shows the NET wired amount). New notify types `officer_assigned/all_caught_up/milestone/digest` are in the CATEGORY_OF/BORROWER_MAJOR_EMAIL/KICKER_OF maps. (4) **Scheduled digests** — `src/lib/notification-digests.js` (booted in server.js, kill-switch `NOTIFY_DIGESTS_ENABLED=0`): weekly borrower "what's still needed", daily per-officer pipeline snapshot, stale-file alerts (`STALE_FILE_DAYS`, default 10), Monday admin summary, **plus two draw reminders (Draw Management enhancement 2026-07-20): `drawFindingsAwaitingBorrowerOnce`** — a delivered inspection result the borrower hasn't accepted/disputed nudges the borrower EVERY FEW HOURS until they act (owner-directed 2026-07-20: the release is waiting on their accept, so keep reminding — `DRAW_FINDINGS_REMINDER_HOURS`, default 4; per-file ≤once/`waitHours`; the moment they accept/dispute the finding leaves 'delivered' status and the nudges stop on their own; only runs in the 8am–6pm NY window so it's never a 3am email) — **and `drawReleaseOverdueOnce`** — an accepted draw whose `wire_due_at` passed with no recorded release alerts the assigned team, per-file ≤once/2days (draw_findings exist only for managed files, so both are go-forward-only by data). Each **self-gates via an `audit_log` stamp** (unique `action`, `entity_id`=app/staff uuid or NULL for global) so it fires at most once per period across restarts/instances; `_gate` fails CLOSED on DB error; the dispatcher runs every 30 min but only sends inside the morning/business-hours window in `America/New_York` (`nyParts`). Tests: `scripts/test-notification-digests.js` (DB-gated skip, in `npm test`). When adding a scheduled email, copy this self-gate pattern; when adding an event email, register its type in the three notify maps and keep the borrower gated to real events (never per-document-upload spam).
  - **Status-change notification is shared across BOTH doors AND ClickUp, with a go-forward inbound watermark (owner-directed 2026-07-20 evening).** The borrower-facing status copy/labels/journey/DECISION-email set live in ONE module `src/lib/status-notify.js` (`STATUS_LABEL`, `MAJOR_STATUSES`, `BORROWER_STATUS_EXPLAIN`, `borrowerJourney`, `borrowerStatusOpts`, `notifyInboundStatusChange`) — `staff.js` imports them (never redefines). Three doors move a file's borrower-facing status and all announce IDENTICALLY: (1) `PATCH /applications/:id` and (2) `POST /applications/:id/internal-status` (the 38-status ClickUp dropdown) both call `notifyStatusTransition` (borrower + team; borrower emails only on `MAJOR_STATUSES`, team `inAppOnly` off only on decisions; fires only when the external bucket actually changed; skips soft-deleted); (3) the **ClickUp INBOUND sync** (`clickup/ingest.js`, existing-app path) calls `notifyInboundStatusChange` so a status changed DIRECTLY in ClickUp also emails the borrower (the team drives statuses in both places). The inbound path is **GO-FORWARD ONLY** via `applications.status_notified_external` (db/187, NULL default, NO backfill): it SILENTLY BASELINES a file the first time it's seen (writes the watermark, sends nothing) so previously-drifted files are never blasted on the first reconcile; the portal doors set `status_notified_external` in lock-step with the status they announce so a ClickUp ECHO of a portal change never re-notifies; inbound skips deleted files and loops the LO in via the borrower email BCC (no separate team blast — a ClickUp change was made by the team in ClickUp). A plain document ACCEPT never notifies the borrower at all (internal step). Tests: `test-notification-status-parity.js` (portal doors), `test-notification-inbound-status.js` (go-forward baseline / echo / deleted / LO-BCC), `test-notification-doc-accept.js`. Never notify inbound without the watermark, and never backfill it (that would blast the backlog).
  - **Routine STAFF events are IN-APP ONLY — the team is not emailed on ordinary borrower activity (owner-directed 2026-07-20 evening: "stop the bombardment with stuff that is not important").** The `notifyStaff` chokepoint (which `notifyAppStaff` fans out through) now defaults `STAFF_INAPP_TYPES = {tool_submitted, doc_uploaded, condition_added}` to in-app-only: a borrower answering a tool/checklist question, uploading a document, or adding the appraisal card writes the staff in-app row but sends NO email (the whole team — LO + processor + assistants — was previously emailed on every such event). An explicit `opts.inAppOnly` always wins (status_change passes its computed value; a genuinely action-needed staff event may pass `false`). Only STAFF is affected — the borrower-facing `condition_added`/`doc_requested`/`doc_rejected` go through `notifyBorrower` (BORROWER_MAJOR_EMAIL), untouched. Actionable staff events still email (assignment, sync_review, mention, new_application, draw_disputed, sow_change_request, esign/ops alerts, product_registered, milestones). Test `test-notification-staff-quiet.js`. When adding a new staff notification, decide email-vs-in-app: routine/FYI → add its type to STAFF_INAPP_TYPES (or pass `inAppOnly:true`); action-needed → leave it emailing.
- **A PART-OF-THE-FILE ASSIGNEE SEES ONLY THEIR PART — role-scoped file notifications (owner-directed 2026-08-10: "the closer, the draw coordinator … are getting notifications not related to their part … The draw coordinator is related to the draws. The closer is related to closing. Only the loan officers on top of the entire file should receive all the notifications.").** ROOT CAUSE: db/392 made the closer a PERMANENT `application_assignees` row (the `closer_id` pointer-sync trigger fires on every "Submit for Closing"), while the whole-team fan-outs selected assignees with NO role filter — so a closer received every countered exception, product registration and draw event on every file she ever closed (the Malky Katz report: a closer as the To of a draw findings email). The rule: **`notify.js STAFF_ROLE_CATEGORIES`** (`loan_officer` = everything, `processor` = everything, `closer` = `closing` only, `draw_coordinator` = `draws` only) × **`categoryOf(type)`** — the SAME category key the draw loop-in is keyed on, so "what is a draw email" has one definition. Enforced by `staffRolesSee(roles, opts)` at exactly the TWO whole-team chokepoints (`notifyAppStaff`'s assignee query + `notifyAppStaffThread`'s To list) and nowhere else: direct `notifyStaff(<person>)` calls, `notifyAdmins`, the 2026-07-28 borrower-email draw loop-in Cc, the DocuSign wire viewers and the closer's own `purchase_advice_missing` chase are all untouched. `closing_docs_in` moved to a staff-side `'closing'` category (NOT in the borrower-prefs `NOTIFY_CATEGORIES`). A FORCED type (security/account/esign) reaches everyone; an assignee role with NO map entry fails OPEN (whole-file) — and the parity check in `scripts/test-notify-role-scope-db.js` (in `npm test`) reads `ASSIGNEE_ROLES` out of staff.js and FAILS on a role without an explicit entry, so a role added later forces the visibility decision at build time. An out-of-scope role loses the in-app row AND the email (the owner's words are "should not receive", and filtering the recipient set is the one-chokepoint shape); consequence, documented in the code: `notifyAppStaff`'s array now means "everyone this event CONCERNS", so an esign event on a file whose only assignees are part-roles escalates to admins — the correct reading of "nobody responsible heard it". When adding a notification type, give it a category in `CATEGORY_OF` (an unlisted type = `'other'` = LO/processor only); when adding an assignee role, add its entry to `STAFF_ROLE_CATEGORIES` in the same commit. The inbound file-thread forward (`file-inbox.assigneesForFile`) is deliberately NOT yet filtered — that widening is an owner call (a closer may genuinely need to read a file's email thread).
- **SharePoint sync marker is "Synced by Pilot" — and the matcher accepts the LEGACY names forever (owner-directed, 2026-07-14).** Automation-created folders are named "Synced by Pilot" (leaf) / "&lt;name&gt;, Synced by Pilot" (suffix marker, comma-prefixed). The matcher MUST keep recognizing the legacy `YS portal syncing` / `, YS portal sync` names: `norm()` in `src/lib/sharepoint-map.js` strips BOTH phrasings, and the leaf/unfiled resolvers (`ensureAliased`, fed by `cfg.sharepointSyncFolderLegacy`/`sharepointUnfiledLegacy`) reuse a legacy-named folder before creating a Pilot-named one — so existing folders are never duplicated or stranded. Never rename an existing folder (hard no-rename policy) EXCEPT the owner-sanctioned backwards repair below.
- **SharePoint paths are kept UNDER the 259-char Windows/OneDrive limit (owner-directed 2026-07-27; the "99% corrupted / can't open" report).** The files were never byte-corrupted (the mirror's own integrity audit reported zero mismatches; sampled PDFs/xlsx read back cleanly). Windows/Office/Acrobat refuse to OPEN a OneDrive-synced file whose full LOCAL path exceeds 259 characters ("the file path is more than 259 characters" — Excel; "Access denied" — Acrobat), and the mirror's deep, redundant folder+file naming pushed nearly every path over it. Two GOING-FORWARD shorteners + one BACKWARDS repair:
  1. **`sharepoint-backup.stripPathEchoes(filename,row)`** at the one `uploadAndRecord` chokepoint removes the address/borrower/officer/LLC slug the export tools bake into a filename (`62_Highland_Street_..._SOW_2026-07-26.pdf` → `SOW_2026-07-26.pdf`) — the folder already names them. ECHO-ONLY (a name without such an echo is byte-for-byte unchanged), extension+date preserved, deterministic (re-mirror/adopt stays stable). A single-token slug must be ≥6 chars (a 4-letter surname can't over-strip); multi-token ≥4. Only the SharePoint copy is affected; the portal keeps the descriptive name.
  2. **The `, Synced by Pilot` marker is created ONCE — on the leaf sync folder** — not repeated on officer/borrower/address (`sharepoint-map.createFolder`, `AUTO_MARKER`/`createMarked` removed). Matching is marker-agnostic (`norm`/`addressCore` strip it), so an existing marked/legacy folder is still reused — no duplicate/rename. The officer-less **Unfiled** branch also fuzzy-reuses (same `pickMatch` pre-step) so a plain create adopts a pre-existing marked folder there too.
  3. **BACKWARDS repair `scripts/sharepoint-shorten-existing.js` (DRY-RUN by default, `--apply` to rename).** Renames the automation's OWN over-long existing items to shorter names — a file → its echo-stripped name, a marked folder → its plain name (`sp.dropSyncMarker`) — so ALREADY-mirrored files become openable. Through `sp.renameOwnItem` — the SECOND sanctioned existing-item mutation after the Version-N move — guarded by `createdByThisApp` (never a human item), in-mirror ancestry (a file inside a sync leaf, a folder under the Pipeline root), a sibling-collision pre-check (never merges/duplicates), extension-preserve, and an If-Match eTag pin. A rename KEEPS the driveItem id so `sharepoint_backup_ref` + the folder cache stay valid; nothing is deleted or moved; idempotent; only touches items whose current local path is over the ceiling. RESIDUAL (not covered): a few very long CONDITION-folder labels (up to 141 chars, from the checklist item `label`) can still exceed 259 even after the fix — shortening the condition-folder segment is the remaining lever if needed. Test `scripts/test-sharepoint-shortpath.js`.

- **MANDATORY two-audit-agent gate on EVERY change (owner-directed, 2026-07-12):** every single change — a new feature, a bug fix, an error fix, a rewrite, a refactor, anything good or bad, large or one line — MUST be put through **at least two dedicated audit agents**, no exceptions.
  1. **Pre-merge audit (before it lands on `main`):** the moment you finish writing a change, spawn a dedicated audit agent whose job is to troubleshoot **every step, every line, and every way of operation** of what you just wrote. It must confirm the change is (a) implemented in **every place it possibly should be** (all surfaces — marketing site, borrower login, staff/internal login; all roles; previous AND future files; backend AND frontend), (b) actually works end-to-end, (c) does **not** break or regress anything else, and (d) does not mess up at all. It reads the actual diff and the surrounding code, not a summary.
  2. **Post-merge audit (after it lands on `main`, at a HIGHER bar):** after the merge, reopen the audit agent at an even more rigorous level to confirm it was **merged correctly** and that **every single thing works as expected after the merge** (verified against the merged `main` state — NOT waiting for a deploy; "works after merge, not after deploy").
  3. **Any issue found in either audit is fixed IMMEDIATELY without asking for confirmation, and then re-audited** (run another audit pass) until clean.
  4. **Pipeline, don't block:** while an audit is running, immediately proceed to start writing the **next** item on the task list; check the audit's results once the next item is written, act on them, then continue. Never idle waiting on an audit.
  5. **Finish the whole list, then sweep:** never end a session before you have worked on **every single item** the owner put on the task list. After the last item, go back and re-review the **entire** session end-to-end to confirm every item was actually built, nothing was forgotten, and everything composes together. Only ask the owner questions at the very end.
  6. **NEVER let a background/audit agent switch git branches (`git checkout`) in the shared working tree (learned 2026-07-12).** Subagents share ONE on-disk working tree with the main session. If an agent does `git checkout main` (or any branch/commit checkout) while you are mid-work, it **detaches HEAD out from under you** — a commit you make then lands on a detached HEAD and is orphaned when the agent checks back, and files from your branch vanish from disk (a migration "disappeared" and a commit was left dangling this way). Audit agents must inspect other commits with `git show <sha>` / `git log`/`git grep <sha>` **without** ever checking out; the current branch already equals `main`'s content right after a restart. If you must diff against another ref, use `git diff <sha>` or a read-only worktree — never a bare `checkout` in the shared tree. If a commit ever goes missing, it's almost always recoverable via `git reflog` + `git reset --hard <sha>`.
- **Fix the ROOT, then every place the root can surface — never just the one spot you were shown (owner-directed, 2026-07-12).** A reported bug is a *symptom*. Before (and while) fixing it you MUST work out the **root cause** and treat the fix as a cross-cutting one:
  1. **Find the root, not the instance.** Ask, literally: *what made the code get written this way?* What assumption / missing contract / copied pattern produced it? A fix that doesn't name the root cause is not done.
  2. **How would writing it differently have prevented it?** Decide the correct original shape (the pattern that makes the whole class impossible), and move the code toward that shape — not a spot-patch that leaves the trap armed for the next field/tool.
  3. **The same bug is almost never in one place.** The instant you understand the class, enumerate **every place it can occur** and fix them all in the same pass: if it happens in one **tool**, it can happen in the **other tools**; on the **borrower login** it will also be on the **loan-officer / staff / internal login** (and the marketing site); if it triggers when you **type one address**, it triggers **everywhere an address (or any autosaved field) is typed**; previous **and** future files. Grep the repo for the pattern, list the surfaces (all tools × all logins × all roles × all fields × frontend AND backend), and fix across the board so it can't come back.
  4. **Ask what ELSE the root already broke.** A root cause usually has downstream damage — e.g. the autosave-duplicate-record root also polluted deal counts, experience tiers/brackets, condition auto-clears, and any report that reads those rows. Hunt for and clean up (or migrate away) the secondary damage it already caused, not only the live symptom.
  5. **Prefer a structural root fix.** When feasible, kill the class at a chokepoint (a shared helper, a stable local↔server identity contract, a server-side idempotency/upsert or dedup guard, a create-once-then-update pattern) rather than N independent spot-fixes — belt-and-suspenders on both client and server, like the SOW budget gate.
  - *Worked example (2026-07-12):* "autosave saves a new track-record line for every keystroke while typing an address." Root: a debounced *sync-the-working-set* that treated any locally-identified item as new (POST) and never adopted the server id back into the tool, so each pass re-created the row. Class: **any autosave/save-as-you-go create path that doesn't reconcile a new item to ONE server identity** — present anywhere a create fires on change. Fix: create-once + adopt the server id (client) on the reported tool, then audit **every** tool bridge, every React autosave/`+ Add`, every address-entry surface, and every repeatable POST endpoint (borrower AND staff) for the same class, plus consider server-side idempotency; and reconcile any duplicate rows already written.
- **Mobile: the portal must render at the DEVICE width, and `overflow-x:clip` on `<html>` is what guarantees it (learned 2026-07-12).** Symptom: on a phone the whole console rendered zoomed-out and tiny (readable only by pinch-zoom) even though nothing looked "broken." Root cause is a cascade — a *single* tiny phantom horizontal overflow (a 290px hover-tooltip that was `visibility:hidden` but still laid out, a flex/grid child missing `min-width:0`, a `white-space:nowrap` run) widens the **layout viewport** past 720px, which silently switches OFF every `@media(max-width:720px)` rule, so the portal falls back to its **desktop** layout and the browser zooms out to ~1292px. The reliable fix is `html{overflow-x:hidden;overflow-x:clip}` (in `app/src/styles.css`): it pins the layout viewport to the device width so the mobile breakpoints actually apply, and `clip` (not `hidden`) does **not** create a scroll container, so `position:sticky` keeps working. Verify mobile by checking **`window.innerWidth === 390`** on an iPhone-12 render — NOT `scrollWidth - innerWidth` (that reads 0 even when the viewport has blown up to 1292, masking the bug). Chromium + a global Playwright are available in this env (`require('/opt/node22/lib/node_modules/playwright')`, iPhone 12 device); boot the server against the local test PG, inject the token via `addInitScript`+`serviceWorkers:'block'`, and screenshot at the phone viewport. Also: form controls must be **≥16px** on phones (`@media(max-width:720px)`) or iOS Safari zooms the page on every field focus; the console navs collapse to a hamburger **drawer** (`.nav-toggle`/`.nav.open` in `StaffLayout.jsx`/`Layout.jsx`); centred `.cv-modal` overlays become bottom sheets on phones; and fixed bars use `env(safe-area-inset-*)`.
- **Understand the intent first, then research, then build (owner-directed, 2026-07-12):** the owner's messages are *intent*, not a literal spec — this CLI does not reason about them for you. Before touching code, work out what they actually mean: the underlying problem, the backend implications, and where they're coming from. Reason it through (an AI/reasoning pass on the message), read the relevant code, and research how comparable systems (e.g. other LOS platforms) solve it. Fix the goal, not the words.
- **NEVER GUESS A BUSINESS / SERVICING / WORKFLOW RULE — ASK THE OWNER AND WAIT (owner-directed 2026-08-13, after a table-funding/draws mistake). HARD RULE.** How the business actually runs — how draws are released, how a loan is serviced, what "sold at the closing table" means for the workflow, who moves money and when, what a status does downstream — is domain knowledge you do NOT have and cannot infer from a field name, an existing skip, or "the likelier reading". If a task's correct behavior depends on a business rule you are not 100% certain of, **STOP and ask the owner in plain language, and wait for the answer.** A wrong guess about money/servicing is far worse than a delayed question. Do not read a column called `table_funded` (or any flag) and invent what it should gate; confirm the real-world meaning first.
  - **THE CONCRETE CORRECTION (owner 2026-08-13): EVERY loan goes through the draw process — table-funded included. YS Capital is the servicer of record on every loan and runs its draws in PILOT, whether or not the loan was sold at closing and whether or not it is sold later.** Table-funding does **NOT** skip draws, and does NOT skip post-closing delivery. What differs is ONLY **who releases the draw money**, which the existing draw system already models (`src/sitewire/investor-delivery.js` funding modes): while YS still holds the loan it **releases the draw itself**; once the loan is sold — or it is **table-funded (sold at the closing table)** — the draw money comes through **investor delivery** (the note buyer funds it: `reimbursement` = YS wires the net to the borrower and the investor reimburses us the full approved amount; `investor_direct` = the investor wires the net to the borrower and our fee to us). For a loan drawn on BEFORE it is sold, YS releases those draws itself, then on selling recoups the advanced money from the investor and requests subsequent draws from the investor directly; a table-funded loan uses investor delivery from day one. The one thing table-funding legitimately skips is the **purchasing desk** (YS's own buy-back/resale workflow — it was already sold), which is pre-existing closing-panel behavior keyed on `closing_workflow.table_funded`. **Never re-add a guard that refuses/skips the Sitewire draw birth (`orchestrator.pushFile`), the borrower request-draw, or the coordinator start-draw for a table-funded file.**
- **Every fix is all-sides (owner-directed, 2026-07-12):** an issue almost always lives on more than one surface. For EVERY reported issue, fix and verify it on ALL affected surfaces — the **marketing site**, the **borrower login**, AND the **staff/internal login** — plus previous *and* future files (see the backfill rule). Default assumption: a condition / tool / behavior bug is present on all three until proven otherwise. Don't call a fix done until every surface is covered.
- **Merge conflicts: BOTH sides' enhancements must survive — never merge past a conflict carelessly (owner-directed, 2026-07-15).** Multiple sessions land work on `main` in parallel. Before EVERY merge: check mergeability first; if there is any conflict, STOP and resolve it deliberately — read both sides, understand what each change does, and produce a resolution where BOTH enhancements survive intact (never "ours"/"theirs" wholesale, never drop the other session's feature to make yours fit). For the built bundle (`web/v2/portal/*`), the only correct resolution is REBUILDING from the fully-merged `app-v2/src` — never pick one side's bundle. For colliding migration NUMBERS, renumber yours (never theirs — theirs may already be applied in prod) and update every reference. After resolving, re-run the test suites + eslint + a module-graph load on the MERGED tree before pushing, and say in the merge commit what conflicted and how both sides were preserved.
- **Commit + merge to main right away (owner-directed, 2026-07-12):** the owner has granted standing authorization to merge. Commit every change immediately and MERGE it to `main` without waiting to be told — never leave finished work sitting on a branch. Workflow per change (or tight batch): restart the branch from `main`, commit, push, open the PR, mark ready, squash-merge. **Deploying can wait** (the owner triggers Render deploys) — but the code must land on `main`.
- **NEVER merge with unresolved conflicts — resolve carefully so BOTH enhancements always survive (owner-directed, 2026-07-15; HARD RULE).** Another agent/session frequently works on `main` at the same time, so `main` moves under you. Every single time you go to merge: **(1)** re-fetch the newest `main` FIRST (`git fetch origin main`) and rebase/merge your branch onto it — never merge a branch built on a stale base; **(2)** if there are ANY conflicts, do NOT take one side blindly and NEVER let a merge auto-drop the other side's work — read BOTH versions, **understand what each change is doing and why**, and hand-write a resolution that KEEPS BOTH enhancements working together (e.g. the ClickUp ingest collision was resolved by keeping the parallel session's `healBorrowerFields(…, taskId)` DOB-review arg AND this session's `C.ssnForStorage` SSN chokepoint — not one or the other); **(3)** re-verify after resolving — `node --check` the changed files, boot the server against a throwaway PG so all migrations apply and the module graph loads, and confirm the markers of BOTH sides are present in the working tree before you push/merge; **(4)** renumber a colliding `db/NNN_*.sql` (two sessions grabbing the same number) to the next free number, and REBUILD the V2 bundle from the merged `app-v2/src` so it contains both sides' UI. A clobbered enhancement (either side's) is a release blocker. When in doubt, stop and re-read the diff rather than guess.
- **Never expose a note buyer / capital partner name** (BlueLake, Temple View, RCN, Churchill, Fidelis, etc.) on any borrower-facing surface — UI text, checklist template hints/labels, emails, PDFs. Borrower-facing copy calls it the **"Gold Standard program."** Staff-only surfaces may keep real names. Checklist items carry `borrower_label`/`borrower_hint` (borrower portal shows those) vs. the internal `label`/`hint` (staff).
- The pricing/guideline engines under `web/tools` (`window.YSP`/`GSP`/`TitleCost`, `termsheet.js` math) are the frozen source of truth. Reuse/wrap/port them into React — **never change their numbers or logic** except by an explicit owner-directed guideline change (then update this note and re-freeze).
- **HARD RULE — NEVER change the pricing/guideline LOGIC or NUMBERS without the owner's EXPLICIT WRITTEN authorization (owner-directed 2026-07-21).** This is absolute and applies to every guideline/pricing surface: the leverage MATRIX, `RA` rate build-up, markup/origination, FICO minimums, LTV/LTC/ARV caps, `MIN_LOAN`/`MAX_LOAN`, the `sizeLoan` waterfall, interest-reserve rules, assignment-fee math (15% cap / financeable fee / recognized price), the exit/profitability gate, rounding, title/liquidity math — in `standard-program.js` + `gold-standard.js` (all 6 copies each), `termsheet.js` (both), `title-cost.js`, `src/lib/pricing.js`, `src/lib/rehab-budget.js`, `src/lib/liquidity.js`, and any future guideline engine.
  - **Enhancements only.** New work must sit ON TOP of the frozen logic (new files, wrappers, routing, UI, notifications, escalation, classification of an existing engine result) and consume the engine output **without altering any formula, number, or matrix value**. Adding fields/gates in NON-frozen files (routes, `manual-program.js`, `terms-notify.js`, `workflow*.js`, React) is fine; touching a number or a sizing formula in a frozen engine is NOT — unless the owner has authorized that specific change IN WRITING.
  - **"Written authorization"** = the owner stating the specific guideline/number change in their own words in the conversation (or a doc they point to). A general "go / fix it / make it work" is NOT authorization to change a guideline number. When a task seems to require a guideline/number change, STOP and ask the owner for explicit written sign-off first; do the enhancement around it in the meantime.
  - **On every guideline/pricing change: re-verify by RUNTIME EQUIVALENCE.** Diff the frozen files against the pre-change baseline AND run both engine versions over a broad scenario battery asserting every numeric field (loan amount, initial, rehab, rate, caps, LTC/LTV/ARV, reserve, cost basis) is identical — the only permitted differences are the exact ones the owner authorized. Record the authorized change in the frozen-baseline notes above and re-freeze.
  - **Audited 2026-07-21 (re-freeze):** the loan-minimum enforcement (#519) + assignment-over-cap-registers/request-exception (#523) work was proven, over 28,800 old-vs-new engine evaluations, to change ZERO guideline numbers/sizing math. The only engine-level changes were the two the owner directed in writing: (1) the Standard assignment-over-cap reason level `MANUAL`→`ELIGIBLE` (a status classification, no number changed — so it registers with the excess as extra cash to close), and (2) the `termsheet.js` `statusText`/`statusLabel` DISPLAY wording for the manual-review status. `gold-standard.js` and `src/lib/pricing.js` were not touched. Everything else (enforcement, escalation, workflow, request-exception) lives in separate non-frozen files. This baseline is re-frozen.
  - **PER-EXPERIENCE-TIER MARKUP (owner-directed 2026-08-04, item 15 — AUTHORIZED engine change + re-freeze).** The owner: *"For the gold program, the top tier is set up in the backend so that it does not have any markup. We need to set that up in the admin pricing settings … for tier one, this markup, for tier two, this markup, for tier three, this markup … In the products and pricing term sheet generator manual section, we should have, for the gold program, a separate section for the top tier … We should be able to place a markup manually. Do research on how to make this model setup so that for every tier we should be able to control the markups for every program differently."* This is the explicit written authorization the HARD RULE requires.
    · **The engine change is an OPTIONAL OVERLAY that is INERT when unset — proven byte-identical.** Each frozen engine (`standard-program.js`, `gold-standard.js`, `silver-program.js` — both `web/tools` + `web/v2/tools` copies) gained `var MARKUP_TIERS = null; setMarkupTiers(m); ` and a tier-aware markup resolver: Standard/Silver `effMarkup(tier)`, Gold `markupFor(tier)`. When `MARKUP_TIERS` is null the resolver returns EXACTLY the historic value (Standard = base/override markup; Gold Tier 1 = 0, other tiers base/override; Silver = clamped base/override, MARKUP_MAX still honored). A configured tier value (a number ≥ 0) wins for that tier only. Proven RUNTIME-EQUIVALENT: original vs edited engines, tiers UNSET, produced BYTE-IDENTICAL output over **77,760 scenarios** (programs × states × loan types × FICO × experience tiers × deals × assignment × IR), re-verified from a clean git stash. `setMarkupTiers` is exposed on each engine's public API; the two copies of each engine stay identical (`test-engine-copies-match.js`).
    · **The config is stored in two places, both NULL/absent by default (so previous AND future files are unchanged):** (1) COMPANY per-tier defaults — `company_pricing_settings.markup_tiers` jsonb (`db/466`), shaped `{ standard:{1,2,3}, gold, silver }` of PERCENTS, set in the Pricing Admin Center (`StaffCompanyPricing.jsx` grid), read by `pricing-settings.cleanMarkupTiers`; (2) the STUDIO's manual GOLD top-tier markup — sticky per-file `applications.file_markup_gold_t1_pct` (`db/466`, written only by the register path, mirrors `file_markup_gold_pct`) + the studio field `tsYspGoldT1`. `pricing.js markupTiersFor(program,input,cd)` merges company defaults (÷100) with the per-file gold-t1 override and `setEngineMarkupTiers` pushes the map before each `quoteProgram` evaluate, RESET to null in every `finally` (all 3 branches) exactly like `setEngineMarkup` — the engine is only ever "hot" during a quote, no leakage.
    · **Gold Tier 1 is now controllable** — the owner's core ask ("we don't even have an option to control the best tier's markup"). Setting it via the company map OR the studio field raises the Gold top-tier note rate by exactly the configured markup; unset it stays 0. A per-file gold-t1 markup is a deliberate exception → routes to admin approval (`markupGoldT1Pct` in `pricing-overrides.js ENGAGED_OVERRIDE_KEYS`). A BORROWER can never set it (`borrowerPricingOverrides` allowlist) and never see it (`stripInputsInternal`). Silver per-tier values stay hard-capped at 1.00%.
    · Test `scripts/test-tier-markup-pure.js` (in `npm test`, no DB): unset ⇒ historic markup; a configured tier ⇒ note rate = pure grid rate + that exact markup; configuring one (program, tier) moves ONLY that program's that-tier scenarios; the per-file gold-t1 override behaves identically and touches nothing else. This baseline is re-frozen — changing the overlay mechanics or a default needs fresh owner sign-off.
  - **TOTAL COST / LTC GO BY THE LOWER OF THE PURCHASE PRICE AND THE AS-IS VALUE (owner-directed 2026-08-05 — AUTHORIZED engine change + re-freeze).** The owner, on the products & pricing term sheet generator: *"The way the system calculates the total cost is wrong … we should always use the purchase price. If the as-is value is more than the purchase price we should not use the as-is value, we should use the purchase price. If the as-is value is less than the purchase price then we can't use the default purchase price, we need to use that as-is value. This is for all the programs … we need to use that as-is value for the cost calculation and not the purchase price."* This is the explicit written authorization the HARD RULE requires. **Don't touch the interest reserve** (owner-directed) — it is added on top exactly as before.
    · **The one line that moved:** `standard-program.js sizeLoan` (both `web/tools` + `web/v2/tools` copies, byte-identical per `test-engine-copies-match.js`; `gold-standard.js`/`silver-program.js` reuse `YSP.sizeLoan`, so all three programs inherit it) — `costBasis0 = (purchase ? pp : aiv) + rehab` → **`costBasis0 = acqDenom + rehab`**. `acqDenom` was ALREADY `purchase ? min(pp, aiv) : aiv` (the number the initial-advance cap `A = maxAcqLTV × acqDenom` uses), so the cost basis and the acq cap now read ONE figure and the LTC can never be measured against a value the appraisal doesn't support. On an assignment `pp` is the effective/recognized price, so it is `min(effPrice, aiv)`.
    · **Proven RUNTIME-EQUIVALENT + correct over 816,480 old-vs-new scenarios** (3 programs × states × loan types × FICO × experience × prices × as-is deltas × rehabs × IR × terms × assignment): BYTE-IDENTICAL whenever the as-is value ≥ the price, on a refinance, and on any product with no LTC cap (the acq-LTV cap binds before the LTC term either way). When the as-is value < the price on an LTC-capped purchase: the acquisition (initial) advance NEVER grows, the loan never exceeds the TRUE LTC cap, and `acqDenom` = min(effPrice, as-is). The only total-loan movement (≤0.83%) is the frozen reserve/rate feedback — a higher true LTC prices at a higher rate, so the financed reserve is a touch larger — always within the LTC cap; the interest-reserve math itself is untouched. The exit/value-add gate (`exitShortfall`/`basisForExit`) deliberately KEEPS the real purchase price (changing it would loosen eligibility on unprofitable deals), which is why the Silver EMCAP workbook still ties out over 204,000 scenarios.
    · **Every surface that carries this basis moved with it:** the studio total-cost DISPLAY reads the engine's `acqDenom` in `termsheet.js calc/calcGold/calcSilver` (v1 + v2) so the printed "Total project cost" reconciles with the LTC (the "Purchase price" line still shows the REAL price); `src/lib/pricing.js normalize` inherits it (it reads `s.costBasis`/`s.ltcPct` from the engine); the underwriting LTC re-derivations that must stay in step — `underwriting/metrics.js` (LTC ratio; LTP/LTV keep their own bases) and `underwriting/structure-underwriter.js` (fallback cost basis) — use `min(price, as-is)`; the AI grounding text in `underwriting/loan-primer.js` and the pre-quote ≈LTC estimate in `app-v2 DealSnapshot.jsx` say the same. Test `scripts/test-cost-basis-as-is-pure.js` (in `npm test`, no DB). This baseline is re-frozen — reopening it (or the exit gate's use of the purchase price) needs fresh owner sign-off.
  - **SILVER (EMCAP) — THE PRICE LADDER STEPS ON VALUE AS WELL AS COST (owner-directed 2026-08-06 — AUTHORIZED engine change + re-freeze).** The owner, on a live deal: *"EMCAP's LTC level is the same between 87.5–90 but based on the ARV I am falling below 70 ARV at 89 LTC which gets me better rates due to the lower ARV, but I'm not able to adjust the LTC to 89 — I can only do 90 or 87.5."* Then, choosing the shape: *"we should build ONE ladder which you can slide down to reduce your loan amount. On the backend it should automatically calculate whenever you get an ARV cut and whenever you get an LTC cut. You shouldn't be able to move it by $1 — you should only be able to move it when you get better pricing."* And: *"we need to make sure that both of them are driving the pricing of both of them, because if you're reducing the ARV it's very possible that LTC is also getting reduced."* This is the explicit written authorization the HARD RULE requires. Design + the full blast-radius table: `docs/SILVER-PRICE-LADDER-REDESIGN.md`.
    · **NO PRICING LOGIC CHANGED, AND THAT IS THE FINDING THAT DE-RISKED THE WHOLE JOB.** The cap-edge lookup at the top of `evaluate` is only a GUESS used to seed the financed interest reserve; the real rate comes from `rateAt(sizing)`, which classifies `totalLoan / arv` and `sizing.ltcPct` — the ratios the sized loan ACTUALLY achieves. So the owner's "both of them are driving the pricing of both of them" was ALREADY the engine's behaviour: cutting one ratio shrinks the loan, which moves the other ratio, and both are re-read and re-priced together. Likewise the ARV wall (`capARV = maxARLTV × arv`) already caps the TOTAL loan with the rehab holdback financed first, so the owner's *"if it's capping something it should cap the initial"* needed no change either.
    · **THE ONE ENGINE CHANGE IS A VOLUNTARY REDUCTION, MIRRORING `targetLTC` EXACTLY** — `if (input.targetARLTV > 0) capsEff.maxARLTV = Math.min(capsEff.maxARLTV, input.targetARLTV)` in `silver-program.js evaluate()` (BOTH copies, `web/tools` + `web/v2/tools`, kept byte-identical by `test-engine-copies-match.js`). It is a MIN on a ceiling, so it can never raise a cap and can never over-lend, and it is INERT when unset. Do not confuse it with `ovrARLTV`, the ADMIN override that CAN raise a cap — different key, different authority. Proven RUNTIME-EQUIVALENT: with the lever unset the engine is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pre-change engine over **8,100 scenarios** (states × strategies × FICO × terms × ARVs × rehabs × experience), with the baseline built by **removing the lever line** from today's engine — NOT by reading git; see the paragraph below, which is the method to copy. A 0 / null / blank lever reads as "no lever", never as a zero cap, and a lever set ABOVE every cap the program allows changes NOTHING — rate, fees and cash included.
    · **A REAL DEFECT WAS FIXED, not only a feature added.** The old Silver ladder's rungs were `edgesDesc(LTC_EDGES_UP, AR_EDGES_UP)` — the LTC edges and the ARV edges MERGED INTO ONE LIST — and every rung was then applied as `evaluate({targetLTC: b})`. So the ARV edges (0.65 / 0.70) were being used as LOAN-TO-COST caps, producing far smaller loans under a "Leverage (LTC)" label. `priceLadder` now builds candidates per family with its OWN lever and keeps a rung ONLY when the note rate genuinely improves (the owner's "you should only be able to move it when you get better pricing"), each rung being the LARGEST loan that earns that better price. **Accepted consequence, flagged to the owner:** the bogus 65% / 70% "LTC" rows disappear and correct ARV rows replace them, so a re-generated Silver sheet will not match one printed before the fix — the SELECTED structure, its rate and its numbers do not change, only the alternatives list.
    · **A RUNG IS IDENTIFIED BY ITS KEY, NEVER BY A LEVERAGE VALUE.** Two rungs from the two families can legitimately land on the SAME LTC, so a number cannot identify a rung: the term sheet would highlight the wrong row on a document that goes out for signature, and the studio would price the wrong axis. Every rung carries `key` (`'max'` / `'ltc:0.875'` / `'arv:0.7'`), `cut`, BOTH achieved ratios, and `ltc: null` on a value-side rung. `applySilverRung` in `termsheet.js` is the ONE place a key becomes an engine input. Standard and Gold still select by LTC value and are unchanged.
    · **THE PAPER AND THE FILE MUST AGREE.** `targetARLTV` rides the same path `targetLTC` does — both studio snapshots, `overridesFromSnapshot`, the `buildInputs` NUMK whitelist, and the borrower door (clamped to a real ratio). Without it a value-side sheet would register the deal's MAXIMUM loan while the borrower signed the smaller, better-priced one, with nothing erroring anywhere. It is deliberately NOT in `ENGAGED_OVERRIDE_KEYS`: like `targetLTC` it is a voluntary reduction anyone may choose, not an admin-zone knob needing approval.
    · **Silver only, for now** (owner-directed). Gold has a flat rate and already suppresses the ladder page; Standard has no ARV band. A manual admin basis still suppresses the ladder for every program.
