Tests `scripts/test-silver-arv-lever-pure.js` (the equivalence proof + the ladder's own invariants + the studio's key parser) and `scripts/test-silver-arv-register-carry-pure.js` (the whole chain end to end, proven to bite), both in `npm test`. This baseline is re-frozen — reopening the lever, the rung rule, or extending the ladder to another program needs fresh owner sign-off.
  - **A TYPED LOAN AMOUNT — the manual box, on ALL THREE programs (owner-directed 2026-08-06 — AUTHORIZED engine change + re-freeze).** The owner: *"we need to add to the manual section for our staff team to manually type in a loan amount. It should also calculate the same thing: first, it's a fully financed budget. If you put in a manual out-of-pocket rehab, the rest is for the initial. Even if the loan amount is more than it's allowed, they also need to put in manually that they allow these limits. It should technically be made to reduce, but it should also be able to increase only if you also fill in the manual program details about exact LTV, LTC, and ARV."* Asked whether it should be Silver-only or every program, the owner chose **all programs** — Standard, Gold and Silver.
    · **IT IS A VOLUNTARY CEILING, NOT A NEW WAY TO SIZE A LOAN — and that is the whole design.** One line per engine (`standard-program.js`, `gold-standard.js`, `silver-program.js`, both copies of each, kept byte-identical): `if (input.targetLoan > 0) caps.maxLoan = Math.min(caps.maxLoan, input.targetLoan)`. **NO SIZING MATH CHANGED.** `sizeLoan` already finances the rehab in FULL first, gives the remainder to the initial advance, and grows monotonically to the nearest wall — so capping the wall lands the structure on the typed amount with the owner's own split intact, for free. `targetLoan` is a MIN on a ceiling exactly like `targetLTC`/`targetARLTV`, so it is inert when unset and can never over-lend.
    · **"REDUCE ONLY" IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A VALIDATION RULE.** Because it can only lower a ceiling, an amount typed ABOVE what the caps allow is simply UNREACHABLE — proven, not asserted (28,512 scenarios, including amounts typed at 125% of the maximum: zero cases where the loan grew). That is what makes the box safe to hand to staff with **no approval**: it is the same class as a ladder rung, so `targetLoan` is deliberately NOT in `ENGAGED_OVERRIDE_KEYS`. **THE WAY UP IS THE MANUAL BASIS** (`ovrAcqLTV` / `ovrARLTV` / `ovrLTC`), which already routes to admin approval — which is precisely the owner's *"it should also be able to increase only if you also fill in the manual program details about exact LTV, LTC, and ARV."*
    · **AN AMOUNT TOO SMALL TO FINANCE THE REHAB IS RAISED, NEVER SILENTLY UNDER-FINANCED.** You cannot fit a $180,000 rehab inside a $168,000 loan — arithmetic, not policy — and the engine already answers that case with a **MANUAL** reason ("the rehab budget exceeds what this program can finance"), so it goes to review. The test pins BOTH halves: while the amount can cover the rehab the construction budget is untouched and the cut lands on the INITIAL advance (20,736 cases); when it cannot, it is flagged every single time (424 cases, 0 silent). Do not "fix" this by trimming the rehab quietly.
    · **THE EQUIVALENCE BASELINE IS BUILT BY REMOVING THE LEVER LINE, NOT BY READING GIT — AND THE STRIP NEEDS THREE GUARDS, NOT ONE. Use this whole pattern for any future frozen-engine overlay; each guard below exists because an adversarial audit defeated the version without it.** (1) A `git show HEAD:` baseline proves inertness only until the change is COMMITTED; the moment it lands, HEAD carries the lever too and the comparison degenerates into "the engine equals itself" — passing forever while proving nothing. (2) The strip regex MUST be `/g` and the guard MUST count MATCHES, not replacements: `String.replace` with a non-global regex rewrites only the FIRST occurrence, so a decoy second line of the same shape leaves the real lever in the baseline and makes the proof compare lever-against-lever. Assert **exactly one** match per file, **and** that no `input.<lever>` reference survives the strip — the two catch different sabotages (an identical-shape decoy vs. a differently-shaped one). (3) A loan-size comparison CANNOT see a change that leaves the size alone, so also assert that a lever set **above** anything that could bind is a COMPLETE no-op, comparing the whole priced result including the RATE. Without (2) and (3) together, engines sabotaged to re-price at the FICO floor and to add a hidden +50 bps markup on every value-side rung both reported ALL PASS. Proven byte-identical over **9,720 evaluations** (3 programs × 3,240 scenarios) with the box empty, and a 0 / blank / NaN box reads as "no amount", never as a zero ceiling.
    · Surface: `tsTargetLoan` in the studio's admin zone (its own group, wording that states the reduce/increase rule), read by `gather()` on EVERY program like the out-of-pocket rehab box. Carry-through: `TermSheetStudio` snapshot + restore, `overridesFromSnapshot`, the `buildInputs` NUMK whitelist. **STAFF ONLY** — the borrower register route's `borrowerPricingOverrides` allowlist never accepts it. Test `scripts/test-target-loan-pure.js` (in `npm test`). This baseline is re-frozen — changing the lever's mechanics, or making the box able to RAISE a loan without the approval-gated basis, needs fresh owner sign-off.
  - **THE COMPANY MARKUP REACHES THE RATE — restating a default must never FREEZE it onto a file (owner-reported 2026-08-20 — AUTHORIZED fix; this changes no formula and no default, it stops a per-file override being created that nobody asked for).** The owner: *"The pricing was previously set for the silver program and for the standard program to 0.4 markup on the rate. I went to admin settings and changed it to 0.5 markup, but the actual rates that are showing for the people and for the staff are still 0.4 … When they're trying to register, every single registration goes for an exception because 0.4 and not 0.5."*
    · **ONE ROOT CAUSE, BOTH SYMPTOMS, REPRODUCED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS CHANGED.** `web/v2/tools/termsheet.js seedAdminDefaults()` PAINTED the company default of the day into `tsYspStd`/`tsYspGold`/`tsYspSilver` (and the origination + fee boxes) as the field's **value**. A value in that box is, by every downstream contract, an EXPLICIT PER-FILE OVERRIDE: `readSnapshot` harvests it → `overridesFromSnapshot` sends `markupStdPct` → the register path STICKS it on `applications.file_markup_*_pct` (db/109). So **every registration silently froze that day's company default onto that file.** Change the default afterwards and (a) `buildInputs` re-applies the frozen number and `quoteProgram` prefers it over the company default — MEASURED at 10.300% against 10.400%, a 10-basis-point gap, which is exactly "still 0.4"; and (b) re-opening the studio restores 0.4 through `adminStateFromEngineInputs`, so the next register sends 0.4 against a 0.5 default and `pricingOverridesEngaged` reads a **discount** → an admin approval on every single registration. The register path's own comment already stated the intended contract — *"a live company-default registration (no markup key) never freezes the default onto the file"* — and seeding the VALUE is precisely what broke it.
    · **THE FIX IS THE CONTRACT THE REST OF THE SYSTEM ALREADY ASSUMED: BLANK MEANS "use the company default".** The studio now shows the default as a **placeholder** (`data-ts-seeded` still stamped, so the "In use" chip is unchanged) and never writes it into `value`; a blank box sends `''`, which `buildInputs` deletes, `hasValue()` reads as not-an-override, and the register path's `hasOwnProperty` check writes NULL over any stale sticky — so **an already-frozen file HEALS on its next registration.** The studio's own math is untouched (`adminNum` has always read `CO` for a blank field). Re-locking the admin panel now clears those boxes rather than re-filling them, for the same reason.
    · **AND THE SAME RULE AT THE SERVER, so a stale cached bundle or an officer typing the default back behaves identically:** `pricing-overrides.normalizeCompanyDefaultKnobs(raw, defaults)` maps a knob whose value **exactly** equals its company default to `''`, applied inside `sanitizeStaffOverrides` (the one chokepoint the register + quote doors already share). **EXACT equality, not the approval detector's cent tolerance** — an exact restatement resolves through the company default to the byte-identical number, so it is PROVABLY price-neutral (108 priced deals across all three programs, zero drift in rate, loan, origination, every closing cost, cash-to-close and liquidity). The detector keeps its tolerance, which is a superset, so a near-miss still raises no approval either way. It answers with `''` and **never by dropping the key**: a dropped key could not CLEAR a stale sticky.
    · **AND THE ALREADY-FROZEN FILES, TWO WAYS.** `pricing-settings.asOf(when)` reads the company defaults that were IN FORCE at a past moment (the settings table is append-only, so this is a FACT, not a guess). `GET /pricing` uses it to blank any admin knob on the CURRENT registration that merely restated the default of ITS day, so the studio opens with an empty box instead of re-arming 0.4 — the stored `product_registrations` row is never rewritten, only the copy the panel prefills from. **db/600** does the same to the live value: it clears `applications.file_markup_{std,gold,silver}_pct` where it exactly equals the default in force when the file's current registration was created, **only on files whose economics are still open** (a clear-to-close / funded / declined / withdrawn file, or one with a LIVE Term Sheet package out for signature, keeps its frozen markup — those terms are settled or signed), auditing each release because the value is gone from the row afterwards. **Triggers are left ON deliberately:** the note rate genuinely moves, so db/126/486 reopening Products & Pricing is the correct outcome — the opposite call to db/399, which disabled them precisely because that change moved no number the engine reads. `file_markup_gold_t1_pct` is untouched: the studio never seeded `tsYspGoldT1`, so any value there was typed on purpose.
    · Tests `scripts/test-company-default-markup-pure.js` (the normalizer's whole truth table, the price-neutrality proof, the blank clearing a frozen markup, the approval rules still standing — a discount still escalates, charging more still does not — the owner's measured 10-bp gap before and after, and SOURCE guards that the studio can never paint a default into a value again) and `scripts/test-markup-heal-db.js` (db/600 against a REAL Postgres through the REAL boot replay: that it APPLIED AT ALL — a migration that throws is logged and skipped SILENTLY, which is the failure mode that test exists for — what it released, what it left alone, the timeline being read from history, the audit line, and a second boot changing nothing). Both are in `npm test`; three mutations of the normalizer and the studio, and four of the migration, were each proven to fail them. **NOT changed, deliberately:** the V1 tool at `/v1` (parked by owner direction 2026-08-04) — its `termsheet.js` also seeds values and additionally ignores `markupTiers` entirely, so a per-tier markup set in the Pricing Center is not applied there; it registers no files, so it cannot freeze anything, but a rate quoted from `/v1` can differ from the server's. Raise it with the owner before touching a parked surface.
  - **OUT-OF-POCKET REHAB IS ALLOWED WHEN IT IS UNAVOIDABLE (owner-directed 2026-08-20 — AUTHORIZED engine change + re-freeze; a VERDICT moves, no number moves).** The owner: *"The system is still not allowing out-of-pocket rehabs when it comes to very small loan amounts … for an extremely small purchase and the construction is very high, the LTC cap is happening so quickly that we can't fund anything on the initial. The gap is happening too fast, and it's blocking us too fast … the initial goes down to zero, but we don't even have enough to fund 100% of the construction budget. Technically, we only have to fund 90% of the construction budget, and the system is still blocking it … If the initial is already at zero, then you should automatically allow out-of-pocket rehab without an exception because there is no way from where it's cut."*
    · **WHAT THE OLD RULE ACTUALLY MEANT.** "We don't allow out-of-pocket rehab" is a rule about WHERE a cap takes its bite — when the LTC or ARV ceiling cuts the loan, the cut comes off the INITIAL advance and the construction budget stays financed in full. It was never a rule that the borrower may not fund construction. `sizeLoan`'s `rehabOverCap` flag raised **MANUAL** unconditionally, so a tiny purchase carrying a large budget was blocked even though the structure was already the best one available. MEASURED on the owner's own shape — a $30,000 purchase with a $400,000 budget — all three programs answered MANUAL while financing **99.4% / 96.8% / 99.4%** of the budget with the initial advance already at ZERO.
    · **THE RULE, in `YSP.sizeLoan` (which all three programs size on, so there is ONE definition):** once the initial advance is zero there is nothing left for the caps to take, so a holdback below the full budget is arithmetic rather than a choice — allowed automatically, no exception, PROVIDED the loan still finances at least **`REHAB_FINANCED_FLOOR` = 90%** of the construction budget (the industry's standard construction-financing floor on this product class, and the owner's own number). Below the floor it is a real feasibility question and still goes to manual review with the original wording. Each engine's `evaluate` swaps its unconditional `add("MANUAL", …)` for `if (rehabOverCap && !rehabOopAuto)` plus an `ELIGIBLE` **disclosure** naming the financed percentage and the out-of-pocket dollars, so the term sheet states it rather than hiding it.
    · **NOT ONE NUMBER MOVES.** Proven over **129,600 evaluations**: with the floor neutralized the engines are byte-identical on every deal the rule does not reach (drift 0), and on the **4,776** it does reach the loan, initial advance, holdback, financed reserve, leverage percentages, payments, caps and note rate are all byte-identical — only the status and the reason line change. `rehabUnfinanced` / `rehabFinancedPct` / `rehabOopAuto` are additive and inert elsewhere, and `pricing.js` carries them onto `quote.sizing` (DISTINCT from `oopRehab`, which is the APPROVED exception amount an admin enters — this is what the wall could not finance in the first place).
    · **HONEST NOTE, MEASURED not assumed: the "initial advance is zero" half of the guard is REDUNDANT in today's waterfall.** Over 129,600 evaluations the sizer produced a capped rehab with a positive initial advance **zero** times — the caps reach the rehab only once the initial has already been clamped to zero. It is kept because it is the owner's stated rule and because a future change to the waterfall must not silently widen the auto-allow; section B1 of the test is what would catch that.
    · Test `scripts/test-oop-rehab-auto-pure.js` (in `npm test`), which also **found a real defect while being written**: the disclosed shortfall and percentage were derived from the internal (unrounded) holdback, so they did not reconcile to the cent with the rounded figure printed beside them — 1,560 scenarios. Three mutations of the production code were proven to fail the suite. The equivalence machinery now lives in `scripts/lib/engine-baseline.js` — ONE definition of the lever-strip baseline, extracted from `test-target-loan-pure.js` and `test-silver-arv-lever-pure.js`, with `assertStripBit` for the levers that are neutralized rather than deleted. This baseline is re-frozen — moving the 90% floor, or widening the rule past a zero initial advance, needs fresh owner sign-off.
  - **A JUDICIAL-STATE PURCHASE UNDER $100,000 IS AN EXCEPTION PRODUCT THAT NEEDS $20,000 DOWN — STANDARD PROGRAM ONLY (owner-directed 2026-08-20 — AUTHORIZED guideline addition + re-freeze).** The owner: *"for any property that is in any jurisdictional state … This is only for the standard. It's not for the gold, and it's not for the silver, and it's not for the manual. Even if the loan amount is more than $100,000, if the property is in the jurisdictional state and the purchase price is less than $100,000, they don't want to do it … it should come up as an exception product … he needs to put down at least a $20,000 down payment. No matter what the purchase price is … They want to see skin in the game … but leverage doesn't change. The loan amount gets maybe lower … but then you can offer the entire budgets with all the same cap according to the experience."*
    · **THE TEST IS THE PURCHASE PRICE, NOT THE LOAN AMOUNT**, and that is the point: a small, cheap property in a judicial (court-supervised) foreclosure state is where a default is slowest and most expensive to work out, and the loan amount says nothing about that — a $60,000 house with a $300,000 budget is a large loan on a small asset. "Jurisdictional state" is the **`JUDICIAL`** list `standard-program.js` already keeps (one definition, so the rate add-on and this rule can never disagree about which states they are). The test runs on `effPurchase`, the RECOGNIZED price on an assignment — the same figure `sizeLoan` subtracts the down payment from, so the floor is arithmetically exact.
    · **HOW IT IS ENFORCED — a dollar FLOOR on the borrower's own money, never a change to leverage.** `capsEff.minDownPayment` rides on the caps row and `sizeLoan` applies it as one more MIN on the acquisition advance: `A = min(maxAcqLTV × acqDenom, price − 20,000)`. Every LTV / LTC / ARV / dollar cap, the experience tier and the whole construction budget are UNTOUCHED — the initial advance, and therefore the total loan, simply cannot rise above the point where the borrower's own $20,000 is in the deal. `downPayment = price − initial` is the engine's own definition, so the floor is satisfied **by construction** rather than checked afterwards, at every later step of the waterfall. It registers **MANUAL**, which is what routes it to the super-admin escalation, and the reason states the requirement in words for the term sheet; `ev.judicialSmallPurchase` publishes the same requirement as data (carried onto `quote.guidelines`) so no surface re-derives it.
    · **EXCLUSIONS, and why each one.** Purchases only (there is no down payment on a refinance). Gold and Silver never set `minDownPayment`, so it is structurally inert there. And never on a MANUAL product — a manual basis is an admin setting the LTV / LTC / ARV themselves, so a hidden extra floor would silently overrule the basis they just approved; the manual test is the same structural-override set `manual-program.js` uses, so the two agree. A price at exactly $100,000 is not "less than", and a price at or under $20,000 leaves no initial advance at all (the reason says so in those words).
    · **THE FLOOR CANNOT BE UNDONE BY ANOTHER EXCEPTION, and that hole was found by adversarial review rather than by the matrix.** `pricing.js normalize()` lets an approved out-of-pocket-rehab exception RAISE the initial advance back toward its ceiling, and that ceiling was `maxAcqLTV × acqDenom` alone — it knew nothing about this rule, so *"raise the initial to its max"* would have lifted the initial straight over the $20,000 floor and silently undone the requirement the term sheet had just printed. `maxInitial` is now the MIN of every ceiling on the initial advance, `price − minDownPayment` included; it is `Infinity` on every deal no rule touched, so the exception behaves exactly as before everywhere else (pinned with a non-judicial control). An admin who genuinely needs more still has the manual basis, which this rule excludes itself from.
    · **THE REASON'S FIRST SENTENCE HAS TO STAND ALONE**, because the staff panel's badge shows only that (`shortReason` in `ProductStudioPanel` cuts at the first `" — "` and then the first `". "`). Leading with the label put *"Manual review needed: EXCEPTION PRODUCT"* on screen and said nothing; it now reads *"Exception product: a purchase under $100,000 in NJ, a judicial-foreclosure state."* and the full text — the $20,000 requirement, why it exists, and what it does to the structure — prints on the term sheet's eligibility notes, where the owner asked for it. Apply the same test to any future engine reason.
    · Proven over **96,768 evaluations**: the rule fires on exactly the deals the owner described and no others (0 wrong, 0 missed, judged against a predicate written from the owner's words rather than the engine's own), everything else is byte-identical to the engine without it, and on the **5,760** it reaches the borrower always puts at least $20,000 in (or the whole price when the price is under that), no cap or tier moves, the construction budget is never financed less, and the loan can only ever REDUCE. Test `scripts/test-judicial-small-purchase-pure.js` (in `npm test`); four mutations — applying it to a manual product, dropping the floor to $5,000, making it ELIGIBLE instead of an exception, and applying it to refinances — were each proven to fail it. This baseline is re-frozen — the state list, the $100,000 ceiling, the $20,000 floor and the Standard-only scope all need fresh owner sign-off to change.
  - **THE CONSTRUCTION FEASIBILITY / PROJECT REVIEW FEE — $1,250 ground-up, $750 heavy rehab, manual on anything (owner-directed 2026-08-21 — AUTHORIZED fee addition + re-freeze; db/609).** The owner: *"On the Term Sheets for Ground Up Construction Projects, add a $1,250 ground up construction feasibility review fee and general project review. Make research for exactly how to quote all this fee. For heavy rehab projects, add the same type of fee … but it should be only a $750 extra fee for this. Implement that into the term sheet generator, into the products and pricing, into the closing cost calculation, and everywhere else where it's considered. Also add this fee type into the manual section in the products and pricing so we can, any time, add it to any other project manually as well."* This is the explicit written authorization the HARD RULE requires.
    · **THE RESEARCH THE OWNER ASKED FOR.** On a construction loan the lender does not take the borrower's budget on trust: a third-party construction risk firm reads the plans, the permits, the contractor's numbers and the schedule and answers whether the project can be built for that money in that time. The industry calls it a **feasibility study** on a ground-up and a **plan-and-cost review** (or document/budget review) on a rehab — the same product at different depths, which is exactly why the owner priced them differently and called them the same type of fee. On this book it is Trinity's report. It is a real third-party cost with an invoice behind it — **never a lender margin** — which is why it is quoted as its own NAMED line rather than folded into the origination.
    · **HOW IT IS QUOTED, AND THE ONE THING THAT NEEDED DECIDING.** Two honest treatments exist and this codebase already contains both: a **closing cost** collected at the table (`closingDueAtClose`, which cascades into cash-to-close and the liquidity the borrower must SHOW), or **paid outside closing** like the appraisal (`appraisalPoc` — listed but NOT in cash-to-close). The report is ordered before closing, which argues for the second; but a fee left out of cash-to-close is a fee we never verify the borrower has, and under-stating what somebody must bring to a table is the expensive direction. **So it is quoted as a CLOSING COST** — the conservative reading, and the one that cannot leave a borrower short. Reopening that is a one-line change in `feasibility-fee.js` and nowhere else.
    · **NOT ONE FROZEN NUMBER MOVES, and that is proven rather than asserted.** The loan amount, the note rate, the initial advance, the holdback, the reserve and every leverage ratio are computed above this line and never read it. `scripts/test-feasibility-fee-pure.js` prices a **576-scenario battery** (4 strategies × 4 rehab types × 4 states × 2 sizes × 3 engines) twice — once with the module NEUTRALIZED, which is exactly "the system without this fee" — and asserts every no-fee deal is BYTE-IDENTICAL while every fee-bearing deal moves ONLY its cost, with cash-to-close and closing costs each rising by exactly the fee. **The baseline is built by neutralizing the module, deliberately NOT by reading git**: a git baseline proves inertness only until the change is committed, after which it degenerates into "the engine equals itself" and passes forever while proving nothing. It also asserts the baseline genuinely DIFFERS somewhere, or "identical" would be a tautology.
    · **`src/lib/feasibility-fee.js` IS THE ONE DEFINITION** — the amounts, which deals attract them, what each is called, the explanation that rides with it, and how a manual amount overrides it. PURE (no database, no config, no requires), so every rule is unit-testable.
    · **THE DEAL-TYPE TEST IS ORDERED, AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING.** `input.heavyRehab` is derived from `/heavy|gut|ground/` on the rehab type, so it is **TRUE on a ground-up as well** — asking it first prices every ground-up at $750. Ground-up is decided FIRST, off the **frozen engine's own classifier** (`normStrategy` → "NC"), and the pure test runs that engine's classifier beside ours over the whole label battery (including "Fix & Flip w/ Construction", the label that trips this every time) and fails the moment they disagree — because a loan priced on the ground-up matrix must never be charged the heavy-rehab fee.
    · **THE STUDIO CARRIES A BROWSER MIRROR, and it is held to the server by a test that runs BOTH.** `web/v2/tools/termsheet.js` cannot require server code (the `lib/payoff.js` arrangement), so it has its own copy of the rule; a studio that PRINTS one fee while the register BOOKS another is the exact drift the one-definition rule exists to stop, so 60 cases are run through both and any disagreement fails the build (mutation-proven in both directions).
    · **THE MANUAL BOX** is `tsFeasFee` in the studio's admin zone, sticky per file (`applications.file_feasibility_fee`, db/609). **Blank = the deal's own fee; a typed amount overrides it — including on a deal that attracts none, which is the whole point; a typed 0 WAIVES it.** Any typed value goes to an admin for approval, **0 included** — waiving $1,250 is the decision an admin most wants to see. That needed a per-key `zeroIsEngaged` flag rather than a change to the shared `engaged()`, whose "0 means unset" reading is correct for every other knob (and `markupGoldT1Pct`, where 0 IS the historic default, is the opposite case). A BORROWER can never set it (`borrowerPricingOverrides` never carries it), and neither can a TPO broker.
    · **db/609 DELIBERATELY DOES NOT WIDEN THE ECONOMICS-REOPEN TRIGGER**, because the column can only ever be written as part of a REGISTRATION (the staff register route, beside the sticky markups it mirrors), so there is never a stale registration for the trigger to catch. **The first cut DID widen it — by reading the live function's own source with `pg_get_functiondef` and splicing a clause in — and that was clever and wrong**: this function has been re-created by SIXTEEN migrations, its anchor text had already drifted, and a splice that misses its anchor leaves the column unwatched while the migration reports success. Section F of the pure test enforces the single-writer claim, so the next door added cannot quietly invalidate it.
    · **NO BACKFILL OF FILES.** `file_feasibility_fee` stays NULL everywhere, which reads as "use the company default", so a live ground-up picks the fee up on its next quote without anybody stamping a number onto a deal priced before this existed; a file already out for signature is protected by the ordinary term-sheet freeze. The company pair is seeded onto the current settings row only where nobody has set their own. **V1 (`web/tools`) is deliberately untouched** — parked by owner direction 2026-08-04 and it registers no files.
    · Test `scripts/test-feasibility-fee-pure.js` (in `npm test`, 48 checks); **five mutations** — asking the heavy-rehab flag first, leaking the fee into the sized loan, letting it move the note rate, an unreadable company setting silently zeroing the fee, and dropping the approval on a waiver — were each proven to fail it, plus two more against the browser mirror. **A test that CRASHES also "fails" and looks like proof** — one assertion here reported a TypeError under mutation and stopped the battery, so it was rewritten to fail cleanly. This baseline is re-frozen: changing either amount, the deal-type test, or where the fee lands in the money needs fresh owner sign-off.
  - **Frozen baseline — Gold Standard interest reserve (owner-directed, 2026-07-06):** the Gold Standard program does **not** finance an interest reserve on **renovation** (Light/Heavy Reno). Any requested reserve — from the structuring / products & pricing / term-sheet studio screens or the loan application — always resolves to **zero**: never financed, never in the cost basis. Gold **ground-up** keeps its full-term (75%-of-term) reserve; **bridge** never carried one; the **Standard Program** is unaffected. Enforced in `gold-standard.js` (all 3 copies), `termsheet.js` `calcGold`, `src/lib/pricing.js`, and the register routes. Treat this as frozen going forward.
  - **Interest reserve — months OR exact dollar amount (owner-directed, 2026-07-12; ADDITIVE, does not replace any rule above):** the financed interest reserve can be requested as a number of **months** (the original behavior) **or** as an **exact dollar amount** (a new `requested_ir_amount` column / `irAmount` input). When an amount > 0 is given it is used directly as the desired reserve and honored over months; when 0/absent the months path is **byte-identical** to before. Everywhere: Products & Pricing / Term Sheet Studio (`web/tools/term-sheet.html` `irAmount` field + `termsheet.js`), both frozen engines (`standard-program.js`/`gold-standard.js` `sizeLoan` + `evaluate` thread `irAmount`), backend `src/lib/pricing.js` (`buildInputs` reads `requested_ir_amount`, whitelisted), `product-registration.js` (persists `requested_ir_amount`), and the portal register flow (`ProductStudioPanel.jsx`/`TermSheetStudio.jsx`). **The amount is still capped by EVERY existing rule** — it is fit through the same leverage caps AND may never exceed the **entire-term interest** (`reserveCapMonths × monthly payment`): full term on Standard, the frozen **75%-of-term** on Gold ground-up, **zero** on Gold reno / locked reserves (ground-up tier-2, tier-1 ≥ $1.5MM), zero on bridge. So a $14,000 amount on a 12-month deal at $1,000/mo caps at $12,000 exactly as 14 months would cap at 12. Implemented as `d.reserveCapMonths` passed into `sizeLoan` + a `capDesired()` ceiling on the requested reserve — purely layered on top of the frozen month caps, changing none of them. A blank amount in the studio is sent as **0** (not null) so switching a file from amount back to months reliably clears a stale amount. Treat as frozen going forward.
  - **Frozen baseline — Assignment-fee financeable cap is 15% of the ORIGINAL (seller's) contract price (owner-directed, 2026-07-17; HARD FREEZE — may not be reopened without the owner's explicit sign-off in their own words):** on an assignment/wholesale purchase, the financeable assignment fee is capped at **15% of the seller's original contract price** — NEVER 15% of the fee-inclusive total purchase price (that was the bug: seller $100k + fee $20k wrongly allowed 15% × $120k = $18k). Correct math: seller $100k + fee $20k → max financeable **$15k** (15% × $100k) → **recognized/effective purchase price $115k** (seller + financeable fee), excess $5k out of pocket at the table. Gold Standard keeps its dollar ceiling on top: financeable fee = **lesser of $75,000 or 15% of the seller's contract price**. Applies to EVERY program, current and future — any new program with an assignment allowance uses the seller-price basis. Enforced in `standard-program.js` + `gold-standard.js` (`maxFee = 0.15 * sellerPP`, all 6 copies each: `web/tools`, `web/v2/tools`, `web/portal/engines`, `web/v2/portal/engines`, `app/public/engines`, `app-v2/public/engines` — the backend `src/lib/pricing.js` requires the `web/tools` pair, so it inherits automatically); wording updated in both engines' MANUAL/escalation messages, `termsheet.js` (v1+v2) and `loan-application.html` (v1+v2) assignment notes ("15% of the original contract price"); `?v=asgseller15` cache-busters bumped on every host page. This section is re-frozen at the same level as the engines themselves.
    - **ADDITIVE display rule (owner-directed 2026-07-17, second correction):** every surface labeled "Purchase price" must show the **REAL total price** the borrower pays (seller contract + FULL assignment fee, e.g. $120k) — never the capped basis. The capped basis appears ONLY under an explicit **"Effective purchase price"** label (with a short reason: the fee counts up to 15% of the seller's contract price), shown when the fee is over the cap. ALL math — initial advance, cost basis, LTC/LTV, sizing — keeps using the effective price (`assignment.recognizedPrice`); the real total is display/record-only and never enters a calculation. Fixed in `termsheet.js` (v1+v2: studio panel `rEff` + new `rEffAsg` row, PDF "Loan structure" + new effective row, Proof-of-Funds letter price, deal-profile export rows) and `term-sheet.html` (v1+v2: new `data-cond="asgEff"` row + tooltip); the loan application already labeled its snapshot "Effective purchase price". Borrower-facing wording says "effective purchase price" (not "recognized price") everywhere. Same freeze level; same owner-sign-off requirement to reopen.
    - **ADDITIVE admin exception override (owner-directed 2026-07-17, third addition):** admins may enter an approved **effective purchase price** in the studio's admin zone (`tsEffPrice` field → `ovrEffPrice` engine input, ADMIN-ONLY: in `ADMIN_ONLY_OVERRIDE_KEYS` + `MANUAL_PRICING_KEYS` in `src/routes/staff.js`, whitelisted in `buildInputs` NUMK) for assignment-fee exceptions (e.g. an approved 20% fee). The engines (both, all 6 copies) CLAMP it to **[seller contract price, REAL total price]** — the effective price may NEVER exceed what is actually paid — then recompute the financeable fee/excess off it; `assignment.overridden` marks it and every surface (studio note, PDF band, MANUAL/escalation messages) states the exception. No override → byte-identical frozen behavior (verified). The admin field always displays the live pre-approved effective price as its placeholder/hint. Applies to assignment purchases only. Same freeze level; owner sign-off required to reopen.
    - **ADDITIVE — an over-cap assignment REGISTERS (it is ELIGIBLE, not manual review); raising the cap is a separate exception REQUEST (owner-directed 2026-07-21):** a fee over the 15% financeable cap follows the program's normal mechanic — the financeable fee is capped, the loan sizes on the effective price, and the excess is brought to closing as extra cash to close (rehab stays fully financed). So `standard-program.js` (all 6 copies) now raises the over-limit case at level **`ELIGIBLE`** (was `MANUAL`), matching Gold (which always treated assignment as a non-blocking note) — the deal registers without manual review. The admin `assignment.overridden` (ovrEffPrice) case still escalates. To FINANCE MORE of the fee (a bigger loan) a borrower/LO uses the **"Request an exception"** action (`POST /api/{staff,borrower}/applications/:id/pricing/request-exception`), which raises an escalation into the super-admin Workflow + Escalations box without changing the registration; a super-admin grants it by setting `ovrEffPrice` and re-registering. This composes with the 2026-07-21 manual-review enforcement (`manual-program.needsSuperAdminApproval`): only a `MANUAL` status blocks/escalates, so an over-cap assignment (now `ELIGIBLE`) is no longer blocked. Rehab-over-cap is UNCHANGED and correctly stays `MANUAL` (rehab exceeding the max/ARV wall = the "bigger loan than the cap" ask = super-admin exception). Test `scripts/test-pricing-exception-escalation.js`. Same freeze level; owner sign-off to reopen.
  - **Frozen baseline — Loan-amount rounding / breakdown reconciliation (owner-directed, 2026-07-09):** the financed loan is reported in **whole dollars, floored DOWN** — never report (or lend) more than the engine sized. The reported breakdown must **reconcile to the penny**: the **initial advance** and **rehab holdback** are floored down too, and the **financed interest reserve absorbs the reconciling residual** (`reserve = flooredTotal − flooredInitial − flooredHoldback`); on a **no-reserve** deal the **initial advance absorbs the residual** instead (`initial = flooredTotal − flooredHoldback`), so no phantom reserve appears. This mirrors the LOS, which floors both the loan amount AND the initial — previously the total floored while the initial rounded to nearest, so the breakdown could sum to $1 over the total. The engine sizing math (`standard-program.js` / `gold-standard.js`) is **unchanged**; only the **reported figures** are floored/reconciled, in `web/tools/termsheet.js` (`calc` + `calcGold`) and `src/lib/pricing.js` (`normalize`). Treat this as frozen going forward.
  - **Frozen baseline — the 1% CLOSING-COST LIQUIDITY BUFFER (owner-authorized 2026-07-31, in the owner's own words: "For the liquidity requirement, add a buffer for extra closing costs … about 1% of the loan amount — a $500,000 loan adds a $5,000 buffer … extra cash to close that we verify, because attorney charges or other charges should never leave us running short. We can waive it on certain scenarios — on the manual side.").** The liquidity requirement everywhere is now `cashToClose + reserveRequirement + oopRehab + closingBuffer` where `closingBuffer = 1% of the total loan` — NEVER added to cash-to-close itself (it is a cushion the borrower must SHOW, not a cost we charge). Implemented in `src/lib/pricing.js` `normalize()` (quote fields `closingBuffer`/`closingBufferWaived`) and mirrored in `web/v2/tools/termsheet.js` `calc`/`calcGold`/`calcSilver` (result fields + the `rLiqBuffer` studio row + the PDF/xlsx derivation lines). WAIVABLE PER FILE by an admin only: `applications.liquidity_buffer_waived` (db/390, default false) via `POST /api/staff/applications/:id/liquidity-buffer` (`manage_pricing`, audited; `liquidity.setClosingBufferWaiver` keeps the current registration's stored quote + the `rtl_p3_assets` condition in step — un-waiving may reopen a cleared assets condition because the requirement genuinely rose; waiving never reopens). The waiver is FILE-OWNED — `buildInputs` ignores any client `waiveLiqBuffer` override, and the studio reads it from the read-only `liqBufferWaived` hidden input (the `coBorrowerPgWaived` pattern). The condition hint + breakdown (`liquidity.js`), the borrower terms email (`product-registration.js`), and the closing cash-to-close gate (which reads the stored breakdown) all carry it. Loan sizing, rates, and every cap are UNTOUCHED — proven by `scripts/test-liquidity-buffer-pure.js` (in `npm test`: buffer = 1% on liquidity only; waived = byte-identical pre-buffer formula; nothing else moves). This baseline is re-frozen — changing the 1% or the waiver mechanics needs fresh owner sign-off.
  - **3-month minimum earned interest is now a TOGGLE, not always-on (owner-directed 2026-07-22 — SUPERSEDES the 2026-07-14 "on every term sheet" rule).** The provision is a minimum earned-interest (interest floor) and must NEVER be worded as a prepayment penalty. Its default DIFFERS by program: **OFF by default on the Standard AND Gold programs** (an admin may ADD it per program in the Term Sheet Studio admin zone / Products & Pricing), and **ON by default on a MANUAL / custom product** (an admin may TURN IT OFF). It populates on the term sheet PDF, the studio/Excel/derivation surfaces, and the borrower "terms are ready" email ONLY when it is on. The super-admin escalation/approval for a manual product STATES whether it was left on (default) or turned off, alongside the accrual type. Canonical model in `src/lib/term-options.js` (`resolveMinInterest(program, explicit)` — manual→ON, Standard/Gold→OFF unless an explicit boolean is given; `MIN_INTEREST_ROW`/`MIN_INTEREST_DETAIL`). Client toggles: `web/v2/tools/term-sheet.html` admin zone (`tsMinIntStd`/`tsMinIntGold`/`tsMinIntManual`) → `termsheet.js` `minInterestOn(prog)` gates every surface. Persisted on `applications.min_interest_enabled` (NULL = program default; legacy files fall back to the program default) + snapshotted in `product_registrations.term_options` (`db/265`). Threaded through both register routes → `persistProductRegistration({termOptions})` → `terms-notify`/`borrowerTermsEmail`. This is DISPLAY/record only — it never touches a frozen engine number. Built on V2 (canonical); V1 (`web/tools`, frozen at `/v1`) keeps the legacy always-on text. When adding a new term-sheet surface, gate the line on `minInterestOn`/`min_interest_enabled` — never hard-code it on.
  - **Term-sheet options — accrual, deferred origination fee, key dates, draw fees (owner-directed 2026-07-22; DISPLAY/record only, never engine math):** alongside min-interest, the term sheet now carries: (1) **Accrual type** — Non-Dutch / As-Drawn by default (`applications.accrual_type='non_dutch'`); an admin may switch to Dutch / Full-Boat in the studio admin zone (`tsAccrual`). The frozen engine input stays `accrual:"Non-Dutch"` UNCHANGED (byte-identical pricing) — the selection is only a printed label + a saved field for future use. (2) **Deferred origination fee** (`applications.deferred_orig_pct`, default 0; `tsDeferredOrig`) — a % paid at EXIT/payoff, shown on the sheet as an exit fee, NEVER added to cash-to-close or the liquidity to show. (3) **Estimated key dates** — from an estimated **closing date** (`applications.est_closing_date`, `estClosingDate` field) we derive the **first payment date** (1st of the 2nd month after closing) and the **maturity date** (first payment + term − 1 months, so a 12-payment loan matures on its 12th payment, never a 13th); stored as `first_payment_date`/`maturity_date` and re-derived on every register from the effective closing date (studio's, else the file's — a re-register never wipes them). Date math is calendar-string only (`term-options.js`), no tz drift. (4) **Draw fees** printed by program: **Gold $250 per draw, physical inspection only**; **Standard $299 hybrid / $499 physical** (Gold's $250 already comes from the frozen engine `drawFee`; Standard's are display literals in `term-options.drawFeeLines`). (5) The term sheet also now shows all **borrower + co-borrower + vesting-entity** names, the **loan purpose** and **program**, the **claimed experience** total, and every **LTV/LTC/ARV** figure at FULL precision (up to 2 decimals trimmed — 87.5 stays 87.5, never rounded to 87; `pcFull`/`pc`). A dedicated **Disclosures & conditions page** prints before the signature page (business purpose, due diligence, title, legal fees, insurance, accrual definition, disclaimer, indemnification). Test `scripts/test-term-options-pure.js` (in `npm test`).
  - **Personal guarantor default + co-borrower guaranty-waiver EXCEPTION (owner-directed 2026-07-22; DISPLAY/record only + a super-admin approval workflow, never engine math).** By DEFAULT every term sheet is **full recourse** and lists **BOTH the borrower and the co-borrower as personal guarantors** (jointly & severally). A rare, super-admin-**APPROVED** exception waives the co-borrower's personal guaranty — they stay a **member of the borrowing entity** but are a **non-guarantor member** (only the primary guarantees). Pieces:
    - **`applications.co_borrower_pg_waived` boolean** (`db/268`, default false) — the EFFECTIVE display flag the term sheet reads. Set ONLY by an approved waiver (never a free studio toggle); the register-route write-back never touches it (approval owns it). It flows: `applications.co_borrower_pg_waived` → `ProductStudioPanel.buildStudioState` → the studio's read-only `coBorrowerPgWaived` hidden input → `web/v2/tools/termsheet.js` `guarantyInfo()` gates the parties sub-line ("Guarantor(s): …" / "Member (non-guarantor): …"), a dedicated **"Guaranty / recourse"** row, the **"Personal guaranty / recourse"** disclosures-page item, and the co-borrower's **signature sub-line** ("Co-borrower / member (non-guarantor)" vs "…/ guarantor"). A waived co-borrower **still signs** (as an authorized member) — only the label changes. Server wording mirror: `src/lib/term-options.js` `guarantySummary()` (used by `borrowerTermsEmail` + `terms-notify`). The register route snapshots the file's REAL flag into `product_registrations.term_options.coBorrowerPgWaived` (ignores any client value — never trust the studio for the flag).
    - **`loan_exceptions` table** (`db/268`) — a clean, purpose-built exception record with its OWN lifecycle (`requested → approved | denied | withdrawn`), a structured `reason_code` + note, and the super-admin's decision. Built general (an `exception_type` discriminator, only `guaranty_waiver` today) so future exception types reuse the queue + UI. One open (requested) per file+type (partial-unique index; a new request supersedes the prior open one). Module `src/lib/loan-exceptions.js`.
  - **EXCEPTION WORKFLOW REDESIGN — `loan_exceptions` is THE policy-exception register (2026-07-24; research + blueprint `docs/EXCEPTION-WORKFLOW-REDESIGN.md`; db/306).** Four types via the `EXCEPTION_TYPES` registry in `src/lib/loan-exceptions.js` (adding a type = one registry entry + a migration widening the CHECK + a `TYPE_META` entry in `ExceptionCard.jsx` — never a per-type ternary): `guaranty_waiver`, `esign_before_ctc`, **`pricing_exception`** (the studio/borrower "Request an exception" now writes a REAL register row — structured reason + compensating factors + `deal_snapshot` — AND still opens the workflow escalation; the notification deep-links to `/internal/exceptions?app=`; the GRANT stays the studio admin-override + re-register, never automated), and **`issuance_override`** (record-only, born approved — every R6.18 super-admin override past a fatal hard-warning lands in the register best-effort via `recordIssuanceOverride`, which NEVER throws/blocks). Governance columns (all NULL = legacy): `compensating_factors` (shared `COMPENSATING_FACTORS` taxonomy), `deal_snapshot` (+ advisory `dealDrift` on read — NEVER auto-voids anything), `expires_at`/`expired_at` + status `'expired'` (**expirable types only — `guaranty_waiver` is engine-refused; a term-sheet flag is never clock-driven**; the digest sweep `exceptionExpirySweepOnce` flips lapsed approvals and `latestEsignBeforeCtc` ALSO fails closed at read time by presenting a lapsed approval as expired before the sweep), `due_at` review SLA (+ `exceptionAgingOnce` super-admin digest), `re_request_of` chain, `withdrawn_by/_at` (withdraw no longer overloads `decided_by`), `requested_by_kind` staff|borrower|system, `exception_seq` (the EX-n reference shown everywhere incl. the xlsx register export `GET /api/admin/exceptions/export.xlsx` and `/metrics`). HARD RULES: decide is gated on **`manage_pricing`** (admins + super-admins) — WIDENED from super-admin-only by owner direction 2026-07-26 ("let any Admin approve"); **requester≠approver is enforced for every role EXCEPT super_admin** (owner-directed 2026-07-31: "I'm requesting an exception myself as super admin and it says I cannot approve my own exception — this is a failure" — the super-admin may decide their OWN request, mirroring the exemption admin-manual-programs.js and underwriting escalations always had; a regular admin still can never decide their own; `mayDecide` in admin-exceptions.js is the one rule, `canDecideOwn` on the list response drives the UI). Do not remove the admin-level control or widen the exemption without separate owner direction, and narrowing the decide gate back is likewise an owner call; an OPEN request can NOT be `cleared` (withdraw/decide first); withdraw = requester or admin only; exception comment audit/notification copy is TYPE-AWARE (the esign/pricing comment must never masquerade as guaranty); the decision certificate's `policy_exceptions` block reads approved register rows (keep it complete when adding types); the file's register renders in `sec-exceptions` (`ExceptionRegisterCard`); no frozen engine number is ever touched by any exception path. Tests `scripts/test-exception-register-db.js` (+ updated `test-loan-exceptions.js`), both in `npm test`.
    - **Workflow** — any staff member REQUESTS on the file (`POST /api/staff/applications/:id/exceptions/guaranty-waiver`; state `GET …/exceptions`; withdraw `POST …/exceptions/:eid/withdraw`, all file-scoped in `staff.js`); an **admin/super-admin** decides in the **Exceptions box** (`/api/admin/exceptions`, `src/routes/admin-exceptions.js`: list/count = `manage_pricing`, decide = `manage_pricing` — widened from `super_admin` by owner direction 2026-07-26, see the HARD RULES line above). Segregation of duties: the approver cannot decide their own request (server-enforced + UI-disabled). On APPROVE the decide route flips `co_borrower_pg_waived=true` in one transaction; on DENY it stays false (both guarantee). Notifications: `guaranty_exception` (request → super-admins) + `guaranty_exception_decided` (decision → file team), both registered in `notify.js` KICKER/CATEGORY maps (action-bearing, so they email). UI: staff file card `GuarantyWaiverCard.jsx` in the studio panel (self-hides with no co-borrower); the admin/super-admin review screen `StaffExceptions.jsx` at `/internal/exceptions` (nav "Exceptions" + live badge from `api.loanExceptionsCount()`). This directly improves the messy old "request an exception" dead-end (which created no reviewable item): a guaranty waiver is a first-class, approvable record with open/view/approve. Test `scripts/test-loan-exceptions.js` (pure wording + DB lifecycle, in `npm test`). Built on V2 (canonical); V1 (`web/tools`, frozen at `/v1`) is unchanged (defaults to both-guarantee). Never hard-code the guaranty line — gate it on `coBorrowerPgWaived` / `guarantyInfo()`.
  - **Frozen baseline — Track-record experience window (owner-directed, 2026-07-07):** only a **completed exit dated within the last 3 years** counts toward the borrower's experience tier / brackets. An exit **more than 3 years ago** counts toward **nothing** — not the tier, not experience, not through anything; a **future-dated** exit likewise does not count until it closes. This applies to the sale date (flips) and the lease/refi date (holds). Enforced in `web/tools/track-record.js` `qualifies()` (`0 <= monthsAgo(exitDate) <= EXIT_WINDOW_MO=36`). Treat this as frozen going forward.
    - **A GROUND-UP EXITS ON WHICHEVER COMPLETION IT HAS (owner-authorized 2026-08-09 — AUTHORIZED amendment to the frozen exit rule + re-freeze).** Asked, in plain language, to choose between three options, the owner picked **"Sold OR rented/refinanced"**: a ground-up is finished when the building is done and they either sold it, rented it, or refinanced it. This is the explicit authorization the HARD RULE requires — the experience count feeds the tier, and the tier feeds leverage.
      · **THE DEFECT.** The base rule sends anything that is not a flip to `COALESCE(rent_date, refi_date)`. A ground-up that was **BUILT AND SOLD** — the most ordinary ground-up outcome there is — carries a sale date and no rent or refi date, so its exit date was **NULL and it counted toward NOTHING**. Confirmed both ways before anything was changed: `exitDateOf({deal_type:'ground-up', sale_date:'2025-06-01'})` returned null, and the identical row through `EXIT_DATE_SQL` on a real database returned NULL. A borrower who built and sold three houses was credited with none of them while `requested_exp_ground` still priced the loan on the claim. **The product had already promised the owner's rule**: `trackRecordMissing` (routes/borrower.js) tells the borrower a ground-up still needs *"a completed exit (sale, rent, or refinance)"* — so the screen has always said a sale finishes a ground-up, and only the COUNT disagreed.
      · **THE SHAPE IS THE PROOF, and it is the whole reason this was safe to ship against live files.** `EXIT_DATE_SQL` is now `COALESCE(<the old expression verbatim>, CASE WHEN <ground> THEN COALESCE(sale_date, rent_date, refi_date) END)` — a COALESCE **over the old rule**, never a rewritten CASE. The fallback is reachable only where the old expression was already NULL, so the new rule returns the old rule's answer whenever the old rule had one, **by construction**: no deal that counts today can stop counting, and no counted deal's exit date can move. That is a property of the expression rather than of a test battery, which is what lets an authorized change to a frozen counting rule land without a borrower losing a tier, a registered loan re-sizing downward, or the experience condition reopening on a signed-off file. `EXIT_DATE_BASE_SQL` is exported ONLY so the test can run the old rule beside the new one — it is the old rule, not a second definition, and nothing else may read it.
      · **DO NOT "SIMPLIFY" IT INTO A CASE.** Writing the ground branch as a plain `CASE ... THEN COALESCE(sale_date, rent_date, refi_date)` reads more naturally and is WRONG: a ground-up rented in 2023 and sold in 2025 moves its exit from the rent date to the sale date, and a `deal_type` spelled "ground-up flip" (which the base rule sends down the FLIP branch) moves off its sale date entirely — either can push a deal out of the 36-month window. The mutation was applied deliberately and reproduced exactly that (`2023-09-20 -> 2025-06-01`), failing 4 assertions.
      · **UNCHANGED, deliberately:** the 36-month window, the future-exit rule, the flip rule, the hold rule, `bucketOf`, and `requested_exp_reo` (still stored, still read by nothing — see the REO rule below). `isGroundUp` reads the SAME two substrings `bucketOf` does and the test asserts they agree on every deal type, so a row can never be counted in one bucket and dated by another. The JS twin `exitDateOf` mirrors the SQL's two-half shape exactly so the twins cannot drift on the additive property. Both `web/tools` and `web/v2/tools` `track-record.js` carry the matching client rule — **inert today** (the tool builds only `flip` and `hold` kinds), written now so that adding a ground-up section cannot silently inherit the HOLD rule and re-arm this exact bug; cache-busters bumped.
      · Test `scripts/test-ground-up-exit-pure.js` (in `npm test`): the additive property asserted EXHAUSTIVELY over every deal_type × all 8 date combinations (152 rows) in JS **and** re-evaluated in Postgres so the twins are compared row for row, plus the owner's own cases and a `RECENT_EXIT_SQL` check proving the amendment reaches the query the tier is actually built from. This baseline is re-frozen — widening the window, changing which date any type exits on, or reopening the additive shape needs fresh owner sign-off.
    - **REO IS THE RESIDUAL LIST, NOT A FOURTH DEAL TYPE (owner-directed 2026-08-09).** Asked what REO means here — there is a `requested_exp_reo` column on every file that is stored and **read by nothing** — the owner: *"List of REO is general stuff: anything that we don't count, anything older than three years, anything that we can't count for any reason goes into the general list of REO. There are certain programs potentially that will allow us to count REO towards certain experience. For now, you're right: it doesn't go into the calculations as general REO if it was not a fix and hold, but some of the REO might be a fix and hold."* So REO is **DERIVED, never stored as a `deal_type`**: it is every track-record line that is not currently counting, each carrying the REASON it is not (outside the 36-month window, no exit date yet, still owned, not verified, a pillar unmet). Consequences: **the three real deal types stay flip / hold / ground-up**; a line in the REO list that IS a qualifying fix-and-hold simply counts and leaves the list on its own, with no reclassification; the reason must be machine-readable so a future program that credits some REO can select on it; and because every line is either counted or in REO-with-a-reason, **nothing a borrower ever entered can fall out of the record** — which is the structural answer to the owner's *"nothing should get lost"*. `requested_exp_reo` stays stored and unread — do NOT wire it into `requestedFromApp` or any tier math without fresh owner direction.
  - **Loan SIZES on the borrower's CLAIMED experience of record — funding is gated by the experience CONDITION, not by re-sizing (owner-directed, 2026-07-14).** The registered loan is sized on the borrower's **claimed** experience (`applications.requested_exp_flips/holds/ground`), falling back to the verified count when no claim is on file — exactly the formula the Term Sheet Studio already displays (`requested_exp ?? verified`, `ProductStudioPanel.jsx`). It is **no longer sized verified-only**: previously a NON-admin registration ran `sanitizeOverrides` (which strips `expFlips/expHolds/expGround`, still correct — a non-admin can't type a fabricated experience override), so `buildInputs` sized off the *verified* tier and persisted a loan **smaller than the studio showed** (e.g. a 187,500 quote landed as 174,921 on the pipeline — Yuda Elbaum / 392 Columbia Ave). The `exp` returned by **both** `loadFileForPricing` loaders (`src/routes/staff.js` + `src/routes/borrower.js`) is now the claimed-of-record, so the quote preview, the register, and the studio all agree. This never over-lends: the experience **condition** (`signOffGate` experience branch) still blocks clear-to-close until the claim is VERIFIED against the track record. A non-admin still cannot inflate — the studio experience override stays stripped and the base is the application's *attested* claim, editable only through the (audited) application/details form. A previously-registered file only picks up the corrected size on its next **re-register** (its `product_pricing` condition reopens on any economics change via `db/071`/`db/072`). Do not revert to verified-only sizing.
  - **THE EXPERIENCE TYPED IN THE TERM SHEET STUDIO IS THE FILE'S CLAIM — a term sheet GENERATED on ten stabilized rentals makes the condition require verifying ten (owner-directed 2026-08-06).** The owner, on a live file: *"they entered under Experience 10, Rentals Stabilized, and on the condition it says that they don't need to verify anything. If they entered any experience on the term sheet generated, they should feed directly into the file, and the condition should require verifying 10. You should not be able to sign it off till you verify 10."*
    · **ROOT CAUSE.** Experience reached `applications.requested_exp_*` from every door EXCEPT the one an officer actually builds a deal in. `POST /pricing/quote` persists nothing by design, and the write-back in `persistProductRegistration` only runs on **REGISTER** — so an officer who typed 10 into the studio's **"BRRRR / rentals stabilized"** box (`expBrrrr`, the HOLDS bucket), generated the term sheet and stopped there left the file claiming **zero**. And zero is not a neutral state anywhere downstream: `experience.js` reads the claim, sees nothing to verify, stamps the track-record condition `notApplicable`, and `signOffGate` returned null on `claimed === 0` — so the condition read *"No experience required on this file"* and signed off freely on a deal priced on ten stabilized rentals. **THE CLASS: a value a user TYPES that only reaches the file on a LATER, OPTIONAL step is discarded for everyone who does not take that step — and every consumer downstream reads the absence as a confident "none", never as "not stated".**
    · **`src/lib/studio-experience-claim.js` is the ONE rule**, fed at the `PUT …/checklist/:itemId/tool-state` save (the chokepoint the panel's debounced autosave AND its close-flush both go through, which is what makes "they entered it on the term sheet" enough — no register needed). **The semantics are the register path's, deliberately**: a real number **including 0** wins VERBATIM so a claim can be LOWERED and stick (the 2026-07-28 *"I remove the 5 and put 0 and it comes back"* rule), and a **BLANK states nothing** and leaves the column alone — byte-for-byte what `claimExpVal` already encodes, because the studio must not mean one thing when you press Register and another when you don't. Junk and unstorable counts state nothing rather than reaching an int4 column. The write is `IS DISTINCT FROM`-guarded (`requested_exp_*` is watched by the db/071/072 reopen trigger, so an echoing autosave must touch no row), audited (`studio_experience_claim`), re-syncs the condition immediately, and can never fail the save.
    · **A FROZEN file is never written, and the freeze check FAILS CLOSED** — unlike `fileLock.structuralLockReason`, which swallows a read error and answers "not locked". That is right for a human pressing Save and exactly wrong for an unattended writer, where a statement timeout would read a FUNDED file as unlocked. Same `_internals` composition, and the same NO-actor call, as the appraisal As-Is writer: this can never inherit a super-admin's unlock.
    · **STAFF ONLY, and that is not a gap in the all-sides rule.** A borrower CANNOT type experience in their studio (`ProductStudioPanel` locks the three fields for them and the borrower register route strips every experience override), so they cannot produce this bug — and wiring their autosave would OPEN one, letting a resumed stale draft silently rewrite the very claim the verification requirement is measured against. The borrower REQUESTS an experience change and the team approves it. The other doors already carried it and are untouched (marketing studio `translate()` → `loan-application.html` → `/api/apply`, borrower Apply, staff new-file, lead convert, details/completeness).
    · **THE SCREEN SAYS SO WHEN THEY GET BACK TO IT.** The conditions list lives BEHIND the studio sheet, so re-fetching it on every debounced pass would churn the page under the user mid-edit. The save response carries the claim it wrote (`experienceClaim`), `ProductStudioPanel` remembers that it happened in a ref (never state — that would re-render the sheet), and `closeStudio` fires the parent's existing `onRegistered` reload ONCE, so the list they land back on already reads "verify 10" instead of the old "no experience required".
    · **GOING FORWARD ONLY — a DELIBERATE exception to the standing "previous AND future" rule, and it is the owner's call to reopen.** The studio's `tool_state` has been autosaving for months, so a back-fill sweep would have plenty to feed — and feeding it would move `requested_exp_*` on a large slice of the book at once, unwatched, which trips the db/071/072 trigger and REOPENS Products & Pricing (and any already-signed-off experience condition) across live files in one pass. That is the same hazard that made the appraisal As-Is sweep going-forward-only by owner direction. So an existing file picks the claim up the next time somebody opens Products & Pricing on it; nothing is swept. If the owner wants the back book fed, that is a separate, bounded, deliberately-invoked pass — do not "restore" it as a boot sweep to satisfy the previous-AND-future rule. **A file whose experience condition was already signed off under the false "nothing to verify" DOES reopen** when its claim is fed — `syncExperienceChecklistForApplication` reopens on `requiredAny && !met`, which is correct: that sign-off was made against a claim of zero.
    · **THE GATE ASKS FOR THE VERIFICATION, NOT A REGISTRATION.** `signOffGate`'s experience branch re-inlined `reg.inputs` and refused an unregistered file outright with *"Register a product first"* — a dead end on a condition about the track record. It now reads `experience.registeredExperienceNeed(appId, db, claim)` — the SAME definition the condition sync and the track-record to-do list use, so the gate can never refuse on a number the condition never showed — which falls back to the file's own CLAIM when nothing is registered. A registered file is unaffected (the registration still governs); an unregistered one is told to *"Verify 10 more holds"*, and a claim that IS verified signs off without a registration (the separate Products & Pricing condition is what requires one). Tests `scripts/test-studio-experience-claim-pure.js` + `-db.js` (both in `npm test`; the DB one walks the owner's story in order and 12 of its assertions were proven to FAIL with the wiring reverted). When you add another surface where experience can be typed, feed it through this module — never a second place the claim can live.
  - **THE PRICING ADMIN ZONE IS OPEN TO EVERY STAFF ROLE, AND EVERY CHANGE TO THE DEFAULTS GOES TO AN ADMIN FOR APPROVAL (owner-directed 2026-07-27 evening; SUPERSEDES the 2026-07-16 "admin-only knobs" rule).** A loan officer reported the Products & Pricing screen had lost "the admin section at the bottom to create manual programs, to send it for exception and override pricing for exception." It had: `ProductStudioPanel.jsx` rendered the studio's admin zone only for `admin`/`super_admin` because the SERVER stripped those keys for everyone else (`sanitizeStaffOverrides`) and refused the register with a 403 the officer could not act on. The owner's rule replaces both halves: **"every single time somebody is changing the defaults and he's overriding something in the admin section this should be sent to the admin for approval, no matter if it's reducing the rate, reducing the fee, or manual programs."**
    · **Nothing is stripped and no role is refused.** `src/lib/pricing-overrides.js` `sanitizeStaffOverrides` is now a pass-through for every staff role (`strippedAdminKeys` is retained in the shape and is always false); the 403 branches in the register + quote routes are gone. What the officer sees in the studio is what registers — the divergence those refusals existed to prevent can no longer occur.
    · **The APPROVAL is the control, and it is keyed on DEVIATION FROM THE COMPANY DEFAULT, not on role.** The same module owns the detector: `pricingOverridesEngaged(overrides, pricingSettings.current())` returns every admin-zone knob moved off the Pricing Admin Center default — `markupStd/GoldPct`, `origStd/GoldPct`, `lenderFee`, `creditFee`, `appraisalFee`, `titleFee` (default NULL = auto-estimate, so any typed number counts), plus the no-default knobs `ovrAcqLTV(Pct)` / `ovrARLTV(Pct)` / `ovrLTC(Pct)` / `ovrRate(Pct)` / `ovrIrMonths` / `ovrEffPrice` / `manualPricing` / `forcePrice`. Typing the default back is NOT a change; an explicitly blanked field is NOT a change (the studio's "use the company default" contract); `engaged()` is byte-identical to `manual-program.js`'s so "is this a manual product" and "does this need approval" can never disagree about the same key. RAISING a fee needs approval too — the rule is any change, not only a discount. With no readable defaults it fails SAFE (ask, never skip). Deal INPUTS (experience, ARV, as-is, price, rehab, term, reserve) are NOT in the zone and never trigger it.
    · **Approval reuses the escalation box, so nothing new had to be invented.** `manual-program.needsSuperAdminApproval({program, status, pricingOverrides, needsApproval})` is the ONE definition; the register route opens a `manual_program_escalations` row with `summary.kind='pricing_override'` carrying `overrideLines` (plain language: "Origination points — Standard: 1.25% → 0.5%"), and the file registers immediately but the borrower terms email is withheld exactly as a manual product's is.
    · **`product_registrations.needs_approval` + `override_changes` (db/343) is what makes the ISSUANCE gate work.** `esign/gate.js` used to re-derive "needs sign-off" from `is_manual`/`status` — both innocent on a pricing override (program Standard, status ELIGIBLE), so a term sheet would have gone out. It now reads the stored flag. The back-date sets it for rows that already needed sign-off under the old rule (`is_manual OR status='MANUAL'`) and deliberately does NOT flag old pricing-only overrides — those were issued under the rule of the day and re-flagging them would freeze live files.
    · **WHO approves widened to any Admin, with requester ≠ approver.** `POST /api/admin/manual-programs/escalations/:id/{decide,counter}` moved from `requireRole('super_admin')` to `requirePermission('manage_pricing')` — matching the loan-exception register's 2026-07-26 widening and the owner's own words ("sent to the admin"). `mayDecide()` in that route refuses your OWN request (server-side, re-read before deciding; the UI's per-row `canDecide` only mirrors it). A **super_admin is exempt** from requester≠approver on purpose: they are the top authority and in a one-super-admin shop a second approver would be a dead end. Changing WHO decides is an owner call.
    · A re-register that still carries the override re-escalates (the terms still deviate) — that is the owner's "every single time", not a bug. An accept-counter passes `needsApproval:false` explicitly: the super-admin authored those terms and the LO's accept IS the approval, so it must never re-hold the term sheet on the approval that just happened.
    · Frontend: `staffAdmin` in `ProductStudioPanel.jsx` is now simply `isStaff`, the non-admin knob-stripping on restore is gone, and the studio warns BEFORE registering ("this goes to an admin for approval", naming the knobs). `StaffEscalations.jsx` renders the new kind + the changed-from-defaults list and gates its buttons on the per-row `canDecide`. Tests: `scripts/test-pricing-overrides-pure.js` (rewritten — the detector's whole truth table) and `scripts/test-pricing-override-approval-db.js` (NEW, real HTTP + real Postgres: LO quotes/registers an override, the escalation + flag + issuance block, a clean default-priced register still confirming immediately, requester≠approver, admin approval lifting the block), both in `npm test`.
- After ANY change under `app/src/`, run `cd app && npm run build` and **commit the regenerated `web/portal/` bundle**, or the change won't deploy. Render does NOT build the frontend (`buildCommand: npm install`).
- **Stale-tab protection: HTML entries are never cached and the SPA self-announces updates (owner-reported 2026-07-15 night).** An officer's long-lived tab ran yesterday's bundle (rendering a timezone-shifted DOB and missing new screens) while a freshly-reloaded admin saw today's — "two people see two different dates of birth" with identical DB data. Two layers, never remove either: (1) `src/server.js` static serving stamps `Cache-Control: no-cache` on every `.html` and `immutable` on hashed `/assets/`; (2) `StaffLayout.jsx`'s stale-build watchdog fetches `/portal/index.html` cache-busted every 5 min + on window focus, compares the referenced bundle hash to the RUNNING one, and shows a "PILOT was updated — Refresh" banner on mismatch. Any new layout shell must include the watchdog.
- **A green `npm run build` does NOT mean the page renders (owner-directed after a live crash, 2026-07-12).** The Vite/esbuild build treats an **undeclared identifier as a global** and emits it verbatim — so referencing a variable that isn't in scope (e.g. a React component that uses `role`/`completer`/any prop it was never passed) **builds cleanly** and then throws `ReferenceError: X is not defined` at render, which the app `ErrorBoundary` turns into the full-screen **"Something went wrong"** card — crashing the whole page (this is exactly what broke "open any file from the dashboard"). A passing build is necessary, not sufficient. So for every frontend change: (1) when a component reads a variable from an outer scope, it MUST be **destructured in that component's props AND passed at EVERY call site** — grep the call sites, don't assume; (2) before committing, run **eslint `no-undef`** on the changed `.jsx` (a minimal flat config with browser + React globals catches this class instantly — `npx eslint --no-config-lookup -c <cfg> <files>`, look for `no-undef` errors only); (3) treat any new/edited React component as guilty until you've confirmed each identifier it uses is a prop, a local, an import, or a known global. The build passing is the trap — never rely on it alone for JSX.
- Every schema change is a **new numbered idempotent `db/0NN_*.sql`** file. Never edit old migrations. `migrate-boot.js` applies them in order on boot.
  - **CHANGING AN INDEX'S SHAPE? KEEP ITS NAME — `IF NOT EXISTS` tests the NAME, not the shape (db/617, owner-reported 2026-08-23).** db/549 declared `lt_loans.loan_number` UNIQUE beside the Encompass GUID as though both were identities; the number is a human LABEL that Encompass does not enforce (a duplicated or re-created file carries another loan's number), so a perfectly ordinary tenant emptied the whole long-term pipeline on every pass. THE MIGRATION TRAP is what to copy: every file replays on EVERY boot, so db/549's `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS lt_loans_loan_number_key` runs forever. Drop that index and re-create it under a NEW name and the old statement finds its name free, tries to build a unique index over data that now legitimately has duplicates, and FAILS — and since each migration file is ONE implicit transaction, the whole of db/549 rolls back on every boot, taking its seventeen further indexes and its entire foreign-key block with it. Keeping the name and changing only the SHAPE (drop the unique index, `CREATE INDEX` the same name, guarded on `indisunique` so it is a no-op from the second boot) makes that statement a permanent no-op instead — PROVEN on a real Postgres: it emits `NOTICE: relation already exists, skipping`, never reads the data, and leaves the index non-unique. Comment the index so the historic `_key` name does not read as a live constraint. **And before declaring any human-assigned label unique, ask what the far system actually enforces** — the identity here was always `encompass_loan_guid`, whose unique index is untouched.
  - **A PER-ROW FAILURE INSIDE A BATCH TRANSACTION IS A TOTAL FAILURE unless a SAVEPOINT scopes it (same report).** The discovery pass mirrored the whole discovered book in ONE transaction with the loop BARE inside it, so the first row Postgres refused aborted the transaction and the catch rolled back every loan: one bad row, 772 lost, on every pass. Postgres puts a transaction into a failed state on ANY error and refuses every later statement until it is rewound, so `SAVEPOINT` per row + `RELEASE` on success + `ROLLBACK TO` on failure is not decoration — without it the NEXT row fails with `current transaction is aborted` and the batch still goes. Keep the batch as one transaction (a mirror is a moment; per-row commits leave a book that is part yesterday and part today), and COUNT AND NAME what was refused — a mirror that quietly skips a real loan is the confident wrong answer. The regression test must make a row fail for a reason unrelated to the original bug and assert the OTHERS still arrive; testing only the duplicate proves the constraint fix and leaves the batch trap fully armed.
  - **A migration that re-adds a CHECK constraint AND then defines a TRIGGER / other trailing DDL in the SAME file is fragile — the constraint's boot-replay can fail and SILENTLY SKIP everything after it (db/529 lesson, CI test-db 2026-08-05).** When a later migration widens that constraint under the SAME name (the db/527 pattern), the earlier file's unconditional narrow re-add fails on replay once a new-value row exists; migrate-boot's superseded-detection recognizes it and skips the REST OF THAT FILE quietly — including a `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` / `CREATE TRIGGER` that sits after the constraint. The trigger then reverts to whatever an EARLIER migration last defined (e.g. db/392's closer-aware `sync_primary_assignee` reverted to db/103's closer-less version once a db/524 `account_executive`/`account_manager` assignee row existed and any test re-ran `ensureSchema`, breaking the closer/draw-coordinator workflow — a failure that only surfaces mid-`npm test`, never on a fresh migrate). THE FIX is the db/375 pattern: a NEW migration numbered LAST re-asserts the trailing DDL (a pure `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` + `DROP/CREATE TRIGGER` with no data-dependent statement, so it can never itself fail) — so the converged trigger is restored every boot regardless of the earlier file's skip. When you author a migration, keep a fragile constraint re-add in its OWN file, separate from trigger/function DDL; and when a boot-replay constraint failure could skip trailing DDL, re-assert that DDL in a later file.
- **Verify every write endpoint**: after a 200, re-fetch and confirm the value persisted. "Returned 200 but didn't save" (camelCase/snake mismatch, `COALESCE`-swallows-updates) is the #1 recurring bug class here.
- **A VALUE MUST FIT ITS COLUMN, AND THE COLUMN DECIDES — never the door (post-merge audits 2026-07-31 + 2026-08-02).** A number too big, or text Postgres cannot parse, reaches the database, raises 22003 / 22P02 / 22021, and the route's catch-all turns it into a **500 "server error"** — which reads as "PILOT is broken", not "that number is too big". None of the money inputs cap their digits, so an ordinary paste gets there. This was fixed FOUR times one column at a time, and then FIVE more doors were found that had never been counted, so the rule is now structural:
  · **`src/lib/number-bounds.js` is the ONE definition.** `COLUMN_KIND` is a table of every bounded numeric column on `applications` and `borrowers` — `money` = numeric(14,2), `pct` = numeric(6,3), `int` = int4, `{precision, scale}` = any OTHER numeric precision (`borrowers.housing_payment` is numeric(12,2), `years_at_residence` numeric(4,1)), `{min,max,what}` = a column whose CHECK is narrower than its type. **Add a numeric column to either table and add it to that table in the same commit** — `test-column-bounds-doors-db.js` reads `information_schema` and FAILS on a column that is missing or carries the wrong kind. That check has to be the DB one: the pure suite's list is hard-coded, and an audit proved it passes both when a real column is added without a kind and when an existing column is given the WRONG kind.
  · **Every door delegates**: `applicationColumnProblem(column, n)` / `tableColumnProblem(table, column, n)`. Never re-inline a ceiling, and never hand-type the limit into a message — use `MONEY_LIMIT_TEXT`. Grep for a raw `Number(`/`parseFloat`/`parseInt` feeding a DB write before adding one.
  · **The ANSWER differs by door, deliberately.** A door with a human at it REFUSES with a plain 400 naming the field and quoting the limit (`PATCH /details`, both completeness panels, the appraisal finding reprice, the info-condition engine, and the five create doors via `fields.applicationNumberProblem`). A door with **no human to answer** DROPS the value instead — the public intake form, the MISMO import, and the ClickUp inbound (`ingest.dropUnstorableCols`, where `cols[k]=null` makes COALESCE keep the real portal value) — because stranding a lead, failing a whole import, or breaking one task's sync forever is worse than losing one unstorable figure.
