  · **`pct` is TEN TIMES tighter than `money`, and it is the one that binds.** `ltv` / `rate_pct` / `dscr_ratio` are numeric(6,3); bounding them with the money helper is three orders of magnitude too loose and has now shipped twice (intake, then MISMO).
  · **Judge what is BOUND, not only what was typed — including a value the code DERIVES.** This has now bitten three times: the assignment `purchase_price = underlying + fee` (two storable parts, one unstorable sum); `borrowers.residence_since`, a DATE computed by subtracting a months count from today, where the count fits int4 perfectly and the date walks off the calendar (`lib/residence.MAX_RESIDENCE_MONTHS`); and the MISMO import's `years_at_residence`, DERIVED from the file's residency-months count into a numeric(4,1). A column check on the input cannot see any of them. When a write derives a value, bound the DERIVED value too.
  · **Judge what is BOUND, not only what was typed** — on an assignment `purchase_price = underlying + fee`, so two comfortably storable parts can produce an unstorable sum.
  · **A NUL BYTE IS STRIPPED ONCE, AT THE REQUEST BOUNDARY.** `src/lib/nul-strip.js` is mounted in `server.js` immediately after `express.json` and removes U+0000 from every string (and every KEY) in every parsed body. This class was fixed THREE times as a list of places — `textColumn` for some text columns, `jsonbText` for 4 of ~17 jsonb binds, then the rest — and each time an audit found more: the PUBLIC lead door still 500-ing on the visitor's own `name` box, the public intake door losing an entire application on `firstName`, both borrower-profile doors on `citizenship`. Postgres refuses a NUL in ANY text (22021) or jsonb (22P05) column, and no field in this API ever wants one, so it is a RULE, not a list. The two helpers remain as backstops for values that arrive some other way — a webhook mounted BEFORE the JSON parser, a ClickUp pull, a parsed document — which the middleware never sees.
  · **Text and JSONB have the same disease.** `fields.textColumn` (trim, strip NUL, cap owned by the COLUMN) for text; **`fields.jsonbText` for every jsonb column fed from a request body** — a NUL byte is refused by jsonb too, with a different error, and it must be stripped from the OBJECT (keys and values, at any depth), NEVER from the serialized JSON: the obvious `JSON.stringify(x).replace(/\\u0000/g,'')` eats a backslash out of text that merely SPELLS the escape and produces malformed JSON.
  · **A refusal is the ANSWER, not a field to skip — and NOTHING is filed until EVERYTHING is acceptable.** The borrower completeness panel wrapped `openRequest` in `catch (_) {}`, so a correct 400 became `200 {ok:true}` with nothing filed and nothing said — the repo's #1 class hiding the one guard that was working. Two layers now: `change-requests.proposalProblem(field, value, opts)` is the ONE definition of every refusal (`openRequest`/`openBorrowerRequest` call it too, so they cannot drift), and the door asks it about EVERY field before filing anything — a post with a good ARV and a bad credit score used to file the ARV *and* answer 400. Behind that, a client-error status from `openRequest` is surfaced rather than swallowed; only a non-4xx is skipped.
  · **A value we OFFER is a value we accept.** The borrower's change-request panel offered `DSCR / Rental` while the server's governed enum did not carry it, so the one program they could pick that we rejected came back (once the swallow was removed) as "must be one of" — listing four programs that did not include the one on their screen. When you add an option to a picker, add it to the server's list in the same commit.
  Tests: `scripts/test-number-bounds-pure.js` + `scripts/test-column-bounds-doors-db.js` (+ `test-audit-hardening-db.js`), all in `npm test`.
- **EVERY "server error" NOW CARRIES ITS REASON — captured at the driver, attached at `res.json`, never at 379 call sites (owner-directed 2026-08-16: "Need to make sure the server error always is displaying more details, exactly what the error is, so we can better troubleshoot it").** 379 handlers end a request with `res.status(500).json({error:'server error'})`, which is all a staffer ever saw, for every cause there is — a number too big for its column, a NOT NULL nobody relaxed, a vendor timeout, a wrong column name. It reads as "PILOT is broken" and tells nobody, including us, which of those it was.
  · **THE FIX IS TWO CHOKEPOINTS, NOT 379 EDITS.** Editing every call site is a hand-kept list that goes stale on route 380. `src/db.js` calls `httpFail.record(e, sql)` on every rejected query — pooled AND transactional (a pooled client is wrapped exactly once, behind a symbol, or a hot client grows a handler per query) — and re-throws untouched, so nothing about how a caller sees a failure changes. `src/lib/http-fail.middleware` (mounted in `server.js` right after the request audit, above every route) binds a per-request slot with **AsyncLocalStorage** — the mechanism `api-rate-limit.runAsHealthProbe` already uses — and wraps `res.json`. A route written next year is covered knowing none of this.
  · **WHAT IS SENT, AND TO WHOM.** Any body of 500-or-worse gains a **`reference`** — the request id already echoed as `X-Request-Id` and already stored on the `request_audit_log` row, so it names ONE row in the request log — and, for a `kind === 'staff'` actor only, the real **`detail`** + Postgres **`code`**. A borrower, a **TPO broker (an outside company)** and an anonymous caller get the reference alone: the wording is internal and can quote what somebody typed. A 4xx and a 2xx are byte-identical to before.
  · **A PG `detail` FIELD QUOTES THE FAILING ROW** — the borrower's name, their DOB, their encrypted SSN — so it is never read and never logged. What is quoted is `message` + code/column/constraint/table, and even that goes through `pii-guard.scan` first, because `invalid input syntax for type integer: "…"` echoes the input and people type Socials into boxes that are not for them. If the scrubber cannot be loaded, a reason that still looks like an SSN is WITHHELD rather than shipped.
  · **IT BECOMES PART OF THE RECORD**: the same reason is stamped on `res.locals.auditError` → `request_audit_log.error` → the file's own audit log under "Include every request". `friendlyError` in `app-v2/src/lib/api.js` renders `error — detail (ref …)` on every screen at once. Nothing here may ever break a response — every step is wrapped and returns the body untouched on failure. Test `scripts/test-coborrower-no-email-db.js` (in `npm test`); all three wirings were mutation-proven. When a catch block has the error in hand, `httpFail.fail(res, e)` names THIS error rather than the request's last one.
- **A CO-BORROWER MAY HAVE NO EMAIL — the column, not the route, was the bug (owner-reported 2026-08-16; db/569).** Typing a co-borrower's name, DOB and Social and pressing Save answered "server error". Reproduced from the real endpoint: `borrowers.email` was `citext NOT NULL` while `attachCoBorrowerToApp` — the ONE function the co-borrower panel and staff file-creation share — binds NULL when the box the screen labels "EMAIL (OPTIONAL — NEEDED LATER TO INVITE OR SIGN)" is blank (owner-directed 2026-08-05, "allow it at intake"). So the feature could never work from either door, on any file. **NULL, never `''`** — an empty string is a value and would collide two different email-less people on `borrowers_email_owner_uk`; and never a synthetic `noemail+…` address either, which is fake data that then has to be recognised and hidden on every screen, in every email, and in the invite button (which would light up and post to nobody). The sync's own shadow addresses exist ONLY to dodge this constraint and say so in `clickup/ingest.js`; db/318 removed the blanket UNIQUE, db/569 removes the other half. Everything downstream was ALREADY written for a missing address — `portal-invite`'s `no_email` state, `esign/orchestrate`'s "a co-borrower may now be added without an email (#22)", `notify`'s `COALESCE(b.email,'') <> ''` — the constraint was the outlier. `borrower_auth.email` (the LOGIN) stays NOT NULL. No backfill: existing `@clickup.local` / `@import.local` shadows are LEFT ALONE (the sync matches them by name and rewriting them in bulk would change how it resolves those profiles).
- **THE AUDIT LOG STOPPED CRYING WOLF — truncation is MEASURED, not inferred (owner-reported 2026-08-16: "This log is incomplete · closing milestones: read 1 records, which is all this page reads").** Nothing was missing. `staffFeed` declared a trail PARTIAL whenever `rows >= its LIMIT`, which reads a LIMIT as a BOUND when it can equally be a STATEMENT OF SHAPE: `closing_workflow` holds ONE row per file, so its trail is written `LIMIT 1`, and every file that ever reached closing read 1 of 1 and put a red "This log is incomplete" over the whole page — permanently, on exactly the files worth auditing. Every other trail carried the same latent false alarm the day a file held exactly `LIMIT` rows. **Now `trailEvents` runs the trail's own SQL with its LIMIT raised by one and drops the extra before mapping** (`auditEvents` does the same with its `$2`), so the events are byte-for-byte what they were and only the VERDICT changes — and a trail that really WAS cut short still names itself. Front end (`ActivityFeed.jsx`): a failed load no longer `.catch(() => setRows([]))` into the same "Nothing yet." an untouched file shows — it says so and offers Try again; loading renders the panel instead of returning `null` (the section simply vanishing is what "it doesn't load well" looks like); and the banner shows ALL the meta rows, not the first of up to three. Test: section 3c of `scripts/test-file-audit-log-db.js`, mutation-proven against the old rule.
- **THE INVESTOR'S APPRAISAL REQUIREMENTS ARE POSTED ONTO THE ORDER, right after it is placed (owner-directed 2026-08-16: "any file that's going to EMCAP, when you order the appraiser … this message should post as a message to the order right after you place the order, so the team can know about the requirement").** `src/lib/appraisal/investor-appraisal-requirements.js` states the requirements ONCE — the numbers included — and `note-buyer-checks.js` (whose anchor rule is a CTC-blocking fatal) imports them, so we can never tell an appraiser one rule and refuse their report for another. `src/lib/appraisal/order-requirements-post.js` puts them on the thread of whichever AMC the order went to: **NAN** (`amc/comments.postComment`, fired at the tail of `createOrder`) and **Class** (`class/messages.note`, fired after the order is recorded `ordered` — a note needs their order number). Richer Values is deliberately NOT wired: their API has no messaging at all, so their thread is email, and an automatic email to an outside desk is a different decision nobody asked for.
  · **THE MESSAGE NAMES NOBODY.** An AMC is an OUTSIDE company, so the standing "never expose a note buyer / capital partner name" rule applies in full: the requirements are stated as OUR review requirements, which from the appraiser's side is what they are, and the text goes through the shared borrower-safe scrub on the way out. The test asserts it against `borrowerSafe.PARTNER_PATTERNS`, not a hand-typed list.
  · **THE ANCHOR COMP'S THREE CRITERIA ARE PRINTED AS THREE, and the rent schedule appears ONLY on a rental exit (owner-directed 2026-08-16, answering the open question: "EMCAP needs three things for the anchor comp — within a year, within the mile, and less than a 15% net adjustment — and make sure you only include the text about the rent schedule if it's a rental exit").** So the owner's "three specific rules" are the anchor's three criteria, not three separate requirements. They were one running sentence and are now `a. / b. / c.` under an "all THREE of" lead, because an appraiser skimming a paragraph misses the third — which is the one they most often miss. The rental line is gated on `ctx.rentalExit` (read from the file's own program / loan type / rehab type through `normStrategy`, never a hand-passed flag), and an UNKNOWN exit reads as NOT rental: asking a flip's appraiser for a 1007 costs money and time for nothing. Pinned both ways — the rental line is proven to be the ONLY difference between the two messages, so changing a file's exit can never quietly move another requirement.
  · **IT CAN NEVER FAIL AN ORDER** (the appraisal is already placed), it **POSTS ONCE** (keyed on the stable first line `MARKER`, looked up on the vendor's own thread table — keep that line stable), and it is **SILENT** on a file with no investor requirements. Which investor governs comes from the file's own note buyer, falling back on a blank one to the REGISTERED PROGRAM's provider — the same derivation `note-buyer-for-program.js` uses to stamp `applications.lender`, so the two cannot disagree about who a Silver loan is going to.
  · **THE 1 MILE, AND THE ZIP THAT WAS STANDING IN FOR IT.** The stored EMCAP rule read "the same ZIP code as the subject"; the owner has now stated the rule itself — within 1 mile. `compVerdict` accepts the appraiser's own stated distance WHEN THE REPORT GIVES ONE (through `research/trilaterate.proximityMiles`, which refuses anything it cannot read cleanly) and keeps the ZIP as the fallback. **The direction is the safety property**: a stated distance can only ever ADD a qualifying comp, never remove one, so no live file can newly fail a fatal — proven over the whole grid in `scripts/test-investor-appraisal-requirements-pure.js`, and mutation-proven by making the mile a hard refusal. Making it hard would TIGHTEN a clear-to-close gate and needs the owner's own words. Tests: that pure suite + `scripts/test-investor-order-message-db.js` (both vendors, real Postgres, nothing sent), both in `npm test`.
- When a bug is reported in one place, grep the repo for the same pattern and fix every instance in the same commit.
- **Scope of Work condition (owner-directed, 2026-07-09; belt-and-suspenders 2026-07-10):** the SOW / rehab-budget tool always **pre-fills** from the application and always **saves** (as a draft) — a total that does not match the file's rehab budget is **never refused** (no "not saved" block; the user can exit and reopen). The SOW **never changes** `applications.rehab_budget` (the budget is frozen, set on the application / registered product). The exact-match rule is purely a **condition gate**: the rehab-budget condition **stays open for every party** (with an `[auto]` note) and may only be **cleared / signed off when TWO numbers each equal the budget EXACTLY (to the cent)** — the **first-page construction budget** (`payload.state.target`, "the total you start at originally", prefilled from the application) **and** the **last-page line-item grand total** (`payload.total`). Checking each against the required budget also transitively forces the start total and the line-item total to equal each other, plus the registered product's budget. Lives in `src/lib/rehab-budget.js` (`checkSowBudget` / `firstPageBudget`) + the three tool-save endpoints (`borrower.js` `/tool`, `staff.js` `/rehab-budget` + `/tool`) + `signOffGate` (`staff.js`, the completion gate) + `web/tools/rehab-budget-portal.js`. The completion path is enforced in **two layers**: the app-layer `signOffGate` (422 on mismatch) **and** a DB **belt-and-suspenders trigger** (`db/069_sow_budget_guard.sql`, `trg_sow_budget_guard` on `checklist_items`) that refuses any write flipping a budget condition to `status='satisfied'` unless start total = line-item total = file budget = product budget to the cent. The trigger uses **`COALESCE` on `tool_key`/template code** — `false OR NULL` is NULL in SQL, which would otherwise block every non-budget condition from being satisfied (verified via end-to-end test; do not remove the COALESCE). The `web/tools/*` assets are **white-first** (`data-theme="light"` is forced) and are **cached hard** — bump the `?v=` query on their `<link>`/`<script>` tags whenever you edit them or the browser serves the stale version.
  - **A budget-NEUTRAL Scope-of-Work reallocation is EXCLUDED from the term-sheet-sent freeze, up to Clear to Close (owner-directed 2026-07-26).** The term-sheet freeze exists for exactly one stated reason — "so the sent term sheet can never silently disagree with the file." Moving $100 off one line item onto another while the construction budget still totals $126,000 cannot cause that disagreement (the term sheet prints the BUDGET, not the line items, and the SOW has never been allowed to write `applications.rehab_budget`), yet it was being refused — forcing the team to VOID a signed term sheet and re-issue an identical one just to re-shape the scope. `src/lib/file-lock.js` now exposes **`sowLockReason(appId, payload, client, opts)`**, which the three SOW save paths (`staff.js` `/rehab-budget` + `/tool`, `borrower.js` `/tool`) call INSTEAD of `structuralLockReason` — it is that function plus the one carve-out. The matrix: term-sheet-SENT + total unchanged → **allowed**; term-sheet-SENT + the total moves → **still frozen** (clear the package, re-register); **Clear-to-Close / Funded → still frozen even when neutral** (past CTC the scope has gone to the investor, so it goes through the existing Scope-of-Work change request in `routes/sitewire.js`, which records before/after budgets and routes a real move for **capital-partner approval** — or a super-admin unlock); declined/withdrawn → unchanged. **"Neutral" is never judged locally** — `sowBudgetNeutral` delegates to `rehab-budget.checkSowBudget` (`ok && !seed`), the SAME gate that decides whether the condition may clear, so the exclusion can never drift from the exact-match rule; anything unprovable **fails CLOSED**. The predicate is on the DATA, not the actor, so it applies identically to the borrower and every staff role. `structuralLockReason` itself is byte-identical in behavior (its two halves were merely factored into `statusFreezeReason`/`termSheetFreezeReason`) — every other economics write path is untouched. The staff refusal quotes `checkSowBudget`'s own cent-precise message so "put it back" is actionable. Tests: `scripts/test-sow-reallocation-lock-pure.js` (the full matrix + fail-closed + a `structuralLockReason`-unchanged guard) and the end-to-end block in `scripts/test-termsheet-freeze.js`. Do NOT widen this to a non-neutral save, and do NOT extend it past Clear to Close.
  - **A TERMS-NEUTRAL re-register is EXCLUDED from the term-sheet-sent freeze — SUPER-ADMIN ONLY, and only when every borrower-visible number is unchanged (owner-directed 2026-08-12).** The owner: after a term sheet is sent the file refuses re-registration, but *"there's a lot of scenarios where something went wrong in the registration … and re-register but the term stays exactly the same"* — e.g. a file registered Standard where re-registering it under the cheaper-for-us Silver keeps the borrower's rate and loan amount identical, or fixing a mis-keyed unit count and holding the quoted rate by raising the internal markup 0.5. The clarifying rule (2026-08-12): *"only a super admin should be able to do it … only if the terms are not changing … if only the program changes but the rate stays the same, even if the BUY RATE changes, if all the details stay the same, reserves everything stays the same, we are good."* So the term-sheet freeze is lifted ONLY for a `super_admin`, ONLY when the borrower-visible figures are byte-identical to the current registration's — the owner's five FINAL numbers (final loan amount, construction/rehab holdback, financed interest reserve, origination fee dollars, note rate) PLUS the loan TERM — while PROGRAM, MARKUP and the internal BUY RATE may change freely. `src/lib/file-lock.js` exposes **`finalNumbersKey(q, term)`** (the neutrality primitive — the five figures + term, EXCLUDING program/markup; `term` is threaded in because it is NOT on the quote object, and on Gold's flat rate a term change need not move any of the five) and the pure **`termsNeutralReregister(lockRow, isNeutral, opts)`** (the freeze decision: STATUS freeze always stands, term-sheet freeze lifts only for `super_admin && isNeutral`). The register route (`staff.js` `/pricing/register`) refuses a STATUS freeze early (unchanged), then — after the new quote + the prior registration's `quote`+`inputs` are loaded — computes `termsNeutral` via `finalNumbersKey` and calls `termsNeutralReregister(lockRow, …)` with the ALREADY-READ row (never a second read — a transient read failure must never lift a freeze the early read established; this is the fix for the pre-merge-audit "fail-open second read"). A NON-super_admin, or ANY moved number/term, gets the ordinary "Clear the Term Sheet package first" 409 (the borrower register route + every other economics write path are UNTOUCHED — they still call `structuralLockReason` and stay fully frozen; a borrower can't switch internal program/markup anyway). The `termsNeutral` verdict ALSO withholds the borrower "terms are ready" email on a neutral re-register (`economicsChanged` is driven by `borrowerTermsKey`, which INCLUDES the program, so a program-only switch reads as "changed" though nothing borrower-visible moved — the redundant nudge is suppressed). **Accepted, owner-directed consequence:** the sent term sheet DOES print a "Program" row, so a cross-program neutral re-register leaves that ONE label historical ("Standard Program" on a file now internally Silver) — the owner authorized this explicitly ("if only the program changes … we are good"); the borrower's TERMS, which the freeze protects, never move. This is the third freeze carve-out (with `sowLockReason` + `payoffContactLockReason`); like them, `structuralLockReason` is byte-identical and unchanged. Tests: `scripts/test-terms-neutral-reregister-pure.js` (the neutrality primitive incl. term, + the pure freeze-decision matrix incl. the super-admin gate) and `scripts/test-terms-neutral-reregister-db.js` (real HTTP + real Postgres: super-admin neutral → 201, a plain admin refused, a PROGRAM change with identical numbers → 201 + no re-notify [the `!termsNeutral` clause, mutation-proven], a moved number/term → 409, status freeze → 409, clearing lifts it). Do NOT widen the carve-out past `super_admin`, and do NOT let it lift the STATUS freeze (a super-admin UNLOCK is the recorded way through that).
  - **AS-IS / ARV SUPER-ADMIN OVERRIDE of the term-sheet-sent freeze — "Save ARV, keep the loan" (owner-directed 2026-08; WIDENED past terms-neutral 2026-08).** On a file whose Term Sheet package is SENT (term-sheet-frozen), a `super_admin` — behind a DOUBLE WARNING + a typed reason — may RECORD a new as-is value + ARV *whether or not* re-pricing at them would move the loan. The owner's clarifying words when a change WOULD re-size: *"Save ARV, keep the loan"* = *"Record the new ARV but keep the loan amount and the sent term sheet exactly as they are. Nothing about the loan changes — you're just updating the recorded value."* THE LOAN IS KEPT FROZEN BY `apply()`'s RE-ASSERT, NOT BY REFUSING THE SAVE: the db/486 trigger reopens Products & Pricing + the signed-term-sheet condition + marks the registration stale on ANY as_is/arv write, so `src/lib/asis-arv-override.js` `apply()` runs ONE transaction — capture those exact rows, write only as_is_value/arv, then RE-ASSERT them to their before-state — so loan_amount, the registration, the sent term sheet and its conditions stay EXACTLY as they are; only the recorded as-is/ARV changes. The pure freeze decision is `fileLock.asIsArvTermSheetOverride(row, isNeutral, opts)` (sibling of `termsNeutralReregister`): allow ONLY `super_admin && opts.overrideRequested` — **neutrality NO LONGER gates** (the `isNeutral` arg is retained for signature stability + audit but no longer affects the decision), and the STATUS freeze (CTC/funded/declined/withdrawn) STILL always stands (a super-admin UNLOCK is the path there, never this override). `evaluate()` still computes the neutrality verdict BEST-EFFORT (never blocking — a throw fails open on the INFO value) purely so the audit trail (`asis_arv_override`, `neutral` field) records whether the override kept the terms or deliberately moved past them. Request must be as-is/ARV-ONLY (any other field → 400) with a typed reason (else 400); a plain admin → 403. Route: `PATCH /applications/:id/details` with `{adminOverride:true, overrideReason}`; the studio's `EditFileDetails.jsx` offers the double-warning override when Save is refused for a frozen term sheet and only as-is/ARV changed. Tests `scripts/test-asis-arv-override-pure.js` (the widened freeze matrix — non-neutral + super_admin + requested now ALLOWED) + `scripts/test-asis-arv-override-db.js` (real Postgres: BOTH a neutral AND a NON-neutral override apply and keep P&P/signed-term-sheet signed off + the registration not-stale + the registered loan amount unmoved, via the same re-assert the capture-set TRIPWIRE guards). Do NOT widen past `super_admin`, do NOT let it lift the STATUS freeze, and do NOT re-add a neutrality refusal (the owner directed recording the value regardless).
  - **EXPERIENCE RE-ALLOCATION between fix-and-flip and fix-and-hold is NOT a term-sheet-clearing change — for EVERY user; and a SUPER-ADMIN with a DOUBLE WARNING may override the term-sheet freeze on ANY Application Details field (owner-directed 2026-08-13).** The owner, on a live file: a term sheet was signed and issued on an application claiming THREE fix-and-flips; verification came back TWO fix-and-flips and ONE fix-and-hold — the same three deals, the same experience level — and the track-record condition could not be signed off because it must MATCH the application, which could not be edited: *"It doesn't let it, and it says that you need to clear the term sheet, you need to delete, you need to change, and then you need to reprice and reissue."* The rule: *"between fix-and-flip and fix-and-hold, as long as it keeps the same backend amount, it should not be considered something that you need to clear the term sheet … The qualified experience stays at 10. You should be able to edit the application, so you should be able to sign off the condition without needing to resend a new term sheet."* And, clarifying: *"I'm basically talking about changing details in the application detail, not changing details in the track record section."*
    · **WHY IT IS SAFE IS A PROPERTY OF THE FROZEN ENGINES, NOT A TOLERANCE — which is what lets it be granted to everyone rather than only a super-admin.** All three engines count the two buckets TOGETHER and never separately: `standard-program.js projectCount(sc, exp)` = `exp.ground` on a ground-up and `flips + holds + ground` otherwise; `gold-standard.js` `renoCount = expFlips + expHolds` vs `groundCount = expGround`; `silver-program.js projectCount` IS `YSP.projectCount`. So with `ground` held equal and `flips + holds` held equal, every engine's project count — hence the tier, hence every number on the sheet — is byte-identical BY CONSTRUCTION. No per-file re-price is needed to trust it, and the predicate is on the DATA exactly like the budget-neutral Scope-of-Work carve-out (`sowLockReason`).
    · **THE TWO REFUSALS FALL OUT OF THE SAME ARITHMETIC — they are not special cases.** *"If somebody changes and he removes ground-up experience, then it does not qualify"* → `ground` is compared on its own and any move refuses (moving a ground-up into flips would keep the renovation count while zeroing the ground-up one). *"If somebody removes either fix-and-flip or fix-and-hold and he puts it through REO, it does not qualify"* → refuses with NO rule about REO at all: REO is the RESIDUAL LIST (2026-08-09), not an engine input, not part of the qualified total and not even watched by db/072, so 10 flips → 5 flips + 5 REO takes `flips + holds` from 10 to 5 and the TOTAL test refuses it. REO may move freely alongside a neutral swap precisely BECAUSE it changes no priced number (a REO-only edit is reported as `inert`, not as a re-allocation).
    · **THE SCOPE TEST IS A VALUE TEST, NOT A KEY TEST, AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING.** The details form posts EVERY field on every save, so a "does the body carry only experience keys?" test would be false on every real save from the very screen this exists for and the rule would never once fire. `experience-realloc.changesOnlyExperience(row, body, fields)` compares each non-experience key to what the file already holds and only counts a key whose value would actually MOVE. It FAILS CLOSED: an unrecognised key, an uncomparable value, and a `propertyAddress` in the body (the form sends it only when the address changed) all read as "something else changed". For that comparison the details door's key→column map moved to **`src/lib/details-fields.js`** — ONE definition, aliased straight into the door's existing `NUM`/`STR`/`DATE` locals so its behaviour is byte-identical. **ADD A DETAILS FIELD THERE, not inline in the route** — a second copy silently disables the carve-out on every file where that field is echoed back.
    · **"WITHOUT CLEARING THE TERM SHEET" IS AN ACTIVE JOB, NOT THE ABSENCE OF A REFUSAL.** db/072 + db/486 reopens Products & Pricing + the signed-term-sheet condition and flags the registration stale on ANY experience write, at the DB layer, so merely letting the save through would leave the owner with the exact reprice-and-reissue they asked to avoid. `src/lib/details-freeze.js` `capture()` reads those rows before the write and `restore()` puts them back after — the same mechanism `asis-arv-override.apply()` uses. **The Scope of Work is DELIBERATELY not re-asserted** on either path: a changed construction budget genuinely invalidates the line items, and db/069's guard refuses that write anyway, so a re-assert would raise, be swallowed and reach the same end state less honestly.
    · **AND THE RE-ALLOCATION HAS A SECOND HALF THE OTHER CARVE-OUTS DO NOT NEED.** `signOffGate` measures the verified track record against the CURRENT REGISTRATION's stored experience (`experience.registeredExperienceNeed`), NOT the application's claim — so editing the application alone would change nothing the owner can see and the condition would go on demanding three verified FLIPS. `syncRegistrationExperience()` moves the registration's stored split with it, which is safe for exactly the reason the carve-out is safe (the engines read `expFlips + expHolds` as one number, so the registration still records the experience the loan was priced on); the `quote` is untouched because not one number in it moves. It is **NEVER** done for the super-admin override — that change is not priced-neutral, and rewriting the priced basis to match would falsify the record of what the borrower was quoted; the db/344 super-admin CONDITION override is the recorded way through from there.
    · **THE SUPER-ADMIN OVERRIDE.** *"superadmin with double warning should be able to overwrite and change anything in the file without clearing the term sheet … Only superadmin, not regular admins."* Asked to choose the breadth, the owner picked EVERY FIELD on the Application Details screen. `fileLock.detailsAdminOverride(row, opts)` is the pure decision — the general sibling of `asIsArvTermSheetOverride`: the STATUS freeze (CTC / funded / declined / withdrawn) ALWAYS stands (a super-admin UNLOCK is the way through those), it lifts ONLY the term-sheet freeze, ONLY for a `super_admin`, ONLY when explicitly requested, and a typed reason is required (400 without one, 403 for a plain admin). Neutrality is deliberately NOT a gate — this override exists precisely for the changes that are not neutral. `EditFileDetails.jsx` offers it behind TWO `askConfirm`s + an `askPrompt`, re-sending the WHOLE form (unlike the as-is/ARV path, which narrows to two fields and says so); the as-is/ARV override now only claims a body `isAsIsArvOnly` proves is as-is/ARV-only, so the two can never both claim a request. Every carved-out save writes its own audit action (`experience_reallocation` / `details_admin_override`) beside the ordinary `edit_application` diff.
    · Tests `scripts/test-experience-realloc-pure.js` (the predicate incl. the two cases that ISOLATE the ground-up rule — the obvious ones also fail the total test, so deleting the rule left them passing until the mutation was run — the value-vs-key scope test, the full freeze matrix, and source guards that the door reads the shared map and still captures/restores) and `scripts/test-experience-realloc-db.js` (real Postgres: a CONTROL proving the trigger genuinely reopens, the refusals, the owner's file end to end with the term sheet intact, the registration's need becoming 2 flips + 1 hold, a re-price at the new split reproducing the stored quote byte for byte, the super-admin override on 10 flips → 5 flips + 5 REO NOT rewriting the priced basis, and a capture-set TRIPWIRE). Do NOT widen the re-allocation past flip↔hold, do NOT let either carve-out lift the STATUS freeze, and do NOT make the scope test a key test again.
  - **A super-admin UNLOCK of a Clear-to-Close / Funded file lifts BOTH freezes — the term-sheet freeze included (owner-reported 2026-07-27; NARROWS the 2026-07-22 “an unlock never bypasses the term-sheet freeze” rule to pre-CTC files only).** Two freezes guard a file's structure in `src/lib/file-lock.js`: the STATUS freeze (#84 — clear\_to\_close / funded / declined / withdrawn, super-admin-unlockable) and the TERM-SHEET-SENT freeze (2026-07-22, cleared by voiding the package). The unlock only ever lifted the FIRST one — and **every file that reaches Clear to Close already has a fully-signed (`completed`) Term Sheet package**, so the second freeze kept refusing the write. The owner unlocked a cleared-to-close file, changed the rehab type, added an assignment fee, hit Save, and nothing saved (YSCAP258134728 / 27 Beacon St): `PATCH /applications/:id/details` returned 409 “Clear the Term Sheet package first” — advice nobody can act on there, since clearing voids a SIGNED term sheet, supersedes the signed document and reopens the term-sheet + application conditions on a file that is already cleared to close. The new predicate **`superUnlockActive(row, opts)`** — `structural_unlocked_at` set **AND** the status is one of `STRUCTURE_LOCKED` **AND** the actor is a staff `super_admin` — now short-circuits `statusFreezeReason` **and** `termSheetFreezeReason`. Scope is deliberately tight: the 🔓 banner only ever appears on those four statuses, the unlock is super-admin-only + audited with a typed reason, and it is REVERSIBLE and leaves the signed term sheet intact (unlike a clear). **BEFORE Clear to Close nothing changed** — an in-flight term sheet still freezes the file for everyone including a super\_admin, with the same “clear the package” message, because there the package is genuinely live and clearing + re-sending it is cheap and correct. A regular admin, and every actor-less (borrower) write path, stay frozen on an unlocked file exactly as before. This also makes the “or a super-admin unlocks the file” escape hatch already promised for a post-CTC Scope-of-Work reallocation (2026-07-26 rule above) actually work. Second half of the same report: `EditFileDetails.jsx` rendered its error/success notice ONLY at the top of a very long form, so by the time you reached the Save button at the bottom a refusal was off-screen and read as “nothing happened” — the same notice now also renders next to the button. Tests: `scripts/test-ctc-unlock-edit.js` (real HTTP: frozen → unlock → saves → admin/borrower still frozen → re-lock → pre-CTC untouched, in `npm test`) plus the updated matrix in `scripts/test-sow-reallocation-lock-pure.js`. Do NOT extend the bypass to a non-super\_admin, to a file that is not status-locked, or to an actor-less write path.
  - **A tool sheet ALWAYS has a way out (owner-directed 2026-07-26).** `ToolModal` deliberately does NOT close when the tool refuses the closing save — the user is meant to stay and fix the number. When the refusal is one they CAN'T fix from inside the tool (the file is frozen, a figure disagrees elsewhere), that same rule became a trap: back, Done and Esc all re-ran the save that just refused. So the sheet now carries a plain **"Leave without saving"** button in its header at ALL times (never disabled, never conditional — `forceExit` calls `onClose` with no handshake), repeated prominently inside the refusal banner, and surfaced inside the saving overlay once a save runs past `STALL_MS` (4s). Esc still saves-and-closes normally, but plainly LEAVES once a save has been refused or stalled. Nothing is lost by using it: the tool autosaves the working draft via `PUT …/tool-state` (never gated by the freeze), so leaving this way only skips regenerating the Excel/PDF exports — the copy says so. Any future full-screen tool host must keep an unconditional exit; a refused save must never be the only way out.
- **Previous AND future (owner-directed, 2026-07-09):** every change here must apply to **EXISTING files too**, never only new ones. When you add or alter a condition, checklist item, field, gate, or any data shape, ship a **deterministic, idempotent backfill migration** (mirror `db/041`/`db/066`: `INSERT … SELECT … WHERE NOT EXISTS`, scoped to active/closed files) so previous files get it on the next boot — do **not** rely solely on the ClickUp sync/reconcile pass to reach old files (it only touches files the sync happens to re-ingest). "It should populate for previous and future."
- **Uploads** use one contract: `{ filename, contentType, dataBase64 }` (raw base64, not a `data:` URL). `api.js` `normalizeUpload()` strips a `dataUrl` if one slips through.
- **Deploys are AUTOMATIC via GitHub Actions → Render Deploy Hook (owner-directed 2026-07-20; closes the recurring "fixed in code but still broken on the live site" gap).** The `deploy` job in `.github/workflows/test.yml` runs on every push to `main`, `needs: test` (so a red test suite NEVER publishes), and POSTs to a Render Deploy Hook whose URL lives ONLY in the repo secret `RENDER_DEPLOY_HOOK_URL` (never committed). If that secret is unset the job SKIPS with a notice (safe to merge before the hook exists). Render's `/api/health` check still gates the actual go-live, so an unhealthy boot is never switched into production. **Turn OFF Render's native GitHub auto-deploy** (service `srv-d94sqalckfvc73ahlqh0`, Settings → Build & Deploy → Auto-Deploy = No) so the tests-gated hook is the SINGLE publish path — otherwise Render also deploys untested code immediately on push. After a merge, the site publishes on its own within minutes; confirm the new bundle hash + `storageWritable` via `/api/health` if verifying. A manual deploy via the Render API is still available as a fallback.
- **Bank-statement / assets condition wording — TWO MONTHS ON EVERY FILE (owner-directed 2026-08-11 — SUPERSEDES the old per-program / note-buyer / manual-stated table: "None of our programs require three months. All of our programs require only two months of bank statements, and a printout after the two statements may be used if the money is not shown yet in the ending balance of the most recent statement").** Every program requires exactly TWO months of bank statements. `src/lib/liquidity.js` `bankStatementMonths` **always returns 2** — the program, the manual "months of liquidity" input (`assetMonths`) and the note buyer no longer change THE STATEMENT COUNT (the args stay on the signature but no longer affect the count; `programMonths` / `noteBuyerMonths` / `NOTE_BUYER_MONTHS` are removed). **The manual "months of liquidity" (`asset_months`) is NOT vestigial** — it is how many months of RESERVES the borrower must SHOW in the ending balance of the most recent statement (owner-directed 2026-08-11: "how many months of reserves you need to show for the manual program … visible in the ending balance of the last statement" — correcting an earlier misread that treated it as a statement count). It is a SEPARATE concept from the statement count and drives ONLY the required-liquidity DOLLAR for a MANUAL program (= cash to close + closing costs + `asset_months` × full monthly payment + the 1% buffer), in `src/lib/pricing.js` `normalize` (reserve block, `program === 'manual'` branch). **The manual program DEFAULTS to THREE months of reserves and the per-file box OVERRIDES that default** (owner-directed 2026-08-11: "on the manual program, the default should be three months' reserves, but that manual box should override the months of reserves you need for the manual") — the default lives in `MANUAL_RESERVE_DEFAULT_MONTHS` (pricing.js, the engine-side fallback when a manual quote carries no box value) and `manual-program.js` `SETTINGS_DEFAULTS.assetMonths` (the company default that pre-fills the register box + is the offer-flow fallback), kept in step at 3; **`db/534`** bumps the untouched seeded company default from 2→3 (an admin's deliberate saved value is never overwritten). A manual quote NEVER uses the Standard 2/4-by-loan-size rule. It is threaded server-side via `inputs.assetMonths` on the register + accept-counter paths (`src/routes/staff.js`), and on the auto-register-from-offer path (`src/lib/term-sheet-offer.js`, which has no per-file months prompt, so it uses the company-default `manualProgram.loadSettings().assetMonths`) — never a client override, never loan sizing/rate/caps. Gold (5% of loan) and Standard/Silver (2/4 months by loan size) are byte-identical. `web/v2/tools/termsheet.js` is deliberately NOT mirrored: the studio has no asset-months input (it is a register-time prompt in `ProductStudioPanel.jsx`, captured after the studio PDF is drawn), so the studio preview keeps the base 2-month reserve while the authoritative liquidity CONDITION comes from the registered quote. `bankStatementLine` states two months, program-named where a program is known (Silver/Gold tested before "standard" so the label "Gold Standard Program" never falls through), and carries the **printout allowance** `PRINTOUT_ALLOWANCE`: a recent transaction printout showing the deposit may be provided IN ADDITION to the two statements when the funds are not yet in the most recent statement's ending balance. The generic pre-registration hint `GENERIC_BANK_STMT_HINT` now also states two months + the allowance (no longer "the number is set once registered"). PREVIOUS AND FUTURE: registered files are regenerated by the boot `backfillLiquidityConditions` (full hint from the stored quote, `noReopen` so a cleared condition is re-worded, never reopened); **`db/533`** re-asserts the new generic on the `rtl_p3_assets` template default + unregistered items (running AFTER `db/070`, which resets them to the OLD generic every boot — the db/374 ordering pattern) and caps any recorded `bankStatements.months` at 2 (belt-and-suspenders for a registered file with no stored quote, which the JS backfill skips). The internal manual-approval emails show the real `asset_months` uncapped as a "Reserve months to show" line (`email/pricing-email.js`) — it is the reserve requirement, which can legitimately exceed two. Everything downstream inherits it: `program-guidelines` note, `note-buyer-effects` (bank statements now read "set by the program, not the note buyer", `fromNoteBuyer:false` — nothing raises the count), the `completeness` label, and `cure.js` required-months. Do NOT re-introduce a per-program or note-buyer month count without fresh owner sign-off. Tests: `test-silver-emcap-effects-pure`, `test-manual-program`, `test-underwriting-program-guidelines`, `test-findings-cleanup-round2-pure`, `test-note-buyer-slot-db`.
  - **A 2-month requirement is LOUD, not fine print (owner-directed 2026-07-30: "it should stay in very bulk on the condition highlighted Quoted that this program requires two months of bank statements according to the registration").** Every registered file's assets hint (all require two months now) is prefixed by `loudMonthsBanner()` — a first line starting with `⚠️` + `TWO (2) MONTHS OF BANK STATEMENTS REQUIRED — "This program requires two months of bank statements according to the registration."` followed by `\n` and the ordinary hint. The `⚠️`+first-line+`\n` prefix is the CONTRACT between server and portals: `app-v2/src/components/LoudHint.jsx` (`splitLoudHint`/`LoudBanner`) renders that first line as a bold gold-bordered callout in BOTH portals' condition views (StaffApplication + borrower Application), and any plain-text surface (V1, emails, ClickUp) still reads loud because the words themselves carry it. Never strip the banner in a new surface — render it or leave the text intact. (The generic pre-registration hint is the one bank-statement wording with no banner — there is no "according to the registration" before a product is registered.)
- **A DIFFERENT-LLC bank account is an OWNERSHIP check, NOT fraud (owner-directed 2026-07-27 — SUPERSEDES the 2026-07-24 FATAL).** The owner: *"using cash and bank statements from a different LLC… this is NOT major fraud — it's because they didn't verify ownership… it's a very good finding, but you need to VERIFY THE OWNERSHIP instead of raising crazy fraud."* So `bank_account_other_entity` is now a **`warning` (advisory, never blocks CTC)**, not the old CTC-blocking fatal — safe because the liquidity engine already EXCLUDES a non-matching holder's balance independently (`holderMatchesFile`), so downgrading the finding can never let unverified funds count or over-lend. Two shapes now: if the file ALREADY has an entity document for the holder (operating agreement / articles / EIN read into `document_extractions` — `file-view.loadContext` exposes their `entityLegalName`s as `entityDocNames` → `subjectFor('bank_statement').entity_docs_on_file`, matched by `entityDocOnFile`), the finding POINTS AT that proof (`docsOnFile:true`, "verify the documents already on file, then add the entity") — the owner's *"a lot of times the operating agreement is uploaded WITH the bank statements — look for it"*; otherwise it suggests posting a condition to collect the OA/articles/EIN. The `bank_account_not_borrower` case (a third-party PERSONAL account) stays fatal — the owner's directive was specifically the different-LLC (business) case. Test `scripts/test-bank-statement-checks-pure.js` cases 4/4a2 pin `severity==='warning'`, `blocksCtc===false`, and the docs-on-file shape. On top of this, the assets review also raises two warning-only nudges when the borrower IS an owner of the account (`src/lib/underwriting/bank-statement-checks.js`): (1) **`bank_business_entity_unverified`** — the holder is a business that matches one of the borrower's KNOWN entities but that entity is not `is_verified` on the file → suggest finishing the LLC section (operating agreement + articles of formation + EIN); it cascades to `rtl_llc_opagmt` via `bank-statement-suggestions.js`. It NEVER fabricates: it only fires when the subject actually carries `verified_entity_names` (an array — `file-view.loadContext` returns `verifiedEntityNames` from `llcs.is_verified`, threaded through `subjectFor('bank_statement')` as `verified_entity_names`; a verified holder → no flag). (2) **`bank_account_shared`** — a JOINT personal account (the borrower plus co-owner(s), detected via `accountOwners`/`splitPersonalHolders` over the holder string's `and`/`&`/`/` conjunctions + the new `BANK_STATEMENT.additionalHolders[]` schema field) → suggest an ACCESS LETTER (a `request_document`, reason `joint_account_access_letter`) from the co-owner(s) confirming the borrower may use the funds. Ownership matching now considers EVERY named owner, so a joint account that includes the borrower no longer false-fires the `bank_account_not_borrower` fatal. Business names are never split (LLC names contain "and"/"&"); `ENTITY_WORDS` recognizes the common entity tokens. Test `scripts/test-bank-statement-checks-pure.js` (cases 4b–4h + the `subjectFor` wiring guard). Keep these advisory and never let them block CTC/funding.
- **THE DIFFERENT-LLC FINDING HAS A WAY OUT — one button that puts the entity on the borrower's profile, carries its documents over, and posts the entity-documents condition (owner-directed 2026-08-02; BUILDS ON the 2026-07-27 rule above, changes none of it).** The owner: *"when he is finding assets on a different LLC it's not major fraud — it's just that you don't have the LLC documents for that LLC. Give a button on that finding to add this LLC to the borrower profile, so it's safe for always and we can always see that he has this LLC on his record; then suggest posting a condition to ask for entity documents to allow him to use this LLC. And if you see in the assets condition that not only bank statements, you also see an operating agreement from that different LLC we're using the funds from — use that operating agreement to satisfy your finding, still SHOW the finding, and take that operating agreement and add it to that new LLC's operating-agreement slot so it's available for the future on his profile."* The finding's severity/wording is UNCHANGED (still the advisory ownership check); what was missing was the action.
  1. **ONE module does all four steps together — `src/lib/underwriting/entity-adopt.js` `adoptEntityToProfile`** (route `POST /api/underwriting/:appId/entity-adopt`, button on the finding card in `UnderwritingPanel.jsx`): create-or-REUSE the entity through the repo's one chokepoint (`llc.findOrCreateLlc` — a name the borrower already has is never duplicated), generate its document slots (`generateLlcChecklist`), COPY every entity document already on the file that PILOT read as belonging to that entity onto the matching slot, and post `rtl_cond_entity_docs`. Idempotent end to end — a second click adds nothing twice. `findOrCreateLlc` / `findLlcByName` / `generateLlcChecklist` / `insertFromTemplate` gained an OPTIONAL trailing `client` (defaulting to the pool) so the whole adoption is one transaction; every existing caller is byte-identical.
  2. **THE ADOPTION IS STAMPED, AND THAT STAMP IS A SAFETY PROPERTY, NOT BOOKKEEPING (`llcs.adopted_from_application_id/adopted_at/adopted_source`, db/400).** `bank-liquidity.js` counts an account as the borrower's when its holder matches ANY entity on the profile — so writing the NAME alone would have moved that account's whole balance into the file's provable liquidity the moment somebody pressed the button, one click after reading "this is not fraud". That is exactly the over-count the finding exists to prevent. So `file-view.loadContext` returns `entityDocsPendingNames` (adopted + not verified + no entity document on any of its slots) and `assessBankLiquidity` filters those names out of `entity_names` — the balance stays in the EXCLUDED column, whose note already says it counts "once the borrower's control of that entity is documented". The hold RELEASES ITSELF when a document lands or the entity is verified; an entity a HUMAN put on the profile is never stamped and never held back, so no existing file's liquidity moves. Do not remove the stamp to "simplify" the adoption.
  3. **THE BYTES ARE COPIED, NOT SHARED.** Purging a file deletes the stored bytes of every document carrying its `application_id`, deliberately sparing entity documents because they are keyed on `llc_id` — so pointing the profile's row at the file's `storage_ref` would take the profile's copy down with the file, defeating the whole "available for the future" point. The copy records `source_document_id` (lineage + the idempotency key) and is NOT auto-accepted: a reviewer still confirms the agreement names the borrower as managing member / 25%+ owner before the entity can be verified. A VERIFIED entity is skipped entirely (the portal already refuses uploads into one).
  4. **THE FINDING IS SETTLED ONLY BY THE OPERATING AGREEMENT.** Articles and an EIN letter prove the company EXISTS; only the agreement says who CONTROLS it, which is the question. So `ownershipProofLanded` gates the resolution: with the agreement on the entity the finding is cleared with the plain-language `adoptionSummary` recorded on it; without it the entity is still added and the condition still posted, and the finding STAYS OPEN. The summary describes the STATE the entity is in, never just this run's activity (a second click carries nothing, and a note written from `carried` alone would read "its slots are still empty" on the very click that settled the finding). The route is gated on FILE ACCESS, not `sign_off_conditions` — a loan officer can already create an entity from the CRM, so gating the button would kill it for the person most likely to press it; only the finding's RESOLUTION needs the sign-off permission, and the response says so when it was left open.
  5. **`rtl_cond_entity_docs` (db/400) is `auto_apply='manual'` and application-scoped**, so the rules engine never attaches or retracts it and `generateChecklist` never dumps it on an unrelated file. This also fixes a live dead button: `bank-statement-suggestions.js` proposed `rtl_llc_opagmt`, which is scope='llc' — the convert-to-condition route only instantiates `scope='application'` templates, so every click answered 400 "unknown template rtl_llc_opagmt". Never propose an llc-scoped SLOT as a file condition.
  Tests `scripts/test-entity-adopt-pure.js`, `test-entity-adopt-db.js` and `test-entity-adopt-route-db.js` (all in `npm test`); the liquidity guard was proven to FAIL both tests with the filter reverted. `entity-adopt.js` is on the `test-ai-no-condition-write.js` allowlist because it carries out a HUMAN's click — PILOT only ever suggests the button.
- **AN ENTITY IS NOT ALWAYS AN LLC — the entity TYPE, its owners' TITLES, and what each type is asked for (owner-directed 2026-08-09; db/509 + db/510).** The owner: *"let's take this table that we currently have for LLCs and leave that for LLCs — put that under a table for entity … on all of the applications from all the sides, staff and borrower and marketing site, when they enter the LLC vesting name it should be changed to VESTING ENTITY NAME, and then they need to select if it's an LLC or a corporation … if it's a corporation, instead of operating agreement, bylaws and stock certificate, which should be uploaded in ONE document slot — we don't need to add another slot … everything created till now should automatically be default LLC, only going forward this change to go in effect … everywhere else I shouldn't say LLC, say entity."* Then, choosing the shape: **LLC, Corporation, Partnership, Trust** (all four), and *"for each member's title we shouldn't need to type it — it should be a drop-down … this should also be for LLC … but it should only be for the staff side to fill out; when the closer gets the closing desk, if it's not filled yet they should tell her that she needs to fill it."*
  1. **`src/lib/entity-type.js` IS THE ONE DEFINITION** (pure — no database, no config, no requires): the four types, the DocLab wording each one produces, which document each is asked for, and which titles its owners may hold. `app-v2/src/lib/entityType.js` is the browser mirror (the portal cannot require server code — the `lib/payoff.js` / `lib/dealBasis.js` arrangement), and `test-entity-type-pure.js` reads BOTH files and fails the moment they disagree: a picker offering a title the server then refuses is a dead end a user cannot get out of. **Never re-inline one of these lists.**
  2. **THE TABLE IS NOT RENAMED, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.** `llcs` is referenced by ~200 files, by `llc_id` foreign keys on nine tables, by the ClickUp field map and by the SharePoint folder resolver. Renaming it would be a mechanical change with a real chance of a silent miss and would buy nothing a human can see. What the OWNER sees is the wording, and the wording is what moved: "LLC" is "entity" on every screen, condition, notification and error message. Same reasoning for the component filenames (`LlcManager.jsx`, `LlcPicker.jsx`).
  3. **THE BACK BOOK IS AN LLC — AND SAYS IT WAS ASSUMED.** db/509's `DEFAULT 'llc'` performs the back-fill (Postgres stamps every existing row as it adds the column, so there is no separate UPDATE to get wrong and no window where a row has no type), and every one of those rows is ALSO stamped **`entity_type_confirmed = false`**. That distinction is the whole point: *"we assumed"* and *"a person chose"* are different facts and only one of them is safe to print on a mortgage. Nothing behaves differently on an unconfirmed entity — it is treated as an LLC everywhere — it just lets a surface that is about to draft real documents admit it is assuming. **`isRecognized()` is what keeps a typo or a blank out**, and it must test the RAW input before `normalizeKey`: that function deliberately falls back to LLC on a blank (so every consumer does not write its own `|| 'llc'`), so asking it about an empty form box gives a perfectly valid `'llc'` back — which is exactly how a form nobody filled in gets filed as an LLC somebody vouched for. The pure test caught that; keep it.
  4. **A TYPE STATED LATER RECORDS, UNDER THREE GUARDS — `llc.confirmEntityType`.** `findOrCreateLlc` returns an EXISTING entity untouched by contract, so this is the one place a type can reach one. It only ever writes an entity **nobody has confirmed** (a type somebody already chose is never overwritten by a different door months later) and **nobody has verified** (its documents have been read and accepted, so its type is settled by evidence — a mis-click on a re-used name must not relabel a finished entity's document slots), and an unrecognised value **states nothing**. Never throws.
  5. **THE SLOT WORDING RIDES THE ITEM, NOT THE TEMPLATE — and that is the mechanism that reaches SharePoint and the TPR export for free** (the owner's *"this change needs to be everywhere else — SharePoint syncing, TPR export and everywhere else"*). There is ONE `rtl_llc_opagmt` template and one row per entity created from it, so the template cannot say "operating agreement" and "bylaws" at the same time. `checklist_items` already COPY the wording at creation, SharePoint names its folders from the item's label, and the TPR export categorises from it — so both follow with no second map to keep in step. `llc.applyEntitySlotWording` is that one writer.
  6. **db/033 UNDOES IT ON EVERY BOOT, AND THE REPAIR IS JAVASCRIPT.** That migration copies the shared TEMPLATE's borrower wording back down onto every item made from these three templates, every deploy — correct for an LLC, wrong for a corporation. Migrations are never edited here, and the usual ordering trick (a later migration re-asserting the converged state, the db/374 pattern) would mean writing the wording table a SECOND time in SQL, where it would drift from the JavaScript — the `pilot_term_norm` / `pilot_property_type_norm` trap this repo has been bitten by twice. So `src/lib/entity-slot-heal.js` re-applies the SAME function at boot, after the migrations, scoped to non-LLC entities (an LLC's correct wording IS the template's, so the whole back book is a no-op). **This is also why `applyEntitySlotWording`'s guard fires on ANY of the four fields being off rather than on the label alone** — db/033 clobbers the borrower wording while leaving the internal label intact, and a label-only test reports "nothing to do" on exactly the rows that were just reverted. The template's own CURRENT wording counts as ours to replace (`OR ci.label = t.label`), or a brand-new corporation's freshly-inherited slot would be refused — the exact row this exists for.
  7. **THE TITLE IS A DROP-DOWN, NEVER A TEXT BOX, AND IT IS STAFF-ONLY.** It prints under a signature line on a recorded instrument and DocLab merges it verbatim, so "managing member", "Managing Member" and "MGR" must not all be reachable; `AUTHORIZED_SIGNATORY` is on every type's list so the picker can never become a dead end. `titleProblem` deliberately accepts ANY type's title — an entity's type can be corrected after its owners are recorded, and refusing the stored title at that moment would either wipe it or block the correction; what it refuses is a title on NO list. Columns land on BOTH owner tables (`llc_borrowers` = the owners who are our borrowers, `llc_members` = everybody else) because a loan document does not care about that distinction, and a column on only one would produce a document missing half the ownership. `parseMembers(raw, pct, {allowOwnerDetails, entityType})` — the borrower's door passes neither, so the three keys are **IGNORED rather than refused** (a borrower must never be answered with an error about a box they cannot see).
  8. **A BORROWER'S EDIT MUST NOT WIPE THE CLOSER'S WORK.** `replaceMembers` replaces the list wholesale (delete + re-insert) and the borrower never sends these three keys, so without carrying them over a borrower fixing a spelling silently blanks a value that prints under a signature. They are carried by normalized NAME (the only stable identity a re-inserted row has); a value the caller DID supply always wins, including a deliberate blank. `replaceMembers` also gained the repo's usual optional `opts.client` so it can run inside a transaction.
  9. **A CORPORATION HOLDS SHARES, AN LLC HOLDS A PERCENTAGE — never both.** `number_of_shares` / `certificate_number` are sent only for a share-based type and `membership_interest_percentage` only for the others: the merge prints whichever the template asks for, so the wrong one lands on a pledge as a fact. A share count is a COUNT — zero, negative and fractional are all refused at the parser AND by the column's CHECK, because printing "0 shares" onto a pledge is worse than printing nothing. **A stock certificate number is not bookkeeping**: a corporation issues NUMBERED certificates, our loans take a pledge of the owners' interests as extra collateral, and the pledge has to name the exact certificate being handed over the way a mortgage names the exact property. An LLC normally has no certificate, which is why the percentage serves there.
  10. **THE CLOSING DESK IS TOLD, NEVER BLOCKED.** `llc.ownersMissingTitles` (both tables; a COMPANY owner is skipped — it holds no title, it signs through its own people on its own row) and the unconfirmed-type notice both render on `ClosingPrepCard` as nudges. `closing-prep.blockers()` deliberately does NOT read them: refusing a whole closing-prep order over something a closer can fix in ten seconds, while they are looking right at it, would be worse than saying so plainly. The attorney email states the entity KIND only when somebody actually chose it — telling outside counsel "limited liability company" about a corporation because of our own back-fill is the confident wrong answer that file exists to avoid.
  11. **Doors covered:** marketing `loan-application.html` (which had ASKED for `eType` since it shipped, printed it on the review summary and **never sent it** — the same collected-then-discarded class as the refinance fields; the answer is now required and the options are the four types), borrower Apply + draft-submit + register, staff new-file + vesting link + register + completeness, both entity screens, the public intake, and the `vesting.js` chokepoint every one of them routes through. **When you add a door that creates an entity, pass `entityType` through `vesting.resolveEntityByName` / `llc.findOrCreateLlc` — never a second create path.** Tests `scripts/test-entity-type-pure.js` + `scripts/test-entity-type-db.js` (both in `npm test`). **Deliberately NOT renamed:** the `tpr-export` category folder `LLC` — renaming it would split every existing investor package and SharePoint mirror between an old "LLC" folder and a new one, which is the one-shot-refolder problem the EMD change had to solve separately; raise it with the owner before touching it.
- **A PARTNERSHIP AND A TRUST ARE NOT ONE THING EACH — the sub-kind, and the dead end it removes (owner-directed 2026-08-09: "do research what else DocLab needs for partnership and trust, let's set up that tables as well"; db/511; the full research is `docs/doclab/DOCLAB-ENTITY-TYPES.md`).**
  1. **THE RESEARCH ANSWER IS THAT DOCLAB NEEDS NOTHING NEW.** Their published dictionary is 103 variables and contains **no partnership-specific and no trust-specific field**. Every entity goes out through the same handful (`type_of_organization`, `acknowledgement_corporate_status`, `bylaws_operating_agreement`/`operating_agreement_or_bylaws`, then either `membership_interest_percentage` or `number_of_shares`+`certificate_number`, plus `signatory_title`) — all wired by db/509. A partnership and a trust travel the PERCENTAGE road exactly as an LLC does; only a corporation takes the shares road.
  2. **THE REAL GAP WAS OURS, AND IT WAS A DEAD END.** `llc.missingForVerification` is a HARD gate — a verified entity satisfies the vesting-entity condition, which gates clear to close — and it demanded an EIN, a formation STATE and a formation DATE from every entity. But a **revocable living trust has no EIN of its own** (it uses the grantor's Social Security number) and is filed with **no state**; a **general partnership** is created by its agreement and is filed with no state either (it does have an EIN — it files Form 1065). So an ordinary family trust could NEVER be verified, its condition could never clear, and nobody could resolve it because the missing documents are not obtainable. `llcs.entity_subtype` is the one answer that says what an entity can actually produce, and `entity-type.requirements()` is the one definition every gate reads.
  3. **THE CHECK IS WRITTEN PER TYPE, and that is load-bearing:** only a partnership may hold `general|limited|llp` and only a trust `revocable|irrevocable`. A sub-kind stored against the wrong type would silently relax the WRONG requirement. `normalizeSubtype(type, v)` normalizes against the type at every door for the same reason, so switching a trust to an LLC cannot leave "revocable" behind.
  4. **AN UNSTATED SUB-KIND RELAXES RATHER THAN BLOCKS — a deliberate asymmetry.** Demand an EIN a revocable trust does not have and the file dead-ends with nobody able to fix it; fail to demand an LP's state certificate and a reviewer simply asks for it (the requirement is togglable on the condition). `requirements()` reports `subtypeKnown:false` so the screens ask, and the closing desk nudges. **"Optional" never means unwanted** — a value that IS present is still stored, validated and sent. The DATE is never relaxed: a trust is legally identified by its name AND its date ("The Smith Family Trust, dated March 3, 2019"), which is also why `describe().dateLabel` reads "Trust date" there and "Partnership agreement date" on a partnership.
  5. **TWO SLOTS STOP GATING WHEN THE DOCUMENT DOES NOT EXIST** — `rtl_llc_formation` when there is no state filing, `rtl_llc_ein` when there is no EIN — written by `applyEntitySlotWording` from `requirements()`, BOTH WAYS (correcting general → limited restores the requirement), and never over a slot somebody has already worked (a document on it, or a status past `outstanding`). **`missingForVerification`'s "document requirements not generated" check had to change with it**: it counted the REQUIRED slots, which conflated "never built" with "deliberately optional" and would have fired on a complete general partnership — it now counts what EXISTS.
  6. **`type_of_organization` IS REFINED FOR A PARTNERSHIP AND DELIBERATELY NOT FOR A TRUST.** DocLab prints it verbatim onto a recorded instrument and a general partnership and a limited partnership are different legal entities with different liability, so the sub-kind supplies "general partnership" / "limited partnership" / "limited liability partnership". A trust stays "trust": that is never wrong, "revocable trust" would be wrong if the sub-kind were mis-set, and the trust's own NAME carries the rest. Overridable per file either way.
  7. **`trustee` IS A FOURTH FALSE FRIEND**, alongside `lender`, `underwriter` and `state_abbrev`: it is the trustee under the DEED OF TRUST — DocLab's own words, "most often the title company at closing" — and has NOTHING to do with a borrowing trust's trustee. Wiring one there would name the borrower's own family member as the party holding legal title for the lender. A trust's trustees reach the documents as signatories with the title "Trustee".
  8. **OPEN WITH PLL, and recorded so it is not forgotten:** `acknowledgement_corporate_status` is published THREE different ways for an LLC (dictionary "operating agreement and its members"; integration document 'Enter "Bylaws and Board of Directors" or "Operating Agreement and Members"'; their own master payload `"operating agreement and members "`) and **not at all for a partnership or a trust** — ours is an analogy, and it merges into a sentence on a recorded instrument. `company_filing_status` (3.1.4) has an EMPTY description and is in NO template's variable list; nothing is sent for it. Ask both in one conversation.
  Tests: `scripts/test-entity-type-pure.js` + `-db.js` (both in `npm test`). The DB suite RUNS db/510 inside its own transaction rather than trusting the ambient database — three earlier migrations re-assert the old wording every boot, so what must be proven is that it CONVERGES, not that some other suite left the row tidy. `getMembers` / `getSlots` / `getLlcBundle` / `replaceMembers` gained the repo's usual optional trailing `client` so an entity can be read and written inside one transaction.

- **Chain-of-title / ownership trace — the ORDERED, MULTI-HOP reconciliation (owner-directed, 2026-07-24; ADVISORY, never block).** `src/lib/underwriting/chain-of-title.js` `buildChainOfTitle(ctx, exts)` follows ownership across every document and confirms the names line up at each hop: owner of record (title `vestedOwners` / appraisal owner) → seller on the purchase contract → buyer on the contract (first assignor) → each ASSIGNMENT's assignor→assignee (ALL hops, ordered by `assignmentDate`) → the vesting entity. It reuses seller-chain's matching primitives (`_internals.sameParty/partyInList/isEntityName`) — no duplication — and adds what seller-chain (single-hop, ignores the assignment doc's own parties) and the tie-out (single-bucket-per-fact) can't express: per-adjacent-pair reconciliation. Advisory findings (all `warning`/`info`, `blocksCtc:false`): `cot_seller_not_owner_of_record` (contract seller ≠ record owner), `cot_assignor_never_held_title` (an assignment signed by a party who never held the contract), `cot_assignment_seller_mismatch` (an assignment names a different underlying seller), `cot_final_buyer_not_vesting` (chain ends off the vesting entity), `cot_unverified_hop` (info — a name is missing so the chain can't be confirmed). NEVER fabricates: a same-party hop with a missing name is `unknown`, not a break; empty inputs → no findings. The borrower's-personal-name case is DEFERRED to seller-chain's `contract_in_personal_name` (no duplicate), and the richer `cot_final_buyer_not_vesting` suppresses seller-chain's info `chain_vesting_not_reached` in the roll-up. Wired in `src/routes/underwriting.js` (desk `chainOfTitle` block + folded into `openRaw`), bridged to `ai_suggestions` via `entity-chain-suggestions.syncChainsToSuggestions({..., chainOfTitle})` (dedupe `chain_of_title:<code>:<field>`), and rendered as the "Chain of title" stepper in `app-v2 UnderwritingPanel.jsx` (`ChainOfTitle`). Pure test `scripts/test-chain-of-title.js`. Rebuild `web/v2/portal` after touching the panel.
- **Gold Standard Program requires a 5% SOW contingency (owner-directed, 2026-07-12):** a file registered Gold must carry a contingency **≥ 5% of the construction (line-item) subtotal** on its Scope of Work. The contingency lives WITHIN the frozen rehab budget (`grand = subtotal + contingency + GC`), so it never changes the budget — it's a **composition gate** layered on top of the exact-match rule. The SOW **always saves as a draft**; the rehab-budget condition just can't be **signed off** without it. Lives in `src/lib/rehab-budget.js` (`goldContingencyOk` / `checkGoldSow` / `enforceGoldSowContingency`), the `signOffGate` budget branch, all three SOW save endpoints, both register endpoints (which **reopen** the condition — clearing any prior sign-off — with a FATAL `[auto]` note when a Gold file's SOW lacks it), the DB trigger `db/069` (belt-and-suspenders), and `db/070` (reopen backfill for previous Gold files). The builder (`web/tools/rehab-budget.js`) reads `?program=gold` and **auto-fills a 5% contingency** (never downgrading a larger one), and submits `subtotal` + `contingency` amounts so the server can verify without the frozen engine. React URL builders pass `program=gold` when `registered_program==='gold'`.
- **Note buyer is a first-class field; CorrFirst opens an EMD condition; Blue Lake also demands the 5% SOW contingency (owner-directed, 2026-07-20).** Three linked changes around the **note buyer** (`applications.lender` — the capital partner, pull-only from ClickUp, STAFF-ONLY, never shown to a borrower):
  1. **`note_buyer` is now a Condition Center rule field** (`src/lib/conditions/field-registry.js` `normNoteBuyer` = lowercase + strip non-alphanumerics, SAME key form as `sitewire_partner_links.label_norm`, so "CorrFirst"/"Corr First"/"corrfirst" all match; enum options Blue Lake/CorrFirst/Fidelis) and joins `engine.loadRuleContext` ctx as `note_buyer`. It is NOT writable from a borrower info-condition (staff-only).
  2. **CorrFirst opens a BORROWER EXTERNAL "verify EMD deposit" condition** — rule-driven template `cond_emd_corrfirst` (`db/191`, `auto_apply='rules'`, `rule_logic` note_buyer eq corrfirst, audience `borrower`, item_kind `document`). The engine attaches it while the note buyer is CorrFirst and retracts it (untouched only) when it changes away. Borrower wording (`borrower_label`/`borrower_hint`) names only the EMD, NEVER CorrFirst. Backfill in `db/191` seeds every existing open CorrFirst file. The engine now also runs on **ClickUp ingest** (`src/clickup/ingest.js`, after `applyChecklistStatuses`) and on the **staff completeness edit** (`staff.js completeFields`) so a note buyer arriving/changing from either surface attaches/retracts it. **The EMD proof files into its OWN "EMD" folder in the TPR export AND the SharePoint mirror — never inside "Contract & Assignment" (owner-directed 2026-07-31):** `tpr-export.categoryFor` maps `cond_emd_corrfirst` (and the earnest/EMD/escrow-deposit keyword fallback) to `C.EMD`; the mirror inherits it through the shared categorizer, and existing mirrored copies stay where they are (no-move policy — the mirror's version stream resets to the new folder on the next upload). PREVIOUS files: `src/lib/sharepoint-emd-refolder.js` (owner-directed 2026-07-31: "run the recategorize script so old files get the EMD folder") is a ONE-SHOT boot pass that renames the mirror's OWN old condition folders to "EMD" — the EMD-scoped slice of `scripts/sharepoint-recategorize-existing.js --apply`, automatic because the owner asked for the outcome and the manual script needs a credentialed shell. Same safety, none of it negotiable: every rename through `sp.renameOwnItem`'s guards (created-by-this-app, in-mirror ancestry, sibling-collision refuse, eTag pin — the comment there names its exactly three sanctioned callers); ONLY a folder whose EVERY current mirrored doc categorizes as EMD (a mixed contract+EMD folder is skipped, never split/merged); nothing moved or deleted; `sync_runtime_state` marker `sharepoint_emd_refolder_v1` stamps only a complete, error-free pass, and ANY inspect error or a hit inspect cap SKIPS the whole rename phase and leaves it unstamped to retry — a truncated view must never "prove" a folder is all-EMD (the inspect-error arm is second-audit hardening: a doc whose enrichment/walk failed may be the non-EMD resident of a grouped folder); kill switch `SHAREPOINT_EMD_REFOLDER_DISABLED=1`; renames audited (`sharepoint_emd_refolder_renamed`). The GENERAL recategorize stays the manual dry-run-first script; do not widen the one-shot past the EMD case without owner direction. Test `scripts/test-sharepoint-emd-refolder.js` (pure decision table + real-schema SQL, in `npm test`).
  3. **The 5% SOW contingency requirement now also fires for a BLUE LAKE note buyer, not just Gold** (extends the 2026-07-12 Gold rule below). `src/lib/rehab-budget.js` `sowContingencyRequired(appId)` = required when program is Gold **OR** note buyer normalizes to `bluelake`; `checkGoldSow`/`enforceGoldSowContingency` are kept as back-compat aliases of the generalized `checkSowContingency`/`enforceSowContingency`. The message is now the program/partner-**agnostic**, borrower-safe `SOW_CONTINGENCY_MSG` ("This loan requires at least a 5% contingency…") — `GOLD_CONTINGENCY_MSG` aliases it; the legacy Gold note text is still matched for clearing. Enforced in `signOffGate` (budget branch), the DB guard (`db/192` CREATE OR REPLACE of `sow_budget_guard()` adds the Blue Lake condition + generic exception), and `db/192`(B) reopen-backfill for existing Blue Lake files lacking the 5%. Do not narrow the requirement back to Gold-only.
  4. **Note buyer is EDITABLE in the system + part of application completeness** (staff-only). `GET /api/staff/note-buyers` (`src/lib/note-buyers.js`) returns every note buyer available in the **ClickUp dropdown** (`registry.optionMap` + `F.PIPELINE.lender`) unioned with the registry set + on-file values. Staff can fill/correct it from a datalist in the completeness panel (surfaces when empty) and inline on the ClickUp-sync panel (`NoteBuyerInline`, both in `app-v2 StaffApplication.jsx`), saved via `complete-fields` (`lender` whitelisted in `COMPLETE_APP_FIELDS`). It is NOT on the borrower completeness whitelist and the note-buyer name never reaches a borrower. `lender` stays pull-only outbound (a completeness enqueue of `lender` is a safe no-op — `mapper` skips `dir:'pull'`), and a lender-only edit is NOT frozen by the economics lock. Tests: `scripts/test-note-buyer-conditions.js` (in `npm test`).
  4a2. **THE REGISTERED PROGRAM CHOOSES THE NOTE BUYER — derived, never picked (owner-directed 2026-08-06).** Asked how the note buyer should travel when a file is started from the Term Sheet Generator, the owner: *"You already have knowledge of which program is which note buyer. Whenever it's a manual program, you leave it untouched and don't select the note buyer — you'll leave that selection for later. If somebody selects the standard program, then use the note buyer for the standard. It shouldn't be visible to anyone other than the staff, but you can know how to select that. Just populate in our system: Fidelis for the standard, Blue Lake for the gold, EMCAP for the silver. Manual should be an open selection."*
    · **NO PICKER IS ADDED TO THE GENERATOR, deliberately.** The generator has no note-buyer field and is not getting one: the pairing is already the backend's own knowledge, so a picker would be a SECOND place for it to live and a chance for the two to disagree. `src/lib/note-buyer-for-program.js` derives it instead, and **does not restate the pairing** — it reads `tapes/program-provider.providerForProgram`, the SAME table the non-admin tape-export gate enforces. Deriving from any other list would let a file be stamped with a buyer whose tape it is then refused permission to export. **MANUAL is already absent from that table** ("a manual loan's program is not locked to any one provider"), which IS the owner's "leave it untouched" — so there is no manual special-case beyond honouring the null.
    · **WRITTEN AT THE ONE CHOKEPOINT, FILL-ONLY.** `persistProductRegistration` is the single path every register door goes through — staff, borrower, marketing intake and accept-counter — so `lender=COALESCE(lender, $24)` covers all sides at once.
