A note buyer a human typed, or one ClickUp pulled in, is NEVER overwritten, on a first register or a re-register. The buyer's CONDITIONS attach on their own: every register route already runs `evaluateApplication` after the commit.
    · **THE LABEL IS THE BUYER'S REAL PRODUCTION SPELLING** ("Fidelis Investors LLC" / "Blue Lake Capital" / "EMCAP Financial"), never the internal key — the key is not a value `applications.lender` ever holds. A load-time self-check (`verifyLabels`, asserted empty by the pure test) proves every label is one its OWN tape module's `buyerAliases` recognises, so a derived buyer can always export its tape. **The standard this is held to is that a DERIVED buyer behaves identically to the same label typed by a human** — pinned by the DB test comparing both files' `note_buyer` key and buyer booleans. Note `normNoteBuyer` is deliberately EXACT, so "Blue Lake Capital" keys as `bluelakecapital`, NOT the bare `bluelake` — true of a real production label too; do NOT "helpfully" stamp the bare key to make an `eq bluelake` rule fire, because that would make derived files behave UNLIKE real ones. Blue Lake's 5% SOW contingency is unaffected either way: it is only ever stamped on a GOLD file, which requires the 5% through the program branch regardless. Tests `scripts/test-note-buyer-for-program-pure.js` + `-db.js` (both in `npm test`). Adding a fourth provider needs the owner to name its label.
  4b. **THE NOTE BUYER HAS A SLOT — one home that EXPLAINS itself (owner-directed 2026-07-27: "we need a better slot … right now I don't believe it's a clear path").** Item 4 made the note buyer settable but gave it nowhere to LIVE. Once set it vanished from the completeness panel (that panel lists only what's MISSING), so the sole way to change it was a ✎ on a muted line inside the **ClickUp sync** panel — a panel about a different subject, behind the "Pipeline details" toggle — and nothing anywhere said what the note buyer actually controls, even though it attaches/retracts conditions, turns on the 5% SOW contingency, can raise the bank-statement count and decides which data tape the file exports. Now: `NoteBuyerCard.jsx` renders ALWAYS in `sec-overview` (`#note-buyer-slot`, under `DealSnapshot`, which also gained a `Note buyer` row in its Parties cluster), the ClickUp-panel editor is retired to a READ-ONLY line that links to it (`NoteBuyerRef` — **do not put a second editor back there**), and the owner-directed inline entry on `cond_note_buyer_missing` stays but points at it. **There is still exactly ONE write path** — `complete-fields` with `lender`, unchanged.
      · **Everything the card says is DERIVED, never restated** (`src/lib/note-buyer-effects.js` `noteBuyerSlot`, read-only, route `GET /api/staff/applications/:id/note-buyer`, file-scoped by the `/applications/:id` middleware): the condition preview runs the LIVE `checklist_templates.rule_logic` trees through the engine's own `rules.evaluateRule` against the file's real context with ONLY `note_buyer`/`note_buyer_is_fidelis` swapped, and reports only templates whose rule MENTIONS the note buyer (`mentionsNoteBuyer`) so nothing pending for its own reasons is blamed on a switch; the standing requirements come from the enforcing modules themselves (`rehab-budget` 5% branch, `liquidity.bankStatementMonths`, `tapes/registry` + `program-provider`) and carry `fromNoteBuyer` so a Gold file's 5% reads as the PROGRAM's, not the buyer's. A drop is previewed only for an item that passes the engine's own untouched test — a worked condition is never promised as disappearing. A terminal/deleted file previews nothing (`previewed:false`) because the engine would attach nothing. `?candidate=` previews a typed name ClickUp doesn't offer. Never throws; degrades to "no preview".
      · **The write path REPORTS what it did**, so nobody hunts the conditions list for the difference: `evaluateApplication`'s `out.added`/`out.removed` entries gained a `code` (additive), and `completeFields` returns `conditionsChanged:[{label, byNoteBuyer}]`. `byNoteBuyer` matters — the pass is a FULL re-evaluation, so a file the engine hadn't run on recently also picks up unrelated conditions; the card shows those in a separate "saving also re-checked the whole file" line and **never credits the switch with something it didn't cause**. When adding a new note-buyer-driven behavior, either express it as a rule (it appears here for free) or add ONE entry to `requirementsFor` — never a second copy of the rule, and never a hard-coded per-buyer blurb in the UI. Test `scripts/test-note-buyer-slot-db.js` (in `npm test`) pins every preview against the engine ACTUALLY running on the same file, plus the real HTTP doors and the file-scope 403.
  4c. **A NOTE-BUYER GUIDELINE MUST MAP TO A CONDITION THAT CAN ACTUALLY BE ON A FILE — and a condition that belongs to one buyer SAYS SO ON ITS FACE (owner-directed 2026-08-02: "CorrFirst requires SS NUMBER VERIFICATION — no condition on the file … make sure we have an EMD condition and rewrite exactly to verify the earnest money deposit, and also a Social Security number verification condition … you can either provide a Social Security card or you can provide an SS-89. This condition should be internal and external, and it should have a mark that this condition is for this particular note buyer. The note should be smaller and professional"). db/398.**
      · **ROOT CAUSE, and the class it belongs to.** CorrFirst condition 1050 ("SS NUMBER VERIFICATION", `corrfirst-fnf-spec.js`) named `pilot_template_code: 'rtl_p1_ssn'` — a template **db/040 set `is_active=false` and DELETED off every open file** in 2026, long before the guideline was mined. So the ISG desk reported the guideline against a row nothing could ever create, `ssn-autoclear.js` looked for a condition that does not exist, `pilot-advice-engine`'s evaluator could never fire, and db/291's reopen trigger guarded a template id that is never on a file. Nothing errored anywhere — every one of them simply matched zero rows, which is why it survived four separate features. **THE CLASS: a `pilot_template_code` pointing at an INACTIVE template is a silently dead mapping.** Guarded going forward by `scripts/test-corrfirst-conditions-db.js` ("every note-buyer guideline template code the spec names is an ACTIVE template") and by the ssn-autoclear suite ("the template ssn-autoclear targets is ACTIVE — a retired one could never be on a file"). When you map a guideline, or point any module at a template `code`, assert the template is active — a retired one can never be on a file, and every consumer will read as "clean" instead of "broken".
      · **`rtl_p1_ssn` STAYS RETIRED.** It is a staff TASK meaning "SSN collected & saved to borrower profile" — a different thing from verifying the number against a document — and it has no rule, so reviving it would put it back on EVERY file and re-litigate the decision db/040 recorded. The replacement is a new, rule-driven `cond_ssn_verify_corrfirst` (note buyer eq corrfirst), `audience='both'` — INTERNAL AND EXTERNAL, the owner's words — `item_kind='document'`, cleared by a Social Security card, a completed **SSA-89** ("SS-89"), or the number matching the verified credit report (which is how `ssn-autoclear` clears it on its own). Both codes are accepted by `ssn-autoclear` + `pilot-advice-engine` so a terminal file still carrying a historical `rtl_p1_ssn` instance behaves identically; only the new one is ever created. db/398 also re-creates db/291's `reopen_ssn_on_change()` keyed on BOTH codes (numbered above db/291, which re-creates its own version every boot — the db/334→db/375 ordering mechanism).
      · **THE MARK IS DERIVED FROM THE RULE, never restated** — `note-buyer-effects.noteBuyerMark(ruleLogic)` (pure, never throws), surfaced as `note_buyer_mark` by `GET /api/staff/applications/:id/checklist` and rendered as a gold chip by `ConditionLine.NoteBuyerMark`. The rule tree IS the reason the condition is on the file, so a column or a per-code lookup could drift from it and this cannot; any future note-buyer-gated condition gets its mark for free. **Only a row that PINS a buyer marks it** (`note_buyer eq|in`, `note_buyer_is_fidelis|_emcap is_true`); an `is_false`/`neq`/`not_in` row is an EXCLUSION — the flood-cert rule (db/335) excluded Fidelis, and labelling that condition "Fidelis" would say the exact opposite of what it means. **STAFF-ONLY**: the route drops `rule_logic` from the response after computing the mark, and the BORROWER checklist route never selects it — a note buyer's name may live in `label`/`hint`, never in `borrower_label`/`borrower_hint` (guarded by `test-condition-voice-guard-db.js`).
      · **The buyer's name moved OFF the label and ONTO the mark**, so the EMD label now says exactly what it verifies ("Earnest money deposit (EMD) verification", was "… — CorrFirst"). The internal HINT still names CorrFirst in words, deliberately: a plain-text surface that cannot render a chip (ClickUp, TPR, email) must still say whose requirement it is — the same discipline as `liquidity.loudMonthsBanner`. Both notes were shortened. **PREVIOUS AND FUTURE**: `checklist_items` copy the template wording at creation, so db/398 updates BOTH tables, each statement guarded on the exact prior text (a hand-edited row survives every boot). The one exception is `cond_emd_corrfirst.borrower_label`, which **db/191 re-asserts to the old value on every boot** — db/398 re-applies the new one each boot under an `IS DISTINCT FROM` guard; do not "simplify" that guard away. New template code also needs an entry in `condition-subjects.CODE_SUBJECT` (asserted live by `test-condition-subjects-db.js`) and in `tpr-export.CODE_CATEGORY`. Test `scripts/test-corrfirst-conditions-db.js` (in `npm test`).
  4d. **ONE CONDITION PER TEMPLATE PER FILE — the engine's duplicate suppression was a READ-THEN-WRITE race (owner-reported 2026-08-02: "check if there is more than one EMD condition on a file … maybe on certain files if there's any potentials we can get more than 1 EMD condition"). db/401 + a per-file advisory lock.**
      · **REPRODUCED, not theorised.** `engine.evaluateApplication` suppressed duplicates by READING the file's `checklist_items`, deciding a template had no instance, and THEN inserting — nothing in between, and `checklist_items` carried NO unique constraint on (application, template). Two passes at the same instant both read "not there" and both inserted. Measured on a real database: two concurrent `evaluateApplication` calls on one CorrFirst file → **2 EMD rows and 2 SSN rows**; sequential → one each. Six at once → six copies.
      · **IT IS ORDINARY TRAFFIC.** `evaluateApplication` is called from the staff completeness save, `PATCH /details`, the borrower edit, the ClickUp inbound ingest, the file-view sync and the boot sweep — and the NOTE BUYER is written from BOTH the portal and ClickUp, so "a staffer saves the note buyer while that card's webhook lands" is the ordinary shape, not a freak case. The class covers **EVERY rule-driven condition** (flood, assignment, condo, cash-out, the CorrFirst pair); EMD is merely where it was noticed.
      · **THE FIX IS A PER-FILE ADVISORY LOCK** (`pg_advisory_lock(hashtextextended('cond-eval:<appId>',0))` on its own pooled connection, released in a `finally`) wrapping the pass — `evaluateApplication` is now a thin lock around `evaluateApplicationLocked`. Database-wide, so it holds across the web process, the worker and every Render instance; a JS-side mutex would not. It **FAILS OPEN** by design: if the lock cannot be taken the pass still runs, because a missed lock costs one duplicate row a human can delete while refusing to evaluate would silently stop conditions attaching. Same shape as `sitewire/orchestrator.js`.
      · **NO UNIQUE INDEX — deliberate, and recorded so nobody re-adds one blind.** A partial unique index on `(application_id, template_id, COALESCE(field_key,''))` was built, tested and then REMOVED. It was RIGHT about production: every real insert path already enforces one-row-per-(file, template) itself — `closing.js`, `vesting.js`, `appraisal/desk.js` and `underwriting.js` all carry `NOT EXISTS (… application_id AND template_id)`; the engine is guarded; `credit/co-condition.js` and `esign/draw-wire.js` carry a distinct `field_key` (the co-borrower CREDIT condition deliberately puts a SECOND row of the same `rtl_cond_credit` template on one file, told apart ONLY by field_key — which is why a `(application_id, template_id)` key would have been wrong anyway); and `staff.js`'s hand-typed conditions, `borrower.js`, `staff-chat.js` and `raise-issue.js` insert no `template_id` at all. What it could NOT live with is the TEST SUITE: instantiating one template twice on one file is a widespread fixture idiom (a cheap way to get two independent conditions to work on), and the index turned each into a hard failure — three suites broke before it was abandoned (`notification-doc-verdict-dedup`, `condition-admin-override-db`, `evidence-reopen`) with ~45 more unscannable. Fixing those means editing unrelated suites to add synthetic field_keys, which risks changing what they actually assert; the safety net is worth more than a second layer under a lock that already closes the hole. **If a future unguarded insert path appears, guard THAT path — do not re-add the index.**
      · **db/401 CLEANS UP the duplicates already on files**, conservatively. Only a provably UNTOUCHED duplicate is removed (engine-owned, still outstanding, never signed off / reviewed / waived, no note, no tool payload) AND only when nothing hangs off it — deleting a checklist item SET-NULLs its documents, credit reports, messages, linked conditions and AI suggestions and CASCADE-DELETES its e-sign envelopes and clearance proofs, so all eleven FK children are re-checked rather than assumed. The keeper is the copy with work on it, else the oldest; where BOTH copies have been worked the pair is LEFT for a human rather than guessing whose work to discard. Each removal writes a `condition_duplicate_removed` audit row on the file. Idempotent.
      · Test `scripts/test-condition-duplicate-guard-db.js` (in `npm test`): the race at 2 and at 6 concurrent passes, the sequential control, the database refusing a second row, the co-borrower credit row STILL being allowed, hand-typed conditions untouched, the cleanup keeping the original and auditing the removal, a both-copies-worked duplicate left alone WITHOUT breaking the boot, the guard self-creating on the next boot, and the lock failing open. When adding a new condition insert path, go through the engine or carry a distinct `field_key` — the index will refuse a true duplicate either way.
  5. **[SUPERSEDED 2026-07-30 — see the "FLOOD CERT ON EVERY FILE" rule below; db/374 reverses the Fidelis waiver and the program/buyer scoping. The mechanics documented here (the boolean rule field, normNoteBuyer exactness, the prefix matcher) still hold and db/335/337 still run each boot — db/374 simply runs LAST and re-asserts the every-file state.]** On a FIDELIS file the FLOOD ZONE decides, not the capital partner (owner-directed 2026-07-27, governing sentence: "if it's a flood zone you should FORCE this condition on, but as long as you don't have evidence that it's a flood zone you should IGNORE this condition"). No flood-zone evidence + Fidelis → the internal flood cert `rtl_cond_flood` is absent (not optional, not outstanding). A PROVEN flood zone → REQUIRED, on every file, Fidelis included. UNCHANGED for everyone else (Gold/Manual per db/207, Blue Lake/CorrFirst per db/281). `db/335` rewrites the rule to `in_flood_zone OR (gold|manual AND note_buyer_is_fidelis is_false) OR note_buyer in (bluelake,corrfirst)` — the DISTRIBUTED form of `flood OR (NOT fidelis AND (program OR buyer))`, because the grammar allows only root + 1 nested level (`rules.validateRule`); the Blue Lake/CorrFirst branch needs no exclusion row since `note_buyer` holds one normalized value and `note_buyer_is_fidelis` is derived from it, so a file can never be both. Three things are load-bearing and must not be "simplified":
     · **The exclusion is keyed on a NEW BOOLEAN rule field `note_buyer_is_fidelis`, not on `note_buyer <> 'fidelis'`.** Two reasons, both silent bugs otherwise. (a) SPELLING: `normNoteBuyer` is an EXACT normalizer (it strips casing/spacing but deliberately never suffix-fuzzy-matches, because an over-match there would let "BlueLake Capital" export the Blue Lake data tape — `tapes/buyer-rule.js exportGate`, guarded by `test-tape-access-gate-pure.js`), so the owner's own ClickUp label "Fidelis Investors LLC" normalizes to `fidelisinvestorsllc`, NOT `fidelis`. The spellings are enumerated in `field-registry.FIDELIS_NOTE_BUYER_KEYS` / `isFidelisNoteBuyer()` (which includes the "Fidelis Investments LLC" form db/151 already records for Sitewire) — add a new spelling THERE **and** in db/335's two SQL key lists, never by loosening `normNoteBuyer`. (b) BLANKS: `rules.evalRow` short-circuits a blank actual to FALSE before the enum compare, so an enum "is not fidelis" row would have evaluated FALSE on a file with no note buyer yet and stripped the flood cert off every un-assigned Gold/Manual file. `engine.loadRuleContext` computes the boolean with `!!`, so it is always concrete and `is_false` is correct on a blank.
     · **The advisory is a BACKSTOP, not the main path** (the AI-never-writes-a-condition HARD RULE of 2026-07-22 — the AI may not create OR edit a condition). Once a flood zone is known the engine attaches the cert itself (`appraisal/desk.js fireFloodCheck` re-runs `evaluateApplication` after the FEMA lookup), so normally there is nothing to advise. `src/lib/underwriting/fidelis-flood-advisory.js` covers the two states the engine CANNOT fix: (1) a flood zone known with NO condition on the file, and (2) a flood zone known while the condition is present but still `is_required=false` from db/335 §3 — the engine suppresses duplicates and never rewrites `is_required` on an existing instance, so it would stay signable-empty on a real flood-zone property (db/335 §4 repairs that at boot; the advisory is the live signal in between, a `kind:'info'` row attached to the condition with NO create button, since a second flood condition is the wrong move). It reads the CURRENT appraisal for the two sources the owner named — the FEMA SFHA flag / FEMA zone, and the appraiser's own stated zone (A*/V* = SFHA, the same derivation as `engine.in_flood_zone`, so PILOT never disagrees with itself) — and records ONE `ai_suggestions` row: `kind:'condition'` + `proposed_action.templateCode` **and** `fields.opensCondition` (both, because the decide route and the app-v2 panel read different keys) so a human gets a one-click "create the flood certificate condition". Severity is `warning` + `important:true` — NOT `fatal`, which would fan the fatal-finding email out to the whole file team for a heads-up. PROVEN-ONLY: no appraisal or an all-NULL determination raises nothing (a flood zone is never guessed); a concrete non-A/V zone ("X") is a real "no". It WITHDRAWS itself (status `dismissed` + a reason, `status='open'` rows only) when the note buyer moves off Fidelis, the flood signal goes, or a flood condition lands on the file — and it is NEVER re-raised once a human decided, because `aiSug.record`'s dedupe only refreshes an OPEN row and would otherwise INSERT a fresh one on the next file view forever (the same trap `desk-sync.js` documents). Wired into the staff file-view sync (`routes/underwriting.js`, inside the `step()` block) and the appraisal import — BOTH `fireFloodCheck` (after the FEMA lookup) and the tail of `runAppraisalImport` (because `fireFloodCheck` is switch-gated on `APPRAISAL_FLOOD_CHECK_ENABLED` and returns early when the geocode is unreachable, which would leave an appraisal plainly stating zone AE raising nothing).
     · **The back-date is four statements, and §3/§4 are mirror images — keep them symmetric.** db/335 §2 DELETEs an UNTOUCHED flood cert off every OPEN Fidelis file with no flood-zone evidence (db/207 §A2's untouched definition, which also catches legacy db/177 items whose `origin_kind` was never adopted); §3 sets `is_required = false` on the touched ones it cannot delete — exactly what lets `signOffGate` clear a document condition with NOTHING attached (the owner's second option); §4 is §3 inverted, forcing an already-OPTIONAL cert back to REQUIRED when the file IS in a flood zone. §4 is not hypothetical: §3 downgrades while no flood zone is known, and a flood zone arriving later is unfixable by the engine, so without it a Fidelis file could sit in a flood zone with a signable-empty flood cert. §2/§3 both carry the no-flood-zone guard and §4 the positive one, so exactly one applies; each strips the other's marker note before appending its own (so a file whose flood status flips across deploys never accumulates contradictory notes) and each is idempotent via an `is_required IS DISTINCT FROM …` guard. `pilot-advice-engine`'s `rtl_cond_flood` evaluator now skips an `is_required=false` item (same guard as `rtl_p1_titlec`/`rtl_p1_insc`) so PILOT does not badge an optional condition "not ready". Tests: `scripts/test-fidelis-flood-advisory-db.js` (in `npm test` — pure spellings + the LIVE template rule read out of the DB + engine attach/retract + every advisory raise/withdraw/never-re-raise path + the db/335 back-date + a real end-to-end sign-off through the endpoint) and the updated flood-rule mirror in `scripts/test-manual-program.js`.
     · **THE BACK-DATE MISSED REAL FILES, AND THE FLAG ALONE WAS NEVER ENOUGH (owner-reported 2026-07-27: "I still see the flood certificate condition… and I cannot sign it off"). db/337 + a LIVE gate.** db/335's rule was right; its three back-date statements were scoped too narrowly, and each gap was reproduced against a real database before being fixed: (a) **item status** — §2/§3 required `ci.status='outstanding'`, so a cert the team had already worked ('requested' / 'received' / 'issue') matched neither and stayed REQUIRED; that is the state of any condition anyone has touched, and the likeliest one the owner hit. (b) **application status** — §2/§3 listed six statuses, but **db/177's backfill re-runs on EVERY boot and is note-buyer- and program-unaware**, re-adding the cert to every file that is not 'withdrawn'/'cancelled' (so 'file_intake', 'funded', 'declined' too). On a GOLD-registered Fidelis file — Gold matters, because db/207 §A2's cleanup spares Gold — nothing removed it again, so the condition came back on **every single deploy**. (c) **note-buyer spelling** — the enumerated alias list could only cover spellings someone thought of, and a real label one character off ("Fidelis Investor LLC", singular) fell through. `isFidelisNoteBuyer` is now a PREFIX test (`normNoteBuyer(x).startsWith('fidelis')`, SQL `LIKE 'fidelis%'`) — "Fidelity National" still cannot match (fidelit… vs fideli**s**), and `normNoteBuyer` itself stays EXACT so the data-tape gate is untouched. db/337 re-runs the back-date with no application-status filter (mirroring db/207 §A2) and no item-status filter ("untouched" is already fully described by the emptiness tests), keyed on the prefix.
       **The durable half is the LIVE gate, not the flag.** `routes/staff.js signOffGate` now reads the note buyer AND the flood determination AT SIGN-OFF TIME for `rtl_cond_flood`: a Fidelis file with no flood-zone evidence is signable with NOTHING attached, whatever `is_required` says. An `is_required=false` flag can only ever describe the file as it looked when the last deploy ran — a file registered Gold today gets the condition, and switching the note buyer to Fidelis tomorrow leaves a REQUIRED condition standing (the engine retracts only UNTOUCHED items and never rewrites `is_required` on an existing instance). That is precisely the file the owner was stuck on, and no boot-time migration can fix it in time. The flood zone still wins: on a Fidelis file in a real SFHA the gate stays fully strict. When a future condition needs a per-partner waiver, put it in the GATE — a migration-stamped flag will always lag a note-buyer change. Guarded by `test-fidelis-flood-advisory-db.js` section (D): all 8 live-gate cases, all 12 back-date states, and a SECOND `ensureSchema()` proving it stays clear on the next deploy.
- **A DATA TAPE'S MANUAL FIELDS ARE FILE DATA — SAVED ONCE, PRE-FILLED (NOT RE-ASKED) ON EVERY RE-EXPORT — AND THE EXPORT GATE MUST RECOGNIZE THE BUYER'S REAL LABEL (owner-reported 2026-08-06: "on a Ground Up Construction there are fields you fill before you export the tape, but if you export the tape again you have to re-enter everything … make them fields as part of the loan file … next time it should pre-fill those details, ready to click export").** The New-Construction supplemental fields (Fidelis/EMCAP: asset purchased, entitlement status — the "fully entitled" question — build status, lot price/date) were ALWAYS meant to persist to `applications.tape_supplemental` (jsonb, db/310) and pre-fill. Two bugs broke it, both rooted in ONE place — the tape EXPORT gate's buyer match:
  1. **`buyer-rule.keyNamesTapeBuyer` matched only the bare key (`fidelis`) or a tape's ENUMERATED `buyerAliases`, and Fidelis/Blue Lake had NONE — so the owner's real label "Fidelis Investors LLC" (→ `normNoteBuyer` = `fidelisinvestorsllc`, NOT `fidelis`) failed the gate.** That silently (a) blocked a NON-admin from exporting the tape at all (`buyer_mismatch`) and (b) made `index.js persistSupplemental` hit its `if (!buyerMatches) return {}` and drop the save with NO error — the export route even logged `supplemental: []`. An ADMIN could export (admin bypass) yet the answers never persisted, so every re-export re-asked. FIX: enumerate the real production spellings as `buyerAliases` on each tape (`fidelis.js`: `fidelisinvestors(llc)`/`fidelisinvestor(s)llc`/`fidelisinvestments(llc)`; `bluelake.js`: `bluelakecapital(llc)`; `emcap.js` already had `emcapfinancial`, added `…llc`). This is the SANCTIONED shape — a CLOSED list, never a prefix/fuzzy match, because the EXPORT direction is the one where an over-match ships a tape to the WRONG buyer (this is why `normNoteBuyer` stays EXACT and the gate deliberately does NOT use the `isFidelisNoteBuyer` prefix helpers). Add a new real spelling HERE; never loosen the matcher. NOTE the CLAUDE.md line under the flood rule ("an over-match … would let 'BlueLake Capital' export the Blue Lake data tape") still holds for `normNoteBuyer` itself — the real label now exports via its ENUMERATED alias, not by loosening the normalizer.
  2. **`index.js tapeQuestions` returned only the UNANSWERED fields (`missingSupplemental`), so once saved they vanished from the questionnaire.** The owner wants them SHOWN pre-filled and editable ("the spot where you're changing this detail to export a new tape"). FIX: it now returns EVERY applicable field (`supplementalFieldsFor`), each `current`-pre-filled from `tape_supplemental`, plus a `supplementalMissing` count; the portals (`StaffApplication.jsx` `TapeExport`, `StaffTapes.jsx`) open the modal pre-filled every time (first-fill vs review copy keyed on `supplementalMissing`). Seasoned-loan confirmation fields stay recomputed-from-draws each export by design (unchanged). Tests: `test-tape-access-gate-pure.js` + `test-tape-fidelis-pure.js` (real labels match; a non-enumerated sibling and "Fidelity National" still don't) and `test-tape-export-db.js` (persist round-trip + pre-fill + a regression guard using the real "Fidelis Investors LLC" label, proven to fail pre-fix). When adding a new tape or a new manual tape field, persist through `tape_supplemental` and enumerate the buyer's real labels — never leave the manual answers export-only.
- **THE REFINANCE PAYOFF TRAVELS ON THE STAFF HAND-OFF TOO (owner-directed 2026-08-02 rule, all-sides gap fixed 2026-08-06).** The "payoff/lender/loan# travel with the refinance from the studio into the file" rule (`scenario.js scenarioToDraft`) was wired on the borrower Apply path but the STAFF Investor-Suite → New-file prefill (`StaffNewFile.jsx prefillToForm`) silently dropped all three — so an officer who priced a refi and pressed "Create loan file" lost the payoff and retyped it. `prefillToForm` now carries `payoffAmount`/`payoffLender`/`payoffLoanNumber` (the New-file form already has these refinance-only fields). Guarded by a new assertion in `test-refi-payoff-identity.js` (which had checked the borrower Apply surface but never the staff prefill — the exact blind spot that let it ship).
- **THE FLOOD CERTIFICATE IS ON EVERY SINGLE FILE — no matter the capital provider (owner-directed 2026-07-30: "I want to remove the waiver that I applied… I want the flood certification condition to populate on every single file whatsoever no matter who the capital provider is… the flood certification should be an internal condition for every single file"; REVERSES the 2026-07-27 Fidelis waiver + the db/207/281 program/buyer scoping).** `db/374_flood_cert_every_file.sql` sets the `rtl_cond_flood` template to `auto_apply='always'`, `rule_logic NULL`, required — the engine attaches it to EVERY non-terminal file with a checklist (staff-only audience, never borrower-facing) and it NEVER retracts, whatever the program or note buyer becomes. ORDERING IS THE MECHANISM: db/177…db/337 are never edited and still run each boot (some delete/downgrade certs); db/374 runs numerically LAST and re-asserts the converged state — re-adds anything they removed this boot, restores `is_required=true`, and strips BOTH db/335 marker notes (they would now mislead). The `signOffGate` Fidelis empty-sign-off bypass in `src/routes/staff.js` is REMOVED — the normal document gate applies to every file; the recorded way through an unobtainable cert is the super-admin override (db/344) with a reason. The `fidelis-flood-advisory` backstop is left wired but structurally moot (every file now carries the condition, so its raise conditions cannot occur; it only self-withdraws stale rows). Test `scripts/test-flood-cert-every-file-db.js` (in `npm test`, REPLACING the retired `test-fidelis-flood-advisory-db.js`): live template shape, engine attach for Fidelis/EMCAP/Blue Lake/no-buyer, never-retracts across program changes, the db/374 back-date (restore + note-strip + re-add) idempotent across boots, and the LIVE gate refusing an empty cert on every buyer with the super-admin override still working. When adding any future per-partner condition waiver, remember this reversal — the owner wants internal conditions universal by default.
- **Term Sheet Studio program cards are a 2×2 GRID — TWO per row, FOUR cards, details BELOW all four; the Register button is greyed-but-clickable until a card is picked (owner-directed 2026-07-30, fixing "three cards on one line… loan amount divided in two lines… rate going out of the box").** `web/v2/tools/term-sheet.html` `.prog-compare` is `grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr` (1 column ≤560px) — NEVER three across. Row 1: Standard + Gold Standard; row 2: **Silver** (silver-tinted `#8A99A8`, mirroring Gold's gold) + **Manual** (dashed border, teal accents). The Manual card keys on `manualBasisOn()` — `tsManualOn` PLUS a STRUCTURAL basis value (tsMLtv/tsMArv/tsMLtc), because the server's `manual-program.resolveProgram` records a Manual Program ONLY on a structural LTV/LTC/ARV override (a rate-only override or the bare checkbox keeps the requested program, with an approval escalation) — never key the card on the checkbox alone (audit 2026-07-30 #2). While the basis is off the Manual card is dimmed/not-selectable; with it ON, the three program cards dim to `pcard-off`, any drill-in auto-switches to `chosenProgram='manual'` at the TOP of recompute (before the leverage slider and detail deal are drawn — audit #4), and the Manual card + xlsx "Manual Program (admin-set basis)" section show the Standard-engine numbers — the studio always displays exactly what would register. A BORROWER's resumed draft strips `tsManualOn` + the tsM* values (the borrower register route strips manual overrides, so the manual state would be a broken dead end for them — audit #3); a manual-product scenario with engine status MANUAL registers normally (`blockReason`'s exception branch excludes `manualLive` — audit #1, the server skips `exception_required` for a structural override). `chosenProgram` now spans `standard|gold|silver|manual`; every new label/export/dispatch on it must handle `manual` (or deliberately fall through to the standard calc, which IS the manual pricing). Register gating (`ProductStudioPanel.jsx`): `registerBlocked` greys BOTH register buttons via `.btn.is-blocked` + `aria-disabled` whenever `blockReason` stands (no program picked, not ready, ineligible, no loan) — but the button stays CLICKABLE and a blocked click surfaces the exact reason (never `disabled`, never a silent dead end), the reason line renders BEFORE any click in the top bar AND next to the bottom button, and the no-program wording names all four cards. `submitExceptionMode` (a MANUAL-status scenario) is never greyed — "Submit exception request" is a real action. Browser-verified by the scratchpad card battery (geometry, one-line loan amounts, no visible overflow, switch/collapse, phone stacking); re-verify visually after touching the cards.
- **AN EMAIL-SIGNATURE IMAGE IS NOT A DOCUMENT — AND THE ONES ALREADY ON THE FILES HAD TO BE CLEANED UP TOO (owner-reported 2026-08-03: "the small tiny pictures from the email signatures of the reply agent … is still coming in as documents and we still need to manually reject it on every file — maybe it's still from the past").** The owner was right that it was from the past, and the reason is a two-commit interaction worth remembering.
  · **WHY IT LOOKED UNFIXED.** `src/lib/order-return-filter.js` (the 2026-07-29 report, merged 2026-08-02 midday) genuinely stops a title / insurance agent's signature logo becoming a returned document — but it is GOING FORWARD ONLY, and nothing ever cleaned up what the pre-filter months had already filed. Eleven hours later **db/420** attached every ORPHANED order return to its title / insurance condition and the file screen began rendering the documents that are not in a slot — so months of already-filed signature images surfaced on the conditions at once, and the team started rejecting them by hand, file by file. **THE CLASS: a go-forward filter plus a back-fill that makes old rows VISIBLE reads to the owner as "the fix does not work". When you ship a filter, ship the retirement of what it would have blocked in the same pass** — the standing "previous AND future" rule.
  · **`src/lib/order-signature-retire.js` is that missing half, and it is JAVASCRIPT, not a migration**, because the verdict comes from calling `classifyReturnAttachment` — the SAME function the two inbound sinks run on a live attachment — so "what is a signature image" keeps ONE definition. A PL/pgSQL twin would drift the first time the rule changed (the `pilot_term_norm` / `pilot_property_type_norm` class). It reads the stored bytes for anything under 2 MB so the pixel rule below applies to the back book as well.
  · **IT RETIRES BY `is_current = false`, AND THAT COLUMN IS THE WHOLE MECHANISM.** It is what the condition's document list, the Orders desk's returned-documents list, the closing chain's box, the TPR / closing packages (accepted + current) and **db/420's own re-attach predicate** already read — so the next boot's migrations cannot undo it, with no tug-of-war and no re-numbering. `review_status` becomes `'superseded'` only when nobody has decided yet: it is a DECIDED state (`document-acceptance.isAwaiting`), so a condition can be signed off without a human clicking Reject on a company logo, and it is deliberately NOT `'rejected'` because the condition row displays the newest REJECTED document's reason and a logo must never become the reason a borrower is shown. **NOTHING IS DELETED** — the bytes stay in storage, the row keeps its provenance (doc_kind still names the order, checklist_item_id still names the condition), Accept still restores it, and because `sharepoint-shelf` reads `is_current` FIRST the mirrored copy is shelved to "Old Versions" rather than sitting in the live folder. Every retirement writes an `order_signature_image_retired` audit row — the row is off every screen, so the audit log is the only place that says it was ever there. It can only ever touch a raster image nobody accepted and nobody filed into a slot; bounded per boot, self-draining, never throws, off with `ORDER_SIGNATURE_RETIRE_DISABLED=1`.
  · **RULE 4 — TOO SMALL TO BE A PAGE.** The two original tells both have a hole: the mail provider does not always expose the inline / Content-ID metadata, and a modern @2x logo or headshot can weigh more than the 150 KB floor (measured: a 400 × 120 retina logo at 200 KB filed cleanly through the pre-fix classifier). What is ALWAYS true of a signature image is its size in PIXELS, so `imageDimensions()` reads the picture's own header (PNG IHDR / JPEG SOFn / GIF / BMP — pure, dependency-free, fully bounds-checked) and a picture whose LONG side is under **700px**, or whose aspect is **5:1 or wider**, never files. 700 is not arbitrary: US Letter at 72 dpi — the lowest resolution any scanner or phone produces — is already 612 × 792, so the floor sits under every genuine page while a logo sits far under it on the other side; 5:1 clears Letter (1.29:1), legal (1.65:1) and a cheque (2.3:1). The reader ABSTAINS (`null`) on anything it cannot read confidently, which falls through to the byte rule exactly as before — **it may never guess, because a guess throws away a real document.**
  · Tests `scripts/test-order-return-filter.js` (extended — the retina-logo case proven to FILE with the pixel rule off, the banner, the smallest real page, the reader's whole truth table and its abstentions) and `scripts/test-order-signature-retire-db.js` (real Postgres — the owner's own state reproduced, the retirement, the human's accept / slot / reject respected, db/420 unable to undo it, the audit row, the off-switch, and the same agent's email now filing only the invoice), both in `npm test`. When you add a new automatic document sink fed by inbound email, run it through `classifyReturnAttachment` — never a second copy of the rule.
- **A DOCUMENT NOBODY ACCEPTED NEVER LEAVES THE BUILDING — and a condition is never signed off over one (owner-directed 2026-08-03; db/424).** The owner, on a live file: *"there was already insurance from a different provider without a mortgage clause, and the insurance condition had documents on it — but that document was not accepted at all. Then we ordered insurance to the system, the documents came in, THAT one was accepted and the condition was signed off. When we did the TPR export and ordered closing prep, all the insurance documents — the ones that were not accepted — went along with them."*
  1. **ROOT CAUSE, and it is one line written independently in four places.** Every selector asked `review_status <> 'rejected'`. That is a test for *"nobody threw this away"*, not for *"somebody checked this"* — and a document that has never been looked at is `'pending'` (the db/013 default), which passes it. So a never-accepted document counted as fulfillment for a sign-off, rode into the investor TPR package, and was attached to an email to an outside law firm. It had been decided on purpose in 2026-07-16 ("a pending doc ships and is LABELED pending in the manifest") — and then the manifest was dropped on 2026-07-30 as a "garbage" technical file, so from that day the label went with it and the pending document shipped **silently**. **THE CLASS: `<> 'rejected'` is never the same question as `= 'accepted'`. Before you write either, say out loud which one you mean.**
  2b. **THE NEAR-MISS worth remembering: the Term Sheet Studio's own PDF rides the ORDINARY upload door.** `doc_kind='term_sheet'` is set only by the studio capturing what it just drew, but it is filed through `POST /applications/:id/documents` (and the borrower mirror), which files everything pending — so the first cut of this rule silently dropped the term sheet from the export's Term Sheet folder AND made `closing-prep.blockers` refuse EVERY closing-prep order on the file for want of a term sheet that was sitting right there. Both doors now accept **that kind only** (`CASE WHEN $12='term_sheet'`), and db/424 §2 is keyed on the doc_kind ALONE for the same reason — those rows carry no `source_type`, so keying on `'system'` would have matched nothing. **When you make an inclusion rule stricter, walk every producer of the things it now excludes, not just the ones with an obvious `source_type`.**
  2. **`src/lib/document-acceptance.js` is the ONE definition** — `ACCEPTED_SQL(alias)` / `AWAITING_SQL(alias)` / `isAccepted` / `isAwaiting` plus the wording. The outbound test is `review_status = 'accepted'` with **NO exception list**. A document PILOT itself generates or orders is **born `'accepted'` at its INSERT** instead (the DocuSign-executed packages, every tool's own Excel/PDF/HTML export, a flood certificate PILOT ordered from the vendor, the draw/inspection reports, the TPR zip itself) — the same treatment db/186 and db/189 already gave appraisal photos and appraisal source documents. An exception list inside the predicate was rejected on purpose: it drifts from the inserts, and it would have swept up the two system-WRITTEN documents that are documented as needing a human first — an entity document copied onto a profile by `entity-adopt`, and a vendor's emailed order return (the document in the owner's own story). **The consequence is that `pending` finally means one honest thing everywhere: a human still has to look at this.**
  3. **NOTHING IS SILENTLY DROPPED — that is half the change.** `tpr-export` runs ONE query body with TWO review tests (`TPR_DOC_SELECT_FOR`), so `selectTprPending` / `selectTrackRecordPending` describe exactly the same corpus as what shipped. The export preview NAMES what was held back and why, the closing-prep card shows `awaiting` separately from `missing` (*"waiting to be accepted"* is a completely different instruction from *"not on file yet"* — one sends you to the condition, the other sends you chasing the borrower for a document that is already in the building), `selectTprMissing` now counts a pending-only condition as would-ship-empty, and the `export_tpr` audit row records the held-back count + filenames, because a ZIP has nowhere to put a note.
  4. **THE SIGN-OFF HALF, and it is TWO rules.** `pendingDocumentsBlock` runs FIRST in `signOffGate`, before every per-condition rule, because it is true of every condition including an optional one: *"every document needs to be either rejected, deleted, or accepted — we can't sign off a condition with pending documents over there."* Then the fulfillment gates ask for ACCEPTED rather than not-rejected — the plain document gate, the reused-profile photo ID, and the SLOT gates (insurance binder + invoice, title, fraud background/criminal, appraisal XML + PDF), which is the exact gate the owner's file walked through: a never-accepted previous-carrier binder filled the "binder" slot. Fails OPEN on a read error (a database hiccup must never make a condition permanently unsignable), and the super-admin override (db/344) is unchanged as the recorded way through — the gate still RUNS under it and stamps `override_blocked_reason`, so the file records what was skipped.
  5. **AN OFFICER CAN REJECT, VISIBLY** (*"an officer should also have the option to reject certain documents"*). The server always allowed it — anyone on the file may reject; only a completer may accept — but `docNextStep` returned `null` for a non-completer, so a loan officer looking at a wrong document saw NO action on the row at all and the button existed only inside "More". It now returns a **ghost** Reject (never primary: rejecting is not the expected next step), and the row fires `step.action` instead of a hard-coded `'accept'`.
  6. **DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED.** The **SharePoint mirror still mirrors everything** — it is the file cabinet and the backup, the one place where dropping a document loses it, and its own policy is that nothing is ever deleted; what the owner listed is what goes OUT. Every INTERNAL surface still shows pending documents (hiding one from the person who has to accept it would be absurd). db/424 back-dates only the system-written kinds and **never mass-accepts a human upload** — that would re-ship exactly the documents this change exists to stop; files signed off before today keep their sign-off, and the export simply stops carrying what nobody vouched for. The env-gated auto-clear modules (`gov-id-autoclear`, `fraud-autoclear`, all OFF by default) still read `<> 'rejected'`: each has its own independent evidence standard (an AI-verified clean read with no open findings) and changing them is a separate owner call — but if one clears a condition, `selectTprMissing` will honestly report that condition as would-ship-empty.
  Tests `scripts/test-document-acceptance-pure.js` (the predicate + a SOURCE guard that the `<> 'rejected'` shape has not crept back into any export selector) and `scripts/test-document-acceptance-db.js` (the owner's story end-to-end: the old binder blocks the sign-off, the ordered one clears it only once accepted, neither package carries the old one, both surfaces name what was held back, PILOT's own documents still ship, db/424 back-dates only the right rows, and the super-admin override still works and still records) — both in `npm test`, and ten of those assertions were proven to FAIL on the pre-fix code. When you add a surface that sends documents anywhere outside PILOT, ask `document-acceptance` — never re-inline a review test.
- **A CREDIT REPORT FROM SOMEWHERE ELSE — upload the PDF, then WAIVE the import by exception (owner-directed 2026-08-03; db/455).** The owner: *"On the credit report condition we only have an Import button. We should also have a button to upload a PDF, and after they upload a PDF they can request an exception to waive — and the admin should sign off the condition even if they didn't reissue the credit. Say they got a credit report from somewhere else and they don't want to reissue and it needs an exception. But first we need to have the PDF upload slot on the document, and then they can request an exception to get the condition signed off without importing credit. And the PDF, if it's accepted, should be included in the TPR export as well and also in the SharePoint syncing."*
  1. **THE IMPORT RULE DID NOT MOVE, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** `signOffGate`'s `isCredit` branch still requires a real IMPORTED `credit_reports` row for every borrower the condition covers — "a bare PDF upload is not enough" (owner-directed 2026-07-23) — because only an import files the PDF AND the data file AND reads the scores that price the deal. This adds the ONE recorded way through it, for the case the owner named: a current report obtained elsewhere that nobody wants to re-pull (a second hard inquiry on the borrower, at cost, for a report we already hold). A file with no waiver behaves byte-for-byte as before.
  2. **`src/lib/credit/waiver.js` IS THE ONE PREDICATE**, and it takes BOTH halves of the owner's sentence, never one: an **ACCEPTED** report on the condition (`document-acceptance`'s standard — the same acceptance the owner tied the TPR/SharePoint half to, and the same document that then ships) **AND** an **APPROVED** exception. Unlike the appraisal no-XML waiver (db/370), which auto-waives for a transferred appraisal, there is deliberately **no self-approving reason** — every credit waiver is an admin's decision ("the admin should sign off"). It FAILS CLOSED: an unreadable waiver, a missing table, a database hiccup all read as "no waiver". The sign-off gate AND `credit/completeness.js` (which feeds the PILOT-advice badge and the env-gated auto-clear) both call it, so "may this be signed off?" and "does PILOT think it is complete?" can never disagree — a badge reading "not ready" on a condition the gate would clear is the one thing that must not happen. `creditCompleteness` therefore takes the ITEM id; without one there is no waiver to find and the strict rule is reported, which is the safe answer.
  3. **THE WAIVER IS KEYED ON THE CONDITION, NOT THE FILE.** A file can carry TWO credit conditions — the file-level one and a co-borrower's own (`field_key='cob_credit'`, db/286) — and waiving the import for one borrower must never quietly waive it for the other, so `credit_import_waivers.checklist_item_id` is the primary key.
  4. **THE PDF RIDES THE ORDINARY DOCUMENT DOOR** (`POST /applications/:id/documents` with `checklistItemId`), never a private upload path — so it inherits dedup, the evidence reopen, the ClickUp condition-status push, the SharePoint mirror and the TPR selection for free. It carries **no doc_kind**, which is exactly how it is told apart from the importer's own two: `UPLOADED_REPORT_SQL` is `doc_kind NOT IN ('credit_xml','credit_pdf')`. **Never widen that to count `credit_pdf`** — that is the importer's own readable report, and counting it would make every ordinary imported file look as though it carried an outside report, so an approved exception would clear the condition on PILOT's own PDF. (`doc_kind` is COALESCEd for the repo's standing three-valued-logic reason.)
  5. **THE ORDER IS THE OWNER'S**: the request route REFUSES (409) until a report is on the condition — an exception is a request to sign off on THAT report, and an admin asked to approve one with nothing behind it is being asked to approve nothing. Acceptance is the reviewer's separate step, and the gate requires it before the waiver counts.
  6. **TPR + SHAREPOINT NEEDED NO NEW CODE, AND THAT WAS VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** An uploaded report on the credit condition categorizes to **"Credit Report"** through `tpr-export.categoryFor` (`CODE_CATEGORY.rtl_cond_credit`) and the SharePoint mirror shares that same categorizer, so both file it in one folder; it is in no never-mirror set, and `TPR_DOC_SELECT`'s accepted-only test is what makes the owner's "if it's accepted" literally true — pending, it is held back AND NAMED in `selectTprPending`. The db/425 shelf rule moves the mirrored copy out of "Waiting for review" into the category folder when it is accepted.
  7. **REMOVING THE WAIVER RESPECTS THE DECISION.** A still-OPEN request is withdrawn; one an admin already DECIDED is **cleared/archived with a note**, never rewritten as "withdrawn" (that would erase that a decision was made) and never left standing as a live `approved` credit exception on a file that no longer has a waiver. An accepted report is never unfiled — withdrawing the waiver is a decision about the SIGN-OFF, not about the document.
  Tests `scripts/test-credit-waiver-pure.js` (the type's shape, the reason codes incl. another type's being refused, the predicate, the full two-half truth table, the fail-closed paths, and the Credit-Report folder on BOTH surfaces) and `scripts/test-credit-waiver-db.js` (real HTTP + real Postgres: the owner's story in order — strict refusal, exception-before-PDF refused, upload, request, both halves binding with the refusal naming which one is outstanding, sign-off with no `credit_reports` row on the file at all, pending-held-back vs accepted-ships, co-borrower scope, withdraw), both in `npm test`. When adding another way to satisfy the credit condition, route it through `credit/waiver.js` — never a second bypass in the gate.
- **THE SOLD STAGE, THE CRITICAL DATES, AND THE PAYOFF DEMAND THAT LOCKS THE DRAW CENTRE (owner-directed 2026-08-21; db/611).**
  · **THE READ CAME FIRST, because the owner was right about which half was broken:** *"Why do you say that you're going to make alerts if you can't read the field? … The main bug is why you can't read the field, not why you're firing."* db/608 recorded WHAT the last read did, which stopped a false chase, and left two states permanently unreadable. `src/sitewire/pa-field.js` fixes both: **`ensureLoanGuid`** finds the loan by its loan NUMBER (one pipeline search, exactly one match or nothing — two loans sharing a number is a real state and picking one would attach this file's sold status to the wrong loan) and caches the GUID, so a funded file that never went through the per-file pull stops being permanently unreadable; **`paFieldCandidates`** asks the TENANT'S OWN cached field catalogue which fields are named for a purchase advice and offers them AFTER the configured id. That is what *"make sure it's looking at the correct field"* means in practice — not restating the number we already have, which is the number that produced the wrong answer. **A field that answers BLANK has answered** and the ladder stops there: moving on to a look-alike would turn a real "not sold yet" into whatever some other field happens to hold. The id that actually answered is recorded on the file, and `syncPurchaseAdviceDate` now takes it, because reading the map under the configured key would report `not_returned` about a field that answered perfectly well.
  · **SOLD IS A STAGE ON TOP OF FUNDED, NOT A NEW `applications.status` VALUE, and this is the decision to read before "finishing the job".** `status` is the SERVICING state and **139 places read it — 27 SQL predicates and 17 JavaScript comparisons test `funded`**, among them the purchase-advice sweep itself, draw eligibility, the investor delivery, the data tapes and the funded-date sync. A sold loan is still funded and still serviced by us: every draw continues. Moving the stored status would switch all of that off silently, and the failure would surface as a draw that never appeared. So `sold_at` + `sold_source` record the stage and every surface DISPLAYS it as the file's status — which is what the owner asked to see. **Table-funded loans never get it** (they were sold at the closing table and receive no advice), and that exclusion is decided in ONE place through `release-party.releaseStateFor`, the same answer the draw desk's own card shows. The stage **clears when its evidence goes**: an advice date corrected away in Encompass must not leave "Sold" standing. `lib/sold-status.js` is the one definition; the back-book backfill runs at boot, **SILENTLY** (`announce:false`) — every file it reaches was sold weeks ago, so announcing them would fan a notice across the whole funded book and move every ClickUp card at once, the same blast the sweep's silent first read exists to avoid.
  · **THE CRITICAL DATES SECTION — `lib/critical-dates.js`, one definition, one answer.** Application started (`submitted_at`, else the row's creation, and it SAYS which), clear to close (the FIRST entry in `application_status_history`, never `status_changed_at` — that is the last change of ANY status and would print a funding date under a "cleared to close" label), funded (`funded_date`, which `encompass-funded.js` fills from CX.FUNDEDDATE), purchase advice, sold, payoff demand. **Every entry says where it came from, and a missing one says WHY** — "no purchase advice yet" and "PILOT could not read the field" are different pieces of work for different people, which is the whole point of the db/608 read state.
  · **A PAYOFF DEMAND LOCKS THE DRAW CENTRE, BOTH SIDES (`lib/payoff-demand.js`).** The owner's reasoning is the rule: *"if somebody is requesting a payoff, then he has a payoff. If we request draws afterward, that can be a mess up."* PILOT refuses on its own — the coordinator's Start-draw door, the borrower's request-draw door, the borrower's own draw composer AND the release ledger, because blocking only the REQUEST leaves a draw already in flight able to pay out against a quoted payoff figure — and the Sitewire property is DEACTIVATED so the borrower's own door is shut too. That deactivate is the owner's *"there's a button in the sitewire actions block draws"*: `orchestrator.setDrawsBlocked` reuses the SAME guarded write `setPropertyLifecycle` uses (extracted as `pushPropertyInactive` — circuit breaker, journal, read-after-write verify, park on failure), and deliberately does NOT touch `lifecycle_state`, because a payoff demand is not "the project finished" and mislabelling it would make the draw desk lie about where the build got to. Lifting a demand can never re-activate a project a coordinator finished. The stamp is **FILL-ONLY** — the date is what the payoff figure was quoted against, so a second click never moves it — and `payoffDemandBlock` **FAILS CLOSED**: with the database refusing to say, refusing a draw costs a retry while allowing one can release money.
  · **A RULE ENFORCED EVERYWHERE AND SHOWN NOWHERE (owner-directed audit 2026-08-21).** The owner asked for the payoff demand *"In the Draw Coordinator section AND ALSO in the Critical Date section"* and *"it should come out big"*; only the second was built. The hold refused at all four doors from the day it shipped and appeared on NEITHER screen, so the only way to learn a file was frozen was to press Start and be refused — the worst moment to find out, and silent until you act. (`routes/sitewire.js` even carried the comment *"screen shows it large"*.) The draw view now returns `payoff_demand`, read through the SAME `payoffDemandBlock` the doors refuse with — one fact, one wording, so the banner and the refusal can never disagree — and `DrawsPanel` leads with it above BOTH branches, saying a different thing for a file whose draws are running than for one whose are not, because the consequence differs. Best-effort on the READ only: an unreadable answer still blocks at the door (it fails closed there) and simply shows nothing here rather than crying wolf. **THE CLASS is `test-draw-routes-wired-pure`'s own subject — a back end is not a feature — so both halves are pinned there**: the view must CARRY the fact and the panel must RENDER it, since either alone is the same silence.
  · Tests `scripts/test-sold-and-payoff-db.js` (51 checks, real Postgres + real HTTP; four mutations — table-funded getting the stage, the stage never clearing, the release door skipping the check, and a missing date saying nothing about why — each proven to fail it). **A DB suite must `await ensureSchema()` before it writes**: booting the server kicks the migrations off asynchronously, so a suite that starts straight away races a brand-new column into existence and reads "does not exist" on the very run meant to prove it. **AND A BOUNDED BACKFILL IS A FLAKY ASSERTION**: ordered by date, a page-sized pass can leave this file behind another suite's fixtures — assert on the row, never on the aggregate count.
- **AN UPLOAD IS STREAMED, THE CEILING IS THE OWNER'S TO SET, AND A REFUSAL APPEARS WHERE THE UPLOAD HAPPENED (owner-reported 2026-08-21: a 23.3 MB executed contract would not upload — *"if Pilot is giving an error and he can't upload something, he should tell exactly what the error is. Right now, it's just not uploading. It's just ignoring"*, *"it's not popping up in the place where you want to upload. It's popping up on top of the file"*, and *"we need to increase the limit of megabytes that we can upload to unlimit it … Why is it 20 MB? The sky is the limit."*).**
  · **RAISING THE NUMBER ALONE WOULD HAVE TAKEN THE SITE DOWN, and that is MEASURED, not assumed.** Every upload door took `{filename, contentType, dataBase64}` as JSON, which express buffers and parses whole: the wire body, express's string of it, the string `JSON.parse` makes of the base64 value and the decoded Buffer together peak at about **five times the file** — **25 MB → 168 MB, 50 MB → 294 MB, 100 MB → 410 MB** (Node 22, measured before anything was changed), on a **Render `starter` web service with 512 MB total**, a good part of which the app already occupies. So "unlimited" through that transport is an out-of-memory kill of the whole site for everybody, not a failed upload for one person. **The answer was a different TRANSPORT, not a bigger number.**
  · **`src/lib/upload-stream.js` IS THE STREAMING DOOR.** The file itself is the request body; it is piped straight to a temp file, hashed on the way past (the integrity system needs that sha256), then handed to storage as a PATH — `storage.saveFile`, which is a rename on the local disk and an 8 MB-at-a-time **multipart** upload on R2 (`storage-s3.saveFile`; any failure ABORTS the multipart upload, because an abandoned one is invisible in a listing and still billed). Peak memory is a few megabytes whatever the size of the document. **A multipart `CompleteMultipartUpload` can answer HTTP 200 and still have failed** — S3 streams whitespace and then writes either a result or an `<Error>` into that 200 — so the body is inspected, or a document whose bytes are not there gets recorded.
  · **TWO CEILINGS, AND THEY ANSWER DIFFERENT QUESTIONS.** `cfg.maxUploadMb` (default **1024**) is what a person may upload, enforced on the streaming door where it costs no RAM — raise it as far as the owner likes. `cfg.maxJsonUploadMb` (default **25**) is all a base64-in-JSON body may carry, and **`JSON_LIMIT_MB` in server.js derives from THAT, never from the document ceiling** — tying the parser's ceiling to the document ceiling is precisely how the first number becomes an outage. Every base64 door now asks `uploadStream.jsonUploadBytes()`; `test-upload-limits-pure` fails the build if one goes back to multiplying out `maxUploadMb`.
  · **ONE HANDLER, TWO DOORS — never a second upload route.** `/documents` and `/documents/binary` on staff, borrower and TPO are the SAME function: `binaryIntake` streams the body, puts the metadata (which rides in the `x-upload-meta` header as **base64 JSON**, because a raw header value is latin-1 on the wire and a filename with an accent or a quotation mark would corrupt silently) on `req.body`, and the handler runs unchanged. A second route would be a second place the condition lookup, the staff-only visibility rule and the notification could drift, and the one that drifts is the one that shows a borrower an internal document.
  · **THREE THINGS DOWNSTREAM WANT THE REAL BYTES** — the appraisal XML auto-import, the research warehouse's XML catch, and the AI classifier — and on the streaming door reading them back costs memory equal to the file. Each asks explicitly, under its own ceiling (`readUploadBytes`), only when it could plausibly apply (an XML-looking name, a configured classifier), and each does nothing when it cannot have them: all three are EXTRAS and none may turn a stored document into a failed upload. **`readUploadBytes` applies its limit BEFORE the in-memory shortcut** — returning a JSON upload's bytes without consulting the limit makes the two doors behave differently for the same file (the borrower-upload email would start attaching 20 MB files on one door and not the other).
  · **THE REFUSAL IS A SENTENCE, AND IT APPEARS ON THE CONDITION.** `tooLargeMessage` names the file, its size and the real limit — *"upload failed" tells nobody anything they can act on* — and the staff screen records the outcome against the condition it was for (`uploadNote`), rendering it on that row both collapsed and open. It used to go to `setErr`, a banner at the top of a very long file screen: from beside the row you were working, the upload simply appeared to do nothing.
  · **NOTHING IS LEFT BEHIND.** The temp file is removed on every path — success, refusal, or a socket dying halfway — and the size guard bites DURING the stream, so a runaway client is cut off rather than allowed to fill the disk. A de-duplicated upload now removes the object it had already stored (both doors store before the dedupe check can run).
  · **"ACROSS THE ENTIRE SYSTEM" MEANS EVERY SURFACE, AND THE FIRST CUT DID ONE OF THREE (owner-directed audit 2026-08-21).** The staff file screen was wired to the streaming door and the BORROWER's — *the very door the 23 MB executed contract came through* — was not, nor was the broker's: both still read the file into base64, so both still hit the JSON ceiling and both still reported the failure in a top-of-page banner, which is the exact *"it's popping up in the wrong place"* complaint. **The guard is what let it pass**: section 6 of `test-upload-limits-pure` named ONE screen, so it read as "the client streams the file" while being true of a third of them. It is a **TABLE of surfaces** now — adding a fourth means adding a row, and it cannot silently cover one out of four again — and each row asserts three things, because a screen can stream the file and still put the refusal in the wrong place. **A note-rendering assertion must pin the GUARD CLAUSE, not just the call** (`if (tgt && tgt.itemId) setUploadNote(`): a mutation that wrapped the call in `if (false)` left the text in the file and the first version of the guard stayed green.
  · **A SOURCE-SHAPE GUARD ELSEWHERE WENT RED ON THIS, AND IT WAS RIGHT TO.** `test-research-xml-catch-pure` pinned the LITERAL that carried the bytes to the research warehouse (`bytes: buf`), which the streaming door replaced — so `npm test` was red for two commits on a change that broke nothing. It is now pinned on BOTH halves: the `bytes` key is passed (a literal `null` fails it) AND it is fed by the read-back under that door's own ceiling — because pinning only the key would let a door quietly pass `null` on the streaming path and stop filing every report uploaded through it. **When you move where a value comes from, grep for the guards that named the old expression** — a stale guard reads as a broken feature and gets 'fixed' by loosening it.
  · Tests `scripts/test-upload-limits-pure.js` (39) and `scripts/test-upload-stream-db.js` (18 — a 12 MB document through the real HTTP door with a 4 MB JSON ceiling, read back byte-for-byte; the staff-only rule holding on both doors; the honest refusal; the mid-flight guard leaving no temp file; the file scope still governing), both in `npm test`; three mutations — a generic refusal, the read-back shortcut, and un-registering the streaming door — were each proven to fail them. **THE DOCUMENT DOORS ARE ALL ON BOTH TRANSPORTS NOW (owner-directed audit 2026-08-21), and the LIST is the guard.** The first pass fixed the condition door and left the rest — so an OPERATING AGREEMENT or a set of ARTICLES, a multi-page scan and routinely the largest thing filed on a loan, was still capped at the JSON ceiling. Converted, each sharing ONE handler between its two registrations (two handlers is two places the scope check, the visibility rule and the notification can drift, and the one that drifts is the one that shows a borrower an internal document): entity documents, track-record documents on BOTH sides, lead documents, and the three condition doors. `api.uploadStream(path, b)` is the client seam for a door whose callers post by path rather than through a named method — hand it a File and it streams, hand it base64 and it does not. **WHAT DELIBERATELY STAYS ON THE JSON TRANSPORT, EACH WITH ITS REASON WRITTEN DOWN** — because "nothing calls this" must always be either a failure or a recorded decision, never an accident. **Draw attachments + lien waivers**: the bytes are genuinely needed in memory (the MIME type is sniffed FROM them, an iPhone HEIC is converted, EXIF location is stripped, and the copy is re-hashed so the stored hash describes the bytes actually stored), and that door carries its OWN stated 25 MB per-attachment ceiling with an honest refusal — raising it is a real change to a byte-manipulating path and deserves its own pass. **Chat attachments**: a message attachment is not a loan document. **The photo ID / ID-card scan**: one image, sized for a phone photo. Section 8 of `test-upload-limits-pure` holds both halves — every streamed door named with its shared handler, and every JSON-only door with a stated reason — so a NEW door landing on the small transport because nobody asked is the failure it catches. **Route a new document door through `takeUpload` + a `/binary` sibling, and add it to that list.**
- **THE TRINITY BUDGET REVIEW (form 159) — the pre-closing feasibility read, and the gate in front of it (owner-directed 2026-08-21).** *"Add a Trinity workflow during the file. This is something else from the Draw Center, which is a 159 Budget Review … should be available in the order section. You should mark over there that it's only intended for real heavy rehabs and for ground-ups. On ground-ups where we post the condition for feasibility review, that condition should get a button where, from that button, we can order this report directly. It should send over the entire scope. Right before you order, it should be gated that you need to have: the scope of work completed / the contractor information completed / the research on which fields they're accepting, because they're asking for the contractor information / it linked with our contractor information that we have / the full budget so they can review it properly. You also need to have the appraisal back before you're ordering this, and the appraisal PDF document should be sent along together with the order to Trinity. It's a separate workflow other than the drawer."*
  1. **THE RESEARCH THE OWNER ASKED FOR — "which fields they're accepting" — read off Trinity's OWN v1.1 swagger** (`docs/trinity/api/swagger-v1.1.json`, captured from the live API), so it is their schema rather than anybody's memory. `POST /api/v1.1/forms/159/new` takes **`DollarLineItem…TotalBudgetedOrderModelProjectModel`** — **THE SAME SCHEMA THE DRAW WE ALREADY PLACE USES** (forms 19 / 139 / 150 / 159 / 1074 / 1079 / 1081 all share it). **CONTRACTOR** (`ContractorModel`): `name`, `companyName`, `emailAddress` all REQUIRED, plus at least one of `phone`/`mobilePhone` (required in practice — verified live 2026-08-16; the swagger marks both nullable and that is not true). **APPRAISAL** (`PropertyModel.appraisal`): `value`, `datePerformed`, `performedBy` — the FIGURES. **The appraisal PDF is a DOCUMENT, not a field**: `POST /api/v1.1/orders/{id}/documents/json`, group **1 = "Appraisal"**. **THE SCOPE** (`order.lineItems[]`): `description`, `itemCost`, plus `amountRequested` / `isRequested` / `previousPercentCompleted` / `remarks`.
  2. **THEREFORE THERE IS NO SECOND PAYLOAD BUILDER.** `mapper.buildOrderPayload` — already verified against the live sandbox — takes `kind: 'budget_review'` and changes **exactly one rule**: a DRAW must have a line asking for money; a REVIEW asks for none by definition, because it sends the whole budget for somebody to READ. Everything else (the contractor rules, the phone rules, the line-key uniquifier that Trinity 400s without) is shared, because a second builder is a second place all of that could drift. An ordinary draw is byte-identical to before, pinned.
  3. **IT IS NOT A DRAW, AND MUST NEVER BECOME ONE.** No `sitewire_draws`, no `portal_draw_requests`, no `draw_findings`, no money ledger — asserted by a source sweep, because the owner's *"separate workflow other than the drawer"* is the whole shape of this feature. A draw is a request for a release against work already done; this is a read of the PLAN before the loan closes.
  4. **WHO MAY ORDER ONE, and the distinction is kept rather than flattened.** Ground-up = allowed AND **expected** ("this is the product for this file"); heavy rehab = allowed but **case by case** ("only if this rehab really warrants it") — the owner said *"maybe case by case"* and that is a real difference the desk should see. Anything else is refused: a light rehab or a bridge has no construction plan to review, so an order is money spent on nothing. It reuses `feasibility-fee.feasibilityKind` — the SAME ordered test the FEE uses — so the file that is CHARGED the ground-up feasibility fee and the file that may ORDER the ground-up review can never be different files.
  5. **THE BUG THAT PROVES WHY THAT MATTERS, caught in test before it shipped.** `applications.program` holds the PORTAL's label, and one of those labels is **"Fix & Flip w/ Construction"** — which contains the word "construction" and therefore reads as a GROUND-UP to the frozen engine's classifier. `pricing.buildInputs` has always normalized it away (`engineStrategy`, and its comment says exactly why); the review read the column RAW, so an ordinary fix & flip looked like a ground-up to the review while the fee correctly priced it as a flip. **The two would have disagreed about the same file.** `engineStrategy` is now exported and the review runs through it. **THE LESSON GENERALISES: anything that asks "is this a ground-up?" off `applications.program` must normalize first.** And the guard that caught it is the one worth copying — comparing raw strings through both rules passes even while the two READ THE FILE differently, so the test also asks both about REAL FILES end to end, including that label.
  6. **THE GATE IS FIVE REFUSALS, NOT WARNINGS, AND IT REPORTS THEM ALL AT ONCE.** Trinity reads what we send and we pay for the read, so an order off a half-finished file buys a confident answer about the wrong project. **Scope of work**: asks `rehab-budget.checkSowBudget` — the SAME cent-exact gate that decides whether the Scope-of-Work CONDITION may be signed off, so "finished enough for Trinity" and "finished enough for us" are one standard. **Contractor**: Trinity's own three required fields plus a phone, read off OUR `service_contacts` record (the owner's *"linked with our contractor information"*) — never typed in, because an inspector telephones this person to get onto the property and a fabricated contact is worse than a refusal. **Appraisal**: back AND with a PDF — two different things, and a file whose appraisal arrived as data with no report is a real state, so it is refused with a sentence saying WHICH half is missing. A rejected report is not a report. **EVERY CHECK FAILS CLOSED** — anything unreadable blocks, proven against a database that refuses to answer. A deal that may not order one reports exactly ONE reason, its own: listing four other blockers would imply the order becomes possible once they are cleared.
  7. **TWO DOORS, ONE CARD — `app-v2/src/components/TrinityBudgetReview.jsx`.** The owner asked for it in two places: an ORDER (*"should be available in the order section"*) and a button ON the feasibility condition (*"on ground-ups where we post the condition for feasibility review, that condition should get a button"*). Both mount the SAME component — the Orders room as `sec-order-budget-review` (its own section, its own deep link, its own rail spoke; `stations.js` `st-orders`), and the `rtl_cond_feasibility` row in the conditions list. **Never a second copy**: two cards means two answers to "may this file order one, and what is still missing?", and the one that drifts is the one somebody presses. The card RE-DERIVES NOTHING — the deal kind, the readiness and every blocker come from `GET /api/trinity/files/:appId/budget-review`, which is the same `reviewGate` the order path re-runs at the moment of sending (a scope can change between opening the screen and pressing the button, so the screen's answer is never trusted). The owner's *"only intended for real heavy rehabs and for ground-ups"* is printed ON the card, so nobody orders one on a light rehab because the button happened to be there. Ordering asks for confirmation first — the report is billable.
  8. **A READ THAT FAILED SAYS NOTHING, and a SKIP IS NOT A SUCCESS.** An unreadable gate renders no card rather than a card claiming the file cannot order one — an unreadable answer is not a "no". And the route refuses to report success on a stand-down: `placeReviewOrder` answers `{skipped:'off'|'outbound_off'|…}` with no error, which is right for a background caller and wrong to hand to somebody who just pressed a button, so `POST /files/:appId/budget-review` turns it into a 409 naming the reason instead of a screen that says "ordered" about an order nothing sent.
  Tests `scripts/test-trinity-budget-review-db.js` (in `npm test`; four mutations — reading the program raw, dropping the appraisal-PDF requirement, failing open on an unreadable file, and letting a review ask for money — were each proven to fail it) and the budget-review section of `scripts/test-file-stations-pure.mjs` (both mounts, the rail entry, the intended-for mark, and no `--ink` text colour — the mount assertion was proven to fail when the condition's button was removed).
- **THE BORROWER'S DRAW CENTRE — what was missing was the LAYOUT, not the features (owner-directed 2026-08-21).** *"We want to set up a view-only draw center for them where they can view everything. They should also be able to approve draws over there, and they should be able to dispute over there … nicely laid out: Every draw / How much the amount was / Pictures / What was approved / What was not approved / Inspector notes … with borrower actions that they have to do. If there's anything else they have already, leave it, unless it's something risky."*
  1. **MOST OF IT ALREADY EXISTED, AND WAS DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE** — `BorrowerDraws.jsx` already showed every draw with its amounts, the budget-vs-released picture, per-line requested / approved / **not approved**, the inspector's note per line, the durable per-line **photos**, Accept and Dispute (with photo evidence), the whole-project PDF, the eligibility preview and the composer. The honest finding was that the owner was describing a screen that was mostly built and **unreadable**, so the work was layout, not features. Section 5 of the test pins every pre-existing piece so a future tidy-up cannot quietly drop one — that is what *"leave it"* means in a test.
  2. **THE ACTION IS SAID FIRST.** The Accept / Dispute buttons live on the result card, which on a real file sits below the budget hero, the line table, the draws table, the attach card, the report button and the eligibility card — so the one action holding up their own money was the last thing on a long page. A callout at the top now names how many draws are waiting and jumps to the first. It renders ONLY when something is genuinely `delivered`, because a banner that fires on a settled draw teaches the reader to ignore banners, and this is the only prompt they get.
  3. **A SETTLED DRAW OPENS COLLAPSED — AND THE ONE AWAITING THEM NEVER DOES.** Six draws into a build the borrower read six fully-expanded result cards, with the one needing an answer indistinguishable from one paid out in March. Collapsed still states everything that matters at a glance (which draw, its state, what was approved, what reached them); what folds away is the step tracker, the line-by-line table and the actions, behind "Show details" and an inline link in the sentence itself. **If that rule ever inverted, the card carrying Accept and Dispute would be the one folded shut and the borrower's money would sit still because the page hid the button** — which is why `open` is initialised as `!settled` and pinned, mutation-proven, rather than left to a default.
  4. **THE CARD NAMES WHICH DRAW.** Every card was titled "Draw inspection results", so four cards read identically and none could be matched to the #1 / #2 in the table directly above. The number comes off the **rollup** — the same source that table and their PDF are built from, so the two can never disagree — and it is **never invented**: with no resolvable number it falls back to the plain title.
  5. **EVERY COLOUR IS AN EXPLICIT DARK, and the test enforces it on the new styles.** `--ink*` is a LIGHT paper colour in this palette (the names are legacy and they LIE), so a `color: var(--ink)` renders white-on-white — the bug that made a whole staff card invisible on 2026-07-26.
