The inline link is **underlined**, not colour-only (WCAG 1.4.1), and carries a visible focus ring; on a phone the callout's button spans rather than being squeezed into a sliver beside the sentence.
  6. **VERIFIED BY RENDERING, not by reading.** A green Vite build proves the file parses and nothing more — an undeclared identifier compiles to a `ReferenceError` at render (the standing trap). So: eslint `no-undef` on the changed component (with the linter itself proven to bite), then the real markup rendered in headless Chromium against the **real built stylesheet** at desktop and iPhone-12 widths, asserting `elementFromPoint` actually HITS the callout and the link, that the text is dark on a light ground, and that `innerWidth === 390` with zero sideways scroll. **`elementFromPoint` only answers about the visible viewport**, so anything below the fold must be scrolled into view first — otherwise a phone check reports "invisible" about an element that is merely further down, which is a fact about the test, not the page.
  Test `scripts/test-borrower-draw-center-pure.js` (in `npm test`); four mutations — collapsing the awaiting card, counting every finding as waiting, an `--ink` text colour, and dropping the underline — were each proven to fail it.
- **THE DRAW COORDINATOR IS TOLD WHEN THE BORROWER APPROVES — and the notice names the draw and the money (owner-reported 2026-08-21).** *"When a borrower is approving the inspection results online, he's clicking on the Approve button from his email or log in, and the draw coordinator is not getting a notification. We need to set up a nice notification to the draw coordinator that this draw number, whatever this amount was, was approved by the borrower through the online system … Make sure, either way, when they approve it, either on the portal or on their email, the draw coordinator and the loan officer should get a notification that the borrower approved it."*
  1. **ROOT CAUSE — TWO RESOLVERS THAT NEVER MET, and this is the class to remember.** `notify.notifyAppStaff` — the ONE whole-team fan-out — selects **`application_assignees`**. But PILOT has **no draw-coordinator POINTER**: a coordinator is identified by what they DID on the file (pressed "Start the draw process" → `sitewire_property_links.draw_setup_started_by`, or holds a live `workflow_items` hand-off routed `to_role='draw_coordinator'`), which is `draw-recipients.drawCoordinatorsForFile` — a completely different lookup. So the borrower pressed Accept, the fan-out ran against the assignee table, and **the one person whose job it is to release the money was structurally not in the list.** The loan officer WAS (the db/103 pointer trigger keeps the primary officer as an assignee), which is exactly why only half the team ever heard. **`draw_coordinator` IS a valid assignee role, and that is the trap** — it looks wired, and nothing on the two real assignment paths ever creates the row.
  2. **FIXED AT THE CHOKEPOINT, KEYED ON THE CATEGORY — never at the call sites.** `notifyAppStaff` unions in `draw-recipients.drawStaffIdsForFile(appId)` when `categoryOf(opts.type) === 'draws'` — the same shape, and the same resolver, as the borrower-email loop-in the owner asked for on 2026-07-28 (`drawLoopInBcc`), so the two can never drift about who a file's coordinator is and **every draw staff notification that exists today and every one added later carries it.** It cannot widen anybody's scope: `staffRolesSee` exists so a part-of-the-file role hears only its own part, and a 'draws' event IS the draw coordinator's part — which is why it is applied to that category and **nothing else** (pinned both ways). The desk-wide fallback is kept (`coordinatorsOrDesk`), so a draw event on a file nobody has claimed still reaches somebody who can act; `is_external=false` on both branches keeps a **TPO broker** — who IS the loan-officer assignee on their firm's files — out of the internal stream. Best-effort: a lookup failure adds nobody and never breaks a fan-out.
  3. **WHAT IT SAYS IS THE OTHER HALF — `src/sitewire/draw-accepted-notice.js` is ONE definition for THREE doors** (the portal, the emailed link, and the TPO broker surface). The old wording — *"The borrower accepted the inspection results — the release is due by …"* — named neither the draw nor the money, so the coordinator had to open the file to learn what they had been told about. It now leads with **which draw**, carries the **ranked money block** and says **where the button was pressed**; sharing one definition is what stops the desk learning to read one door's message and ignore the others. **The money is NEVER recomputed here** — `drawEmailBlocks` → `rollup` → `approval.drawMoney`, the ONE source of per-draw money, so this notice can never quote a figure that disagrees with the report attached to it. A **broker's** approval is deliberately never described as the borrower's: the coordinator is about to move money and who authorised it is the first thing they should read. It **never invents** a draw number (an unresolvable draw says "A draw was approved" rather than printing an empty number) and never prints an unreadable deadline. It **never throws** — the acceptance has already happened by the time it runs, and a broken decoration must not turn a successful approval into a 500 for the borrower.
  3b. **AND THE SHARED DESK INBOX GETS ONE COPY (owner-directed 2026-08-21: *"you can always notify our group email, which is draws@yscapgroup.com"*).** `draws@yscapgroup.com` is a shared MAILBOX, not a person, so it has no `staff_users` row — and `notifyAppStaff` emails one such row at a time, which means **no resolver could ever have put it there.** It was already copied on every BORROWER draw email (`drawLoopInBcc`), on the wire-form DocuSign viewers and on `notifyAppStaffThread`; our OWN team notifications were the one place it was missing, which is exactly where the borrower pressing Accept lands. It is sent ONCE, at the same chokepoint and keyed on the same 'draws' category, **never Bcc'd onto each staffer's email** (a shared inbox holding six copies of one event is the noise a shared inbox exists to avoid), and it honours BOTH `opts.inAppOnly` — load-bearing, because `notifyAppThread` sets it precisely when the borrower's email already carried this address on a visible Cc, so sending here too would reintroduce the duplicate that helper removed — and the `alreadyEmailed` rule. A coordinator whose OWN address is the desk inbox is deduped out, so the desk is emailed once in total. Best-effort: it can never break the fan-out it rides on.
  4. **The coordinator's own offline recording (`mark-accepted`) stays `inAppOnly`** — they just did it, so emailing them about it is noise; the change only means their in-app row now reaches them at all.
  Test `scripts/test-draw-accepted-notice-db.js` (in `npm test`, real Postgres + the real notify path): the gap itself (a coordinator who owns the draws and is not an assignee), both doors reaching both people, the category gate refusing a non-draw event, the wording, the never-invent rules, a broker never being a recipient, and — asserted on the WIRE PAYLOAD with the mailer stubbed, because a passing send against the `none` provider proves nothing about who was addressed — the coordinator being EMAILED rather than merely given an in-app row (`draw_accepted` staying OUT of `STAFF_INAPP_TYPES` is the only thing that makes it one, so it is pinned on the wire rather than inferred from that list) and the shared desk inbox getting exactly one copy, none on a non-draw event, none on an in-app-only fan-out, and none twice. **Eight mutations were each proven to fail it**, including reverting the loop-in — which reproduces the owner's report exactly. When you add a draw notification, give its type the 'draws' category and it is looped in for free; when you add another way a draw is approved, route it through `notifyDrawAccepted` rather than hand-building a message.
- **THE PIPELINE DOES NOT SCORE FILES — the two red AI stamps are off the list (owner-directed 2026-08-21: *"please review these two stamps outside of the file in the pipeline view for this AI risk. The risk score and the AI stamp — the red stamps outside of the file"*, then, choosing: *"take them off the pipeline"*).**
  1. **WHAT THEY WERE.** `FatalAiChip` — a red/amber "AI 3 · 5d" counting open FATAL `ai_suggestions` and darkening with the age of the oldest — and `RiskScoreChip` — "RISK 84 · CRIT", a 0–100 sum over every open suggestion (fatal 25 / warning 8 / info 2), amber at 50 and red at 80. Both sat in the pipeline row's status cell, immediately beside the file's real status pill.
  2. **WHY THEY WERE WORTH REVIEWING, and it is the standing HARD RULE rather than taste: AI findings are ADVISORY and never block anything** — not a sign-off, not clear-to-close, not funding, not an issuance. A RED stamp on the one screen the whole team scans to decide what to work reads as a STOP on that file, which is the opposite of what an advisory is; and it sat next to the status pill, the one thing on that row that IS authoritative. The risk number had the additional problem that **no action attached to it** — nobody can clear an 84 — so it could only ever be atmosphere.
  3. **NOTHING WAS LOST, AND THAT IS WHAT MADE REMOVAL THE RIGHT ANSWER RATHER THAN A TRADE.** Every one of those findings still renders on the FILE, in the Document review & PILOT findings section, in front of the person who can actually resolve one — including its own "File AI risk score" panel (`UnderwritingPanel.jsx`). What was removed is the summary stamp on the LIST, never a finding.
  4. **THE THREE SUBQUERIES WENT WITH THEM.** The pipeline query ran three correlated scans of `ai_suggestions` PER ROW to feed those chips — an open-fatal count, the age of the oldest, and the risk sum — on the most-loaded screen in the app. Leaving them computing values nothing renders is the shape this repo's rules call cheap; they were removed once it was verified (V1, both portals, every script) that nothing else reads `open_fatal_ai` / `open_fatal_ai_oldest_days` / `ai_risk_score`. The `ai-suggestions` require in `routes/staff.js` went too, as its last use.
  5. **THE GUARD ASSERTS ABSENCE, DELIBERATELY, AND IT COULD NOT HAVE BEEN AN ENTRY IN THE OLD TABLE.** `test-advisory-not-scored-pure.js` keeps a list of scoring consumers and checks each carries the shared advisory filter — so a re-added risk subquery WOULD PASS that table (it would carry the filter) while putting the stamp straight back on the pipeline. The three pipeline entries were therefore removed from the list and replaced by a test that the pipeline query does not compute those columns and the row does not render those components, with the screen's COMMENTS stripped first — the note explaining the removal necessarily names them, and a guard that read comments would fail on its own explanation and then get "fixed" by deleting it. Two mutations — a filtered risk-score subquery coming back, and the chip coming back on the row — were each proven to fail it, and `eslint no-undef` was proven to catch a dangling `<RiskScoreChip />` (a green Vite build would not: an undeclared identifier compiles to a render-time `ReferenceError`).
  **Do not re-add a scoring stamp to a LIST screen.** Where a file's AI standing matters, it belongs on the file.
- **ONE SIGNING EMAIL, FROM PILOT, FOR EVERY SIGNER — AND AN EXECUTION NOTICE WITH THE DOCUMENT ATTACHED (owner-directed 2026-08-21).** *"They're receiving an email from DocuSign directly to sign, and they're also receiving an email from Pilot to sign. When they're clicking on the DocuSign link, it comes up an error … The only link that works is the link that is coming directly from PILOT."* And: *"The Loan Officers and the Admins that are signing … are only getting the DocuSign. We want to make them a nice Pilot email … with the direct link to sign."* And: *"we need to add a notification for every document that is completed … the ISKA once they sign / the Wire Form once they sign / the Term Sheet Package once everyone has signed … with the document attached."*
  1. **ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS ONE PROPERTY.** Every recipient is CAPTIVE (embedded): `clientUserId` is set, which is what lets PILOT open the signing session inside the portal and is why DocuSign suppresses its own email for that recipient. The signer builder ALSO set **`embeddedRecipientStartURL: 'SIGN_AT_DOCUSIGN'`** — the "hybrid" shape that puts DocuSign's email back on. So DocuSign emailed a link that, for a captive recipient, lands on a page they cannot sign on, right beside PILOT's link that works. **Two invitations, one of them broken, on every package we have ever sent.** The property is gone from the send path (`orchestrate.js`) and from the correction path (`recipient-email.js`); the DocuSign CLIENT still SUPPORTS it (`integrations/docusign.js` is a generic client and a future non-captive recipient may legitimately want it) — what must never come back is a CALLER setting it.
  2. **A SIGNING TOKEN NAMES EXACTLY ONE IDENTITY — a borrower or one of ours, never both, never neither.** `magic-link.mintSigningToken` carries `bid` XOR `sid`, and `verifySigningToken` REFUSES a token naming both or naming nobody: `/api/esign/sign` matches the recipient on the identity in the token, and a token naming nobody would open whichever recipient the query happened to return first. The staff branch additionally pins `r.borrower_id IS NULL` and requires an **ACTIVE** staff row.
  3. **AN EMAIL LINK STILL NEVER MINTS AN INTERNAL CONSOLE SESSION.** The borrower's return hand-off (`mintReturnAuth`, which lands them signed-in inside their own file) is DELIBERATE and unchanged; a STAFF signer gets `null` and `dest=staff`, so they land on the internal file and sign in normally. The token remains a bearer for signing ONE envelope and nothing else. Never widen it.
  4. **OUR OWN SIGNERS GET OUR OWN EMAIL.** `notify-signers.js` widened its role filter to `('borrower','co_borrower','loan_officer','admin')` and dropped `borrower_id IS NOT NULL`; a staff recipient is resolved to the roster **by the address the envelope was addressed to, ACTIVE only**, and one we cannot place is SKIPPED rather than emailed a signing bearer. They get `esignStaffReadyToSign` (internal voice, names the borrower whose file it is), never the borrower letter — which greets them as the borrower and reassures them about documents they never asked for. The borrower's join is on `r.borrower_id`, which is NULL for a staff recipient, so the email would otherwise have named nobody — hence the `file_borrower_name` join.
  5. **A COUNTER-SIGNER IS INVITED WHEN THEIR TURN COMES, EXACTLY ONCE.** DocuSign holds a routing-order-2 recipient at `created` until everyone before them signs, so emailing them at SEND time says "your signature is needed" when DocuSign will not let them sign and the link fails — the very experience being fixed. `notifyReadyToSign` skips a recipient whose `recipient_status` is not sent/delivered, and `webhook.applyRecipients` uses a **CTE that captures the previous status in the same UPDATE** to detect the transition FROM THE ROW rather than inferring it — so a repeated `delivered` webhook, which DocuSign sends freely, cannot re-email them. A row from before recipient statuses were tracked has none and is treated as their turn (the historic behaviour).
  6. **THE EXECUTION NOTICE — `src/lib/esign/completion-notice.js`, fired from `notifyTerminal` on `completed`.** That is the right hook for the same three reasons the closing-chain announcement beside it uses it: it runs only for the WINNER of the terminal claim (`${col} IS NULL RETURNING`), so a redelivered webhook racing a poller tick cannot send twice; `handleCompletion` has already filed the signed PDF, so there is something to attach; and a counter-signed package reaches `completed` only after the lender's admin signs — which IS the owner's *"once everyone has signed"*, with no extra bookkeeping. **It attaches the copy THIS ENVELOPE produced** (`esign_envelope_docs.completed_document_id`), never "the newest signed copy on the file" — on a re-issued package those are different documents, and the wrong one on an execution notice is worse than none. Oversize is honest: `closing-prep.buildAttachments` reports what it withheld and the email says the copy is in the portal instead of promising an attachment that is not there. **A package with no wording in `PACKAGE` is not announced at all** — "your documents are executed" about something this module cannot name is worse than the silence it replaces, and adding a package is one line.
  7. **BEST-EFFORT THROUGHOUT, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** The webhook's invitation runs AFTER the status write (an email that fails must never cost the status update the rest of the system reads); the execution notice never throws (a completed envelope may not be reversed or re-driven by a mail failure). Neither can turn a successful signature into an error.
  **AND IT IS PROVEN ON THE WIRE, NOT ONLY IN THE SOURCE (owner-directed audit 2026-08-21).** The whole restructure first shipped covered by ONE suite that reads the source and checks wording — which proves the property is gone and the sentences are right, and cannot prove an email leaves the building, reaches the person the owner named, or carries the document. `scripts/test-esign-one-invitation-db.js` drives the REAL functions against a REAL database with the REAL catalog rendering and **`src/lib/email`.sendMail stubbed and inspected**: the officer is actually emailed and the body carries `/api/esign/sign?t=`, the counter-signer is left alone until their turn, a departed officer's address gets nothing, and each completed package attaches **the executed PDF byte for byte** — asserted on the bytes, because an email that claims a copy is attached and carries none is worse than one that says it is in the portal. **Two things learned writing it, both worth keeping**: `esign_envelope_docs.document_id` is DocuSign's own index within the envelope (an integer), NOT a foreign key — the uuid lives in `completed_document_id`; and the unnamed-package cases are the ones `chk_esign_purpose` can actually reach (the connection SELF-TEST and a NULL purpose), not an invented value the database refuses. **A MUTATION CAN BE MASKED BY AN INCIDENTAL CRASH**: neutralising the active-staff guard made the send throw into its own catch, so no email went out and the "never emailed a bearer" assertion passed for the WRONG reason — the faithful mutation is dropping `AND is_active = true`, which sends cleanly to a departed officer and does bite. **AND THE WEBHOOK'S HOOK NEEDED ITS OWN ASSERTION**: the DB suite calls `notifyExecuted` directly, so deleting the `status === 'completed'` hook would have left every test green and no borrower ever told again — the pure suite now pins that wiring, mutation-proven.
  Tests `scripts/test-esign-one-invitation-pure.js` (in `npm test`; SEVEN mutations — restoring `SIGN_AT_DOCUSIGN`, letting a token name both identities, dropping the staff roles from the notifier, minting a return-auth for a staff signer, attaching the newest signed copy instead of this envelope's, announcing a package with no wording, and putting the "you will also get a DocuSign email" line back — were each proven to fail it) plus the re-pointed captive-only assertions in `test-esign-orchestrate.js` and `test-esign-recipient-email.js`, which had PINNED the hybrid shape and are the reason this survived so long. **When you add a signer role, add it to the notifier's role filter and give it wording; when you add a package, add it to `PACKAGE` — never a second invitation path, and never ask DocuSign to email a captive recipient.**
- **A CHASE FIRES ON A STATED ANSWER, NEVER ON AN EMPTY COLUMN — what the last read of the purchase advice field actually DID (owner-reported 2026-08-21; db/608).** File YSCAP258134650 received *"No purchase advice 64 days after funding"* naming CorrFirst — and the loan HAS a purchase advice date and is EMCAP. *"Please do research to see if you're looking at the correct field … you need to refresh your entire system … Please give me the field that you have. There are a lot of files that receive an email that are missing a PA date, but most of the files already have it."*
  1. **ROOT CAUSE — THE CHASE ASKED THE WRONG QUESTION, and this is a CLASS, not one bug.** `notification-digests.purchaseAdviceMissingOnce` fired on `applications.purchase_advice_date IS NULL`, a column only ever written when a per-file Encompass pull actually reads field **2370** back. THREE ordinary things leave it NULL on a loan that plainly has an advice date, and none of them is evidence about the sale: the file **has not come round on the pull rota yet** (`sync/encompass-sync.js` pulls ONE file every 15 minutes, round-robin by staleness across the whole book — days to weeks per file); PILOT **holds no Encompass loan guid** for it, so no field was ever read; or the read ran and **Encompass did not return that id** — which is exactly what `client.readFields` produces for an invalid or unpermitted field, because it splits its id list on the 400 and **merges what SUCCEEDED**, so a bad id goes MISSING from the map rather than raising. In all three PILOT had never once asked about the loan and told the closer it was unsold anyway. **THE RULE: a NULL is not an answer. Before a sweep tells anybody something is missing, it must be able to say that we ASKED and were told nothing — never that we have nothing written down.**
  2. **SO THE READ ITSELF IS RECORDED.** `release-party.syncPurchaseAdviceDate` stamps `applications.purchase_advice_read_at / _read_state / _field_id` (db/608) every time it looks: **`value`** (a date came back — sold), **`blank`** (returned and empty — **the ONLY chaseable state**), **`not_returned`** (the read ran, the id was not in the answer), **`no_field_id`** (nothing configured on this deployment), **`no_loan_link`** (no Encompass loan to ask about). **`purchase_advice_read_at` ALWAYS moves** — it is what the sweep drains on, so a state-only guard would make the sweep re-read one file forever. **A FAILED READ IS DELIBERATELY NOT STAMPED**: that is transient, and stamping it would drain a real file out of the sweep and let one bad minute stand as an answer about the loan.
  3. **THE CHASE NOW FIRES ON `read_state='blank'` ALONE, and going quiet is the SAFE direction.** Accepted, stated consequence: until the sweep has worked the funded book the chase says nothing about the files it has not reached. A false chase costs the closer's trust and sends them hunting for a date already on the loan; silence costs a few days. **AND SILENCE CANNOT HIDE A BROKEN FIELD** — `purchaseAdviceUnreadableOnce` reports the not-judged pile as its OWN thing, to **super admins only** (never the closer, never the shared `NOTIFY_ADMINS` inbox), in different words for different work: *"PILOT cannot tell"* is for whoever can fix the connection, *"chase the investor"* is for the closer. A never-asked file only counts there once it is older than the chase window — below that it is a queue, not a fault.
  4. **"REFRESH YOUR ENTIRE SYSTEM" IS `release-party.sweepPurchaseAdviceOnce`** (its own timer in `encompass-sync`, `ENCOMPASS_PA_SWEEP_MIN` default 10 min; off with `ENCOMPASS_PA_SWEEP_DISABLED=1`; on demand at `POST /api/admin/encompass/purchase-advice/sweep`). It reads **the purchase advice field ALONE** — one field by number, never the whole loan and **never a pipeline search** (cached guid only, the expensive path stays the round-robin's) — bounded per pass, paced, draining on `purchase_advice_read_at NULLS FIRST` (db/608's partial index) so a partial pass resumes where it stopped. Client INJECTABLE like `db`, so the whole path is provable with no network. It never reports a bare count: a pass that says only "25 files" cannot tell a working sweep from one stamping twenty-five `no_loan_link`s.
  5. **DISCOVERING A DATE IS NOT THE SAME EVENT AS A DATE ARRIVING, and only the second is news.** A first read lands a March purchase advice as `changed`, which would fire the post-purchase *"this loan has been sold"* hand-off AND drag the ClickUp card forward **for the whole back book at once on the first deploy** — a blast dressed as a notification. So the sweep asks for a SILENT first read (`silentDiscovery`, keyed on the file never having been asked before): the date still lands, so every downstream reader treats it as sold from that moment, and the file is COUNTED as `discovered` rather than announced. **Every LATER read of that file announces and moves the card exactly as before, and the per-file pull is untouched** — the suppression is about the first read, never about the sweep, so it can never go quiet permanently.
  6. **AND A SECOND GAP CLOSED WITH IT.** Both the poll's priority lane and `refreshSoldSignal` require a live Sitewire draw project, so a funded file without one got neither — the sweep covers every funded file regardless.
  7. **"GIVE ME THE FIELD THAT YOU HAVE" IS ANSWERED FROM THE TENANT'S OWN DATA — `src/lib/purchase-advice-diagnosis.js`.** Restating the number somebody configured back at them proves nothing; it is the number that produced the wrong answer. It asks `encompass_field_catalog` (db/245) what the tenant's OWN Encompass calls the field we read and what else they have whose name mentions a purchase advice (key, label AND the raw object — a description we happened not to map would otherwise hide the very field). **An EMPTY catalogue answers `null`, never `false`** — "your field list has never been pulled" is a different answer from "your list does not contain this id", and reporting the second sends an admin hunting for a field that is fine. The plain-language order is deliberate and asserted: field off → id not in the tenant's list → reads coming back without it → a queue that has not drained → all clear. Surfaced at `GET /api/admin/encompass/purchase-advice`, and per file on the Encompass panel (`PA_READ_NOTE`, ONE wording table).
  8. **THE EMAIL QUOTES THE BUYER, NEVER ASSERTS IT.** `applications.lender` is pull-only and can be stale, and stating it flatly ("has to be sold to CorrFirst" on a deal the owner knows as EMCAP) makes the whole message read as nonsense even when the rest is right. It now says *the file records the buyer as X*, and tells the reader what to do if the loan does have a date in Encompass.
  9. **NO BACKFILL, DELIBERATELY.** Stamping a state on the existing book would re-arm the same false alarm in the other direction — `blank` everywhere would chase the whole back book on the next sweep. Every existing file reads as "we have never asked", which is the truth, and the sweep answers it a few files at a time.
  Tests `scripts/test-purchase-advice-diagnosis-pure.js` (the wording's whole truth table + the field list read through a STUB, so no database is needed) and `scripts/test-purchase-advice-read-state-db.js` (real Postgres: the columns and the CHECK, the owner's file both ways, the sweep incl. the failed read leaving nothing stamped, the silent discovery and its later announcement, the unreadable alert, and the diagnosis) — both in `npm test`; **seven mutations of the production code were each proven to fail them.** When you add a sweep that tells somebody something is missing, record what the last look actually DID and fire on the stated verdict — never on an empty column.
- **NOTHING WAS EVER DOWN — THE HEALTH PROBES WERE MEASURING OUR OWN QUEUE (owner-reported 2026-08-09: "the admins are getting bombarded with emails saying 'this is now down' … but after a few minutes it's getting back up. What is the bug? Why is everything going down every day a few times and getting back up so soon?").** The owner also proposed the fix for the noise ("maybe anything that is down should not get the alert as long as it's not out for 30 minutes") and, asked, chose **super admins only** and **one combined email**. Four parts; the first two are the ROOT CAUSE and the last two are the belt to those braces.
  1. **ROOT CAUSE A — a probe queued behind PILOT'S OWN RATE LIMITER.** `health-registry` wraps every probe in an **8-second** `timebox`, while `api-rate-limit.acquire()` deliberately **WAITS up to 60 seconds** when a bucket is empty. Six vendor clients pace themselves through it (`clickup/client.js takeToken`, `encompass`, `sitewire`, `trustpoint`, `sharepoint`, and `osm-gate`, which additionally enforces a 1-per-second gap — *always* longer than the probe's whole deadline). ClickUp's 90/min bucket is shared by the web process AND the worker and empties during ordinary sync activity, so during any busy period the probe sat in our own queue, blew its 8 seconds, and reported a perfectly healthy vendor as "not reachable". **THE CLASS: a health check that shares a caller's pacing measures the pacing, not the far end.** Fix: `api-rate-limit.runAsHealthProbe(fn)` marks the call tree with **AsyncLocalStorage** and `acquire()` reads `inHealthProbe()` → `maxWaitMs = 0`. AsyncLocalStorage rather than an option argument ON PURPOSE: the wait happens deep inside six different clients, threading a flag through each is six chances to miss one, and the one missed keeps flapping — the context follows the call, so a client added next year is covered without knowing the rule exists. A probe still **SPENDS** a token when one is free (a health check is a real request and is never exempt from the budget, only from the queue) and is **never counted in `waits`** — that counter is the owner's signal that a real cap is squeezing real work, and a health check inflating it would send them raising a limit that is not in anyone's way.
  2. **ROOT CAUSE B — 28 probes at once.** `probeAll` was `Promise.all` over the whole registry, every 15 minutes AND on every page load; several probes are full OAuth token exchanges, so on one shared-CPU instance they competed for TLS handshakes, sockets and rate-limit tokens under that same 8-second deadline and the slowest of the herd timed out. Now a bounded pool (`mapWithConcurrency`, `HEALTH_PROBE_CONCURRENCY`, default 6) that keeps results **in registry order** — exported through `_internals` so the pooling is unit-testable, since `probeAll` itself makes 28 outbound calls no test can run.
  3. **NOTHING IS SAID UNTIL AN OUTAGE IS 30 MINUTES OLD** (`INTEGRATIONS_MONITOR_ALERT_AFTER_MIN`, the Prometheus `for: 30m` shape). The clock starts at the **first failed observation and is never restarted** — a service flapping around the boundary would otherwise reset itself forever and a genuine outage would never be reported. `notifiedDown` is set **only when an alert was actually announced**, which is what makes a blip completely silent: no "down", and therefore **no "back up" either** — a recovery notice for an outage nobody was told about is pure noise. A failed SEND rolls the flag back for that sweep's down-alerts so the next tick re-sends (same class as the closing-chain dedupe: the "already told them" record must only be written once they were actually told); a failed RECOVERY notice is deliberately not retried, because reviving the row's stale down-state to force one would make the table claim a healthy service is down for a whole interval.
  4. **ONE EMAIL PER SWEEP, TO SUPER ADMINS, NEVER THE SHARED INBOX.** `composeAlert(alerts)` is PURE and builds a single `{title, body}` covering every service down and every service recovered — a restart takes several down at the same instant, and six emails about one event is the complaint in its purest form. `notifyAdmins` gained two ADDITIVE options, both off unless asked (every other call site is byte-identical): **`emailRoles`** (a recipient outside the list keeps the in-app row and loses only the email — it can only ever NARROW, an explicit `inAppOnly` still wins, an empty list is ignored) and **`skipSharedInbox`**. Why the pair, for "no loan officers should receive it": `notifyAdmins` only ever selects `role IN ('admin','super_admin')`, so a loan officer could never be reached that way — the one list that COULD carry one is **`NOTIFY_ADMINS`**, a hand-typed set of ADDRESSES in the hosting settings with no role attached, so this alert skips it outright rather than trusting what is in it today. Every admin still SEES the alert in PILOT; they are simply not mailed. Widening it back is one word and is the OWNER'S call.
  5. **The page STATES the policy** (`describe()` returns `alertAfterMin` + `emailRoles`; `MonitorBanner` renders them). Without it the quieting reads as the alerts being broken — "why did the emails stop?" has to be answerable on the screen. Both numbers come from the server so the page can never describe a policy the monitor is not running (a 0-minute window falls back to honest wording rather than printing "more than 0 minutes").
  Tests `scripts/test-health-probe-no-queue.js` (the probe context incl. concurrent isolation + a throwing probe, the bounded pool, an ordinary caller waiting where a probe does not, and the `waits` counter) and `scripts/test-integrations-monitor.js` (the silent window, the blip, announced-once, `composeAlert`'s wording at one/six/mixed, and — through the REAL notify with the mailer stubbed and a control fan-out proving the assertions would bite — the super admin emailed, the admin in-app only, the loan officer neither, the shared inbox skipped), both in `npm test`; nine mutations of the production code were each proven to fail them. When you add a new health probe or another outbound client, call it inside `runAsHealthProbe` — never give a probe its own bypass of the limiter.
- **EVERY MESSAGE PILOT SHOWS IS PILOT'S OWN — never the browser's `alert()` (owner-reported 2026-08-06, with the screenshot: "the error message is coming from OnRender.com. We need to update the branding. It should come directly from our system whenever you have an error message").** Clicking Sign off on a credit condition that was not ready produced a dialog titled **"yscap.onrender.com says"** — the native `alert()`, which stamps the ORIGIN HOSTNAME on the message and cannot be styled. So PILOT's own carefully worded plain-English refusal was presented to the team as if it came from a hosting provider they have never heard of, on the one screen where the wording is doing real work.
  · **`app-v2/src/lib/dialog.js` + `components/AppDialog.jsx` are the message box.** `showMessage(text, {title})` for a message, `askConfirm(text, {...})` for a question; the host is mounted ONCE in `App.jsx`, **outside** the `ErrorBoundary** (a screen that crashes must not take the dialog down with it) and outside `Routes` (so the public screens — login, e-sign, guest chat — are covered too). It renders on `.cv-modal` / `.cv-modal-back`, inheriting the solid card surface and the phone bottom-sheet behaviour every other modal here has, so it can never render transparent.
  · **IT FALLS BACK TO THE BROWSER ON PURPOSE.** With no host mounted, `showMessage` calls the native dialog immediately rather than queueing. **A swallowed error is strictly worse than an unbranded one** — branding is worth nothing if the price is a refusal nobody sees — and this file must never be the reason someone misses a message. Same on unmount.
  · **BOTH ARE MIGRATED — the 63 `alert()` sites first, then all 81 `window.confirm()` sites (owner-directed 2026-08-09: "fix both"). SUPERSEDES the original "`window.confirm()` DELIBERATELY DOES NOT [migrate] … do not bulk-swap" line.** The reason for that caution was real and still is: the native pair is SYNCHRONOUS while `askConfirm` returns a PROMISE, **and a promise is TRUTHY** — so a call site that loses its `await` reads as "the user said yes" on EVERY click and fires the destructive action nobody agreed to, silently, with a green build. The caution was never "this cannot be done", it was "this cannot be done by find-and-replace". What made it safe to finish:
    **(1) `await` in a non-async function is a SYNTAX ERROR**, so the compiler — not a regex — found every enclosing function that needed `async` (11 files, then 9 more nested inside them, iterated to clean).
    **(2) Making a function async is only safe where the RETURN VALUE is discarded.** Every call site was audited: all are event handlers (`onClick`/`onChange`/`onKeyDown`), whose returned promise React ignores. The ONE function that returns a value — `lib/condition-override.askOverride`, which hands back the override PATCH fields — is now `async` with all 5 call sites awaited by hand. **Check this before making any further handler async.**
    **(3) `e.target` after an `await` is safe here ONLY because this is React 18** (event pooling was removed in 17). On an older React the pooled event would be recycled by then.
    **(4) THE TRAP THAT ALMOST SHIPPED:** 7 files already imported `showMessage` from dialog.js, so an "add the import if absent" step skipped them — leaving `askConfirm` undeclared. esbuild emits an undeclared identifier VERBATIM, so the build was green and it would have thrown `ReferenceError` on click, which the ErrorBoundary turns into the full-screen "Something went wrong". This is the exact class the "a green build does NOT mean the page renders" rule warns about; it was caught by a guard, not by the build.
    Three PERMANENT guards in `test-app-dialog-pure.mjs` section C2 keep all of it closed: no `window.confirm(` outside dialog.js, **every `askConfirm(` is preceded by `await`**, and every file that calls it imports it. The await guard was proven to bite by removing one `await`. **When you add a confirm, write `await askConfirm(...)` and make the handler `async` — the guard will fail the build otherwise.** **`window.prompt` IS MIGRATED TOO — all 48 sites (owner-directed 2026-08-09: "do the prompt one too"), completing the set.** `askPrompt(message, {defaultValue, placeholder, multiline, title, confirmLabel, cancelLabel})` renders a real text field on the same card, and the same three guards cover it (no `window.prompt(` outside dialog.js, every call awaited, every caller imports it).
    **THE RETURN CONTRACT IS `window.prompt`'s, EXACTLY, AND IT IS TWO-VALUED — a STRING (possibly `''`) when they typed and submitted, `null` when they CANCELLED.** That is not pedantry: `EncompassSyncPanel.decideException` returns SILENTLY on `null` and shows *"Add a short reason to grant an exception"* on `''`. Collapse the two and you scold somebody for backing out; collapse the other way and a deliberate empty answer is dropped. So Cancel, Escape AND a backdrop click all answer `null`, the Save button answers the text, and a test pins all four paths — the collapse was mutation-tested (`|| ''` on the fallback) and the guard fails. The `defaultValue` is re-seeded per request id so a queued second prompt never inherits the first one's typing.
    Same async discipline as the confirm: only 5 functions needed `async` (most call sites already were), each audited as an event handler whose return value is discarded. The field is `font-size:16px` deliberately — **iOS Safari zooms the whole page on focus of any control under 16px**, which on a phone throws the dialog off screen — and its colours are explicit darks per the hard rule. One test had over-specified the SOURCE SHAPE of a function this made async (`test-esign-encompass-blocker.js` asserting `const reissueSend = \(e\) =>`); its stated subject is that Re-issue has its own send path, so the regex now tolerates `async` rather than the code being bent to fit it.
  · **The colours are explicit darks** (`#141B22` / `#3A4550` / `#4B585C`) per the hard rule — an `--ink*` token is a LIGHT paper colour in this palette and renders white-on-white. The body is `white-space:pre-wrap` because these messages carry real line breaks (a sign-off refusal is two paragraphs) and `overflow-wrap:anywhere` so a long filename cannot push the card sideways on a phone.
  · Tests: `scripts/test-app-dialog-pure.mjs` (in `npm test`) — the fallback with no host, the host path never touching the browser, the promise settling only when answered, a second message QUEUEING rather than overwriting an unread one, and a **source sweep asserting no portal screen raises a browser `alert()` any more** (one missed call site reproduces the bug on that screen; the guard was proven to bite by putting one back). Note the sweep matches `alert(` with NO space before the paren — a call never has one, ordinary English does, and the first cut flagged the label "No-draw alert (days)". Rendering was verified in headless Chromium against the REAL built stylesheet at desktop and iPhone-12 widths (dark text on an opaque card, line breaks kept, no sideways scroll) — a class collision only shows up with the real CSS applied. The CONFIRM variant (a second button, which the alert variant never exercised) was verified the same way. **NOTE the harness must carry `<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width">` exactly as `portal/index.html` does, or the phone render lays out at 980px and the `innerWidth === 390` check is meaningless.** **When you need to tell the user something, call `showMessage` — never `alert`; to ask a yes/no, `await askConfirm` — never `window.confirm`; to ask for a note, `await askPrompt` — never `window.prompt`. All three guards fail the build otherwise.**
- **A CLICKUP "CLEAR TO CLOSE" IS CONFIRMED ONLY WHEN THE FILE IS ACTUALLY READY — the confirm consults the SAME gate the portal door does (owner-directed 2026-08-06: "it's possible for a file to go to Final CTC. I saw a file that was CTC without having a Final Executed Term Sheet Package, which means a few conditions were still open … even if it's getting CTC in ClickUp, this should go to manual review and not really be CTC till the file is really ready for CTC. This should be a gate. But afterwards, if it's coming up closed or funded in ClickUp, it should get updated in our system as well. It should not gate the further steps. It should only gate the actual clear to close.").**
  · **ROOT CAUSE — the hold asked the wrong question.** The 2026-07-27 confirm-gate already HELD every pre-CTC → CTC inbound move and parked a review, so the owner's file did go to a human. But `inbound-ctc-confirm.confirmCtc` then wrote `status='clear_to_close'` STRAIGHT to the row — it never called `advancementBlockers`. So the entire readiness apparatus the portal status door enforces (every open required condition, plus the REAL executed-term-sheet-package gate of 2026-08-01, `esign/ctc-gate.js`) was bypassed the moment the move originated in ClickUp. The reviewer was asked "does ClickUp mean this?" and never "is this file finished?", with NOTHING on the review row telling them the term sheet had never been executed. **THE CLASS: a confirm-a-status-change review is not a readiness check. Whenever a second door can reach a gated state, that door must consult the SAME gate — a hold that only proves intent proves nothing about the file.**
  · **ONE definition, never a second one.** `ctcReadiness(appId)` calls the portal door's OWN `advancementBlockers(appId,'clear_to_close')` (lazily required — staff.js is a route module that requires half this library back) and filters the SAME `AI_BLOCKER_SOURCES`, so "is this file ready?" has one answer in this codebase and the two doors can never drift; a parity test reads the set out of `staff.js` and pins them together. AI findings stay ADVISORY per the HARD RULE.
  · **REFUSED, NOT SILENT, AND NEVER A DEAD END.** A confirm on an unfinished file throws a 409 `ctc_not_ready` whose message NAMES what is outstanding (the gates first — the executed package is the one nobody can clear by ticking a box), the review row STAYS OPEN, and nothing is written. The readiness is also snapshotted onto the review when the move is held, so the card itself says what is missing instead of sending someone hunting — decorative only, because `confirmCtc` re-reads it LIVE at decision time (the whole point is that a file gets finished between the hold and the confirm). An ADMIN can still advance it with `confirm_ctc_override` — the same recorded escape hatch every other gate on this file has: it captures the outstanding list BEFORE the write into the `clickup_pull_ctc_confirmed` audit row, so the file answers "what was skipped?" years later. "Real gate" means nobody reaches CTC by accident, not that a loan with a genuine reason can never move.
  · **FAILS CLOSED, RETRYABLY.** An unreadable readiness check refuses with a 503 rather than advancing — an unreadable file is not evidence that it is finished — and because this is a deliberate human click (unlike the sync, where refusing would break the pull) nothing is permanently blocked: the next click re-asks.
  · **FUNDED / CLOSED IS DELIBERATELY NOT GATED.** `shouldHoldCtc` fires only on the move TO Clear to Close, so an inbound `funded` applies untouched — including on a file PILOT is holding pre-CTC, which then moves straight to funded exactly as ClickUp says. Do not extend the gate to the later statuses.
  · Same pass: this door now records real **stage history** (`stage-history.record`, source `clickup`, carrying `forced`). It never had, so a file that reached Clear to Close from ClickUp had no row saying when, and every "days in stage" answer was computed over the portal-driven minority of moves. Test `scripts/test-inbound-ctc-confirm.js` (pure — the staff route is stubbed in the require cache and the row reads/writes go through the `client` parameter, so the whole confirm path runs with no database): the AI filter, the summary wording, the refusal, force-without-authority, the admin override + its audit, the ready case, fail-closed, and funded never being gated. Every gate assertion was proven to FAIL with the refusal mutated out.
- **SUPER-ADMIN CONDITION OVERRIDE — every condition has a way through, and it is never silent (owner-directed 2026-07-27: "add an admin override for each and every condition… if we're unable to clear it the admin should be able to overwrite and clear the condition without a document attached to it or without fulfilling the requirement of that condition. Only super admin").** The fulfillment gates (`routes/staff.js signOffGate`) are UNCHANGED and still apply to everyone: the ordinary Sign off / Waive buttons refuse an unfulfilled condition for a super-admin exactly as they do for a processor, so the 2026-07-20 "major fatal" accident (signing off the government-ID condition with nothing uploaded) is still impossible **by accident**. What is new is a SEPARATE, EXPLICIT action beside the refusal. `src/lib/conditions/admin-override.js` is the ONE rule — pure, never throws — and every surface that can complete a condition routes through its `evaluate(actor, body)`: **(a)** the caller must ASK (`adminOverride: true`; never inferred from the role — holding super-admin clears nothing); **(b)** the actor must be `super_admin` (a hard-coded role, deliberately NOT a grantable capability — do not convert it to one without owner direction); **(c)** a REASON is required; **(d)** it must accompany a real completion. All THREE completion doors are covered — `signedOff`, `waived` (so a REQUIRED condition can be waived without first editing the file's requirements) and the status dropdown's `satisfied` — because covering two of three would leave a silent bypass through the third. **The gate still RUNS under an override; it just stops blocking** — its refusal text is stamped on the condition as `override_blocked_reason`, so the file itself answers "what was missing when this was cleared?" years later. `db/344` adds `override_by/at/reason/blocked_reason` to `checklist_items` and `override_by/at/reason` to `conditions`; the stamps are CLEARED on un-sign / un-waive / push-back (a reopened condition is not "cleared by override", and a later ordinary sign-off must not inherit an old reason). Every override writes an `admin_override_condition` audit row AND lands in the policy-exception register as the new record-only type **`condition_override`** (`loanExceptions.recordConditionOverride`, born approved, mirroring `issuance_override`) — which is what puts it on the Exceptions screen, in the EX-n export and in the decision certificate's `policy_exceptions` block for free. Both writes are best-effort AFTER the sign-off (they may never reverse or 500 the human action). Client half: `app-v2/src/lib/condition-override.js` (one wording for the warning, the reason prompt and the display) — the override is offered **at the moment of refusal** on both the file's conditions list and the personal task queue, plus an up-front "Override" button for super-admins on every condition row, and an overridden condition SAYS SO on its face (badge + gold line naming who/when/why/what-was-missing). Tests `scripts/test-condition-admin-override-db.js` (in `npm test`; the pure rule + every refusal path + all three doors + the stamps, audit, register row, undo and reopen) and the untouched `test-condition-signoff-gate.js`, which now doubles as the proof that the gates did not move. When adding a new condition surface or a new completion path, route it through `admin-override.evaluate` — never write a second bypass, and never let one clear a condition without recording it.
- **A CONDITION IS NAMED FOR THE THING IT WAITS ON — and when that thing comes from the INVESTOR, the label says so (owner-directed 2026-07-29: "Final review requested / CTC — Clear to Close received … this is stuff that needs to come from the investor").** The two Phase-5 clear-to-close gates were named after OUR half of the step, so both read as done the moment we acted: "Final review requested" describes clicking send (the file's actual hold-up is the investor's answer coming back), and "CTC — Clear to Close received" led with an abbreviation, spelled that same abbreviation out, restated the status in the label ("received"), and never said who ISSUES a Clear to Close — which is the entire point of the row. `db/367` renames them to **"Investor final review"** and **"Investor Clear to Close (CTC)"**, both leading with "Investor" so the two steps that wait on the same outside party read as a pair in the Closing group, and moves the instruction into the HINT where every other condition here keeps one ("Clear this when their approval comes back — not when we send it"; "The Clear to Close is issued BY the investor … PILOT never issues one"). WORDING ONLY — the codes `rtl_f_review`/`rtl_f_ctc`, `audience='staff'`, `is_gate`/`is_milestone`, phase and sort order are untouched, because `advancementBlockers`, `condition-subjects.js` and the blueprint are all keyed on the CODE. PREVIOUS AND FUTURE: `checklist_items.label`/`.hint` are COPIED from the template at creation, so the migration updates both tables, each statement guarded on the exact old text (a hand-edited row survives every boot). Same rule as db/363 — the row is the SLOT, its state lives in the status column — and the same rule to apply to the next one: name the condition after what is awaited and who it comes from, never after the action we already took. Test `scripts/test-investor-condition-wording-db.js` (in `npm test`; the LIVE template rows, an existing file re-worded on the next boot, a hand-edited label left alone, idempotency, and the gate flags proven unmoved).
- **EVERY GROUND-UP CONSTRUCTION FILE CARRIES THE FEASIBILITY REPORT AND THE GC INFORMATION, IN THE CONSTRUCTION SECTION, ON PREVIOUS FILES TOO (owner-directed 2026-08-20; db/601).** The owner: *"Under the construction budget section for every project that is a ground-up construction, also go back to previous projects. For future projects, update the condition list for ground-up construction projects under the same section where you have the scope of work. Add two more conditions into that section … Feasibility Report (If Ground Up Construction) and another condition GC Information (If Ground Up Construction)."*
  · **THE SECTION IS THE `construction` SUBJECT GROUP** (`app-v2/src/lib/condition-subjects.js`) — the one that already holds the construction / rehab budget (`rtl_p1_budget`), the Scope of Work (`scope_of_work` / `rtl_p3_sow1`) and plans & permits (`rtl_p1_plans`). Both codes are mapped into it in the same change, or `test-condition-subjects-db.js` fails and they land in the catch-all "Other" bucket.
  · **THE FEASIBILITY REPORT IS NOT DUPLICATED — `rtl_cond_feasibility` (db/285) ALREADY EXISTED and was simply never applied.** It was `auto_apply='manual'`, created only so the investor-guideline desk could raise a coverage-gap fatal and offer a human an "attach condition" button, so on a ground-up file it was never on the list until somebody clicked. db/601 switches it to `rules`. A second "feasibility" template would split the ISG crosswalk (`bluelake-rtl-spec` cond 200 → `pilot_template_code: 'rtl_cond_feasibility'`) from the condition staff actually see — **the exact defect db/285 was written to fix.** HEAVY REHAB IS UNCHANGED: the desk still raises its gap there and a human still attaches it by hand (`origin_kind='manual_library'`), and the engine only ever retracts what IT created and nobody has touched (`inst.origin_kind === 'auto'`), so a hand-attached instance is safe. The label is widened to name the owner's condition first while still saying heavy rehab out loud, so a heavy-rehab attachment still reads true.
  · **`rtl_cond_gc_info` IS NEW** — no template existed in any spelling. `audience='both'`: the general contractor is the BORROWER's, so the borrower is who can answer, and underwriting is who reads it. It carries real borrower wording deliberately — a borrower-visible template with a blank `borrower_label` is silently applied STAFF-ONLY by the engine's #78 phantom guard, so the borrower would never see it.
  · **ONE DEFINITION OF "GROUND-UP", AND THE BACKFILL IS WHERE THAT RULE IS EASIEST TO BREAK.** The rule tree is declared ONCE in db/601 (`ground_up_rule`) and both templates get that exact value; it is the Condition Center's own vocabulary (`program_strategy` OR `rehab_type` = `ground_up`), which reads the same three columns `generateChecklist` already reads for the ground-up plans & permits placeholder. **"Also go back to previous projects" is done by the ENGINE, not by an `INSERT` in the migration** — `engine.backfillGroundUpConstructionConditionsOnce()` runs `evaluateAllOpen` once at boot (marker-guarded in `data_migrations`, silent, bounded, fire-and-forget). A hand-written `WHERE … ~* 'ground'` in the migration would be a SECOND definition of ground-up, free to drift from the rule the day either normalizer changes. **There is deliberately no ground-up predicate in SQL anywhere in db/601 — do not add one.**
  · **THE BACKFILL IS SILENT (`notify: false`) ON PURPOSE.** It is history being filled in, not news, and a boot task must never fan a borrower notification out across every existing build at once; a file opened from now on notifies normally through the engine's ordinary path.
  · **ORDERING: db/285 re-asserts its OWN definition — including `auto_apply='manual'` and the old label — UNCONDITIONALLY on every boot.** db/601 is numbered above it so it replays LAST and is the final word each boot (the db/475-over-db/398 mechanism). The `IS DISTINCT FROM` guards are therefore load-bearing, not decoration: they are what makes that re-assert a single cheap UPDATE per boot instead of a rewrite of a row nobody changed. **Do not "simplify" them away, and do not delete db/285** — it is the file that created the template.
  · **BOTH FILE WITH THE SCOPE OF WORK, not in "Other Documents".** `tpr-export.CODE_CATEGORY` gains both codes (the SharePoint mirror shares that categorizer, so the investor package and the team site agree for free). The keyword fallback deliberately does NOT match "feasibility" or "general contractor" — words that mean other things elsewhere on a loan file — so without the code map every one of these documents would land in the catch-all folder on every ground-up file.
  · Test `scripts/test-ground-up-construction-conditions-db.js` (in `npm test`, real Postgres): the live templates, the shared rule proven VALID against the registry and proven to read a ground-up file from either column, the engine attaching both and reaching no other file, retraction being untouched-only with the hand-attached ISG instance surviving, the backfill reaching a previous file silently and running once, the Construction group, the Scope-of-Work folder, the borrower-safe wording, and db/601 converging after db/285 replays. **Six mutations of the production code were each proven to fail it.**
- **FREE AND CLEAR IS OFFERED ON THE PAYOFF CONDITION, AND ONE LIST DECIDES WHAT IT ANSWERS (owner-directed 2026-08-24).** *"There are a few conditions: asking for a payoff, asking to verify the payoff, asking maybe for a VOM. Those payoff conditions are about the old mortgage. If you mark over there in that condition, there is already logic to mark the property free and clear. In that condition, you should be able to attach that logic and mark over there that the property is free and clear, and that should waive the payoff condition, the verified payoff condition, and the VOM condition."*
  · **THE LOGIC EXISTED; THE PLACE DID NOT.** The confirmation, the waiver of BOTH payoff conditions, the $0 payoff of record and the reversal have all worked since db/575 — so the owner's first two conditions were already answered. What was missing was the DOOR: the control lived in the Payoff section and, inline, on the STAFF `cond_payoff_internal` row. The condition a processor is actually looking at when they discover there is no mortgage is the BORROWER-facing `cond_payoff_external` ("Current mortgage / payoff statement"), and that row offered nothing at all. `app-v2/src/components/FreeAndClearControl.jsx` is the action lifted out of `PayoffCard` so a second surface is a MOUNT rather than a copy — two copies of a confirm dialog is how one screen ends up promising something the other does not do. It renders `compact` on the condition (that row is about the borrower sending a statement, so it gets the one action that answers it, not the whole section) and the section notice everywhere else; `CONFIRM_ON`/`CONFIRM_OFF` are the one wording, guarded.
  · **`payoff.FREE_AND_CLEAR_WAIVES` IS THE ONE LIST.** The route named the set THREE times — waive, un-waive, strip the stale note — and the failure of a fourth hand-typed copy is silent and ASYMMETRIC: a code added to the waive list but not the un-waive list can never be reopened, so turning free-and-clear back off would leave it waived on a file that genuinely has a mortgage to pay off.
  · **THE GUARD IS DERIVED, NOT RESTATED — and that is what makes a VOM safe to add later.** A condition belongs in that list for exactly one reason: its own rule says it should not be on a free-and-clear file. So the test checks the list against THE DATABASE'S OWN RULES (`rule_logic` carrying `property_free_and_clear` + `is_false`) rather than against a copy of itself — retyping the two codes in a test would prove only that the test agrees with itself. Give a new condition the ordinary free-and-clear exclusion and the suite fails until it is in the list. `cond_cashout_letter` is refinance-gated and deliberately EXCLUDED: it asks why the borrower is taking cash out, a question about THIS loan, not about an old mortgage.
  · **THERE IS NO VOM CONDITION ON THE SHORT-TERM SIDE — checked, not assumed** (the template library, the rule-driven refinance set, and every hand-typed condition on file: none). It exists only in the LONG-TERM Encompass condition library, a different product that may not be copied across without written authorisation in `docs/LONG-TERM-AUTHORIZED-COPIES.md`. Whether RTL should ask for one at all is a BUSINESS RULE and therefore the owner's to state — never guessed. Recorded as a fact by the suite so it stays true or says so.
  · Tests `scripts/test-free-and-clear-condition-db.js` (17, in `npm test`) alongside the untouched `test-payoff-free-and-clear-db.js` (29, which already proves the end-to-end waive / reverse / note behaviour); five mutations — dropping a gated condition from the list, adding a stray one, reverting the un-waive statement to a hand-typed list, removing the control from the condition, and copying the wording into a second file — were each proven to fail it.
- **A REFINANCE IS SIZED ON THE AS-IS VALUE, AND CARRIES NO PURCHASE PRICE (owner-directed 2026-08-02).** The owner, after a cash-out file turned up with "purchase-style economics — a purchase price and an as-is value shown while both original purchase price and payoff are missing": *"Once a file is refinance then instead of asking for the purchase price just ask him right away for the as is value because that's what it counts for, it doesn't count for the purchase price … we need to build up all the pricing all the tools to understand that as is value is the one that sets how much initial he can get instead of looking on the purchase price … then you can add some extra fields later on what was the original purchase price of the property and when exactly it was purchased so we can count seasoning."*
  1. **THE FROZEN ENGINES ALREADY AGREED — not one number moved.** `standard-program.js` sizes the acquisition (initial) advance off `acqDenom = purchase ? min(pp, aiv) : aiv` and the cost basis off `(purchase ? pp : aiv) + rehab` (later `acqDenom + rehab` — see the 2026-08-05 total-cost note above); `gold-standard.js` reads the as-is value for its heavy-rehab basis, budget basis and exit test the same way. NO frozen file was touched. What was wrong sat entirely ABOVE them, and there were four separate holes: the forms asked a refinancing borrower for a purchase price, the row stored one, the completeness panel demanded one, and `src/lib/pricing.js buildInputs` quietly passed that stored price to the engine AS the as-is value whenever the as-is box was blank (`num(app.as_is_value) || totalPrice`). Proven by RUNTIME EQUIVALENCE over **324,000** old-vs-new scenarios (4 purposes × 4 programs × 5 states × prices × as-is values × ARVs × rehabs × FICOs × experience × assignment): **zero** purchase input differences, **zero** purchase priced-quote differences across standard/gold/silver, and zero refinance differences outside the four documented keys.
  2. **ONE PREDICATE, DELIBERATELY THE ENGINE'S OWN.** `src/lib/deal-basis.js` `sizesOnAsIsValue(loanType)` is byte-for-byte the substring test `pricing.js loanTypeOf` uses to pick the engine's denominator, so "which number does this screen ask for?" and "which number does the loan size on?" have ONE answer. `app-v2/src/lib/dealBasis.js` mirrors it for the portal (the `lib/payoff.js` arrangement, and pinned the same way). `payoff.isRefinance` answers the NEARBY question — is there a loan to retire — and stays the richer reading; `scripts/test-refi-basis-pure.js` runs the portal enum, the ClickUp crosswalk values and the legacy spellings through all of them and fails the moment they disagree. **Delayed Purchase Financing is a PURCHASE** here, exactly as `normLoanPurpose` and the engine already treat it.
  3. **`buildInputs` on a refinance:** `asIsValue` no longer falls back to the purchase price (that fallback is right on a PURCHASE — "worth what I'm paying for it" — and meaningless on a refinance), `purchasePrice` becomes the as-is value (parity with `termsheet.js`'s `purchasePrice: isRefi() ? num("asIs") : effPurchase()` and the public loan application, which have always sent it that way — the SERVER was the odd one out), and the new `asIsMissing` flag says plainly when a refinance has no denominator at all. The invariant is RE-ASSERTED after overrides, because `loanType` and `purchasePrice` are both overridable keys. All three register doors (staff register, borrower register, accept-counter) refuse `asIsMissing` with a message naming the field; the studio already refused it client-side (`missingFields`).
  4. **A REFINANCE NEVER STORES A PURCHASE PRICE, AT ANY DOOR.** The chokepoint is `fields.assignmentFields` — the same helper that has forced the assignment flag off on a refinance since #96, now with `loanType` passed by EVERY caller (staff create, borrower create, borrower draft-submit, public intake, MISMO import). On top of it: `PATCH /applications/:id/details` (clears a price sent on a refinance — one-sided ON PURPOSE, see 6), both completeness panels, the Condition Center's info-field writer (`engine.writeFieldValue`, same parity rule it already applies for freezes), the appraisal reprice door (`REPRICE_COLS` still lists the column; the door refuses it on a refinance), and the ClickUp INBOUND pull (`cols.purchase_price = null` → COALESCE keeps the file's, so it can only decline to write, never clear).
  5. **PREVIOUS AND FUTURE — `db/399`.** For every refinance on disk: the implicit as-is value is MATERIALIZED (`as_is_value = purchase_price` where the as-is was blank — byte-for-byte the number `buildInputs` was already feeding the engine, so no loan changes size and no dead-end file is left behind), then the leftover `purchase_price` is CLEARED, with the prior value recorded on the file's own audit trail (`refi_purchase_price_cleared`) first. It is deliberately NOT moved into `original_purchase_price`: nobody can know whether that figure is what the borrower paid years ago, a mis-entered value or a stale draft, and writing a guess into a field the team reads as fact (it feeds seasoning) is worse than leaving it blank for a human to answer. **USER TRIGGERS ARE DISABLED for the duration** — nine triggers on `applications` watch these columns and would flag every touched registration stale, un-sign live term sheets and reopen cleared conditions across the whole back book for a change that moves no number the engine ever read. `migrate-boot.js` runs each file as ONE implicit transaction, so a failure rolls the DISABLE back with it. Idempotent.
  6. **THE PURCHASE SIDE IS DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE, and that is a bug fix, not an omission.** The first cut also nulled the refinance-only columns on a PURCHASE, which broke `payoffContactLockReason`'s carve-out: a request naming ONLY who is paid off and their loan number — the one edit allowed through a sent term sheet, and the entire reason that carve-out exists — had both values silently nulled on the way to the UPDATE, so it answered 200 and wrote nothing (caught by `test-termsheet-freeze.js`). The details door also only clears a price the REQUEST ITSELF SENT, never one the row merely holds: clearing on every save would touch the column (tripping db/072's pricing reopen) on saves that changed nothing, and would drag a payoff-contact-only request out of its freeze carve-out. `EditFileDetails` already clears the payoff trio when the purpose is switched, and a payoff contact somebody recorded is never something to wipe because of a dropdown.
  7. **SEASONING IS A FIRST-CLASS SIGNAL.** `original_purchase_price` + `acquisition_date` (db/032, previously collected on the public form and then THROWN AWAY by `intake.js`, and never accepted at all by the staff new-file door) now persist on every door, and `deal-basis.seasoningMonths` is the ONE definition of how long a property has been held — calendar-string arithmetic only, and **null rather than a guess** when there is no date, it cannot be read, or it is in the future. Exposed as the read-only rule field `ownership_seasoning_months` (Condition Center), as a fact on the whole-loan underwriting context, and on the staff + borrower file screens. It prices nothing.
  8. **THE SURFACES.** Marketing site (`web/v2/tools/loan-application.html` — it already asked for all three refinance fields and sent NONE of them; the as-is value is now marked required on a refinance, and `data-cond` toggles use `$$` not `$` so every marked node responds). Borrower portal (`Apply.jsx` clears the other side's economics on a purpose switch; `Application.jsx` snapshot, completeness and Property & transaction). Staff portal (`StaffNewFile.jsx` gained the whole refinance branch it never had, `EditFileDetails.jsx`, the completeness panel, `DealSnapshot.jsx` — whose fallback ≈LTC denominator was also wrong on a refinance — `ProductStudioPanel`, `ClosingPanel`, `ChangeRequestPanel`). V1 (`app/`, `web/tools`) is frozen at `/v1` and untouched; the server chokepoints cover it. Tests `scripts/test-refi-basis-pure.js` + `scripts/test-refi-basis-db.js` (both in `npm test`). When you add a surface that asks for, shows or stores a deal's headline figure, route it through `deal-basis`/`dealBasis` — never re-inline a `/refi/` test, and never ask a refinance for a purchase price.
- **THE As-Is VALUE IS READ OFF THE APPRAISAL — XML first, then the strongest OCR + AI, and a confident reading BELOW the purchase price LOWERS the file (owner-directed 2026-07-28).** The owner: *"OCR should click Control F on the PDF appraisal and look for the As is value, and if he's confident that he found that as is value and the as is value is less than the final purchase price he should overwrite the as is value in file and post the condition that he reduced the asset's value based on his OCR reading … if you can't read it you should write on the note that you can't confidently read the AS IS and a human should read the AS IS and enter the as is value to clear the condition."* Four pieces, in one place each:
  1. **The reading is a LADDER, cheapest-and-most-certain first — `src/lib/appraisal/as-is-reader.js` `readAsIs` (PURE, every IO dependency injected, never throws).** (a) the MISMO **data file**, when `extract()` already resolved a DEFINITE As-Is — measured at **21 / 33** real appraisals, so the owner's *"a lot of times the XML is perfect"* is right about two thirds of the time (the full measurement, the per-vendor attribute list and the decoys are in `docs/appraisal-xml/as-is-value-sources.md`); (b) the report **PDF read by the strongest OCR available** — `ai/ocr-router` (Azure Document Intelligence → Google Document AI → Mistral), with the legacy OCR.space reader as the last resort (it caps at ~1 MB, so on a real 5–30 MB appraisal it usually could not even try — which is why the old advisory so often came back empty) — then **Ctrl-F'd deterministically**: line by line AND over every adjacent LINE PAIR joined, because an OCR page/column break routinely lands between `As-Is Value:` and `$312,500`; (c) the **AI as a LOCATOR, never a source of truth** — it must return a VERBATIM quote, and its answer survives only when that quote is genuinely in the OCR text **and** our own scanner independently re-reads the same amount out of it. A hallucinated number is structurally impossible, and the ARV can never sneak in (an "as-is" at or above the ARV is dropped as the ARV misread). An XML **estimate** is still never promoted — `extract.js`'s `// NEVER estimate-store` stands, and the 7 / 33 comp-cluster-only files go to OCR, not to a cluster average.
  2. **THE WRITE RULE IS CONFIDENCE, AND ONLY CONFIDENCE (owner-directed 2026-07-28, correcting the first cut: *"as long as you're confident you can write it no matter what it was — I just made a mistake when I said that only if it's a reduction … and if you're not confident you should always ask in the condition for the loan officer to look on the appraisal and enter it"*).** `decideAsIsApply` (PURE) writes a confident reading whether it LOWERS or RAISES the file's As-Is, and whether or not it lands below the purchase price — the appraisal is the authority on what the property is worth today, and this is exactly the "replace" a human was already doing by hand on the `asis_mismatch` finding. Below-the-purchase-price is REPORTED (`belowPrice`, which the wording and the existing fatal `asis_below_price` finding turn on), never a gate. What still stops a write is only about TRUST in the number or about who may write at all, never about direction: **confidence** (anything under `high` goes to a human); the file **not being frozen** (term-sheet-sent / clear-to-close / funded — `structuralLockReason` is called with NO actor, so PILOT can never inherit a super-admin's unlock); and it being a **real change** (`same_value` is skipped so the reprice trigger is not churned). The UPDATE is pinned to the value read a moment earlier, so a concurrent change is never clobbered. **A RAISE can never quietly increase a loan** — any change to `as_is_value` reopens Products & Pricing (db/071/072), so the loan amount does not move until a human re-registers the product; the condition note says exactly that. Because a confident read is now written in EITHER direction, PRECISION carries the safety, so three guards sit under "confident" beyond the ARV ceiling and the $10k–$100M bounds: (a) a line that is not about OUR subject's value is dropped WHOLE (`NOT_OPINION` — a comparable's sale price, an asking/listing price, a tax assessment, a rent, an insurance replacement cost — any of which would put a stranger's number on the loan); (b) a LABELLED hit must read as a statement of VALUE (`VALUE_WORD` — value/opinion/apprais*/market/worth/estimat/indicat), so a terse `as-is: $430,000` surfaces only as a weak candidate for a human; (c) **the ten-fold digit slip** (`scaleSlip`) — `$430,000` OCR'd as `$43,000` is plausible, below the ARV and properly labelled, so every other check passes it; a candidate landing within 1% of the ARV, the purchase price or the file's own As-Is after ×10/×100/÷10/÷100 is treated as a misread and can never be confident. Kill switch `APPRAISAL_ASIS_AUTO_ENABLED` stops the WRITE only — the reading still runs and still surfaces on the condition.
  2b. **THE GUARDS ARE THE FEATURE — every one of these was a real way a wrong number reached the loan, found by adversarial audit (2026-07-28).** Do not relax one without replacing it. **(a) `"As-is" Value of Site Improvements` is a PRE-PRINTED line on Fannie Mae Forms 1004/1025/1073/2055** — filled on essentially every appraisal with a cost approach, labelled "Value", plausible, below the ARV — and on a plain 1004 it is often the ONLY as-is-labelled money line in the report, because the real opinion of value reads "my opinion of the market value … is $430,000" with no "as is" on the line at all. Unguarded, a $15,000 driveway becomes the property's As-Is; `NOT_OPINION` drops it along with depreciation / cost-new / reproduction / land / site value. **(b) A per-unit RATE on a 1025** ("As-is value per unit: $150,000" on a 4-family) is a quarter of the property and passes plausibility, the ARV ceiling and the digit-slip check — `PER_UNIT_RATE` drops it. **(c) A STACKED LABEL BLOCK is not a wrap**: layout OCR of a two-column value box emits `As Repaired Value / As Is Value / $450,000 / $312,500`, and the line-PAIR pass would hand the As-Is label the ARV's amount — caught only by the ARV ceiling, which is NULL on every straight purchase. When the PREVIOUS line is a rival as-is/after-repair label carrying no money, the pairing is unknowable and nothing is claimed (a section HEADING is not a rival label, so the genuine page-break wrap still works). **(d) The AI must ground on a LABELLED re-read, never a `near` one** — "The subject sold as is on 05/2019 for $215,000" is a real sentence the model will happily return, and it is a PRIOR SALE PRICE; both grounding gates would pass it if `near` counted. **(e) The APPROACH DECOYS** (cost / income / site / sales) are vetoed on an after-repair-basis report — the income approach can sit BELOW the ARV, so the ceiling misses it — and never on an as-is-basis or condo report, where the As-Is legitimately equals them. **(f) A FLOOR of 15%** of the biggest number the file already trusts: no residential as-is is under a sixth of its ARV / purchase price / current value, and a gut rehab at 25–40% is never vetoed. **(g) The ARV CEILING falls back to `appraised_value`** because a CONDO (1073) normally has no ARV at all, which would silently disarm the single most effective guard. **(h) The DIGIT SLIP** (`scaleSlip`) earns its keep where ceiling and floor cannot reach — a doubled zero on a file with no ARV. **(i) ABSTAIN** when the read itself failed: "40 characters of noise came back" and "the report states no As-Is" are different messages (re-scan vs type it in), calibrated like `ocr-router`'s own `primaryLooksEmpty`. **(j) A HUMAN'S DECISION IS FINAL** — a value someone typed on the condition (`as_is_confirmed_*`, while the file still shows it) or an `asis_mismatch` finding they resolved with keep/custom is NEVER re-litigated; without this the "Read the appraisal again" button sitting one control from the box would undo them on the next click, and the boot sweep would undo the whole book. A DISMISS is deliberately not in that list: it sets a finding aside, it does not choose a value. **(k) THE APPRAISAL MUST BE ABOUT THIS PROPERTY** — an open FATAL `address_mismatch` / `units_mismatch` / `property_type_mismatch` stops the write; we may be holding someone else's report. **(l) THE FREEZE CHECK FAILS CLOSED** — `structuralLockReason` swallows errors and returns null (right for a human's edit, exactly wrong for an unattended writer, where a statement timeout would read a funded file as unlocked), so the two halves are composed directly off `file-lock._internals`. **(m) The AI reads WINDOWS, not the head** — the analyzer truncates head-first and the As-Is, when it is only a note, lives in the ADDENDUM at the END; `aiExcerpt` sends ±1,500 characters around every as-is/reconciliation/addendum anchor. **(n) A failed read is RETRYABLE and is NOT stamped** — stamping it would drain the file out of the sweep forever on one OCR outage. **(o) The applied stamp is never DOWNGRADED** — a second read returns `same_value`, and blindly writing `applied=false` would erase the record `undoAppraisalImport` needs to take a PDF-read value back off the loan. **(p) THE UNDO REVERSES WHAT PILOT RECORDED WRITING, NOT WHAT MERELY MATCHES.** `undoAppraisalImport` used to blank `as_is_value`/`arv` by RULE — "the file equals the appraisal, and the import only ever fills a blank, so the previous value was NULL". The day the desk started making the file AGREE with the appraisal, "equal" became the NORMAL state of a healthy file, so that rule deleted As-Is and ARV values officers had typed by hand, on the very condition that asked them to type one. The undo now restores `as_is_file_value_before` / `arv_file_value_before` and only where the file still shows exactly `as_is_applied_value` / `arv_applied_value` — a value PILOT did not write is not PILOT's to remove. Appraisals imported before that record existed get it from **db/356**, ONE-SHOT (guarded by a `sync_runtime_state` marker) and only where the old rule would have fired, so the back book behaves exactly as before; re-running it later would stamp a HUMAN's number as PILOT's and hand the undo permission to delete it, which is why it can never run twice.
  2c. **THE ARV IS WRITTEN TOO, AND AN ARV MAY NEVER BECOME THE As-Is (owner-directed 2026-07-28).** Two halves of one instruction. **(A) The ARV is rewritten from the appraisal on every import** — *"we already have logic and XML of the appraisal finding that reads the ARV value that I'm much more highly confident about, and we don't even need OCR for it."* Right: a MISMO appraisal has ONE structured `PropertyAppraisedValueAmount`, and on a subject-to report that figure IS the after-repair value; `extract.js` already resolves it `definite`, and it was recovered from **33 / 33** files in the corpus. So `decideArvApply` has NO ladder, no OCR and no AI — a `definite` XML ARV is written, anything else is left alone (`not_confident`).
