Same guards as the As-Is and none about direction: not frozen, no human decision (`arv_mismatch` resolved keep/custom — tracked SEPARATELY from the As-Is, because deciding one value says nothing about the other), the appraisal must match the property, a real change, and **the ARV must be ABOVE the As-Is** (`not_above_as_is` — at or below means the two values are swapped or one is misread). It rides the SAME condition (one condition beats two) and the same reprice reopen. **(B) `aboveArvOk` — the owner's own logic: *"if you can't find another ARV value that is higher than the value you think the as is value is, then probably your value that you found IS the ARV."*** On a RENOVATION deal there are two values and the after-repair one is always larger, so a candidate As-Is is only believable when an ARV can be pointed at ABOVE it (the appraisal's, else the file's). With none anywhere, the number in hand is very likely the ARV wearing the wrong label — reported, never written. "Renovation deal" is read from the signals the desk already has: the MISMO basis enum (`SubjectTo*`), an ARV on the appraisal, an ARV on the file, or a construction budget. A STRAIGHT as-is purchase has no ARV by definition and the rule correctly does not apply to it. This sits ON TOP of the existing ARV ceiling, the EXPLICIT_BASIS guard on the headline value, and the ARV_LINE/ARV_LABEL exclusions — four independent ways the after-repair value is kept out of the As-Is, which is the point.
  2d. **GOING FORWARD ONLY (owner-directed 2026-07-28: *"Only make this going forward"*) — a DELIBERATE, owner-granted exception to the standing "previous AND future" rule.** The reason is specific to this feature: the sweep WRITES loan values, so re-reading the back book would rewrite numbers on files people have already worked, all at once, unwatched. New appraisal imports are read; previously-imported appraisals are left alone. `desk.backfillAsIsReadsOnce` still exists and is still correct, but it is **not called at boot** and `APPRAISAL_ASIS_SWEEP_FILES` defaults to **0** — set it and invoke the function by hand for a deliberate, bounded pass. Do NOT "restore" the boot call to satisfy the previous-AND-future rule; it was removed on purpose.
  3. **ONE condition carries both outcomes, and it only exists after an appraisal does.** `appraisal_as_is_verify` (db/137, relabelled "Confirm the As-Is value on the appraisal" by **db/353**) is attached ONLY by the appraisal desk after a successful import — which is the owner's *"this condition should only be relevant after the appraisal has been uploaded"* — and is removed by `undoAppraisalImport`. Its `[auto]`-guarded note says either *"PILOT LOWERED/RAISED the As-Is from X to Y … please re-review; if PILOT read it wrong, type the correct value here"* or *"PILOT could not confidently read the As-Is — nothing has been filled in; read it off the report and enter it here"*. It is raised in EVERY state except the settled one — PILOT confident AND the file already showing exactly that value (the appraisal and the loan file agreeing, confirmed from two sides) — which is the owner's *"if you're not confident you should ALWAYS ask"* and also keeps a clean file's conditions list quiet. The **internal field the owner asked for** is `CondAsIsEntry` in `app-v2 StaffApplication.jsx`, fed by `GET /api/appraisal/:id/as-is`: what came in, from WHERE (data file / OCR engine / AI), the exact WORDS it was read from, the file's value before and now, the purchase price, why nothing was changed when it wasn't — plus a box to overwrite it (`POST …/as-is`, `sign_off_conditions`, audited, freeze-respecting) and a "Read the appraisal again" action. The condition is `audience='staff'` — none of this is ever borrower-facing. `signOffGate` refuses to clear it while the file has NO As-Is value: *"read it off the report and type it in the box"* — the owner's clearing rule. A fresh reduction REOPENS a signed-off instance, so a sign-off can never stand on a value that has since moved.
  4. **Previous AND future.** The reading is recorded on the `appraisals` row (`as_is_read_*` / `as_is_applied*` / `as_is_confirmed_*`, db/353), and `desk.backfillAsIsReadsOnce` sweeps appraisals imported before this existed — a FREE tier (the data file already states it: no OCR, no AI, no storage read; `APPRAISAL_ASIS_SWEEP_FILES`, default 25/boot) then a hard-bounded PAID tier (`APPRAISAL_ASIS_BACKFILL_FILES`, default 5/boot). Stamping `as_is_read_at` drains each row, so it self-terminates. Scoped to EARLY-STAGE files only (`file_intake`…`underwriting`): on an `approved` / `clear_to_close` file the write would be refused by the freeze anyway, but the CONDITION would still attach — a fresh required uncleared item lands in `advancementBlockers` and would block clear-to-close and funding on files that were ready to fund, for a reading nobody asked for. The GPT locator obeys the per-file `costMeter` cap; over the cap the deterministic Ctrl-F still runs. Tests `scripts/test-appraisal-as-is-reader.js` (pure — the ARV can never be returned, the wrap case, every hallucination gate, the whole write truth-table) and `scripts/test-appraisal-as-is-db.js` (real HTTP + real Postgres — the write, the reprice reopen, the audit, the condition wording, the sign-off gate, the human overwrite, the undo revert, the sweep), both in `npm test`. When adding another way to establish an As-Is, route it through `readAsIs`/`decideAsIsApply` — never a second write path, and never an automatic RAISE.
- **APPRAISAL FINDINGS ARE ENFORCED, AND THEY LIVE ON THE APPRAISAL TAB (owner-directed 2026-07-30 — NARROWS the 2026-07-27 advisory-only HARD RULE below for the APPRAISAL review ONLY; do not "fix" this back to advisory).** The owner: "all the findings that you find from the XML appraisal should be on the appraisal findings page and that should be enforced … you cannot clear this condition, you cannot get a CTC till you clear the appraisal findings … till you reviewed and confirmed and fulfilled all the appraisal findings, until human entered/confirmed the as-is value." Document-review findings STAY advisory (the document-review desk is on hold — "unsuccessful"); everything below applies to the `appraisal_findings` desk only.
  1. **The findings moved BACK to the Appraisal tab.** The 2026-07-20 fold-in (appraisal findings rendered + resolved inside the Document-review section) is REVERTED: `AppraisalPanel.jsx` renders the full findings list with resolve actions for staff (borrower read-only view unchanged), `UnderwritingPanel.jsx` no longer fetches or renders appraisal findings (the `AppraisalFinding` re-export is gone — do not re-add a fold-in), and the split is by TABLE: everything in `appraisal_findings` renders on the Appraisal tab, nothing from `document_findings` does.
  2. **`appraisal_review_cleared` is a REAL gate again** (`advisory-policy.appraisalReviewEnforced()` — default ON, kill switch `APPRAISAL_FINDINGS_ENFORCE=0`; independent of `AI_FINDINGS_ENFORCE`). `signOffGate` refuses it while (a) any open fatal `blocks_ctc` appraisal finding exists, (b) the `appraisal_as_is_verify` condition is still open, or (c) the file has no `as_is_value` — the owner's "human confirmed the As-Is" prerequisite. `advancementBlockers` returns it under `conditions` (section `sec-appraisal`), so no clear-to-close until it is signed off; `underwriting_review_cleared` stays in `advisories`. `db/375` re-arms db/155's reopen-on-fatal trigger (a fresh fatal finding un-signs a cleared review; NOTE db/334 no-ops that trigger every boot and 375 re-creates it AFTER — keep 375 numbered above 334) + attach/reopen backfills for previous files (scoped to pre-clear_to_close; cleared/funded files were cleared under the rule of the day). The db/154 satisfied-GUARD trigger stays retired ON PURPOSE — it cannot see the actor and would refuse the super-admin condition override (db/344), which remains the deliberate way through.
  3. **EMCAP's extra appraisal requirements are CODED, deterministic, and are FINDINGS — never auto-posted conditions** (`src/lib/appraisal/note-buyer-checks.js`; owner: "you don't even need AI for this … don't be stupid in opening conditions"). On an EMCAP file (`isEmcapNoteBuyer`) with a current appraisal, `syncNoteBuyerFindings` writes `appraisal_findings` rows with `source='note_buyer'`, codes `emcap_*`: an ANCHOR As-Is comp AND an anchor ARV comp (each a settled sale within 12 months of the loan SUBMISSION date — `COALESCE(submitted_at, clickup_created_at, created_at)` — under 15% net adjustment, in the subject's ZIP; grid = `appraisal_comparables.comp_set`), a rental analysis on a rental-exit loan (fix & hold OR DSCR; a 1025 satisfies it, a 1004 needs a 1007 — evidenced by `est_market_monthly_rent`, per-unit rents, a rent-schedule FORM name, or a MULTIFAMILY_RENTAL grid, all read into the fields jsonb `report.*` keys by extract.js), interior photos (metadata-evidenced; ALWAYS a warning — heuristic), and the appraisal in OUR name (only when a lender is actually NAMED — never an always-on nag; a transfer letter does not cure it for EMCAP, so the all-buyers `isg_transferred_appraisal_letter` excludes emcap, and `isg_emcap_missing_1007` was REMOVED from the ISG table — the desk owns it now). NEVER-FABRICATE: a provable "no" is fatal; missing data (no comps readable, split undetermined, no submission date, no photo metadata) is a verify-by-hand WARNING. Sync runs on import (`desk.runAppraisalImport`, awaited), on note-buyer change (staff `completeFields` + ClickUp ingest), and a boot backfill (`desk.backfillNoteBuyerFindingsOnce`); it retires the set when the buyer moves off EMCAP and honors `finding_decisions` (a dismissed finding never comes back). STAFF-ONLY as a class: `borrower.js` hides `source='note_buyer'` findings from the borrower appraisal view (their titles name a note buyer) while still counting a hidden fatal in the neutral under-review placeholder. Tests `scripts/test-appraisal-note-buyer-checks.js` (pure) + `scripts/test-appraisal-enforce-db.js` (the whole chain), both in `npm test`; `test-appraisal-advisory-db.js` now pins the REOPEN behavior and `test-underwriting-route-db.js` pins "the two actor-blind guard triggers stay retired, the reopen trigger is re-armed". When adding another note buyer's appraisal requirements, extend `note-buyer-checks.js` with a new compute + the same sync channel — never a second write path, and never a condition.
  4. **THREE THINGS THE PRE-MERGE AUDIT CAUGHT, each now pinned by a regression test — do not undo them.** (a) **The sync's skip set is every NON-SUPERSEDED row of the code on the CURRENT appraisal, not just the open ones.** Skipping only OPEN rows meant a dismissed finding was invisible to the next sync, which inserted ANOTHER `carried_forward` row — and the ClickUp ingest calls the sync on every re-ingest of the card, so one dismissal grew a row per webhook, forever (reproduced: 1 dismissal + 3 syncs = 4 rows). A re-import supersedes the whole set and mints a fresh `appraisal_id`, so a genuinely new appraisal still re-raises everything. (b) **db/375 §3 skips `override_at IS NOT NULL`.** The boot pass re-runs every deploy, so without that guard it silently un-signed a super-admin override — the one recorded way through this gate — every night, forever. The LIVE trigger deliberately still reopens an overridden row: a fresh fatal finding is NEW information the override never saw; a boot pass re-litigating an old decision is not. Both the trigger and §3 also clear the waive stamps (a reopened row must never read "waived" + "received"), and the trigger clears the override stamps per db/344 semantics. (c) **A plain WAIVE of `appraisal_review_cleared` runs the gate.** "Make it optional, then waive" is a legitimate move on ordinary conditions and would have cleared this one with open fatals and no recorded override — the exact bypass the enforcement forbids — because db/154's satisfied-guard is retired. The waive door now consults `signOffGate` for this ONE code regardless of `is_required`. (d) **The FIRST half of that same manoeuvre — just flipping it to optional — is closed too** (re-audit finding #1): `advancementBlockers` exempts `appraisal_review_cleared` from its `is_required = true` filter while enforced (a `requiredExemptCodes` param), so an optional row still blocks clear-to-close; and the `is_required=false` toggle is refused at the write door for this code (super-admin override still the recorded way through). Pinned by `test-appraisal-enforce-db.js` F4. The skip-set OPEN arm is keyed on the FILE (finding #2 — the cross-door race), and `PATCH /details` + `completeFields` re-sync the EMCAP checks on a `program`/`loan_type`/`rehab_type` change, not only `lender` (finding #3). Also from the audit: the trigger's `[auto]` note text is its OWN (db/334's tail clears the exact db/155 string every boot and would have blanked the reviewer's explanation), `PATCH /applications/:id/details` re-syncs on a `lender` write like the other two doors, and the boot backfill pre-filters EMCAP in SQL so a real EMCAP file can't be starved out of the window by files that will never gain a row.
  5. **THE SPLIT IS BY SUBJECT, NOT BY TABLE — and EVERY appraisal finding must be answered, not only the fatal ones (owner-reported 2026-08-02: "make sure that the appraisal findings page has ALL the appraisal findings and not some of the appraisal findings is going over to the document findings section"; "appraisal findings should need to be resolved before you clear the appraisal review condition").** The 2026-07-30 rule split the two desks BY TABLE, which was necessary and not sufficient: several desks that write to the DOCUMENT side also READ the appraisal, so findings whose only evidence is the appraisal report kept landing on the document-review desk. **`src/lib/appraisal/finding-subject.js` is now THE definition** (pure, never throws) and both routes consult that ONE predicate, so the two screens can never disagree about where a finding lives.
     · **THE BIG ONE WAS THE TIE-OUT DUPLICATING THE APPRAISAL DESK, FACT FOR FACT.** `tieout.js` carries the appraisal as a comparison source, and its `PERDOC_COVERS` suppression list ("a dedicated per-document check already raises this one") had an entry for the contract, the ID, title, bank statements, the SOW and the payoff — and none for the appraisal, even though `lib/appraisal/findings.js` compares exactly those facts appraisal-vs-file. So an appraisal whose ARV disagreed with the file produced TWO cards in two places (`arv_mismatch` on the Appraisal tab, `tieout_arv` "After-repair value doesn't match the file — Appraisal" in Document review) and resolving one left the other standing. `APPRAISAL_DESK_FACTS` (property_address, purchase_price, as_is_value, arv, units, property_type) is that missing entry — one list, kept in step with `computeFindings()`; every entry must name a real finding there. The MATRIX still shows every appraisal cell, exactly as it does for the contract; only the duplicate FINDING is suppressed. NOT on the list, on purpose: `seller_name` (appraisal owner-of-record vs the CONTRACT's seller is a cross-document reconciliation — chain-of-title's job) and year_built / living_area / market_rent (the file holds no value, so the tie-out only ever fires them document-vs-document).
     · **SUPPRESSING THE DUPLICATE FINDING NEVER REMOVES THE APPRAISAL FROM THE DATA COMPARISON — they are two different things (owner-directed 2026-08-02: "the data-comparison table should still show the data comparison of the appraisal even if the flag is already raised on the appraisal findings screen … the file ARV and the appraisal ARV, the file as-is and the appraisal as-is, the property type, the unit count, the address and everything").** A FINDING is the flag raised once, to be answered once; the COMPARISON is the standing side-by-side an underwriter reads, and it must state a fact whether or not a finding was ever raised on it and whether or not that finding has been answered. The document desk's tie-out MATRIX always kept every appraisal cell (`PERDOC_COVERS` gates only the discrepancy, never the row) — and `routes/appraisal.js appraisalComparison(appId)` now ALSO lifts that column onto the Appraisal page itself, next to the report it describes, as `comparison` on `GET /api/appraisal/:id` → `DataComparison` in `AppraisalPanel.jsx`. It REUSES `tieoutForFile` — the same engine the matrix renders — so the two screens can never state different things about one fact; every fact the appraisal can carry is listed INCLUDING the ones that agree ("these match" is exactly what a reviewer is looking for), a fact the appraisal cannot carry (`status:'na'`) is left out, and it is STAFF-ONLY (the borrower's property report shows the appraisal, never our internal reconciliation). Best-effort — a failure returns null and the tab still renders. Pinned by section F of `test-appraisal-finding-home-db.js`, which sets the appraisal at odds with the file on ARV / as-is / units / property type / address — every one of them a suppressed finding — and asserts all five are still COMPARED, with both numbers.
     · **THE OTHER PRODUCERS** are the AVM-consensus check (`document_findings` source `avm_consensus` — a judgement about the appraised value, rendering on the one desk an appraisal reviewer would not look at) and any tie-out row whose ONLY disagreeing document is the appraisal. A conflict the appraisal shares with ANOTHER document deliberately STAYS on the document desk — that is a cross-document reconciliation, and moving it would hide half the disagreement from whoever is working the other document. `tieout.js` now stamps `docType` on each `sources[]` entry (the human `label` is display text and must never be matched on); a legacy row with no `docType` fails toward the document desk — showing a finding in its old home is cosmetic, dropping it from both is not.
     · **WHERE IT SHOWS AND HOW IT RESOLVES.** `GET /api/underwriting/:id` splits them off after triage (so `openAll`, the counts, the risk roll-up and every per-section `live()` array exclude them) and returns them as `appraisalFindings`, which the desk renders as a one-line pointer — nothing is hidden. `GET /api/appraisal/:id` rebuilds the same set (`deskAppraisalFindings`: the stored appraisal-source rows, plus `tieoutForFile` filtered through the shared predicate and the durable `finding_decisions` ledger) and merges it into `findings`. A stored row is RESOLVABLE from the Appraisal page: `POST /api/appraisal/:id/findings/:fid/resolve` falls through to `document_findings` (scoped to the appraisal sources only) and goes through the document desk's OWN `store.resolveFinding` — same validation, same durable ledger, same AI-mirror close, same tiered `exceptions.canApply` authority. A DERIVED tie-out row has no row to resolve, so it renders read-only with a plain "this clears itself" note.
     · **THE GATE COUNTS EVERY OPEN APPRAISAL FINDING, ACROSS BOTH TABLES — and DERIVED rows never gate.** `signOffGate`'s `isAppraisalReview` branch used to count only `severity='fatal' AND blocks_ctc`, while the owner's direction (2026-07-30 AND again 2026-08-02) was "reviewed and confirmed and fulfilled ALL the appraisal findings". It now refuses while ANY open row exists in `appraisal_findings` OR in `document_findings` for an appraisal source; a fatal still gets the dealbreaker wording FIRST. Every finding has a one-click answer (Replace / Keep file value / **Mark reviewed** / Dismiss — `acknowledge` was added to the card for the non-value findings), and the super-admin override (db/344) is still the recorded way through. A derived advisory is deliberately EXCLUDED from the count: it has no button, and gating on one would be a dead end. `appraisal-advisory.appraisalCompleteness` mirrors the widened gate 1:1 (`openFindings`), per its own file header — a badge that says "ready" on a file the gate refuses is the one thing it must never do. **`appraisalTermSheetHold` stays FATAL-ONLY** — the 2026-07-31 term-sheet rule was explicitly about the fatals, and holding a term sheet on a comp-distance warning would be wrong. Kill switch: the same `APPRAISAL_FINDINGS_ENFORCE=0`. Tests `scripts/test-appraisal-finding-home.js` (pure — the predicate's truth table + the tie-out proven both directions, so an over-suppressing regression is caught too) and `scripts/test-appraisal-finding-home-db.js` (real Postgres — routing, resolution, the widened gate, the derived-never-gates rule, the kill switch), both in `npm test`. When you add a desk that reads the appraisal, teach `finding-subject` about it — never a second hand-written suppression.
  6. **THE MAIN PICTURE OF THE HOUSE IS A PHOTOGRAPH, NEVER THE APPRAISER'S SIGNATURE (owner-reported 2026-08-02: "sometimes for the main picture of the house it comes up the appraiser's signature … look on the way the system reads the XML, find the bug").** **THE OWNER IS RIGHT THAT THE PHOTOS COME FROM THE XML, AND HERE IS THE MECHANISM** (asked again 2026-08-02: "I'm sure that the XML does carry some photos because even when we don't upload the PDF you still get some photos … do further digging on the XML structure"): a MISMO 2.6 appraisal XML carries the ENTIRE report PDF inside itself, base64, as `<EMBEDDED_FILE _Type="PDF"><DOCUMENT>…`, and `xml.embeddedPdfBase64()` lifts it out — so an XML-only import has ALWAYS produced photographs, and nothing was ever read off an uploaded PDF that could not be read from the XML alone. What the XML does NOT carry, on the measured corpus, is per-photo PIXELS: `<IMAGE>` elements are METADATA-ONLY placeholders and ~60% of files carry none at all (`docs/appraisal-xml/photos-comps-variation.md`). So the photos come from `photos.js` reading the embedded PDF, and the bug was there: candidates were filtered on SIZE alone (`MIN_W`/`MIN_H` 200×150, whose comment claimed it dropped "signature marks") and stored in raw PAGE order, with the hero taken as `photos[0]`. A scanned signature on the URAR CERTIFICATION page is routinely 400×200 or larger AND sits on page 6 — BEFORE the subject photo page — so it was the first image found and became the face of the property report.
     · **`classifyImage(imageStats(px,w,h,ch))` decides what an image IS, from its own pixels** (pure, exported, unit-tested). A page of ink on paper has two signatures a photograph never has — it is overwhelmingly WHITE with almost nothing in the midtones (paper + strokes, no shading), and it carries almost no COLOUR — and both must agree before an image is demoted, plus two decisive shape/flatness cases (a ≥4:1 strip is a banner or a signature line; ≤4 of 32 luminance bands is artwork). Deliberately conservative in ONE direction: a misjudged photograph is only moved to the END of the gallery, never dropped, while a misjudged signature becomes the property's main picture. The test fixtures all carry sensor grain, so the intended rules — not a "perfectly flat" shortcut — are what pass them, and they include the hard cases that must NOT be demoted: an overcast exterior, a white house under a blown-out sky, a dim interior.
     · **ORDER IS THE CONTRACT.** `extractPhotos` returns photographs first (page order), then the form's graphics (page order), assigning `seq` over that final order — so photo #1 is the subject front shot. Separate caps (`MAX_PHOTOS` 24 photographs + `MAX_GRAPHICS` 6) mean the form's own artwork can never crowd real photographs out of the stored set, which the old single first-come cap allowed because the form comes first in the PDF. `desk.js` stores the verdict in `appraisal_photos.category` ('photo'|'graphic'), and `AppraisalPanel` picks the hero as the first non-graphic as belt-and-suspenders.
     · **WHAT THE XML *DOES* GIVE US IS THE APPRAISER'S OWN LABELS — `src/lib/appraisal/photo-meta.js`** (pure, regex over the raw XML because a `<DOCUMENT>` blob is multi-megabyte and `xml.parse()` deliberately drops text). `photoSlots(xml)` reads the ordered slot list — `FORM/@AppraisalReportContentType` (SubjectPhotos | SalePhotos | RentalPhotos | LocationMap | Sketch | Exhibit | CoverPage) first, then `IMAGE/@_Identifier` (`SubjectFront` | `SubjectRear` | `ComparablePhoto1` | `Photo3` | …), skipping `_Name="NoImage"` (an empty slot) and `_Name="AppraisalForm"` (the PDF wrapper) — and `labelPhotos()` names the PDF-mined photographs from it. **STRICT BY DESIGN: the labels apply ONLY when the slot count equals the photograph count**, so position means the same thing on both sides; a mismatch (no metadata, a de-duplicated repeat, an extra image in the PDF) labels NOTHING rather than mislabelling, because a wrong label on the property's main picture is the bug this exists to fix. When they DO line up, the hero is the appraiser's own `SubjectFront`, not a guess. A slot the XML calls a sketch/map/exhibit is demoted to a graphic even when the pixels read as photographic — the appraiser's label beats our inference when we have it. `embeddedImages(xml)` additionally reads per-photo PIXELS when a vendor ships them (an `<EMBEDDED_FILE>` whose type is an image rather than the PDF, with its label attached, MIME confirmed by MAGIC BYTES); the measured corpus has none, but it is 4 vendors and the field has more, so a vendor we have not seen stops being a blank gallery. When XML images ARE present they ARE the gallery and the PDF is not mined — mining it would re-add the same shots unlabelled with no way to tell them apart. `desk.extractAndStorePhotos(…, xml)` takes the XML on EVERY path (import, the "Pull photos" button, both boot backfills — `xmlForAppraisal` recovers it), and `AppraisalPanel` mirrors `photo-meta.heroIndex`: subject-front, then any subject photo, then the first photograph, never a graphic.
     · **PREVIOUS AND FUTURE.** A gallery stored before this was ordered wrong ON THE WAY IN, so no front-end logic can fix it: `desk.backfillAppraisalPhotoKindsOnce` (booted in `server.js`) re-pulls and re-stores any current appraisal whose photo rows carry NO category. Self-draining (a re-pull always writes one), bounded per boot, and a file whose PDF can no longer be recovered is stamped `'unclassified'` so it is not re-decoded every boot. Test `scripts/test-appraisal-finding-home.js` (section C).
  7. **THE APPRAISAL'S PROPERTY TYPE IS A CATEGORY, AND AN ATTACHMENT STYLE IS NOT ONE (owner-reported 2026-08-02, quoting the comparison row verbatim: "Property type | Detached | Multi 2–4" — "you're taking the property type from the wrong place … we're not interested in the detached or if it's attached. We need you to find if on the appraisal, if it's a single family, or if it's a 2–4, or if it's a condo, or any other property type … we have this issue with appraisal findings, with document findings, and with data validation data compare"). db/405.**
     · **ROOT CAUSE — ONE WRONG ATTRIBUTE, FOUR SURFACES.** `extract.js` read the subject's property type from `PROPERTY/STRUCTURE/@AttachmentType`, whose ENTIRE controlled value list is `Detached` / `Attached`. That is a statement about whether the building touches its neighbour; it says nothing about how many dwellings it holds or what kind of ownership it is — a detached building is as easily a duplex as a single-family house. That one value was stored as `appraisals.property_type` and from there fed **the Appraisal tab**, **`file-review.js` → the tie-out matrix AND the appraisal-tab Data comparison** (the owner's screenshot), and **`facts.js` DOC_CLAIMS.appraisal → the document-review desk**. The 2026-07-27 patch made a bare style UNCOMPARABLE (`canonPropertyType` → null), which stopped the false mismatch and left the real problem standing: the appraisal column printed a word that is not an answer to the question the row asks.
     · **THE CATEGORY WAS ALWAYS IN THE FILE.** `src/lib/appraisal/property-category.js` `derivePropertyCategory` (PURE) reads it from the appraisal's own evidence, in the portal's own vocabulary: **the FORM** — 1004 → one-unit, 1025 → 2–4, 1073 → condo, an appraiser cannot report a duplex on a 1004 — REUSING `property-type.appraisalFormExpectation` rather than restating it, so "what does a 1025 prove" has ONE answer in this codebase; **`STRUCTURE/@LivingUnitCount`**, the authoritative dwelling count (≥5 → Multi 5+, 2–4 → Multi 2–4); and **the ownership signals** that separate the one-dwelling kinds — `PropertyCategoryType`, the UAD `GSE_PUDIndicator`, the `PROJECT` block, `_DesignDescription`. NEVER GUESSES: the style decides nothing (it is carried through as its own fact, never allowed to become a category); no unit count and no recognizable form → `null` (uncomparable), never the common bucket; **a condo is claimed only from the 1073 or the appraiser's own category words — never from an HOA fee or a project name, because a PUD carries both**; and when the form and the unit count disagree the form wins the band but `conflict` is set and the confidence drops to `likely`, matching what `appraisal-underwriter.appraisal_form_units_mismatch` already puts in front of a human.
     · **ONE CHOKEPOINT.** `extract.js` writes the derived label into `subject.propertyType` (so every existing consumer is fixed at once) and keeps the style in `subject.attachmentType`; `import.js` stores `property_type` = the category label, `property_category` = the canonical key, `attachment_type` = the style. **PREVIOUS FILES**: `src/lib/appraisal/property-category-heal.js` at boot re-reads the category from what each row already holds and moves the style to its own column — **JavaScript, not a migration**, because a PL/pgSQL twin of a derivation that weighs form + units + ownership together would drift from the live one (the `pilot_term_norm` / `pilot_property_type_norm` class). Bounded, self-draining (an unreadable row is stamped `unreadable`, the `unclassified` pattern), pinned to the values it read, never throws, and the style is never destroyed. On top of that **every READ surface re-derives live** (`property-category.applyPropertyType` / `categoryLabelFor` in `routes/appraisal.js`, `routes/borrower.js` and `file-review.js`), so the fix is right on the first deploy rather than after the sweep drains.
     · **THE DOCUMENT DESK NEVER CLAIMS A STYLE.** `facts.js` drops a bare-style property-type claim at the claim (`categoryOnly` → `isAttachmentStyle`, the SAME definition the appraisal derivation uses, so the two desks can never disagree about which words are styles), which reads as "this document didn't state it" — the truth. An AI-read appraisal PDF that genuinely says "Single Family" / "Condo" still ties out normally.
     · **THE APPRAISAL FINDING NAMES A REAL CATEGORY ON BOTH SIDES.** `property_style_note` compared the FORM CODE, so it showed the reviewer "FNM1004" as the appraisal's property type and could only ever see two forms — silent on a townhouse, a PUD or a 5+. It now compares the derived category to `property-type.propertyTypeKey(file.property_type)`, covering every category. **PUD and Townhouse are ONE bucket on purpose** — `facts.canonPropertyType` already collapses them, and splitting them here would make the Appraisal tab disagree with the data comparison about the same two words. The unit-range FATAL (`property_type_mismatch`) is untouched and still owns the count-vs-category disagreement. `property_type` is still NEVER written back from a finding (keep/dismiss only) — the reason is no longer "the appraisal has no valid category" but the durable one: it is a PRICING INPUT (db/071/072 reopen Products & Pricing on any change) whose one validated door is the application form. A previously-dismissed `property_style_note` re-surfaces ONCE with corrected wording, because the decision ledger keys on the value text — accepted deliberately: the old card stated a form code.
     · The sibling-scanning `stAttr` read exists because Appraise-It splits the subject improvements across three `<STRUCTURE>` siblings. It feeds the CATEGORY only and is deliberately NOT written back onto `subject.units`: that count feeds the CTC-blocking `units_mismatch` fatal, and on those vendors' files it currently reads blank — a real pre-existing gap, worth raising on its own rather than changing under a property-type fix. Test `scripts/test-appraisal-property-category.js` (in `npm test`; the style proving nothing, all three forms, every unit band, each one-dwelling kind, the never-guess battery, the conflict case, the portal-vocabulary contract, the claim drop, legacy/repaired/unreadable stored rows, and the finding stating both sides in plain words). When you add another way to read what a property IS, route it through `derivePropertyCategory` — never a second private table, and never the attachment style.
- **AI FINDINGS ARE ADVISORY ONLY, AND A HUMAN'S DECISION ON ONE IS DURABLE (owner-directed 2026-07-27; HARD RULE — the APPRAISAL review is the one owner-directed exception, see the 2026-07-30 rule above).** Two owner reports, one session. **(A) "It should really be advisory only."** PILOT still runs every check, still labels a fatal a fatal, and still shows everything on the desk — but NOTHING it finds may hold a human up: not signing off a condition, not clearing to close, not funding, not issuing/exporting a term sheet, tape, TPR or MISMO, and it must not even READ as "outstanding" before CTC. One switch: **`AI_FINDINGS_ENFORCE=1`** restores the old gating everywhere (`src/lib/underwriting/advisory-policy.js`, read at CALL time). What was retired: `signOffGate`'s `isAppraisalReview`/`isUnderwritingReview` refusals; the DB backstops **db/154** + **db/202** and the auto-reopen **db/155** (all three triggers dropped by **db/334**, function bodies kept as documented no-ops so re-running 154/155/202 re-arms them); `issuance-backstop.decideBackstop` never raises `hardWarning` (so CTC/funding/term-sheet/tape/TPR/MISMO/bulk-tape all proceed — and record NO override, or the exception register would fill with entries nobody chose to make; it reports `advisoryOnly`/`advisoryFatal` so a screen can still say PILOT is unhappy); and `advancementBlockers` now returns a THIRD array **`advisories`** — the underwriting dealbreaker, the fraud/authenticity signals AND the two PILOT-review conditions (`appraisal_review_cleared`, `underwriting_review_cleared`) live there, never in `conditions`, so `ready` and every status door ignore them (belt-and-suspenders: `AI_BLOCKER_SOURCES` filters them out of the enforceable set too). Leaving those two conditions in the blocking list would have moved the hold-up behind a proxy — the file would still read "not ready" because of the AI section. **(B) "I dismiss it / the super-admin says it's OK and a minute afterwards it populates again."** ROOT CAUSE was one class with three doors, each of which re-created the row the decision was written on: (1) `ai_suggestions.record()` deduped only against `status='open'`, so a DISMISSED row was invisible and a brand-new open row was inserted by the next sync — which runs on every file view; (2) `store.saveAnalysis` re-inserts every finding on each re-read, with no memory of the previous row's resolution — and the un-funded **re-read sweep** (db/332) re-reads the whole book on a schedule, which is literally the "a minute afterwards"; (3) the DERIVED desks (tie-out, chains, liquidity, experience, reasonability, note-buyer guidelines) have no row at all — they are pure functions of the file, so a Dismiss had nothing to attach to. THE FIX is the durable ledger **`finding_decisions`** (db/333) + `src/lib/underwriting/finding-decisions.js`, keyed on the finding's **IDENTITY** — `finding-claims.claimOf`, the same key the desk already uses to merge the six desks that notice one fact — so the decision outlives whichever row carries it. Wired at every door: `store.resolveFinding` records it (and `closeMirroredSuggestions` closes the AI-panel copy of the same finding in the same breath); `saveAnalysis` + `appraisal/import` CARRY IT FORWARD (the new row is still written for the audit trail, born `dismissed` with resolution `carried_forward`, so `summarize()`/the roll-up drop it); the file view filters `openRaw` AND every per-section array through it and returns the settled ones as `settledFindings`; `ai_suggestions.record` consults it AND refuses to re-raise a row a human decided; the escalation queue's **"No action needed"** now genuinely settles the finding (it used to close only the queue item), and `/escalations/:id/apply` records it for a DERIVED finding that has no row to close. HARD RULES: only SETTLING verbs suppress — `post_condition`/`request_document` must keep the finding live until the follow-up lands, and `answered` is the super-admin replying to a question, not a judgement; scope is per-FILE (portfolio-wide muting stays `ai_silenced_codes`, db/253); `reopen()` un-suppresses so a dismissal is never a one-way door; every ledger read/write runs inside a SAVEPOINT and fails OPEN (it may show a finding again, never hide one, and can never poison the caller's transaction). Tests `scripts/test-finding-decisions-pure.js`, `test-finding-decisions-db.js` (each case proven to FAIL on the pre-fix code) and `test-ai-advisory-only-db.js` (the blockage sweep + the switch-back), all in `npm test`. When adding a new finding producer or a new surface that raises one, consult the ledger — never add a second suppression mechanism.
- **"Findings to review" is a DECISION desk, not an inbox — every action, and it clears the FINDING (owner-directed 2026-07-26).** The escalation queue (`StaffFindingEscalations.jsx`, the AI Command Center's "Findings to review" tab + Main nav) showed the reviewer one real button, "Mark resolved" — which closed the QUEUE ITEM and left the finding sitting open on the loan file. The action row it *did* have only rendered when the escalation's stored `suggested_actions` SNAPSHOT happened to be non-empty, and it fired a two-call client sequence that 400'd on every action needing a note. Four linked fixes; never regress any:
  1. **The menu is computed, never snapshotted.** `escalations.withEffectivePage` decorates every listed row with `availableActions` from the SAME `underwriterActions()` catalog the file's finding card uses (`routes/underwriting.js` `decorate`), fed the LIVE finding's verbs/severity via new `finding_*` aliases on `LIST_SELECT`, falling back to the snapshot for a DERIVED finding. So a legacy row with a NULL snapshot still offers post-condition / request-document / fix-the-file / grant-exception / clear / dismiss / decline / severity — exactly what the desk offers. Never hard-code an action menu in a screen.
  2. **ONE door does the whole job.** `POST /api/underwriting/escalations/:id/apply` (gated `sign_off_conditions`) validates the action + its required note/value BEFORE writing anything, resolves the finding through the shared `applyFindingResolution()` — extracted so this door and `POST /:appId/findings/:fid/resolve` can never drift (same tiered `exceptions.canApply` authority, same `apply-fix` write-back, same audit) — then closes the queue item recording WHICH action was taken. It reports `findingResolved` / `reason` (`stays_open` | `derived` | `already_resolved`) so the screen never implies a finding was cleared when it wasn't. A derived advisory (no stored finding) still writes a `fix_file` correction onto the loan file — that is what makes the discrepancy go away. The old advice-only `…/decide` door stays, relabeled "Close with advice only".
  3. **A finding no longer waits on a super-admin.** `escalations.mayDecide({viewer,row,can,hasFileAccess})` is the ONE decide rule (route + UI both read it; the response carries per-row `canDecide` plus `canApplyActions`/`canWaive` so the screen never shows a button that would 403): a super-admin, the person it was routed to, **or any admin / processor / underwriter who signs off conditions on that file**. `listEscalations`/`pendingCount` take `anyOnFile` (true when the viewer holds `sign_off_conditions`) so a signer ON THE FILE sees escalations addressed to a super-admin — still strictly inside the per-file scope, so nothing leaks to a staffer who isn't on the file. The requester≠approver control is KEPT (you cannot decide one you raised; super-admin exempt) — do not remove it without separate owner direction. NOTE the params discipline in `viewerScope()`: the role placeholder is only added when the SQL references it (an unreferenced placeholder fails the bind — the same class as the borrower-view scope bug).
  4. **`processor` now holds `waive_conditions` by default** (`permissions.js` ROLE_DEFAULTS) so a processor handling a finding can FINISH it — including clearing a clear-to-close-blocking dealbreaker with a recorded reason — instead of being forced to escalate. Admins and underwriters already had it; loan coordinators / closers / loan officers still do NOT, and `exceptions.elevatedPermissionFor` is unchanged. An admin can revoke it per-person from the Team screen. Test `scripts/test-finding-escalation-actions-db.js` (real HTTP, in `npm test`).
- **AI Command Center — ONE admin hub for everything AI (owner-directed 2026-07-24; phase 1 shipped).** The admin's AI-training surfaces were 5 scattered nav links. `app-v2/src/screens/StaffAiCenter.jsx` is a thin TAB SHELL at `/internal/ai` (admin+) that EMBEDS the existing self-contained screens UNCHANGED — no rewrite: Overview (`StaffInsightsDashboard`), Findings to review (`StaffFindingEscalations`), Teach PILOT (`StaffTrainingProposals`), PILOT's questions (`StaffAiAdminInbox`, super), Label documents (`StaffLabelingConsole`, super), Muted alerts (`StaffAiSilencedCodes`, super). Tabs are role-gated (`allowed(min,role)`), read from `?tab=`, and each carries a beginner-friendly one-liner. The 5 old routes (`/internal/training|labeling|ai-inbox|ai-silenced-codes|insights`) now `<Navigate>`-redirect into the hub (`?tab=…`) so old emails/bookmarks keep working; `StaffLayout.jsx` collapses those nav links into ONE "AI Command Center" entry (badge = `fescCount`). "Findings to review" also stays in Main nav (it's a cross-role queue). **Fixed a live bug in the same pass:** Insights' deep-links used `/staff/applications/:id`, which `LegacyStaffRedirect` rewrites to the NON-EXISTENT `/internal/applications/:id` → Fallback (every "open file"/"Review AI →" link dumped the admin on the pipeline); corrected to `/internal/app/:id` (the real route). PHASE 2 SHIPPED — **grounded back-and-forth reasoning chat** (`AiReasoningChat.jsx`, mounted in the file's AI Findings panel `AISuggestionsSection`): a staff underwriter asks PILOT "why did you flag this?" / "what does the file say about X?" and gets an answer grounded ONLY on `loan-primer.groundingBlock(appId)` — route `POST /api/underwriting/:appId/reason` (staff-only; `azureOpenai.available()` env-gate, `costMeter.allowSpend` per-file cap, `groundingBlock` facts; NON-authoritative system prompt — "never invent a number", "you explain, the underwriter decides"; never throws → always a shaped `{available, answer, reason}`). Ephemeral (client holds the transcript, last-12-turn window sent back each turn; NO thread table). `api.aiReason(appId, question, history)`. Degrades to a plain "not turned on / hit the spend cap" note. PHASE 3 SHIPPED — plain-language shadow→production copy on the Teach-PILOT tab (`StaffTrainingProposals.jsx`): a "What these steps mean" explainer panel (Shadow test = "try it quietly on new files, nothing a borrower/officer sees changes"; Approve = "reviewed and good"; Promote = "turn it on for real"; Reject) for the non-developer owner; beginner-friendly button labels + per-button tooltips; and a `window.confirm` guard before the real "Turn on for real (promote)" action. Copy/UX only — no backend change. #247 is fully shipped across all 3 phases. When adding an AI-ops screen, add it as a TAB here (role-gated) rather than a new top-level nav link; rebuild `web/v2/portal` after touching it.
- **In-loan progress + collapsing long condition/appraisal surfaces (owner-directed, 2026-07-20).** Three V2 presentation changes to de-clutter the staff file view (and the borrower appraisal view):
  1. **Loan progress INSIDE the loan.** The stage progress ("where the loan is up to") was only on the pipeline (`StaffQueue`) and the borrower `StatusTimeline`. `app-v2/src/components/LoanProgress.jsx` (a compact horizontal stepper over the canonical status path `file_intake→…→funded`, terminal declined/withdrawn) now renders at the TOP of the staff File overview (`StaffApplication.jsx` `sec-overview`, above `DealSnapshot`). Pure — driven off `app.status`, no fetch. CSS `.loan-prog`/`.lp-*` in `styles.css`.
  2. **Conditions collapse to their header when done.** In `BorrowerConditions` (`StaffApplication.jsx`) a satisfied+signed-off condition (`it.status==='satisfied' || it.signed_off_at`) renders as a ONE-LINE header (wording + badge + Expand) in the "All conditions" list, and the **entity/vesting (LLC) condition auto-collapses once `app.entity_verified`** (its embedded `LlcManager` panel is huge). Per-row manual override via an `expandedConds` Set + `toggleCond(id)` (the LLC row uses synthetic id `'__llc'`); mirrors the existing `Item` collapse pattern. Default "mine" filter still HIDES done items entirely — collapse is what the "All conditions"/"Signed off" views show.
  3. **Appraisal report sections collapse.** `AppraisalPanel.jsx` gained `ApprSection` — a native `<details>` wrapper (auto-collapsed) around the long dossier/evidence sections (Property details, Valuation, Comparable sales, Prepared by, Original appraisal); the hero/value-story/findings/photos stay open. Same for staff and the borrower's read-only view. The full-report overlay (`.appr-print-root`) and `@media print` force every section OPEN (rules in `PRINT_CSS` + `styles.css`) so the printed/saved "Property profile report" PDF is complete. NOTE: the borrower already sees the scrubbed appraisal ("property profile report") via `Application.jsx` `sec-appraisal` → `<AppraisalPanel readOnly>`; the code-enforced borrower scrub (SCRUTINY_CODES findings, ARV-defensibility, ThreeCapPanel, capital-partner/lender/AMC names, source docs) is the real staff-only boundary and must stay — there is NO blanket "appraisal is staff-only" rule.
- **Multiple LOs + processors per file — assignees model (owner-directed, 2026-07-14; assistants get FULL access).** A file's team is `application_assignees` (`db/103`): one PRIMARY per role (`loan_officer`/`processor`) mirrored from the denormalized `applications.loan_officer_id`/`processor_id` pointer, plus full-access ASSISTANTS. The pointer stays the source of truth for the ClickUp/SharePoint integrations (they read the single officer — no change); a DB trigger `trg_sync_primary_assignee` keeps the primary assignee row in lock-step on EVERY write (staff assign, file create, ClickUp inbound), promoting-in-place so an assistant→primary swap doesn't collide with the partial-unique indexes. **The access chokepoint is `VISIBLE_OFFICERS_SQL(alias, p)` in `src/routes/staff.js`** — it now also matches `application_assignees` (active row), so every gate routed through it (the `/applications/:id` path middleware, `canTouchApp`, `canSeeBorrowerId` [SSN], `canSeeDocument` [downloads], the pipeline/search/borrowers/LLC scopes) recognizes an assistant at ONE definition. The chat modules (`chat.js` `staffCanAccess`, `staff-chat.js` lists) use the shared `assigneeExistsSql(alias, p)` exported from `src/lib/permissions.js` — never re-inline the scope SQL, always route through these two helpers (grep for a hand-written `loan_officer_id=$… OR processor_id=$…` before adding one). Because the backfill gives every existing file a primary assignee row, adding the assignee term is behavior-identical until an assistant is actually added. Only an ADMIN may change the PRIMARY; assistants are portal-only (never pushed to ClickUp). Reassigning the primary removes the demoted person (matches the old single-pointer semantics).
  - **The PROCESSOR syncs BIDIRECTIONALLY, but inbound requires BOTH ClickUp processor fields to AGREE (owner-directed 2026-07-19).** ClickUp carries the processor in TWO places: the **"Processor" people-field** (`F.PIPELINE.processor`, id `926bad3b…`, the human pick) and a **"Processor Email" text field** (`F.EXTRA.processorEmail`, id `4f7b2c03…`, filled by a ClickUp automation from the people-field). Root cause of "Lisa Katz (role `processor`, really the DRAW coordinator) keeps popping up as a processor nobody assigned": duplicating a task left the two fields DISAGREEING (people-field cleared, stale email still Lisa), and we trusted the email alone — re-asserting her on every pull. The rule (`src/clickup/ingest.js` `linkOrCreateApplication` → the pure, unit-tested `decideInboundProcessor(userId, emailId)`): **INBOUND** — the two fields are turned into `{adopt, conflict}`: **both set + SAME person → adopt**; **both set + DIFFERENT people → conflict → CLEAR** the stale portal processor (audited `clickup_processor_conflict_cleared`; the `db/103` trigger retires the assignee access row); **people-picker set + email empty → ADOPT** (owner-directed GRACE WINDOW, 2026-07-20: the email text field is filled a moment later by the ClickUp automation, so people-set/email-empty is a *fresh* assignment whose email is lagging — the old strict rule wrongly refused it and even cleared the portal during that window); **people empty + email set → adopt nobody, NEVER clear** (a stale email alone is the Lisa Katz duplicate artifact — never trust email alone, but never clear off it either); **both empty → keep the portal value** (no clobber on ClickUp silence). So a portal processor is only ever CLEARED when both ClickUp fields are set and name two different people (the genuine stale-duplicate signature). Tests: `scripts/test-clickup-processor.js` (in `npm test`). **OUTBOUND** — a Pilot processor pick writes BOTH ClickUp fields (`src/clickup/mapper.js` `buildTaskFields` + `resolveOnly` case `processor`; email from `orchestrator.js` `ctx.processorEmail`) so they always agree and the pick round-trips cleanly. The sync stays two-way — never sever it (that was tried 2026-07-19 as #330 and reverted). No code path may DEFAULT a processor onto a file. `db/129` cleans up the pre-existing erroneous Lisa pointers (clears where it points at Lisa UNLESS an admin genuinely picked her via `/assign`; audited + idempotent).
- **Economics change reopens P&P; budget change also reopens SOW (owner-directed, 2026-07-12):** a DB trigger on `applications` (`trg_reopen_on_budget_change`, defined in `db/071`, broadened in `db/072`) reopens conditions whenever the deal economics change from ANY side (borrower/staff application edit, ClickUp inbound sync, product re-register). The **`product_pricing`** condition reopens (→ `received`, sign-off cleared) on a change to **any** pricing input — `loan_amount`, `purchase_price`, `as_is_value`, `arv`, `rehab_budget`, `program`, `loan_type`, `property_type`, `units`, `requested_ir_months`, `is_assignment`/`underlying_contract_price`/`assignment_fee`, or `requested_exp_*` — because the registered structure was priced off stale inputs and must be re-registered. The **`rehab_budget`/Scope-of-Work** condition reopens (→ `issue`, sign-off cleared) **only** when `rehab_budget` itself changes (it's tied to the construction total). Both fire only on a real change (`IS DISTINCT FROM`) and only reopen conditions that had been cleared (no churn), with `[auto]` notes. A trigger is used so every write path is caught centrally (all sides).
- **A free-TEXT pricing input is compared by MEANING, never by spelling — and a value that isn't that kind of thing is REFUSED at the door (owner-reported 2026-07-24 `term`, 2026-07-26 `property_type`; HARD RULE for every future field of this shape).** Two reports, one root cause: `applications.term` and `applications.property_type` are TEXT, several doors write DIFFERENT spellings of the SAME value, and the reopen trigger compared the RAW text — so a formatting echo flagged the registration STALE and demanded a pointless re-register, forever ("Term: 12 → 12 Months", "Property type: FNM1025 → Multi 2–4").
  1. **One semantic key per free-text input, with a JS half and a SQL twin that MUST stay in sync.** `term` → `src/lib/term-text.js` `termKey()` + `pilot_term_norm()` (db/288). `property_type` → `src/lib/property-type.js` `propertyTypeCompareKey()` + `pilot_property_type_norm()` (db/322): "Multi 2-4" (the ClickUp dropdown) ≡ "Multi 2–4" (the portal forms + the `crosswalk.js` key) ≡ "multi_2_4" (the Condition Center enum). The DB test `scripts/test-property-type-stale-trigger-db.js` asserts the SQL and JS agree on every sample — a drift between them re-arms the whole class. An UNRECOGNIZED option (a brand-new ClickUp value) is never guessed into a category; it compares to itself, so it can neither false-fire nor be silently collapsed.
  2. **Every consumer of that input uses the shared key — never a raw compare.** Today: the reopen trigger (db/288 + db/322), `inbound-economics-freeze.fieldSame`, and `underwriting/stale-detector.same`. Grep for a raw `===`/`IS DISTINCT FROM` on one of these columns before adding a new detector.
  3. **`FNM1025` IS NOT A PROPERTY TYPE — it is the Fannie Mae appraisal FORM number** (Small Residential Income, 2–4 unit; 1004 = URAR single-family, 1073 = condo). It reached the file from the appraisal desk, whose property-type finding carries the FORM CODE as its "appraisal value" and used to be writable back onto the loan. That specific door is shut (`REPRICE_COLS` in `src/routes/appraisal.js` excludes `property_type`; the finding offers keep/dismiss only), but the real gap was that **no door ever REFUSED the value**. `property-type.sanitizePropertyType` / `propertyTypeProblem` now run on EVERY write path — staff `PATCH /details`, staff + borrower `complete-fields`, and the ClickUp inbound `cols` — mirroring the #95 `sanitizeLoanType` pattern (the human doors answer 400 saying WHY; the inbound returns null so its COALESCE keeps the real value and the sync never breaks). Add the same guard to any new door that writes `property_type`.
  4. **Replacing bad data is a REPAIR, not a re-price.** The trigger treats "an appraisal form code → a real property type" as a correction (the loan was never priced on a form number), so fixing it does not demand a re-register. Filling a genuinely BLANK type, and a real type change, still trip it exactly as before.
  5. **Heal what the root already broke, and heal it for MULTI-item reasons.** db/288's heal only matched a Term-ONLY reason (`NOT LIKE '%;%'`), so the owner's combined "Property type: … ; Term: …" was never cleared and the file stayed stuck. `pilot_reason_is_cosmetic()` (db/322) parses the whole itemized list and clears only when EVERY item is provably a no-op; any reason naming a real change is untouched. db/322 also repairs files already storing a form code (1004 → SFR, 1025 → Multi 2–4, 1073 → Condo — the forms' own definitions; any other form number is left alone rather than guessed), audited per file as `property_type_form_code_repaired`. Condition statuses / sign-offs are deliberately NOT auto-restored — a human re-verifies.
  6. **Write the CANONICAL spelling, don't rewrite a good one.** `product-registration.termWritebackText` preserves the file's existing term text when it already means the registered months; `conditions/engine.js` canonicalizes a property-type enum answer to the portal LABEL (writing the raw rule key left the file in a spelling no other door uses — it wouldn't even map to the ClickUp dropdown option).
- **The borrower PROFILE is the person's record, and it is LINKED — SSN, primary contact, housing, officer (owner-directed 2026-07-26).** Five connected complaints, one theme: the profile knew less about a person than the loan file did, and there was no way in.
  1. **SSN + primary contact + housing are editable ON THE PROFILE** (`app-v2/src/screens/StaffBorrowerDetail.jsx`). The SSN could only be REVEALED there — adding one had to be done from inside a loan file, which is backwards for a person who has no file yet; it now uses the same audited `POST /api/staff/borrowers/:id/ssn` (still cross-borrower collision-checked, still scoped-pushed to every linked card). Housing (owns/rents, monthly payment, time at the address), employment and dependents already existed as `borrowers` columns mapped BOTH ways to ClickUp's Primary Housing fields — only the staff surface was missing; `PATCH /borrowers/:id` now accepts them and re-anchors `residence_since` so the duration keeps counting. `borrower_contacts` (every email/phone accumulated across the person's files) went from read-only to managed: `POST /borrowers/:id/contacts` + `…/contacts/primary` add one and PROMOTE it to `borrowers.email`/`cell_phone` — the value every email, term sheet and ClickUp push actually reads — keeping the old primary in the list.
  2. **An SSN conflict NAMES the other profile and can be resolved.** "It's already linked to a different borrower profile" was a dead end: no way to see WHICH profile, and the SSN nowhere to be found on the one you were looking at. Root cause is over-split profiles — a second one is minted whenever an inbound ClickUp card carries an email we can't corroborate — so the number lands on the profile nobody is looking at. The 409 now returns `conflict:{borrowerId,name,sameLastName,looksLikeShadow,canResolve}` (name only when the actor may see that profile), and `resolveConflict:'same_person'` MOVES the number over, audited on both sides, recording a `borrower_dedup_candidates` pair + `borrower_profile_links` so the duplicate gets worked rather than worked around.
  3. **An officer OWNS the person, not just the RTL file.** `GET /borrowers` / `/borrowers/search` / `canSeeBorrowerId` derived an officer's book purely from loan FILES, and only RTL ClickUp cards ever become files — so a client who has only ever done DSCR business was invisible to the officer who owns them (no profile to auto-fill, so a duplicate got typed in). `src/clickup/ingest.js` now stamps `borrowers.primary_officer_id` (FILL-ONLY) from EVERY card's officer, RTL or not, and records `clickup_task_index.loan_officer_id`; the new `VISIBLE_BORROWER_SQL(alias,p)` chokepoint in `staff.js` matches that owner OR a visible file. **Never re-inline a borrower scope — route it through `VISIBLE_BORROWER_SQL`** (the file-level equivalent is `VISIBLE_OFFICERS_SQL`). The profile + list + typeahead surface the person's non-RTL cards as "other deals" from the masked `clickup_task_index` snapshot, so a DSCR-only client never reads as an empty record. `db/318` backfills the officer from existing files (primary AND co-borrower) and task-index rows.
  4. **Two borrowers MAY share one email** (a husband and wife on one mailbox). `borrowers.email` carried a blanket UNIQUE, so the second person was refused at the staff doors and given a `noemail+<task>@clickup.local` shadow by the sync — the very shadows that swallowed SSNs. `db/318` replaces it with `shares_email boolean` + the PARTIAL unique index `borrowers_email_owner_uk ON borrowers(email) WHERE shares_email = false`: exactly ONE profile OWNS an address, any number of deliberately-flagged people sit alongside. **Every `ON CONFLICT (email)` on `borrowers` must name the same predicate** (`ON CONFLICT (email) WHERE shares_email = false`) or Postgres cannot infer the index — all 11 call sites were updated. A sign-in must resolve to one person, so ONE portal login per address is enforced by the `trg_borrower_auth_one_login_per_email` trigger (raised as 23505) with friendly pre-checks on the invite / set-password doors; the second person keeps their profile and files, just not the login.
  5. **The subject property address REACHES ClickUp again.** A ClickUp `location` field requires real coordinates (`mapper.addressField` correctly refuses to write one without lat/lng), and `orchestrator.withCoords` was calling `require('../lib/address').geocode` — a function that has NEVER existed on that module. So no address ever gained coordinates and BOTH location fields (subject property + borrower home) were silently dropped from every push: an address typed in PILOT could not reach the card at all. It now calls the new `src/lib/address-canon.js` `geocode(text)` — Google Geocoding (cached forever in `address_canon_cache`) falling back to the SAME keyless OSM Nominatim provider that already backs the address autocomplete — and writes the provider's own `formatted_address`, which is the string ClickUp's location picker produces, so the value lands SELECTED rather than as loose text. Resolved coordinates are written back onto the file/profile (fill-only). Degrades to "not pushed", never to a bad or location-clearing write.
      · **THAT FIX WAS GO-FORWARD ONLY, AND A TRANSIENT GEOCODE FAILURE WAS CACHED FOREVER (owner-reported 2026-07-28, file YSCAP258134791 / card FILLE-1990 "MOSES WEIL": every other field on the card had synced — name, SSN, DOB, FICO, LLC, purchase price, even the push-only "Date File Submitted" — and BOTH `location` fields were empty).** Two separate root causes, both fixed; neither may be reintroduced.
        1. **`address_canon_cache` is PERMANENT, and a row with a NULL `place_id` means "there is no such place" — so it must only ever record an answer that was actually ABOUT THE ADDRESS.** Both providers were writing that row for ANY non-match, including answers about US rather than the property: an OSM 429/403/5xx (Nominatim rate-limits hard), or a Google `OVER_QUERY_LIMIT` / `OVER_DAILY_LIMIT` / `REQUEST_DENIED` / `INVALID_REQUEST` / `UNKNOWN_ERROR` — all of which arrive as an **HTTP 200 body, not a thrown error**, so they sailed past the `catch` that already knew the rule ("network failure: DON'T cache — retry later"). One blip marked a real property unresolvable FOREVER, and since a ClickUp `location` field REQUIRES coordinates, that address could then never reach the card no matter how often the file was re-pushed. Now: `googleDefinitive()` / `osmDefinitive()` (pure, exported) decide whether the provider actually answered about the address (`OK`/`ZERO_RESULTS`; a results ARRAY on a 200), only a definitive answer is cached, and even a definitive "unresolvable" is re-checked after `GEOCODE_NEGATIVE_TTL_DAYS` (30) — new construction does get added to the map. `cachePut` upgrades or refreshes a negative row but NEVER rewrites a RESOLVED one (`samePlace` compares `place_id` identity, which must stay stable forever). **The class, for any future permanent cache: never store a negative you cannot prove is about the subject rather than about the provider.**
        2. **Outbound is enqueue-on-write ONLY — there is deliberately no dirty sweep — so a field re-pushes when a HUMAN edits it, and nobody edits an address that is already correct in PILOT.** `lib/address-heal` repairs the stored FORMAT of an address but never re-pushes it. So every file pushed before 2026-07-26 keeps a blank subject/home address on its card forever: the fix reached new files only, violating the "previous AND future" rule. **`src/clickup/address-backfill.js` `sweepOnce()`** is the repair half — bounded (`CLICKUP_ADDRESS_BACKFILL_FILES`, 25/tick), resumable from a durable `sync_runtime_state` cursor, cycling every `CLICKUP_ADDRESS_BACKFILL_CYCLE_DAYS` (7) so it also covers the go-forward hole (a geocoder unreachable at file-create time drops the address and nothing would ever try again). Wired in `clickup-sync.start()` inside the `CLICKUP_OUTBOUND_ENABLED` block; off-switch `CLICKUP_ADDRESS_BACKFILL_DISABLED=1`.
        3. **IT CAN ONLY EVER ADD.** It READS the card first and asks only for the fields blank THERE, and the push itself runs with the new **`opts.fillOnly`** so the orchestrator re-checks each field against its own pre-write read and SKIPS anything ClickUp already holds — "*Borrower Address" is a PII-shielded identity field and a sweep nobody asked for must never be able to clobber one. With no before-image it writes nothing. It never creates a task (a scoped push structurally cannot), never renames one, never deletes anything, and never touches another field; an address the geocoder can't place is simply not pushed, exactly as before.
        4. **Blankness is ONE definition — `mapper.isBlankClickupValue`** — used by the fill-only guard AND by the PII overwrite shield's `oldBlank`, because a `location` comes back as `null`, as `{}`, or as an object with a blank `formatted_address` and no coordinates, and reading one of those as OCCUPIED is what leaves a blank card permanently un-fillable. It rejects a null coordinate BEFORE `Number()` — `Number(null)` is `0`, which is finite (the same trap `addressField()` guards on the write side, and the one the test caught in review). Test `scripts/test-clickup-address-backfill.js` (pure, in `npm test`).
- **EVERY FIELD ON THE BORROWER SECTION IS EDITABLE, FOR EVERY BORROWER — ONE shared editor, keyed on a borrower id, mounted on the FILE OVERVIEW (owner-directed 2026-07-27: "in the BORROWER profile section you can only edit a few fields from the first borrower, you can't edit the fields from the 2nd borrower, and you can't even ADD all the fields from both borrowers — you need to be able to edit any field on the entire BORROWER section from any BORROWER").** ROOT CAUSE was per-surface duplication, not a missing endpoint: the loan file's Borrower panel, the co-borrower block, the two completeness panels and the borrower CRM profile each hand-rolled their OWN subset of the person's fields, so "can I change this?" depended on which screen you stood on AND on whether the value happened to be blank. Concretely: the file's Borrower panel printed eight READ-ONLY rows with exactly TWO editable (DOB + SSN); the co-borrower block printed the same rows with NONE editable (Remove / Invite only); the completeness panels could only ADD a field while it was still MISSING — a filled field drops off the panel, so it could never be corrected — and marked email / SSN / home address `edit:false` pointing at a panel that had no such control.
  1. **`app-v2/src/components/BorrowerProfilePanel.jsx` is THE editor** — the one definition of the person's record (`BorrowerProfileForm` + `BorrowerSsnRow` + the self-loading default panel), used by the borrower CRM profile screen AND by the loan file, once per borrower. It is keyed on a **borrower id**, so the primary and the co-borrower are literally the same code path. **Never re-grow a private borrower editor on a screen** — that is exactly how the co-borrower ended up read-only; add the field HERE and every surface gets it at once. The panel is mounted on the **file OVERVIEW**, not inside "Application details": that section is collapsed by default and a Borrower editor inside it is invisible until you go looking, which is how this surface stayed read-only. Exactly ONE mount per person (the editor test counts them) — two editors for one record on one page is worse than none. `CoBorrowerBlock` keeps only what is about the LINK rather than the person: find / add / remove / invite.
  2. **`PATCH /api/staff/borrowers/:id` accepts `fico`** (shared 300–850 sanitizer; an out-of-range score is REFUSED with a reason rather than silently dropped — the "returned 200 but didn't save" class — and an explicit blank clears it). A credit pull still overwrites it; the point is that a score the team already has on paper had no way in, and a wrong one no way out. SSN keeps its own audited door (`POST …/ssn`, with the duplicate-profile "same person — move the number here" resolver), which the co-borrower now gets too.
  3. **A CO-BORROWER edit may NEVER be pushed with the primary's keys.** Every `t:'b'` column in `clickup/mapper.js FIELD_MAP` is the FILE'S PRIMARY borrower, so enqueueing `first_name`/`email`/`cell_phone` for a co-borrower would have written the PRIMARY's values onto the card. The route enqueues the scoped key **`co_borrower`** (`resolveOnly` → coBorrowerFlag + coBorrowerName + secondBorrowerEmail + secondBorrowerCell, and NOTHING else) on files where the person is the co-borrower, alongside the ordinary primary-keyed push on files where they are the primary. ClickUp has no second-borrower DOB / FICO / address field at all — those simply live in PILOT, which is safe because the inbound heal is fill-only (COALESCE).
  4. **Employment is a FIXED list, not free text** (`EMPLOYMENT` in `app-v2/src/lib/enums.js`, spelled exactly as the `employment_type` crosswalk knows). It is a two-way mapped ClickUp dropdown, so a value the crosswalk cannot translate is dropped from the push in silence and read straight back over on the next pull — the same trap the PROGRAMS list fell into.
  5. A completeness pill this panel can't edit itself (`ssn`, `co_email`, `co_ssn`) is now a CLICK-THROUGH to the Borrower section instead of a dead grey "Missing:" chip naming a control that didn't exist.
  Tests: `scripts/test-borrower-profile-editor.mjs` (pure — ONE definition, mounted for both people, the Social never rides along in the ordinary save) and `scripts/test-borrower-fields-db.js` (in `npm test` — the FICO + canonical-employment coverage, the panel really being on the overview, the co-borrower push never touching a primary field, and every field round-tripping through the REAL HTTP endpoints for the FIRST and the SECOND borrower at plain loan-officer authority, re-read from the server and never trusted from the write's own 200, plus clearing, refusal of junk, and the unchanged 403 scope).
- **AN ADDRESS IS ALWAYS THE MAILING ONE-LINE — never a geocoder display name (owner-reported 2026-07-26: "Pilot addresses got messed up again with all this long addresses").** A file's subject property and a borrower's home address were showing as `"26, South 10th Street, Williamsburg, Brooklyn, Kings County, New York, 11249, United States"` instead of the form ClickUp shows, `"26 S 10th St, Brooklyn, NY 11249, USA"`. ROOT CAUSE: the keyless OSM Nominatim fallback added for the ClickUp address push returned its raw `display_name` (neighbourhood + county + spelled-out state + "United States") and `withCoords` wrote that string onto the record as `formatted_address` — and nearly every reader prefers `formatted_address` over `oneLine`, so the long form won on every surface AND was pushed to the card. THE FIX IS A CHOKEPOINT, not a spot-patch: `src/lib/address.js` owns what a canonical address looks like (`canonicalOneLine` / `compactFormattedAddress` / `canonicalizeAddressValue` / `abbreviateStreet` / `normalizeCityName` / `preferBorough` / `osmComponentsToAddress`) — USPS-style abbreviations, mailing city ("Lakewood Township" → "Lakewood", "Village of Spring Valley" → "Spring Valley"), NYC borough rule, 2-letter state, no county/neighbourhood, "USA". Every producer routes through it: the geocoder (`address-canon.osmGeocode` asks Nominatim for `addressdetails=1` and BUILDS the mailing form — it must NEVER store `display_name`, and it compacts on cache READ because that cache is permanent), the ClickUp push (`orchestrator.withCoords`), the ClickUp INBOUND parser (`mapper.normalizeClickupLocation` — compacting first also stops `parseAddress` storing "Kings County" as the city), and the autocomplete route (`routes/address.js`, which now SHARES the borough/component helpers instead of duplicating them). Previous files are repaired at boot by `src/lib/address-heal.js` (`applications.property_address`, `borrowers.current_address`/`prior_address`, `track_records.property_address`, `leads.property_address`, plus the `address_canon_cache` rows) — it only ever touches a record that IS in the long form, re-checks the row inside the UPDATE (compare as **jsonb**, never `::text` — key order/spacing differ, and a text compare silently no-ops), and is idempotent. **The repair rewrites a spelled-out state to its code, which is why `db/326` adds `pilot_state_norm()` and makes the reopen trigger compare the subject state by MEANING** — the same free-text-compare class as `term` (db/288) and `property_type` (db/322): "New York" → "NY" is not a re-price and must never flag a registration stale. JS twin `address.stateCompareKey` — keep them in sync. Test `scripts/test-address-format.js` (pure + DB-gated, in `npm test`). When adding any new address producer, emit `canonicalOneLine`/`compactFormattedAddress` output — never a raw provider string.
- **AN ADDRESS IS COMPARED BY MEANING, AND PILOT'S ADDRESSES END AT THE ZIP (owner-directed 2026-07-26 evening; AMENDS the rule above — the country suffix is NO LONGER emitted).** The sync-review queue filled with ~54 "Borrower home address / Encompass → PILOT" rows where both sides were plainly the same home — `5701 15 Ave 4D, Brooklyn, NY 11219` vs `5701 15th Ave Apt 4d, Brooklyn, NY 11219, USA`. Two directives, both shipped:
  1. **No country.** Everything PILOT generates stops at the ZIP — `canonicalOneLine` never appends "USA" (the `country` option is accepted and IGNORED so no caller breaks), and `compactFormattedAddress` strips a trailing country from whatever it is handed. Past records are deliberately NOT rewritten (owner: "you don't need to replace everything from the past"); they simply compare equal.
  2. **`address.sameAddress(a, b)` is the ONE place that decides whether two addresses are the same PLACE**, and every comparer routes through it: the Encompass enrichment conflict check (`encompass/enrich.js` — the producer of those rows), the sync-review re-check (`sync-review-recheck.valuesAgree`, plus a new Encompass-address branch so "Re-check" settles a row with NO Encompass call), and the ClickUp no-op suppression (`mapper.fieldValueEquivalent` location fallback — without it, dropping ", USA" would read as an overwrite and churn writes / queue PII reviews). IGNORED as pure spelling: the country, case, punctuation, ZIP+4, abbreviations ("Avenue"/"Ave", "South"/"S"), ordinals ("15 Ave" = "15th Ave", typo'd "61th" = "61st"), unit KEYWORDS ("Apt 5B" = "Unit 5B" = "#5b" = "5B"), an empty unit slot ("St Apt" with nothing after), a leading descriptive part ("Bedford Gardens, 74 Ross St"), a house-number RANGE covering the other's number ("27-29" ⊇ "27"), and **the CITY NAME whenever both sides carry the same ZIP** (the ZIP is the authority — Cedarhurst/Hempstead, Spring Valley/Ramapo, Jackson/Jackson Township are the same place). NEVER ignored: a different house number, street, ZIP, state, or two units that are both present and different. **One side missing the unit is NOT a conflict** — same address, less detail, and the review offered no decision. Conservative by construction: anything it cannot read on both sides (no house number / no street) returns false, so a review is KEPT rather than silently closed.
  3. **The rows already queued are retired at boot** by `src/lib/address-review-close.js` (`closeEquivalentAddressReviewsOnce`) — provably-equal open `current_address` rows only; a different house number / ZIP / unit stays open (several of those are real typos worth fixing). Bounded, idempotent, never throws. **`sync_review_queue.id` is a BIGINT — compare it as text (`id::text = ANY($1::text[])`); a `::uuid[]` cast throws inside the catch and the pass silently reports "closed 0" while finding every row.**
  4. Same pass: `enrich.addrString`/`partyAddress` refuse a non-string address slot instead of rendering the literal **"[object Object]"** (one review row displayed exactly that as its Encompass value).
  Tests `scripts/test-address-same.js` (in `npm test`) — the owner's REAL queue as fixtures: every same-home pair must compare equal, every genuinely different pair must stay a review, a negative battery proves nothing collapses two real addresses, plus the DB closer. When you add any new address comparison anywhere, call `sameAddress` — never a letters-only key.
- **A REVIEW EMAIL MAY ONLY ASK FOR WHAT IS ACTUALLY WAITING — report the BACKLOG, never the week's churn (owner-reported 2026-07-28: "why am I still getting these emails, most of this was resolved already and it's not coming up as a manual review required").** The weekly digest read `109 opened, 67 auto-closed … Top reasons this week: encompass_address_differs: 77` while only TEN rows were open — so the system's own successful clean-up was mailed back as a to-do list, and clicking through showed almost nothing. Three fixes, none of which may regress:
  1. **The digest reports the OPEN queue.** `sendReviewDigestOnce`'s "what's waiting" list was `GROUP BY reason WHERE created_at > now() - interval '7 days'` — every row RAISED in the window, settled or not. It is now `WHERE status='open'` (whenever it was raised — an open row from three weeks ago is still work), grouped by `field_key` AND `reason` so two different problems with one field stay distinct. The 7-day activity numbers stay, worded as activity that needs nothing from the reader. **An EMPTY queue now sends NOTHING** — a digest whose only content is "the system worked and left you nothing" is the noise itself. The whole decision + wording is the PURE `digestMessage(stats, byReason)` so every branch (including the silent one) is unit-tested without a DB; the 6-day throttle claim is deliberately KEPT on a quiet week (releasing it would turn one weekly decision into a daily re-check that eventually finds one row and mails everybody), and the audit row records `sent:false, why:'nothing_open'`.
  2. **The CTA said "Open the loan file" and these do NOT appear on a loan file.** `notify.js` defaults a staff CTA to that label; all three sync-review emails (per-row LO notice, 7-day admin escalation, digest) pass `link:'/internal/sync-reviews'` and no label, so every body said "open the Sync review screen" under a button promising a loan file. The link was always right — only the label lied, and the label is what people read. Every sync-review notify now passes `ctaLabel:'Open the sync review screen'`. The staff file screen has NO sync-review surface at all; if one is ever added, this label is the thing to revisit.
  3. **A CONFLICT IS IDENTIFIED BY THE PERSON AND THE VALUE — never by the integration record it arrived on.** The 2026-07-27 "a decided Encompass address review must not come back" guard was keyed on `task_id` (the borrower's LATEST Encompass loan) plus the proposed string EXACTLY, and BOTH halves of that key move on their own: the pass picks whichever mirrored loan ranks newest, so one newly pulled loan re-keys the same conflict onto a new `encompass:<guid>`; and the proposed string is regenerated by the address canonicalizer, which changed twice in three days. Either shift slipped past the guard AND past the queue's own one-open-row-per-(task, field, proposal) index — so a dismissal was quietly replaced by a fresh open row, sometimes a SECOND card alongside the first. `enrich.verifyPrimaryAddress` now reads the borrower's prior `current_address` rows (any status) and compares by MEANING via `sameAddress`: still-open → don't duplicate; rejected/resolved → a human decided, never nag again; a genuinely new address (they moved) is a different place, so it still surfaces. **Apply the same shape to any future review producer** — key the "already asked" test on the subject and the value, not on the row the fact travelled in.
  4. **A producer must never assert a disagreement it cannot read.** `sameAddress` answers false for anything it cannot parse a house number + street from on BOTH sides — correct for the queue CLOSER (an unreadable row stays open for a human), backwards for a PRODUCER, which turned "I can't read either of these" into a review row claiming the two addresses differ (two PO boxes were the common shape — `sameAddress` cannot confirm even two IDENTICAL ones).
