The pass now stays silent only when NEITHER side is readable (one readable side against an unreadable one is still a real question), and counts what it held back in the summary (`addressesUnreadable` / `addressesAlreadyDecided` / `addressesAlreadyQueued`) so a suppression is never invisible to the system — watch a climbing `addressesUnreadable` in the `[encompass] borrower-profile enrichment:` log line. Test `scripts/test-sync-review-noise.js` (in `npm test`; pure digest branches + a real-Postgres producer run whose 7 DB assertions were each proven to FAIL on the pre-fix code). `db/352` indexes the new borrower-keyed lookup.
- **A GEOCODER SUPPLIES COORDINATES, NEVER THE ADDRESS TEXT — it may restyle an address, never contradict it (owner-reported 2026-07-28, YSCAP258134762 / Christopher Rodriguez & Patrick Kamara; HARD RULE).** ClickUp's own activity log read *"You changed \*Subject Property Address from `1727 2nd St, Piscataway, NJ 08854, USA` to `2nd St, Piscataway, NJ 07063`"* — and "You" was OUR API token. Correcting the file never stuck: the owner re-typed the real address again and again and the next push re-corrupted it. ROOT CAUSE: `orchestrator.withCoords` adopted the geocoder's `formatted_address` **unconditionally**. A geocoder answers a house number it cannot place with the **ROAD** it sits on — no `house_number`, and the road's OWN postcode. Reproduced live against Nominatim: querying that exact address returns `addresstype:"road"`, `display_name:"2nd Street, Piscataway Township, …, 07063"`, which `osmComponentsToAddress` + `canonicalOneLine` render as the corrupted string byte-for-byte. It then went out to the card AND was written back onto `applications.property_address`, AND was cached FOREVER in `address_canon_cache` — so every later correction re-derived the same wrong text instantly, with no network call. That permanent cache row is what turned a one-off bad match into a self-reinforcing loop.
  1. **The chokepoint is `address.geocodeRewriteIsSafe(ours, provider)`** (pure, unit-tested). A provider answer may only RESTYLE: it is refused when it drops or changes the **house number**, disagrees on a **ZIP we already hold**, or drops a **leading directional** ("S 2nd St" and "2nd St" are two different streets). Everything legitimate still passes — abbreviating ("26 South 10th Street" → "26 S 10th St"), normalizing the mailing city, and FILLING a ZIP we never had. `withCoords` now takes the provider's **coordinates** either way (so the address still reaches the ClickUp location field — the 2026-07-26 bug) but keeps OUR text unless the rewrite is safe.
  2. **Precision is enforced at the source too.** `address-canon.parseOsmResult` (extracted pure, mirroring `parseGeocodeResult`) marks a match with no `address.house_number` as `precision:'road'` and returns `formatted:null` + `zip:null`, so nothing downstream — and no cached row — can adopt a road's text; `parseGeocodeResult` now rejects `route`/`postal_code`/`neighborhood`-level Google matches outright, which matters twice because `canonicalize` also backs **`samePlace`**: a road-level `place_id` would make every house on one street compare as the SAME property. For the same reason `withCoords` does not adopt a road-level `place_id`.
  3. **The INBOUND half is what closed the loop.** ClickUp still held the corrupted value and `property_address` COALESCE-overwrites on every pull, so each correction was read straight back over. `clickup/ingest.js` refuses an inbound subject address carrying **no house number when the one we hold has one** (`cols.property_address = null` → COALESCE keeps ours), audits it (`clickup_pull_address_no_house_number`) and re-pushes the good value to repair the card. Deliberately NARROW — every real address has a number, so a human's edit in ClickUp (including a house-number CHANGE) still flows in untouched.
  4. **Previous files: `address-heal.healDowngradedGeocodeCache`** at boot. A row whose QUERY named a house number and whose cached answer doesn't (judged by the same `geocodeRewriteIsSafe`) is provably downgraded: an `osm:` row is blanked to `formatted=NULL, zip=NULL` keeping its coordinates (exactly what the fixed parser now caches), a Google row is DELETED (its `place_id` is the road's identity). Self-draining, idempotent, never throws.
  5. **A SECOND corruption on the same file proves the rule is about more than street-level matches.** Google normalizes "1727 S 2nd St, Piscataway, NJ 08854" to "1727 S 2nd St, Plainfield, NJ 07063" — a PRECISE, rooftop match. The US Census geocoder resolves BOTH: they are **two different houses ~130 m apart** on opposite sides of the Piscataway/Plainfield municipal line, where S 2nd St keeps its numbering across the boundary. Adopting the "better" answer moved the file to a different building. The ZIP check catches it. **A geocoder is not an authority on which property the file is about.**
  Test `scripts/test-address-downgrade.js` (in `npm test`; pure + DB-gated) pins the owner's real strings, the verbatim live Nominatim payload, both corruptions end-to-end through `withCoords`, and the cache repair. When adding any new geocoder or any code that writes a provider's address string onto a record, route it through `geocodeRewriteIsSafe` — never adopt `formatted_address` because "the geocoder answered".
- **ON HOLD is a real status in PILOT, not just in ClickUp (owner-directed 2026-07-26).** A file parked at ClickUp's `inactive / on hold` was showing the borrower-facing status **File intake** — which means the exact opposite (the FIRST stage, a brand-new prospect). The `'inactive / on hold' → on_hold` mapping already existed in `src/clickup/status.js`, but it only applies WHEN A TASK IS RE-INGESTED: a file that entered while the card said `starting` (→ `file_intake`) and was parked later keeps the stale status forever, because nobody touches a held card and the reconcile poll is windowed on `date_updated`. Three fixes, all needed: **(1)** `db/319` re-asserts the invariant on EVERY boot (internal status means hold → `status='on_hold'`), stamping `status_notified_external` so healing months of history is SILENT; **(2)** `applyInternalStatus` self-heals the derived status when the two halves have drifted (picking the status a file already has used to be a no-op that fixed nothing); **(3)** `on_hold` was MISSING from `APP_STATUS` in `staff.js`, so PILOT could not park a file at all — it is now settable, and `PATCH /applications/:id {status:'on_hold'}` routes through the internal-status door so the ClickUp card is parked in lock-step (`statusMap.ON_HOLD_INTERNAL`). A held file is genuinely paused: already excluded from `INACTIVE_FILE_STATUSES` (KPIs, tasks, reminders, digests), and `notify.notifyAppBorrowers` — the ONE borrower fan-out chokepoint — now drops every borrower message for it (`opts.evenIfOnHold` is the escape hatch). Staff notifications are deliberately NOT gated. Tests: `scripts/test-borrower-profile-linking.js` (in `npm test`; pure + DB).
- **"SHOW ME EVERY BORROWER I EVER CLOSED WITH" — the profile sweep + many-to-many officer ownership (owner-directed 2026-07-26, follow-up to the borrower-profile work above).** An officer's borrower list STILL missed the people he had closed DSCR deals with. Two independent root causes, both fixed:
  1. **COVERAGE — most of ClickUp was never read.** `reconcileOnce` polls with `date_updated_gt` (a rolling watermark), so a deal that CLOSED months ago is never touched again and never comes back in the window — its borrower simply does not exist in PILOT. The ONLY pass that walks everything is `runBackfill`, and it runs solely when someone sets `CLICKUP_RUN_BACKFILL` and restarts the service — a manual one-shot that in practice was never run, so the historical book was never imported. **`src/sync/profile-sweep.js` is the missing third loop**: it pages the WHOLE pipeline space (`cfg.clickupPipelineSpace`, NOT `routing.PIPELINE_FOLDERS()` — that registry is hand-maintained, excludes departed officers whose past clients still matter, and cannot know about a folder created since the last deploy), `includeClosed:true`, **no date window**, a few pages per tick from a durable resumable cursor in `sync_runtime_state` (`clickup_profile_sweep`), cycling again `CLICKUP_PROFILE_SWEEP_CYCLE_DAYS` (7) after it finishes. It builds the PERSON — profile (name/email/phone/SSN/DOB/home address/citizenship/employment/housing, all FILL-ONLY), entities (`upsertLlc`), track record (`upsertTrackRecord` on every CLOSED card), officer link — and **`createFile:false` on EVERY call, always** (owner: "we don't need to start looking on the DSCR files for real files"). Pure inbound: it cannot write to ClickUp, create a loan file, or delete anything. A pass that reads ZERO cards WITH errors is marked `failed` and retried in 15 min instead of serving the weekly rest, so one ClickUp outage can't stall the import for a week. Off-switch `CLICKUP_PROFILE_SWEEP_DISABLED=1`; admin `GET`/`POST /api/admin/clickup/profile-sweep` (progress + "Re-import all borrower profiles" in `StaffClickup.jsx`).
  2. **OWNERSHIP — one officer per borrower could not express the truth.** `borrowers.primary_officer_id` is single-valued AND fill-only (it must be — stealing an existing relationship would be worse), so the FIRST officer to touch a person owned them forever and every other officer they closed with stayed locked out. **`borrower_officers` (db/327) makes it many-to-many** — one row per (borrower, officer), recorded by `ingest.ingestTask` from EVERY card in EVERY status, backfilled from applications (primary AND co-borrower, including soft-deleted/descoped files) + `clickup_task_index` + the existing pointer. `VISIBLE_BORROWER_SQL` now matches that table (plus `visible_officer_ids` delegation) as well as the primary pointer and any visible file; `primary_officer_id` keeps its old meaning as the CRM owner. `borrower_officers.deals` is display-only and **RECOMPUTED** by `profile-sweep.recountDeals()` at the end of each cycle — never incremented per task, because the same card is ingested many times and a running counter could only drift. Tests: `scripts/test-borrower-book-db.js` (in `npm test`; pure contract + a real-DB end-to-end over a synthetic CLOSED DSCR card proving profile/LLC/track-record/officer-link, that a second officer's card makes the borrower visible to BOTH without stealing the primary, that an unrelated officer still gets 403, and that re-sweeping never duplicates a profile/entity/track-record line).
- **A RENAMED CLICKUP CARD RENAMES THE PERSON — the nickname trap (owner-reported 2026-07-26).** A file was opened under the nickname the officer calls the borrower ("Avi"), which created the profile under that name; the card was then corrected to the real name ("Abraham") and **the profile never updated**. Root cause: `healBorrowerFields` is strictly FILL-ONLY on the name — it writes a name only when the stored one is blank or a placeholder (`''`/`unknown`/`co-borrower`). That is right for an ordinary re-sync (a stale card must never clobber a real name), but it means the FIRST value ever seen is frozen forever, so a nickname can never be corrected from ClickUp. The fix mirrors the DOB **human-edit-wins** rule exactly — the task's stored SNAPSHOT is the evidence that ClickUp CHANGED since we last read it, which proves a human renamed it at the source. Guarded so it can only fix a name, never swap in a stranger: the new name must be real (never a placeholder); a **same-SURNAME** change (Avi → Abraham Klein) is the nickname case → **ADOPT**, audited `clickup_name_edited_at_source`, closing any stale `first_name` review; a **WHOLE-name** change may be a different human → never guessed, it queues the normal two-sided `first_name` sync review (already fully resolvable both ways via `sync-autoresolve.applyReviewWinner` IDENTITY_FIELDS). Our own pushes can't trip it (we push the name we already store, so nothing differs). The OUTBOUND half ships with it: the legal name is now editable on the borrower profile (`PATCH /borrowers/:id` accepts `firstName`/`lastName`, validated against `isPlaceholderName`, never blank) and a correction pushes to every linked card via the existing `first_name` scoped-push key — otherwise the next inbound pull would read the old card value straight back over the fix. Tests in `scripts/test-borrower-book-db.js`.
- **TWO PROFILES, ONE PERSON — duplicate compare + MERGE (owner-directed 2026-07-26).** The sync deliberately OVER-SPLITS (an inbound email it cannot corroborate mints a distinct profile rather than hang one person's loans and PII on another), which is the right trade but guarantees real duplicates — and nothing could put them back together. `src/lib/borrower-merge.js` + `db/323` + `GET /borrowers/:id/duplicates`, `GET /borrowers/:id/compare/:otherId`, `POST /borrowers/:id/merge`, `GET /borrowers/:id/merges` (staff.js) + the **Duplicates** tab in `StaffBorrowerDetail.jsx`. The owner's rules are the contract: the two become ONE; a single-valued field the two DISAGREE on is decided by a HUMAN (the route returns **409 with `undecided[]`** rather than guess — never auto-resolve one); a field only the loser has is pure gain and is taken automatically; **emails, phones, entities and track records ADD UP** and are never offered as a choice. Merging is the most destructive action in the app (it re-points every file, document, condition and message and then deletes a profile), so: ONE transaction; the WHOLE losing profile is snapshotted into `borrower_merges.merged_snapshot` first (row + contacts + entities + officers + track records) alongside which side won each field and a per-table moved count; the SSN moves as the **triple** (`ssn_encrypted`+`ssn_last4`+`ssn_hash`) or the profile would DISPLAY one number and MATCH another, and the loser's `ssn_hash` is always cleared so a freed social stops blocking re-entry; the portal login follows the person when only the loser had one; and the foreign keys are **DISCOVERED LIVE from `information_schema`**, so a table added next year is carried instead of silently orphaned. **NEVER rebuild the dedupe predicate generically** — a "clever" builder emitted an unqualified `lower(btrim(llc_name))` inside the `NOT EXISTS` subquery, which bound to the INNER row and compared it to itself, so every entity looked like a duplicate and a REAL LLC was DELETED instead of moved; it is now an explicit per-table `MATCH` map that THROWS for an unknown table (refusing to merge beats losing rows), and every predicate guards `d.<col> IS NOT NULL` because `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` would make two UNKNOWN keys look like one duplicate. A pair a human already confirmed as two people sharing a mailbox (`borrower_profile_links`) is shown last and flagged "do not merge". Tests: `scripts/test-borrower-merge-db.js` (in `npm test`; pure contract + a real-DB end-to-end that asserts the non-colliding row is MOVED **by name**, not merely that a count went down).
- **ACTIONS ARE GROUPED AND WEIGHTED — a row of identical buttons is not a design (owner-directed 2026-08-03: "everything needs to be a little better design, everything is a little messed up … the button is not a nice place, we need every button a little more modern on the recent stuff that was recently enhanced").** The draw desk had grown to NINE buttons in one wrapping row, every one the same weight and the same outline, so **Approve** — which moves real money — read exactly as loudly as **Draw packet**, which downloads a spreadsheet; and the new Investor delivery sat under the card as a lone bordered button attached to nothing. Four reusable pieces in `app-v2/src/styles.css`, all ADDITIVE and none of which changes a button's SIZE (`min-height` and padding are untouched, so no layout can reflow — it is weight, colour and grouping only):
  · **`.btn.soft`** — a utility action (open a document, export a sheet): filled with `--surface-soft`, no outline, weight 550. Most of that nine-button row should be this. `.btn.ghost` also drops 600 → 550 so a secondary action stops competing with the primary, and `.btn:focus-visible` finally draws a real ring (there was none).
  · **`.act-bar` / `.act-group` / `.act-label` / `.act-sep`** — actions clustered by WHAT THEY DO with a hairline between clusters and a small uppercase eyebrow naming each ("Documents"). The eyebrow is a PLAIN class, never a child combinator: the first cut scoped it to `.act-group > .act-label` and every standalone use silently rendered as ordinary body text.
  · **`.act-card`** — a section that OWNS its action: a title, one line saying what it does, and the button on the right. **A bare button floating under a card is the thing this replaces** — if an action needs explaining, it gets a card, not a lone button.
  · **`.seg`** — a segmented control for picking one of two modes, replacing two look-alike buttons where only one can be true. Stacks vertically under 720px (two long labels wrapped to four lines side by side on a phone).
  · **`.act-figs`** is a two-column money read-out whose total is separated by a `.rule` element spanning `grid-column:1/-1`. Bordering the `dt` and the `dd` separately drew two stubs with the column gap showing through — **in a grid, a rule that must look continuous spans the columns; it is never a border on the cells.**
  Verified by rendering the real markup against the REAL built stylesheet in headless Chromium at 1000px and at iPhone-12 width (`window.innerWidth === 390`, zero horizontal overflow) and reading the screenshots — the two bugs above were both found that way, not by inspection. When you add an action to a recently-built surface, reach for these classes rather than another bare `btn ghost`.
  · **Applied to the BORROWER draw screens too (owner-directed 2026-08-03, same session):** `BorrowerDraws.jsx` — the whole-project report card became an `.act-card` (its hand-rolled flex row had no `flex:1` on the text, so the button broke onto its own line and floated under the sentence), the results card's three-button row became DECISIONS (Accept / Dispute) then a hairline then a `soft` Download, and the two blocking/next-step bullet blocks collapsed into one `.dd-note` class with two tones; `DrawAccept.jsx` — the review/dispute mode toggle became a `.seg` (two `primary`/`ghost` buttons made a TAB look like the action). Also fixed a mobile collision that predates all of this: `.dd-card-h` had no wrap rule, so on a phone a long card title and its status pill fought for one line and overlapped — the header now wraps and the pill drops underneath. That rule is shared by several screens, and wrapping can only help them.
- **HARD RULE — all portal text is DARK/BLACK on the white canvas; never white/light body text (owner-directed 2026-07-26).** The V2/PILOT portal is white-first. EVERY piece of text you build — field labels, headings, table cells, helper/explanatory copy, status/summary text, empty-state text — must be an explicit DARK color that is readable on white (`#141B22` ink for primary; a dark slate like `#3A4550`/`#4B585C` for secondary). NEVER use `#fff`/near-white for body text, and do NOT lean on a `var(--x, fallback)` for text color unless you have CONFIRMED that variable resolves dark in the actual portal context — the white-on-white Encompass comparison panel (2026-07-26) happened because its text used `var(--ink)`/`var(--muted)` that rendered light in that container. Status colors (green/amber/red) are fine on pills/badges, but the surrounding label + value text stays dark. When building any new staff/borrower component, default to explicit dark hex for text; verify contrast on the white background before shipping.
  - **THE SPECIFIC TRAP — `var(--ink)` is a LIGHT color, NEVER a text color (the "Findings to review" white-on-white, owner-reported 2026-07-26).** In `app-v2/src/styles.css` the legacy dark-theme token NAMES were kept but REMAPPED to the white-first palette, so the names now LIE: **`--ink` = `#F6F3EC` (light PAPER / page background)**, `--ink-1` = `#FFFFFF`, `--ink-2` = `#F4F1EA`, `--ink-3` = `#EAE4D7` — every `--ink*` token is LIGHT. The DARK ink text token is **`--ivory` = `#141B22`** (that is what `body{color:var(--ivory)}` uses) and its semantic alias **`--text` = `#141B22`**; `--muted` = `#4B585C` is also dark and fine for secondary text. So `color:'var(--ink,#141B22)'` renders the text at `#F6F3EC` (light) on white — the `#141B22` fallback NEVER applies because the variable IS defined (to a light value). This is what made the whole Findings-to-review card (borrower name/address, finding title, "Our file:" value, the action buttons, the "What they need" box) invisible. **NEVER use any `--ink*` token for a text `color`** — use an explicit `#141B22` (primary) / `#4B585C` (secondary), or `var(--ivory)`/`var(--text)`. Grep for `color:\s*['"]?var\(--ink` before shipping any staff/borrower screen; every hit is this bug. Fixed across `StaffFindingEscalations.jsx`, `StaffApplication.jsx`, `StaffAiCenter.jsx`, `AiReasoningChat.jsx` (2026-07-26).
- **ENCOMPASS BUILDS THE BORROWER PROFILE — weekly, READ-ONLY, and its disagreements go to manual review (owner-directed 2026-07-26).** `src/encompass/enrich.js` already added prior subject addresses + the vesting LLC; the owner asked for the whole person. The pass (`encompass-sync.enrichPassOnce`, **weekly** — `ENCOMPASS_ENRICH_DAYS` default 7, opt-in `ENCOMPASS_ENRICH_ENABLED=1`, full-tenant snapshot refresh now ON by default with `ENCOMPASS_ENRICH_BULK=0` to disable) now walks EVERY party on EVERY mirrored loan and adds: the subject property → track record; **every entity the file names, including the free-text field 1859 list** (`vestingLlcs` splits on `;`, newline, `|`, and a SPACE-delimited `/` or `&` — **never a plain comma**, because "Smith Holdings, LLC" is ONE company and comma-splitting would manufacture a bogus row on a huge share of files); **every email and phone → `borrower_contacts`** (`addContactIfAbsent` — accumulate only, it NEVER promotes to `borrowers.email`/`cell_phone`, which is a human decision and the value every email/term sheet/ClickUp push reads); and it **verifies the primary home address against THE LAST FILE** (`loanDateMs` ranks by the loan's own closing/funding date, not our re-pull stamp, so a re-read can't make an ancient loan look newest). Address outcomes: profile empty → **FILL** (audited `encompass_address_filled`); the two agree → nothing; they DISAGREE → **never overwrite**, queue a `sync_review_queue` row. NON-NEGOTIABLE: it **never creates a loan file** and **never talks to Encompass at all** (it reads the `encompass_loan_snapshot` mirror; the client stays structurally frozen), and the ONLY `UPDATE borrowers` in the module is the fill-only address — the pure test asserts all three by reading the source. **`sync_review_queue.source` (db/328)** is what makes a third system safe in a queue built for ClickUp ↔ PILOT: an Encompass row's `task_id` is a namespaced `encompass:<loanGuid>` (so the existing one-open-row-per-(task,field,proposal) index dedupes per loan), which is NOT a ClickUp task — `applyReviewWinner` branches to `applyEncompassWinner` **before every ClickUp branch** and `sync-review-recheck` returns `unsupported`, or both would call `clickup.getTask` on a guid and make the row permanently unresolvable. Winners are `'encompass'` (adopt into PILOT, then best-effort push on to the borrower's ClickUp cards) / `'portal'` (keep ours, write nothing) / `'custom'`; **nothing is EVER written back to Encompass**. `queueReview` takes an optional `dbc` so a caller already inside a transaction queues on its own connection. Tests: `scripts/test-encompass-profile-enrich.js` (in `npm test`; 39 pure + 30 DB).
  - **ONLY A CLOSED DEAL GOES ON THE TRACK RECORD (owner-directed 2026-07-27: "the subject property, even before it's closed, is going on the track record automatically… the subject property or any other property that is not closed yet should not go automatically on the track record").** The enrichment above was adding the subject property of EVERY mirrored loan, so a borrower's IN-FLIGHT file — the property they are buying right now — appeared on their own track record tagged "Added from Encompass history". A track record is a record of deals DONE, so `enrich.loanClosed({raw, loanFolder, app})` now gates the address write and **the default with no evidence is NO** (entities, emails and phones are NOT gated — those are facts about the PERSON, equally true on a live file). Order of authority: **(1) OUR file wins** — when the loan is linked to a PILOT application (the snapshot's `application_id`, LEFT JOINed in the pass) anything short of `status='funded'` / a `funded_date` means NOT closed, and no Encompass field overrules it (`authoritative:'pilot'`, same doctrine as the field registry); **(2)** a dead Encompass state (adverse / declined / withdrawn / cancelled / trash) is never experience however far it got; **(3)** the money moved — a NON-FUTURE funding / funds-sent / funds-released / disbursement date, read from both the V3 paths (`closingDocument.fundingDate`…) and the classic funding-entity ids (1997 / 1999 — NOT 1401, which is the Encompass "Loan Program", not a date: owner-corrected 2026-08-11, so 1401 was dropped from `FUNDED_FIELD_IDS`), since the tenant populates one or the other per loan; **(4)** a closed lifecycle word on the loan folder, the current milestone/stage, or a COMPLETED milestone. Milestone names are tenant-renamed ("LO Prep" / "PREQUAL"), so this reads MEANING, not a fixed list — and deliberately never matches "closing": `Clear to Close` / `Scheduling Closing` / `Active Closing` / a current `Funding` are a file on its WAY to a closing, which is exactly what must not count (a COMPLETED `Funding` milestone does count — that is the one extra word `DONE_CLOSED_WORDS` adds). This matches the ClickUp profile builder, which has always gated its track-record write on a funded status (`CLOSED_STATUSES` in `src/clickup/ingest.js`) — those are now the only two automatic writers, both closed-only. **The pass also UNDOES itself**: `removeInferredTrackRecord` takes a property back OFF when its loan has not closed, but ONLY a pristine row this enrichment wrote (origin `encompass`, unverified/inferred, its own note, no entity/economics/documents/condition — see `PRISTINE_ENRICHMENT_ROW`); the moment a human or ClickUp touches the line it is theirs and is never deleted (`documents.track_record_id` CASCADES, so the documents check is load-bearing). Each removal writes an `encompass_track_record_removed_not_closed` audit row (behind a SAVEPOINT, like the address fill) — the row is gone from the profile, so that line is the only record it was ever there. Removals are settled AFTER the loan loop against the set of (borrower, property) pairs a CLOSED loan supports, so a refinance in flight can never delete the row its own closed purchase earned. The summary reports `addressesSkippedNotClosed` / `addressesRemovedNotClosed` — watch them in the `[encompass] borrower-profile enrichment:` log line; a huge skip count would mean the tenant records closings some way this does not read yet. Tests: `scripts/test-encompass-enrich-pure.js` + `-db.js`.
- **TPO PORTAL — the external brokerage front door (owner-approved 2026-08-04; design + locked decisions + phased roadmap `docs/TPO-PORTAL-BLUEPRINT.md`; db/523 + db/524 + db/525 — the whole TPO block was renumbered to db/523–531 to avoid collisions with main's migrations).** A wholesale / third-party-originator CHANNEL for the RTL product: brokerage FIRMS and their users originate the RTL loans we fund. It is **RTL, not a third product** — a TPO loan is an ordinary `applications` row flagged `is_tpo`, so the product-separation gate stays green (`tpo_*` / `is_tpo`, never `lt_`). NON-NEGOTIABLE invariants:
  1. **Identity.** A TPO user is a `staff_users` row flagged `is_external=true` with `tpo_firm_id` set and role `tpo_officer` / `tpo_processor`. Storing them in staff_users (not a parallel table) is deliberate — `applications.loan_officer_id`, `application_assignees.staff_id`, the "Your loan officer" card (`notify.fileContext`) and every file-scope gate already point at staff_users, so the broker-as-loan-officer mapping (broker = loan officer; our LO = `account_executive` assignee; our processor = `account_manager` assignee) works with no rewrite. DB CHECK invariants (`staff_users_external_firm_check`, `applications_tpo_firm_check`) make an UNSCOPED external identity or an is_tpo file with no firm structurally unwritable.
  2. **Session `kind='tpo'`.** The token carries `kind='tpo'` (NOT `staff`), so `requireStaff`/`requireBorrower` refuse it and `requireTpo` (the /api/tpo wall) is the only door that accepts it. Auth reads/revokes off the SAME staff tables (a tpo row IS a staff_users row); `can()` returns false for a tpo actor, so a tpo session can NEVER satisfy a staff capability gate. The staff door + the borrower-page cross-surface fallback exclude `is_external`, and `/auth/tpo/login` accepts ONLY `is_external=true` — so a broker signs in ONLY at the TPO door and an internal staffer/borrower cannot.
  3. **Firm isolation is the #1 risk, and it lives in ONE place.** `permissions.tpoFirmScopeSql` (files) + `tpoBorrowerScopeSql` (borrowers) are the single definition of "which files/borrowers a TPO user sees" — their own firm's TPO files/borrowers and NOTHING else (never a retail file, never another firm's). Every `/api/tpo` query routes through them (the same discipline as the staff `visibleOfficersSql`); never re-inline a firm scope. A TPO actor is always firm-bounded — there is no `see_all_files` escape for an external user.
  4. **A curated `/api/tpo` router, NOT holes in `/api/staff`.** An external party gets a small, deliberate surface that reuses the lib layer with the borrower-safe filters applied — so a staff-only capability or an internal integration can never be reached by accident. Feature endpoints (pricing, conditions, orders, credit, documents, draws) land here phase by phase.
  5. **Locked decisions (owner 2026-08-04)** — borrower keeps their own login by default, broker may turn it off per borrower (`borrower_portal_enabled`); draws view/accept/dispute; own-Xactus for CREDIT only (flood always ours), optional (our account is always an option); condition sign-off/waive/override is ALWAYS ours (a broker uploads + requests); brokers/borrowers never see internal company contacts; AE/AM can "view as TPO" (Phase 6, mirroring borrower-view). **Sending the official term sheet on DocuSign is lender-only** — gated by a `send_term_sheet` capability a TPO never holds (added in Phase 4; grant it to every existing internal staff role so internal behavior is byte-identical). **Hidden from TPO**: fraud/background/criminal, capital-partner names, internal pricing margin, exceptions/overrides, data tapes, ClickUp/Encompass/SharePoint, closing/purchasing/draw-rules/team/pricing-admin/audit.
  6. **Every INTERNAL `staff_users` roster/picker/fan-out query MUST exclude `is_external=true` — SWEPT in Phase 2, and a STANDING rule for anything new.** Because a broker IS the loan-officer on their firm's files (the identity model), they appear in every unfiltered internal query — roster dropdowns, team lists, assignee/mention pickers, see_all lists, AND the notification fan-out. Done so far: `GET /api/roster`, the internal team list + welcome-all (`admin.js`), global-search officers / mentionables / team / picker (`staff.js`), `mentions.js`, `workflow.js`, `clickup-sync.js`, the Dashboards officer-filter (`dashboards.js`), the public `?lo=` resolver (`leads.js`); and the notification chokepoint — `notify.notifyStaff` returns early for an external user, `notifyAppStaff` filters `is_external=false`, AND `notifyAppStaffThread` filters `is_external=false` on its assignee query (this path emails a DIRECT To list, NOT per-recipient `notifyStaff`, so `notifyStaff`'s external early-return does not cover it — the filter has to live on the query) plus the draw-recipient resolvers `draw-recipients.fileLoanOfficerEmails` / `drawCoordinatorsForFile` / `drawDeskCoordinators` (a TPO file's loan_officer IS the broker, so without this the broker would be looped into internal draw threads and draw emails), so a broker never receives an internal-format staff email or digest (they get the /api/tpo surface instead). `is_external` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, so `= false` never drops a legitimate internal row. Guarded by the TPO-broker-excluded section in `scripts/test-draw-loop-in-db.js`. When you add ANY new internal roster/picker/see_all query or staff notification fan-out, add the `is_external=false` filter. Phase 2 (onboarding + the front door) is BUILT — the internal admin firm surface (`admin-tpo.js`), the firm-admin team invite (`tpo.js`), the `app-v2` third door (`TpoLogin`/`TpoAccept`/`TpoLayout`/`TpoPipeline`/`TpoTeam` + `isTpo` route guards), and `db/525` (the `tpo` invite kind + firm/firm-admin flags). Tests `scripts/test-tpo-foundation-pure.js` + `scripts/test-tpo-identity-db.js` (both in `npm test`). Build the front end on V2 only.
  7. **Phase 3 (files, borrowers & PII) is BUILT, and two structural invariants must never be broken.** (a) **BORROWER RESOLUTION NEVER ADOPTS A PROFILE OUTSIDE THE FIRM.** `POST /api/tpo/applications` (enter a loan) resolves the borrower SAFELY: a picked `borrowerId` must already pass `tpoBorrowerScopeSql` (be in the firm's book), else 404; otherwise a FRESH `borrowers` row is created — and when the email is already owned by another profile (`shares_email=false`), the new row is `shares_email=true` (a DISTINCT person who happens to share the address). It NEVER does `ON CONFLICT (email) DO UPDATE` onto an existing owner, because a broker's TPO file pointing at a retail/other-firm borrower would expose that borrower's PII through the firm scope. Every borrower read/write (`GET/PATCH /borrowers/:id`, `GET/POST /borrowers/:id/ssn`) is guarded by `borrowerInFirm`; an SSN clash is refused GENERICALLY (never the staff cross-profile move flow — a broker must not learn another profile exists). SSN reveal/set and every PII edit are audited (`actor_kind='staff'`, the tpo user's id, `detail.via='tpo'`). (b) **THE BORROWER-LOGIN TOGGLE IS ENFORCED AT THE ONE `OWN_FILE_SQL` CHOKEPOINT** (`src/lib/change-requests.js`, which `routes/borrower.js` delegates to). `POST /api/tpo/applications/:id/borrower-portal` flips `applications.borrower_portal_enabled`; the chokepoint appends `AND NOT (is_tpo=true AND borrower_portal_enabled=false)`, so a portal-disabled TPO file vanishes from the borrower's entire portal (list, detail, checklist, documents, messages — everything routes through `OWN_FILE_SQL`) — AND, from 2026-08-11, from their EMAIL too: `notify.notifyAppBorrowers` returns `[]` for a portal-disabled TPO file so no file-activity email (which would link to a portal they can't open) goes out (see item 18). It is a NO-OP for every retail file (`is_tpo=false`), both columns are `NOT NULL`, and retail borrower access is byte-identical (proven by `test-borrower-view-db` + `test-borrower-change-approval`). Test `scripts/test-tpo-files-db.js` (in `npm test`). When adding a TPO write path, route borrower/file access through `borrowerInFirm`/`appInFirm`/the scope SQL — never a raw id check.
  8. **Phase 4a (conditions, documents + the term-sheet-send lock) is BUILT.** (a) **SENDING THE OFFICIAL TERM SHEET IS LENDER-ONLY, ENFORCED BY A CAPABILITY.** `send_term_sheet` (`permissions.js`) is granted to EVERY internal staff role — so internal send behavior is byte-identical (verified by `test-esign-send`) — and `routes/staff.js` gates `POST /applications/:id/esign/send` AND `POST /esign/:id/resend` on `can(req.actor,'send_term_sheet')`. `can()` returns false for ANY non-staff actor, so a TPO can NEVER hold it; combined with the send route being staff-only, a broker is doubly blocked from sending the signable package. A broker uploads + requests; WE send. NEVER add `send_term_sheet` to a TPO role, and NEVER remove the gate. Pinned by `test-tpo-foundation-pure` (every internal role has it; a tpo actor forging it onto `perms` still gets false). (b) **THE TPO CONDITION + DOCUMENT SURFACE IS BORROWER-SAFE BY THE SAME `audience` MECHANISM AS THE BORROWER PORTAL.** `GET /api/tpo/applications/:id/checklist` returns ONLY `audience IN ('borrower','both')` items, with `borrower_label`/`borrower_hint` wording, capital-partner names scrubbed (`borrower-safe.scrubFields`), and the internal `ci.notes` NEVER selected — a staff-only condition is structurally invisible. `GET /api/tpo/applications/:id/documents` returns ONLY `visibility='borrower'` documents (a `staff_only`/`internal` doc — fraud report, internal appraisal source — never appears), scrubbed. `POST /api/tpo/documents` uploads against a borrower-facing condition on the firm's file (`uploaded_by_kind='staff'`, the broker's id; `visibility='borrower'`); new evidence clears any prior sign-off so the LENDER re-reviews — a broker never signs off/waives/clears (those stay staff-only). All firm-scoped via `appInFirm`. The staff-only order conditions (flood cert / credit / appraisal received) are DELIBERATELY still hidden — they carry no `borrower_label`, and revealing them ("some of the internal conditions") is a Phase 4b migration that adds borrower-safe wording. Test `scripts/test-tpo-files-db.js`.
  9. **Phase 4b (the TPO Term Sheet Studio — PRICE / REGISTER / generate the term sheet) is BUILT.** A broker prices and registers a product on their firm's file exactly as a borrower does — the SAME frozen engine, the SAME borrower-safe scrub — and the studio generates the term-sheet PDF; but WE send the official DocuSign package (lender-only, per Phase 4a). Invariants:
      · **The margin scrub + the override allowlist are SINGLE-DEFINITION.** `stripQuoteInternal`/`stripInputsInternal`/`borrowerSafeQuoteBundle` moved from `routes/borrower.js` into `src/lib/borrower-safe.js`; `borrowerPricingOverrides` moved into `src/lib/pricing-overrides.js` (co-located with `sanitizeStaffOverrides`). Both `routes/borrower.js` and `routes/tpo.js` require them — so a broker can never be handed a wider override allowlist, or shown internal margin, by drift. Every broker-facing quote (`GET /pricing` history rows + live quote, `POST /pricing/quote`, the register response) is scrubbed of `adminPricing`/`markup*Pct`/`fico`; a broker's overrides pass through `borrowerPricingOverrides` (clamped: targetLTC/irMonths/irAmount/term/fico/exp* — no markup/origination/fees/manual basis) and the register DELETES exp* (the registered basis uses the file's experience of record). Never re-inline the scrub.
      · **`GET /api/tpo/applications/:id` returns the full BORROWER-SAFE application shape** (deal economics + party names + refinance payoff + waiver flags + higher-of-two FICO) so the frozen studio prefills — DELIBERATELY absent: `applications.lender` (the NOTE BUYER) and internal margin. `payoff_lender` IS returned and is borrower-safe (the borrower's EXISTING loan being paid off on a refinance, never our capital partner).
      · **`POST /api/tpo/applications/:id/pricing/register`** mirrors the borrower register's guards (`structuralLockReason` actor-less, econVersion, `asIsMissing`, vesting refusal, INELIGIBLE/no-loan/MANUAL-exception, `quoteStorageProblem`) + the same post-commit condition side effects (`evaluateApplication`, `syncLiquidityCondition`, `enforceGoldSowContingency`, product_pricing reopen), with `registeredByStaffId = the broker` (a valid `staff_users` FK). THREE deliberate differences: firm-scoped via `loadTpoFileForPricing` (→ `tpoFirmScopeSql`); **NO borrower "terms ready" email** (WE send); **NO ClickUp push** (a TPO file doesn't sync to ClickUp in this phase — matching the create route). It returns `termSheetFinal` (like the STAFF route) so the studio stamps the exact PDF FINAL/INITIAL — a fresh file is always **INITIAL** (send-gate blockers present), and sending stays lender-only.
      · **The studio's term-sheet PDF uploads through `POST /api/tpo/documents` with `docKind:'term_sheet'` ONLY** (a broker can NEVER forge another kind — the "that kind only" pattern, db/424 §2b): born ACCEPTED, auto-attached to the product_pricing condition, records `term_sheet_final` (a description of the bytes, never an authorization — db/404), and supersedes the prior so exactly one is current. Front end: `ProductStudioPanel` gained `mode==='tpo'` — it ALIASES borrower behavior for every scrub/lock/admin-hidden decision and takes the parties' names from the file like staff; only the data-load / register / upload API calls branch. Mounted as a card in `TpoFile`. Test `scripts/test-tpo-pricing-db.js`.
  10. **Phase 4c (the lender-progress REVEAL + the broker INFO-FIELD writer) is BUILT.** (a) **THE REVEAL IS A TPO-SURFACE ALLOWLIST, NOT A CONDITION-AUDIENCE CHANGE.** `src/lib/tpo-conditions.js` is the ONE definition of which staff-audience order-driven conditions a broker may SEE, read-only, with HAND-AUTHORED broker-safe wording: exactly **`rtl_cond_credit`** ("Credit report") and **`rtl_cond_appraisaldocs`** ("Appraisal"). `GET /api/tpo/applications/:id/checklist` returns them in a separate `progress[]` array (`readOnly:true`, `item_kind:'progress'`) alongside the actionable `checklist[]`. It NEVER changes a condition's `audience`, so the borrower portal and every staff surface are byte-identical, and a broker can never act on a revealed row (the upload door already refuses a staff condition). The internal label / hint / note / rejection reason of a staff condition is NEVER exposed — only the fixed safe wording + the raw status. **HARD RULES baked into the allowlist — never add a code that breaks one:** the FLOOD CERTIFICATE (`rtl_cond_flood`) and FLOOD INSURANCE (`rtl_cond_flood_insurance`) are NEVER revealed (owner-directed 2026-07-30 — flood cert is internal on every file); fraud (`rtl_cond_fraud`), the review gates (`appraisal_review_cleared`/`underwriting_review_cleared`), investor review/CTC (`rtl_f_review`/`rtl_f_ctc`) stay hidden; title/insurance CONTACT (`rtl_p1_titlec`/`rtl_p1_insc`) + EMD (`cond_emd_corrfirst`) are already `audience='both'` and shown by the ordinary path, so they are deliberately NOT on the allowlist (never revealed twice). (b) **THE INFO-FIELD WRITER handles DEAL (applications) + custom fields ONLY.** `POST /api/tpo/applications/:id/checklist/:itemId/info` — a broker answers an information condition (audience borrower/both, `tool_key='info_field'`), the ONLY door a broker has to fill a missing DEAL field (there is no TPO details-edit door). It routes through the ONE `conditionEngine.writeFieldValue` (single-definition governance: validates + fits the column, and for an applications field applies the file freeze via `payoffContactLockReason` called ACTOR-LESS so a broker never inherits a super-admin unlock, + the refinance purchase-price guard) — so a broker gets the SAME refusal, same wording, on the same frozen field as a borrower/staffer. **PARITY WITH THE BORROWER on a REGISTERED file (audit 2026-08-05):** once a product is registered, a change-requestable economics field (`purchase_price`/`as_is_value`/`arv`/`rehab_budget`/`units`/`program`/`loan_type`/`property_type` — `changeRequests.isChangeRequestable` + `isBorrowerLocked`) may NOT be written LIVE; it becomes a `change_requests` row the loan team approves (the broker is a `staff_users` row, attributed `requested_by_kind='staff'`, marked `via:'tpo'`; the existing generic approve/reject flow applies it) — confirming the value already on file (`unchanged`) just marks the condition received. The shared staff approve/reject handlers (`routes/staff.js`) gate the borrower "your requested change was approved/reviewed" notification on `requested_by_kind='borrower'` — a BROKER's request never misattributes to the borrower and never reaches a portal-disabled TPO borrower (the broker sees the result on their file; a dedicated broker notification channel is future TPO work). Do NOT un-gate that notification. The FREEZE is checked FIRST inside that branch (a frozen file's economics change must 409, never silently become a change request — the exact ordering the borrower door documents). A PERSON field (`fico` → borrowers) is NOT answerable inline (the checklist marks `answerable:false`) and the route redirects it to the borrower's profile door (its own change-request governance) — never written from a condition answer. The `answerable` flag is precise: true only for an applications write-target field or a CUSTOM field (`!BY_KEY`); a non-writable built-in (`note_buyer`/`ltv`/`ys_loan_number`) is `answerable:false` AND refused by the writer (double-defends the staff-only note buyer). The reveal `progress[]` is deduped by code (a co-borrower's separate credit pull makes a 2nd `rtl_cond_credit` staff item; the broker sees ONE "Credit report", least-advanced status). NO ClickUp push (a TPO file doesn't sync). Notifies OUR staff (`notifyAppStaff` filters external users, so the broker is never a recipient), audited `tpo_answer_info_condition`. `app-v2 TpoFile` renders an inline answer input for `answerable` rows + a read-only "Lender progress" card from `progress[]`. Test `scripts/test-tpo-conditions-db.js` (in `npm test`; 26 assertions — reveal excludes a seeded flood cert, credit/appraisal shown safe, info answer writes a deal field + firm isolation + fico redirect + freeze parity). (c) **RESOLVED (owner-directed 2026-08-11 — see item 18): a broker's brand-new intake file now DOES carry its rule-driven conditions right away.** A TPO file is still created at `file_intake`, but the conditions engine now admits a TPO intake file (`engineOpenStatus`), so `evaluateApplication` attaches the `always`/`rules` conditions (flood cert, EMD/CorrFirst, condo, cash-out, flood insurance, feasibility) at creation instead of waiting for our team to activate the file — and the note buyer is stamped from the program at creation. The change is scoped to `is_tpo` files only; a non-TPO file at `file_intake` is gated exactly as before.
  11. **Phase 5a (a broker ORDERS a credit pull) is BUILT.** `POST /api/tpo/applications/:id/credit/order` orders a fresh LIVE credit pull on OUR Xactus account, reusing the ONE staff machinery `credit.importCredit` — the SAME server-side FCRA consent gate (`opts.consent===true`, enforced in `credit/index.js`) and prerequisite checks (SSN + address; a missing SSN is refused before any wire call). **THE RESULT IS BORROWER-SAFE — this is the invariant to protect:** `importCredit` produces the parsed `credit_reports` row, the `credit_pdf`/`credit_xml` documents at `visibility='staff_only'`, and the FICO→`borrowers.fico`→Products-&-Pricing reopen (db/126), exactly as a staff pull — all of which stay staff-only. The TPO route NEVER returns the `importCredit` `out` object (which carries `middleScore`/`ficoWritten`/report ids); it returns only `{ok, message}`. `GET /api/tpo/applications/:id/credit` returns per-borrower READINESS only — name / hasSsn / canPull / `missing` PII field names / `hasReport` (a boolean off `preview`'s `reissueReportId`) — and DELIBERATELY DROPS `preview`'s `profileReport.middleScore` / `reissueReportId` / `fromLoanNumber` (a cross-file leak). Firm-scoped via `appInFirm`; `pull_credit` is NOT (and can't be) held by a TPO actor, so the firm scope + consent IS the authorization on the `/api/tpo` door. A broker order is always `requestType:'new'` (fresh pull) on our account; the pull uses `config.xactusProd` (the provider seam `credit/provider.js:25`). `app-v2 TpoFile` has a "Credit" card (readiness + an Order-credit button with a `window.confirm` consent). Notifies OUR staff in-app (`tool_submitted`), audited `tpo_order_credit`. Test `scripts/test-tpo-credit-db.js` (in `npm test`; stubs `provider.pull`+`configured` — no live Xactus — and asserts the readiness + order results leak NO score/report id, the `credit_reports` row + condition→received + staff-only docs DID land, the consent gate, the missing-SSN refusal, and firm isolation). **own-Xactus (per-firm encrypted credit credentials) is now BUILT — see item 17.** **Title/insurance ORDERING (except RCN) + the intake-conditions/note-buyer-stamp answers are now BUILT — see item 18; flood ordering stays staff-only (owner-directed: "a broker cannot order flood insurance").** When adding a broker-facing credit surface, NEVER return the `importCredit` output object or a score/report/document to the broker — return only a safe confirmation + readiness.
  12. **Phase 6a (a broker VIEWS the borrower-safe appraisal — the "property profile report") is BUILT.** A broker SEES the same scrubbed appraisal the BORROWER sees, read-only, and can take NO action on it. **THE SCRUB IS SINGLE-DEFINITION — this is the invariant to protect:** the borrower's inline appraisal scrub was EXTRACTED into `src/lib/appraisal/borrower-safe-view.js` `buildBorrowerSafeAppraisalView(db, appId)`, and BOTH `routes/borrower.js` (`GET /api/borrower/applications/:id/appraisal`, refactored to call it — byte-identical, proven by `test-borrower-hidden-info`) AND `routes/tpo.js` (`GET /api/tpo/applications/:id/appraisal`, firm-scoped via `appInFirm`) return it, so the two surfaces can NEVER drift and leak the lender's internal view to an external broker (the TPO #1 risk). The scrub DROPS the staff-only appraisal columns (`lender_name`/`amc_name`/`owner_of_record`/`lender_address`/`fields`/`warnings`/appraiser contact/supervisor/`software_vendor`/`imported_by`/source-doc ids), HIDES the underwriting-scrutiny findings (`SCRUTINY_CODES`: arv_defensibility/value_vs_comps/value_not_bracketed/asis_below_price/comp_split_review) and every `source='note_buyer'` finding (their titles name a buyer), SCRUBS visible finding titles via `scrubText`, sets `score.arv=null` (ARV-defensibility is staff-only), and — when a HIDDEN finding is a fatal CTC blocker — surfaces ONE neutral `appraisal_under_review` placeholder so a broker is never told a gated file is clear. `appraiser_company`/`appraiser_name`/`borrower_name` are DELIBERATELY kept (standard "prepared by" disclosure + the broker's own borrower), exactly as the borrower route does. Because the payload is byte-identical to the owner-blessed borrower boundary, the broker surface is exactly as safe as the borrower's — never more exposed. **THE PHOTO ROUTE IS NOT A DOWNLOAD-ANY-DOCUMENT HOLE:** `GET /api/tpo/appraisal-photo/:docId` authorizes via the `appraisal_photos → appraisals → applications` join filtered by `perms.tpoFirmScopeSql` + `d.is_current` (the research-photo discipline), so the ONLY bytes a broker can fetch are appraisal photos of THEIR OWN firm's files — never an arbitrary staff-only document by id; the filename is scrubbed. Front end: `AppraisalPanel` gained a `source` prop (`'tpo'` channel via `photoFetcherFor` + `api.tpoAppraisal`/`api.tpoAppraisalPhotoBlob`, threaded to `PhotoGallery`/`HeroPhoto`); every existing caller is unchanged (`source` defaults null → the historic readOnly/staff split). `app-v2 TpoFile` mounts `<AppraisalPanel appId={id} readOnly source="tpo" />` in an Appraisal card. Test `scripts/test-tpo-appraisal-db.js` (in `npm test`; asserts the drops/hides/placeholder/arv-null, NO partner-name leak anywhere, byte-identical to the shared view, firm isolation on the report AND the photo bytes, the photo route refusing a non-appraisal / cross-firm / unknown doc id, and the empty-appraisal shape). When adding another broker-facing appraisal surface, route it through `buildBorrowerSafeAppraisalView` — never re-inline the scrub, and never widen the photo route into a generic document download.
  13. **Phase 6b (a broker VIEWS the draws — the construction-draw progress) is BUILT, READ-ONLY.** A broker SEES the same borrower-safe draw picture the BORROWER sees — the construction budget vs. what's released, the per-line rollup, each inspection result + its photos, and the branded PDF — and can take NO action (accept/dispute starts a wire, a borrower money decision, deferred to a later increment with the owner). **THE SCRUB IS SINGLE-DEFINITION — this is the invariant to protect:** the borrower's inline draw scrub was EXTRACTED into `src/sitewire/borrower-safe-draws.js` — `borrowerSafeRollup(rollup)` (scrub SOW-line labels + drop the per-draw `fee_kind` [our fee SCHEDULE] + the STAFF `net_explanation`, re-adding the BORROWER wording, + delete `rollup.fees` [our fee income ACROSS the project]) and `loadDrawFindings(db, appId, {photoUrl})` (scrub each line name / inspector comment / media note of a capital-partner name, drop media GPS) + `loadDrawList(db, appId)`. BOTH `routes/borrower-draws.js` (the rollup + findings routes, refactored to call them — byte-identical, proven by `test-sitewire-borrower-draw` + `test-draw-dispute-evidence`) AND `routes/tpo.js` (`GET /api/tpo/applications/:id/draws`, firm-scoped via `appInFirm`) use them, so the two surfaces can NEVER drift and leak OUR fee income / fee schedule or a capital-partner NAME to an external broker. The per-draw money the borrower is PAID (net_release_cents) stays — the broker sees exactly what the borrower sees.
      · **THE PHOTOS ARE FIRM-SCOPED, NOT THE reply_token.** The borrower draw findings embed the borrower's per-finding **`reply_token`** in photo URLs — and that token is a PUBLIC capability (`/api/public/draw-findings/:token/…`) that ALSO permits **accept + dispute** (a wire). So the TPO surface must NEVER receive it: `loadDrawFindings` takes a `photoUrl(finding, media)` callback (the borrower passes the reply_token URL; TPO passes `/api/tpo/draw-media/:id`) and the module NEVER emits the `reply_token` field/value. `GET /api/tpo/draw-media/:mediaId` authorizes via `draw_media → applications` filtered by `tpoFirmScopeSql` (image/video only, `setMediaHeaders` allowlist + sandbox CSP), so the ONLY bytes a broker can fetch are inspection media of THEIR OWN firm's files — never the public token, never a download-any-document hole. `GET /api/tpo/applications/:id/draws/report` forces `mode='borrower'` (the borrower-safe PDF; a broker can never get the staff copy). Front end: `app-v2/src/components/TpoDraws.jsx` (NEW, slim read-only — photos blob-fetched WITH auth like the appraisal photos, since an `<img src>` can't send the bearer token), mounted as a Draws card in `TpoFile`. Test `scripts/test-tpo-draws-db.js` (in `npm test`; asserts the fee-income/fee-schedule drop, the partner-name scrub across line name / comment / media note, GPS dropped, the reply_token NEVER present, photo urls firm-scoped, byte-identical to the shared scrub, firm isolation on the endpoint + report + media bytes, the media route refusing a non-image / cross-firm / unknown id, and the empty shape). When adding another broker-facing draw surface, route it through `borrower-safe-draws.js` — never re-inline the scrub, and never expose the reply_token to a broker.
  14. **Phase 6c (AE/AM "view as TPO" — the impersonation feature) is BUILT.** An internal account executive / account manager / admin steps INTO a broker's login and sees exactly what the broker sees — the EXACT mirror of BORROWER VIEW (`src/lib/borrower-view.js`), one identity swapped (a broker is an external `staff_users` row, not a borrower). `src/lib/tpo-view.js` + `src/routes/tpo-view.js` (`/api/tpo-view/{eligible,start,session,exit,history}`, mounted OUTSIDE /api/staff so session/exit work with a `kind='tpo'` token) + `tpoView.guard` (mounted globally in server.js) + `db/526_tpo_view_sessions.sql`.
      · **THE TOKEN IS A REAL `kind:'tpo'` ACCESS TOKEN + THE SAME IMPERSONATION ENVELOPE** (`imp/impBy/impRole/impTv/impSid/impAt`), so every /api/tpo route works unchanged. `authenticate()` re-validates BOTH identities on every request: the BROKER's token_version (the main session query, since `claims.sub` is the broker — so a firm SUSPEND, which bumps the broker's tv, kills the view) AND the INTERNAL staffer's row (still active, **is_external=false**, token_version unchanged, session inside its 4h absolute cap anchored on `impAt`). A tpo-view dies the moment the staffer logs out / is deactivated / the firm is suspended / the cap elapses. The impersonator is INTERNAL-ONLY — an external broker can NEVER start a view (checked in `startSession`'s staff query AND the auth re-validation).
      · **THE TWO IMPERSONATION SURFACES ARE STRICTLY SEPARATE.** `tpoView.readImpersonation` keys on `claims.kind==='tpo'`, `borrowerView.readImpersonation` on `'borrower'` — mutually exclusive, so one surface's guard/refresh never fires on the other's token. `req.impersonation.surface` (`'tpo'` | `'borrower'`) is set in `authenticate()` and the sliding-refresh re-mint BRANCHES on it, so a tpo-view token ALWAYS re-mints into another tpo-view token (kind:'tpo' + envelope, same `impAt`), NEVER a borrower or plain-tpo token — getting this wrong would strip the envelope and turn a bounded, audited view into a permanent unattributed session on the wrong portal.
      · **FIRM ISOLATION IS THE #1 RISK AND LIVES IN ONE PLACE.** `tpoView.ELIGIBLE_TPO_SQL(p)` reuses the SAME `permissions.visibleOfficersSql('a', p)` file scope: a staffer reaches a broker ONLY when that broker's FIRM has a file the staffer can already open (`is_tpo=true AND tpo_firm_id = t.tpo_firm_id AND NOT deleted AND visibleOfficersSql`). On a TPO file `loan_officer_id` is the broker (external), so a staffer never matches through it — the AE/AM match comes through the assignee (`account_executive`/`account_manager`) / workflow terms. `see_all_files` (admins) drops the scope clause (the fragment is a FUNCTION of the placeholder — a hardcoded `$1` would 42P18 like the borrower-view bug). `resolveViewable`/`listEligible` additionally require the broker `is_external=true`, `is_active=true`, and the firm `status='active'`. `can()` returns false for a tpo actor, so inside a view the staffer can never satisfy a staff capability gate; `requireStaff`/`requireBorrower` refuse the tpo-view token.
      · **THE BLOCKLIST is the tiny borrower-view-style set** (`tpoView.BLOCKED`, guarded structurally in `tpoView.guard`): **order credit** (`POST /api/tpo/applications/:id/credit/order` — a LIVE Xactus pull is an irreversible hard inquiry in the broker's name, the TPO analogue of borrower-view's e-sign block; WE can order credit from the console under our own identity), `/auth/logout` (would sign the real broker out of their devices), `/auth/mfa/*` (their 2FA), and nesting (`/api/tpo-view/start`). There is deliberately NO term-sheet-send block — a broker structurally can't send (send_term_sheet is lender-only + the send route is staff-only + `can()` refuses a tpo actor). Everything else is FULL PARITY, audited with the real staffer's id (`impersonator_staff_id`, db/320; the tpo user audits as `actor_kind='staff'` — the audit_log CHECK is borrower|staff|system).
      · **Front end (app-v2):** `StaffTpoView.jsx` picker at `/internal/tpo-view` (nav "Broker view", scoped by `/api/tpo-view/eligible` so AE/AM see their firms' brokers, admins see all); `TpoViewBanner.jsx` (self-fetches from `/api/tpo-view/session`, gold sticky bar + "← Back to my console") mounted in `TpoLayout` (which also swaps its nav "Sign out" → the exit while in a view, so a staffer can't `/auth/logout` the broker and strand themselves); `startTpoView`/`exitTpoView`/`isTpoView` in `auth.jsx` (mirroring the borrower-view handoff — park the staff token in sessionStorage, swap in the tpo-view token; `isBorrowerView` was refined to require `kind==='borrower'` so a tpo-view isn't misread as a borrower-view). Tests `scripts/test-tpo-view-pure.js` (90 assertions — token/envelope/cap/blocklist/guard + the two surfaces never resolve each other) + `scripts/test-tpo-view-db.js` (46 assertions — the firm-level scope, suspended-firm/inactive-broker refusal, internal-only impersonator, session register lifecycle, the impersonator audit stamp), both in `npm test`. When adding another broker-facing view surface, route it through `tpo-view.js` — never a second impersonation mechanism, and keep the `surface` branch in the auth refresh exact.
  15. **Phase 6d (a broker ACCEPTS / DISPUTES a construction-draw inspection result) is BUILT — the ONE thing on the broker draw surface that MOVES MONEY.** Owner-locked decision 2 (2026-08-04): brokers "view / accept / dispute on funded files, **like a borrower**". Phase 6b built the draw VIEW and held accept/dispute; this completes it. `POST /api/tpo/applications/:id/findings/:findingId/{accept,dispute}` (db/527).
      · **AUTHENTICATED + FIRM-SCOPED, NEVER the reply_token — the #1 invariant.** Accepting flips the finding `delivered→accepted` and starts the wire SLA, exactly as the borrower's AUTHENTICATED accept does (`routes/borrower-draws.js`). The broker acts through this `/api/tpo` door (a real `kind='tpo'` session; firm isolation via `appInFirm` AND the finding PINNED to the named file — `findingOnFile` selects `WHERE id=$1 AND application_id=$2`, so a finding on another file 404s even for the owning firm). The borrower's per-finding **`reply_token`** — a PUBLIC capability that ALSO permits accept/dispute (a wire) — never reaches a broker: these routes take a `findingId`, not a token, and Phase 6b already served draw photos through the firm-scoped media route for exactly this reason. NEVER add a broker path that consumes the reply_token.
      · **ATTRIBUTED — a broker IS a `staff_users` row, so the money action is traceable.** db/527 widens `accepted_via`/`disputed_via` to allow `'tpo'` and adds `disputed_by_staff_id` (parity with db/454's `accepted_by_staff_id`); accept stamps `accepted_via='tpo'` + `accepted_by_staff_id`=the broker, dispute stamps `disputed_via='tpo'` + `disputed_by_staff_id`, and both write a semantic `audit_log` row (`tpo_accept_draw`/`tpo_dispute_draw`, `actor_kind='staff'` via `tpoAudit`, `impersonator_staff_id` set when an AE/AM is inside a "view as TPO" session). The guarded `WHERE status='delivered'` transition means whoever acts first (the borrower on a portal-enabled file, or the broker) wins; a lost race is a clean 409, never a double action. Accept is idempotent (a repeat → `already:true`); dispute-after-accept and re-dispute → 409.
      · **On a portal-DISABLED TPO file the broker's accept is the ONLY path** (the borrower has no portal — `OWN_FILE_SQL` excludes the file), which is precisely why the owner locked broker accept/dispute. `appInFirm` does not read the `borrower_portal_enabled` toggle, so the broker can act regardless.
      · **A staffer inside a "view as TPO" session CANNOT accept/dispute** — `tpo-view.js BLOCKED` includes `POST …/findings/:id/(accept|dispute)` (code `draw_action`), the SAME segregation-of-duties line as the credit-order block (and the borrower-view e-sign block): a money decision in the broker's name is the broker's own, and a staffer makes it from the staff draw desk (`accepted_via='staff'`, attributed to them). Draws VIEW/report/media stay available in a view.
      · **db/527 widens the CHECKs IN PLACE under db/454's/db/193's OWN constraint names** (guarded by `pg_get_constraintdef … LIKE '%tpo%'`), NOT under new names. Every migration replays on every boot, so db/454/db/193 re-run BEFORE db/527 and re-add their constraint `IF NOT EXISTS(<own name>)`; widening under a NEW name deletes their names, so they re-add the NARROW list and FAIL against a `'tpo'` row — and migrate-boot's benign-superseded detection keys on a LATER file re-adding the SAME name, so it can't recognize it. Re-adding under their names means their guard finds the wide constraint and skips. The `test-tpo-draws-db.js` replay assertion (re-apply db/193+454+479 with a `'tpo'` row present) is the regression guard. Apply this pattern to any future CHECK-widening in a chain.
      · **The dispute-evidence normalizer is SINGLE-DEFINITION — `src/sitewire/dispute-media.js`** (`sniffImageMime` + `normalizeDisputeMedia`), extracted from the borrower route so the borrower AND broker surfaces store dispute evidence IDENTICALLY on this security-critical sniff/strip/cap layer. Image type is derived from the BYTES (a `text/html`/`image/svg+xml` upload is stored XSS against the staff who open it → rejected), GPS is stripped, size + per-line count are capped, and only a real upload (`dataBase64`) is honored — a client-supplied `storage_ref` is NEVER trusted. `routes/borrower-draws.js` now requires it (byte-identical, proven by the borrower draw tests). Front end: `TpoDraws.jsx` `TpoFindingCard` gained Accept (behind a confirm — money moves) + a per-line Dispute editor (amount + note; server-scrubbed) for a `delivered` finding, calling `api.tpoDrawAccept`/`tpoDrawDispute` and reloading on success. Tests `scripts/test-dispute-media-pure.js` (the sniff/reject/cap truth table) + Phase-6d section of `scripts/test-tpo-draws-db.js` (firm isolation, IDOR, attribution, durable GPS-free evidence, idempotency, the portal-disabled scenario, audit rows), both in `npm test`. When adding another broker draw action, route it through `/api/tpo` with `appInFirm` + a file-pinned lookup, stamp `*_via='tpo'` + the `*_by_staff_id`, and never touch the reply_token.
  16. **Phase 6e (broker ↔ our-team MESSAGING) is BUILT — the LAST deferred TPO item.** Owner-approved scope: "messaging with our team". A broker IS a `staff_users` row, but must be DISTINCT from our internal team in the roster and attribution, so they post/join as a NEW kind **`tpo`** (`messages.sender_kind`, `conversation_members.member_kind`, `conversations.kind`), never as `staff`.

A broker is never emailed a chat message in v1 (a borrower-safe broker EMAIL is deferred to v1.1); OUR staff (the AE/AM seated `member_kind='staff'`) ARE emailed normally. Never add an email path that reaches a `tpo` member without running the borrower-safe scrub on the body first.
      · **`ensureTpoConversation(appId)`** creates the kind='tpo' conversation (🤝 "Broker ↔ Team", **NOT `borrower_visible`**) for an `is_tpo` file, seats the broker (`loan_officer_id` when `is_external`) as `member_kind='tpo'`, and seats our AE/AM (`application_assignees` role IN account_executive/account_manager, active, `is_external=false`) as `member_kind='staff'`. **`tpoCanAccess(actorId, conv)`** = `conv.kind==='tpo'` AND the app is in the actor's firm via `permissions.tpoFirmScopeSql` — the SAME firm-isolation chokepoint every other `/api/tpo` query routes through; never re-inline a firm scope.
      · **A SECURITY FIX rode with this: `ensureConversationsForApp` no longer seats an EXTERNAL staffer as a `staff` member of the internal/borrower/lo_processor chats.** On a TPO file the `loan_officer_id` IS the external broker, so the pre-existing member-seeding was seating the broker as a `staff` member of the internal "Loan Team" chat — which would have emailed our team's raw internal messages to the broker. The seeding WHERE now excludes `kind IN ('custom','tpo')` AND external staffers; **db/528's DELETE cleanup removes any external `staff` membership already seated on a non-tpo conversation** (a broker's ONLY chat is the tpo one, so an external `staff` membership anywhere else is always wrong).
      · **`src/routes/tpo-chat.js`** is the firm-scoped, borrower-safe router (mounted inside `tpo.js` via `router.use(require('./tpo-chat'))`), mirroring `borrower-chat.js`: `/chat/conversations`, `/chat/conversations/:cid[/messages|/read|/unread|/delivered|/typing|/open|/draft|/shared]`, and a firm-scoped `/chat/attachment/:docId`.       · **Front end:** `ChatThread.jsx` gained `surface='tpo'` — the broker gets a CLEAN message thread (react/pin/edit/delete/rename/mute/search/mentionables all null; download via `tpoDownloadChatAttachment`); `TpoMessages.jsx` fetches `api.tpoConversations(appId)` for the cid + `me` and renders `<ChatThread surface="tpo">`; mounted as a "Messages" card in `TpoFile.jsx`.   17. **Phase 5b (own-Xactus — a brokerage firm pulls CREDIT on its OWN Xactus account) is BUILT, and the safety property is that it is INERT UNTIL CONFIGURED.** Owner-locked decision 5: own-Xactus for CREDIT only (flood always ours), OPTIONAL (our shared account is always an option).   18. **A broker ORDERS title & insurance (except RCN), and a broker's brand-new file gets its conditions + the note-buyer stamp RIGHT AWAY (owner-directed 2026-08-11: "They should allow ordering for everyone other than RCN … It should be for our staff … they only see our mortgage clause … updated for the TPO as the loan officer. A broker cannot order flood insurance … a broker's brand-new file should get the conditions right away and have the note buyer's stamp on it").** Four owner directives, all here.
      · **INTAKE CONDITIONS — the conditions engine now admits a TPO intake file.** A TPO file is created at `file_intake` (outside `OPEN_STATUSES`), so `evaluateApplication` used to early-return and the `always`/`rules` conditions never attached until our team activated the file. `conditions/engine.js` `engineOpenStatus(app)` = `OPEN_STATUSES.includes(status) OR (is_tpo === true AND status === 'file_intake')` — the ONE predicate at the line-313 gate AND (as `openStatusSql(p)`) in the two bulk sweeps, kept in step. So a broker's brand-new file carries its rule-driven conditions (flood cert, EMD/CorrFirst, condo, cash-out, flood insurance, feasibility) at creation, via the `generateChecklist → evaluateApplication` call the create route already makes. SCOPED TO `is_tpo` ONLY — a non-TPO file at `file_intake` is gated exactly as before. PREVIOUS AND FUTURE: `engine.backfillTpoIntakeConditionsOnce()` (booted in `server.js`) runs the engine over existing intake files once so they pick up their conditions too; idempotent (per-(file,template) suppression), bounded, best-effort. **The register path now fires the ordinary borrower-safe `condition_added` notifications (it didn't before — the engine early-returned), which is consistent with retail; BUT a broker who turned OFF the borrower's login must not then be emailed (owner-directed 2026-08-11: "stop their emails too"), because the email links to a portal the borrower can no longer open.** So `notify.notifyAppBorrowers` (the ONE borrower fan-out chokepoint) now returns `[]` for `is_tpo && borrower_portal_enabled === false` (escape hatch `opts.evenIfPortalDisabled`), silencing EVERY borrower-facing email on a portal-disabled TPO file — the notification analogue of the `OWN_FILE_SQL` portal gate. Retail files and portal-enabled TPO files are untouched (`a.is_tpo &&` short-circuits); staff notifications are NOT gated.
      · **NOTE-BUYER STAMP AT CREATION — `tpo.js autoSetTpoNoteBuyer(appId, program, req)` is the ONE definition**, called at file-create AND register (register still needs it — a file created under one program then registered under another on its FIRST register would keep the stale creation stamp, since persistProductRegistration's correct-our-own branch can't fire with no prior registration). It writes the CANONICAL production spelling (`noteBuyerForProgram` — "Fidelis Investors LLC" / "Blue Lake Capital" / "EMCAP Financial", the SAME label the retail path uses, per the owner's "real production spelling" rule), FILL-ONLY / correct-our-own: it writes only when `lender` is NULL or is one of OUR OWN derived label forms (short OR canonical, tracked in `TPO_OWN_STAMP_KEYS`) that is not the current program's — NEVER a human value (RCN, CorrFirst, hand-typed). Runs BEFORE `evaluateApplication` so note-buyer-driven rules reflect it; STAFF-ONLY (the TPO scrubs strip the note buyer). The old inline register block (short labels "Blue Lake") was replaced by this helper — TPO is now consistent with retail.
      · **ORDERING — a broker orders TITLE & INSURANCE for their firm's file, EXCEPT RCN, NEVER flood.** New firm-scoped `/api/tpo` routes: `GET …/orders` (borrower-safe state — NEVER returns `applications.lender`), `POST …/orders/:kind/vendor` (add the title company / insurance agent), `POST …/orders/:kind/place`. `src/lib/tpo-orders.js` is the pure gate: `isTpoOrderKind` admits ONLY title/insurance (flood is refused 400 — there is no flood order type at all, and the flood certificate + flood-insurance condition stay staff-only), `brokerOrderingBlocked` = `isRcnNoteBuyer(lender)` (RCN → 403 `staff_handled`, and the note buyer is NEVER revealed — the broker is told only "your loan team handles this"), and `tpoOrderState` shapes the borrower-safe per-kind view (never the note buyer / mortgagee clause; the vendor shown is the broker's OWN title/insurance company). The place route REUSES the SAME exactly-once path the staff Orders desk uses — `orders.getOrderData` / `buildOrderEmail` / `placeOrder` — so for FREE the order email shows OUR (YS Capital) mortgage clause (never the investor's; RCN is blocked so its servicer clause never reaches a broker context) and signs as the file's loan officer, which on a TPO file IS the broker ("for the TPO as the loan officer"). The exactly-once claim → send → settle CORE was EXTRACTED into `orders.placeOrder(...)` — ONE chokepoint shared by the staff route and the TPO route, so the money-adjacent logic can never drift; the staff route is behavior-identical (proven by `test-order-desk-hardening.js`). A broker-placed order never CC's the borrower directly (the broker manages the wholesale relationship). Notifies OUR staff in-app (`notifyAppStaff` filters external users), audited `tpo_order_placed` / `tpo_order_vendor_set` / `tpo_note_buyer_auto_set`. Front end: `app-v2/src/components/TpoOrders.jsx` (add-vendor + order buttons via `askConfirm`), mounted in `TpoFile`. Tests `scripts/test-tpo-orders-pure.js` + `scripts/test-tpo-orders-db.js` (both in `npm test`). When adding a broker order surface, route it through `/api/tpo` + `appInFirm` + `tpo-orders`/`orders.placeOrder` — never a second order path, never expose the note buyer, and never add a flood order kind.
- **BORROWER VIEW — staff step INTO a borrower's portal; full parity, dual-identity revocation, never silent (owner-directed 2026-07-26; design `docs/BORROWER-VIEW.md`, db/320).** A loan officer / processor / admin opens a borrower's own portal and sees exactly the screens that borrower sees, to walk them through a condition live. Built by handing the staffer a **REAL borrower access token** carrying an impersonation envelope (`imp/impBy/impRole/impTv/impSid/impAt`) — NOT a read-only mirror of the borrower screens. That is the whole point: every borrower endpoint is already scoped off `req.actor.id`, so the SPA runs the ACTUAL borrower app against the ACTUAL borrower API, and a borrower screen shipped tomorrow appears in borrower view for free with no second implementation to keep in sync. NON-NEGOTIABLE rules:
  1. **Dual-identity revocation on EVERY request.** `authenticate()` re-validates the borrower's `token_version` AND the staffer's row (active, `token_version` matches `impTv`, session inside the 4h `MAX_SESSION_SEC` cap anchored on `impAt`). Staffer logs out / is deactivated / borrower logs out everywhere / cap elapses → the view stops. The sliding refresh **re-mints WITH the same envelope and the same `impAt`** — never let a borrower-view token slide into a plain borrower token (that would silently turn a bounded, audited 4-hour view into a permanent unattributed borrower session). `routes/events.js` (the one surface that bypasses `authenticate`) repeats the staff-side check at SSE connect.
  2. **The blocklist is TINY and structural.** `borrowerView.guard` is mounted GLOBALLY in `server.js` **above `/auth`** (position matters — mounted after it, the `/auth` routes are uncovered) and refuses exactly four things: the e-sign ceremony (`…/esign/sign-view` — executing loan docs in another person's legal identity), `/auth/logout` (it bumps the BORROWER's token_version and would knock them off their own phone), `/auth/mfa/*` writes, and nesting another view. Everything else is FULL PARITY — the owner asked for "the same options … same permissions". Do NOT widen the blocklist for ordinary workflow actions; that breaks the feature's reason for existing.
  3. **Never corrupt the borrower's own state.** Read receipts are NOT consumed (`borrower.js /messages`, `borrower-chat.js /read` skip the write when `req.impersonation`) and the `last_seen_at` presence heartbeat is skipped — a staffer looking at a screen must not tell the team the borrower read a reply or is online.
  4. **Never silent.** `borrower_view_sessions` (who looked, as whom, from which file/IP, request count, why it ended) + `impersonator_staff_id` on BOTH `audit_log` and `request_audit_log`, so every click made inside someone's portal is attributable to the real human while the row still records the identity it ran under.
  5. **Scope = the staff file scope.** `ELIGIBLE_BORROWER_SQL(p)` mirrors `VISIBLE_OFFICERS_SQL` term-for-term (primary LO/processor, `visible_officer_ids`, `application_assignees`, open `workflow_items`; never a soft-deleted file) + co-borrowers; `see_all_files` reaches anyone. Add a fifth way a staffer reaches a file → add it here too (the pure test asserts the parity). It is a FUNCTION of its placeholder like every other scope fragment — a hardcoded `$1` becomes an unreferenced parameter when a `see_all_files` caller drops the clause (Postgres 42P18; it broke borrower view for every admin until the DB test caught it). Tests `scripts/test-borrower-view-pure.js` + `scripts/test-borrower-view-db.js` (both in `npm test`).
- **THE OFFICER'S BRANDING BAR GOES WHERE THE PAGE'S HEADER LETS IT — and a `position:fixed` header must HOLD it (owner-reported 2026-08-03: "when a loan officer clicks on his personal LO link, every page of the entire marketing website gets branded to him with a header on top. Only the homepage does not").**
  · **ROOT CAUSE, and it is a whole class, not a home-page quirk.** `brand.js injectBar()` anchored the bar on `.topbar` and otherwise fell back to `document.body.insertBefore(bar, body.firstChild)`. That fallback is only correct for a header that is IN THE FLOW. The HOME PAGE has no `.topbar` — its header is `.nav`, which is **`position:fixed`**: out of the flow, opaque, `z-index:50`, painting over the top of `<body>`. So the bar was injected on every page all along and on the home page it was simply **underneath the nav**, invisible. Nothing threw, nothing logged, and a test asserting "`#loBar` is in the DOM" would have passed the entire time — which is why this survived. **THE RULE: when something must sit next to a page's header, the header's computed `position` decides where it can go. A fixed header can only carry it by HOLDING it.**
  · **Three page shapes, one decision, all in `injectBar`:** a static `.topbar` (the tools) → insert after it, UNCHANGED; a `position:fixed` header (`fixedHeader()` — the home page **and the four legal pages**, all `header.nav`) → mount the bar INSIDE the header, between the nav row and the mobile menu, tagged `.lo-bar-fixed`; anything else, incl. a `position:sticky` header (`suite.html`'s `.ssx-nav`, the tools' `.tool-bar`) → top of `<body>`, UNCHANGED — a sticky header does not overlay its own initial position, so the bar is visible there.
  · **A header that grew must push the page down by exactly what it grew, and that is MEASURED, never a constant** (`offsetForFixedBar`): `body.paddingTop` moves the content the fixed header paints over, and `html.scrollPaddingTop` moves where every in-page `#anchor` lands — both added ON TOP of whatever the page already declared (`scrollPaddingTop:auto` reads as 0, never `NaN`). Re-applied on resize, on `load` and via `ResizeObserver`, because the bar wraps to two lines on a phone and grows again when the web fonts arrive.
