  · **`YSBrand.link` now PRESERVES an `lo` the href already carries.** `enhanceHomeCards` builds each of the 16 team cards as `suite.html?lo=<that officer>` and `propagateLinks` runs immediately after — `sp.set("lo", CODE)` stamped the CURRENT officer over every one of them, so on a branded home page every card led to the same person. Invisible until the home page started branding, and it would have shipped as the fix's own bug.
  · **The legal pages never loaded `brand.js` at all**, so the `?lo=` chain — which the file's own header promises "sticks through the whole session" — died the moment anyone opened Privacy/Terms/Disclosures/SMS terms. One `<script>` tag each; they now brand and propagate like every other page.
  · **Fixed in V1 (`web/`) and V2 (`web/v2/`) together — the two `brand.js` files are byte-identical and the test asserts they stay that way.** V1 is frozen at `/v1` for STYLING and for the pricing engines; this is neither, it is the same root cause on the same feature, and leaving it would put half the site back where it started. Cache-busters bumped (`brand.js?v=n3`, `style.css?v=pilot5`/`?v=n2`) — these assets are cached hard.
  · Tests: `scripts/test-lo-branding-bar.js` (in `npm test`; pure — runs both `brand.js` copies under a minimal fake DOM and pins WHERE the bar lands for all three header shapes, the measured offsets, the roster allowlist, and the link-preservation rule; every assertion was proven to FAIL on the pre-fix code) plus a real-Chromium sweep of all 10 V2 + 5 V1 pages at desktop and iPhone 12 asserting `elementFromPoint` at the bar's centre actually HITS the bar, the officer's name is painted, the page content still clears the taller header, and there is no sideways scroll. CI has no browser, hence the pure test. **When adding a marketing page, give it a `.topbar`, a sticky header, or a fixed one — all three are handled — but always include `brand.js`, or the officer's link silently stops at that page.**
- **Embedded tools are white-first (owner-directed, 2026-07-10):** every static tool the portal embeds — Scope of Work / Rehab Budget, Products & Pricing (Term Sheet Studio), Track Record — must follow the portal's **white** coloring, never dark. The tools support both themes via the shared `web/suite.css` `:root[data-theme="light"]` overrides; the portal is white-first, so the theme is forced to **light** in BOTH layers: (1) the tool HTML itself (`web/tools/*.html` inline `data-theme="light"` stamp) and (2) the portal iframe hosts — `app/src/components/ToolModal.jsx`, `StaticToolFrame.jsx`, and `TermSheetStudio.jsx` all stamp `data-theme='light'` on the embedded document (they previously forced `'dark'` to match an older dark portal — do not revert). If a tool ever appears black inside the portal, the culprit is one of those three wrappers stamping the wrong theme. This is CSS/theme only — the pricing/guideline engine numbers stay frozen.
- **THE RESEARCH DATABASE — every property, comparable and appraiser we have ever been shown (owner-directed 2026-08-02).** Everything in the appraisal XML was already parsed and stored, but stored PER FILE (`appraisals`/`appraisal_comparables` hang off one `application_id`), so it could only answer "what did THIS report say?". `db/409` re-files it as a cross-file warehouse and `db/410` adds the valuation tool on top. Design + the full rationale: **`docs/PROPERTY-COMP-DATABASE-RESEARCH.md`**; the three research reports behind it are in `docs/research/` (industry survey, search-engine design, and the fact-coverage audit that found what the parser was dropping).
  - **The four tables.** `properties` (one row per real-world address; its columns are a ROLL-UP, recomputed from the observations, never authored) → `property_observations` (one row per report × property × role — what ONE appraiser said on ONE date, never overwritten, the actual content) → `property_sales` (distinct transactions, deduped on property+month+price) → `property_photos` (the link from an `appraisal_photos` document to the property it SHOWS). Plus `appraisers` + `appraiser_licenses` + `appraiser_contacts` (the licence is the identity: `lic:NJ:42RG00123400`, falling back to name+firm; contacts accumulate, a new spelling never overwrites the old one). `property_ingest_log` is the ledger that makes the back-fill re-runnable and every skip countable.
  - **`src/lib/research/property-key.js` is the identity rule and it is PURE + OFFLINE on purpose.** `lib/address-canon` (Google place_id) is deliberately NOT used: it costs an HTTP round trip per string and returns null with no API key, and a warehouse that cannot dedupe without a vendor stores every property twice. Three rules never to relax: the UNIT is never dropped (unit 2 and unit 5 sell for different prices — and the ingest reads `condo_unit_identifier` because the importer never writes `subject_unit`); the ZIP is never part of the key when a city is present (ZIP+4 / missing / wrong ZIPs would split one house into three); and no house number / no state / no locality → NO KEY and the row is SKIPPED, counted on `property_ingest_log.rows_skipped` with the address as stated.
  - **FIVE CORRECTNESS RULES that look right and are wrong if you touch them.** (1) A renovation report's SUBJECT condition describes the house AFTER the repairs — `condition_basis='as_repaired'` is recorded and the roll-up SKIPS it for condition/quality, or the warehouse would claim a gutted 1930 house is in like-new shape today. (2) A LISTING IS NOT A SALE: an active/pending comp's price is an ASKING price → `last_list_price`, never `last_sale_price` (and `sale_status IS NULL` means *closed*, db/157). (3) UAD condition/quality are ORDINAL and RUN BACKWARDS — C1 is best; "C3 or better" is `condition_rank <= 3`, and a string comparison means the opposite (generated `condition_rank`/`quality_rank`/`baths_total` columns exist so the filter cannot get this wrong). (4) A SUPERSEDED report is the same report re-imported, not a second opinion — it is RETIRED (observations + photo links removed, properties re-rolled, ledger `skipped`), or every comp count doubles; its SALES are kept, because a prior draft is not a retraction. (5) Every FK from an observation/photo back to a report is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, never CASCADE — `appraisals.application_id` cascades, so a CASCADE here would mean deleting ONE loan file erases every comparable sale that report taught us.
  - **What the parser was dropping, now fixed in `extract.js`/`import.js`/`desk.js`/`photo-meta.js`:** a comparable's WORDED condition/quality (`condition_text`/`quality_text` — the UAD whitelist silently discarded "Good"/"Avg-Good", i.e. the owner's most important comp fact was null on every non-UAD vendor); `gla_basis` (a 1025 grid falls back to gross BUILDING area under the same element); a comp's year built / lot / style / garage, mined out of the appraiser's own ADJUSTMENT LINES (no MISMO element carries them per comp); and the photo SLOT LABEL (`appraisal_photos.identifier`/`comp_seq`), which is what finally attaches a comp photo to the right comp — matched on the caption's ADDRESS first (the only reliable join), the slot ordinal second. A comparable's property TYPE and UNIT COUNT are genuinely absent from most MISMO 2.6 grids: never inherit them from the report's subject — the warehouse answers it properly when that same address turns up as some other report's SUBJECT.
  - **The search engine (`src/lib/research/search.js`) is plain Postgres.** Every predicate is APPENDED only when supplied — never `($1 IS NULL OR col = $1)`, which is a planner trap once the plan goes generic. Address search is `tsvector` + GIN (CORE Postgres; `pg_trgm` is contrib and cannot be assumed on Render) with per-word prefix terms, so it is word-order-free and indexed — never `ILIKE '%…%'`. "Used as an ARV comp" is denormalized to `arv_comp_count`/`asis_comp_count` with partial indexes (a JOIN there multiplies the property row per observation and corrupts every facet count and the LIMIT). Radius = bounding box + haversine refine, and the longitude delta must come out about a third wider in degrees than the latitude one (a degree of longitude shrinks by cos(lat)) — using one delta for both is the bug everyone writes. **THE BOX MUST BE A SUPERSET OF THE CIRCLE, and two plausible constants made it a hair SMALLER than one** (measured: 0.11% narrow, which silently drops real comparables off the east-west edge of every radius search and nothing anywhere says so). Two rules, both load-bearing: **the box and the haversine must be sized on the SAME EARTH** — the refine measures on a sphere of radius 3958.7613 mi (69.0932 mi/degree) while the box had been sized with the ellipsoid's 69.0546 and 69.1710, correct numbers about a different planet — so `MI_PER_DEG` is DERIVED from that same radius (`2π × 3958.7613 / 360`) and can never drift; and **the longitude delta is sized on the box edge FURTHEST from the equator, not on its centre** (at 40.7°N with a 10-mile radius, `cos(centre)` overstates the far edge by 0.24% — about 125 feet of missing coverage at the corners), plus 0.1% floating-point slack. The exact circle is then cut IN SQL, never in JS afterwards: the LIMIT and the `count(*) OVER ()` total both run first, so a JS filter leaves the page short and the total overstated by up to 27% (the box's area over the circle's). `scripts/test-research-geo-box-pure.js` proves containment by walking 360 bearings at a battery of latitudes and radii — extend it, never weaken it.
  - **Build-your-own valuation (`src/lib/research/valuation.js`, screen `/internal/research/valuation/:id`).** SNAPSHOT, NEVER REFERENCE: the subject and every comp are COPIED into the valuation, because `properties` is a live roll-up and a valuation that re-read it would silently change its own answer. A SUGGESTION NEVER OVERWRITES A HUMAN — once a line is `source:'user'` the suggest pass will not touch it again. EVERY DERIVED RATE MAY REFUSE: too small a sample, or a sample pointing the wrong way (a market where the 4-beds are the tired ones yields a NEGATIVE per-bedroom rate — refused with a reason, because a grid line that subtracts money for a bathroom is worse than a blank one). The GLA adjustment is ~40% of the average price per foot, NOT the full figure. **The "15% net / 25% gross" pair is NOT a current Fannie Mae rule** (removed from the Selling Guide in Dec 2014; the 1-mile radius and 90-day recency are lender overlays) — they are kept as OUR review flags and the copy must never call them a GSE requirement. Nothing here is an appraisal or USPAP work product; the disclaimer is stamped into every result and must travel with the number.
  - **PLACING THE PROPERTIES WE LENT ON, from the comparables around them (`src/lib/research/trilaterate.js` + `place-subjects.js`, booted in `server.js`).** Every one of the 132 subjects in the warehouse is UNPLACED — appraisers give coordinates for their COMPARABLES and not for the subject — so the property the loan is secured against is the one no radius search, no distance filter and no map can find. But 102 reports carry three or more comparables that have real coordinates AND state their distance on the grid ("0.55 miles SW"), and three distances from three known points determine one location. Measured on the real corpus: **97 of 102 REPORTS resolve — median residual 17 feet, worst 73**. Note the two numbers answer different questions and both belong in any claim: per REPORT it is 97 of 102, but per PROPERTY — which is what the boot pass reports and what an officer sees — it is **90 of the 132** (42 refused), because 37 subjects appear only on a report carrying fewer than three usable comparables (`too_few`, not a refusal to judge) and 5 are refused by the mirror test. Every one of those figures is MEASURED by running the pass over a clone of the warehouse — they were once stated one too high in five places at once because they had been copied between comments instead of re-read off a run, so re-measure and correct them together — a free, offline placement out of data already paid for, which is why the paid geocoder is a nice-to-have rather than a blocker. **AN ESTIMATE IS NOT A MEASUREMENT and everything here exists to keep the two apart**: the write is FILL-ONLY and stamped `geo_source='comp_trilateration'` (it may never sit on top of a real coordinate, and nothing may mistake it for one); below three circles it REFUSES (two circles meet at two points and nothing chooses between them); it REPORTS its residual and refuses past a quarter mile — a stated distance is rounded to a hundredth of a mile so a small residual is expected, a large one means a distance or a coordinate is wrong and the answer would be confidently somewhere else. **THE REAL TRAP IS THE MIRROR, and it is PROVED rather than guessed at with an angle threshold**: comparables strung along one road leave a second answer reflected across that road, so the reflection is actually COMPUTED (across the point set's principal axis) and scored the same way — because measured on the corpus every borderline set fits its stated distances to within 2–107 FEET, so a residual test would have waved all of them through with total confidence. **THE ORDER OF THE REFUSALS IS ITSELF LOAD-BEARING**: fewer than three → all-zero distances → the arithmetic floor → **residual → mirror**, in that order, because a set whose distances CONTRADICT each other fits badly everywhere INCLUDING at its mirror, so asking about the mirror first blames the geometry for a number that is simply wrong (reproduced: one distance three miles out was refused as "nearly in a line"). `proximityMiles` REFUSES a description with no explicit mile figure ("2 blocks", "same street") rather than guessing, and refuses an implausible one (a 120-mile "comparable" is a data error, and a wrong big number drags a least-squares fit further than a wrong small one). Test `scripts/test-trilaterate-pure.js` — note its mirror fixture states distances at real grid precision (0.01 mi) ON PURPOSE: given EXACT distances even a nearly-collinear set resolves itself, and it is the appraiser's own rounding that makes the two answers indistinguishable.
  - **Back-dating + wiring.** `ingest.backfill()` runs at boot (400/pass, self-draining via the ledger, oldest report FIRST so the end state matches filing each report as it arrived) and on demand at `POST /api/research/backfill` (platform_setup only). `fireResearchIngest` runs after every import, again after photo extraction (the photo links need the pictures to exist), and after `backfillAppraisalCompSplitOnce` (which rewrites `comp_set` on old appraisals — without re-ingesting, the warehouse answers the ARV/as-is question with the pre-split answer forever).
  - **EVERY XML THAT ENTERS PILOT FEEDS THE WAREHOUSE, FROM EVERY DOOR (owner-directed 2026-08-04: "anybody that is importing a XML into any file in the system or saving an XML into the appraisal condition, that XML should be imported also into the property resource at the same time and bringing in all the comparables into the system"). db/462 + db/463.**
    · **WHAT WAS BROKEN.** The warehouse had two feeds — an appraisal that imported CLEANLY onto a loan file, and the hand-upload screen. Every other way a report arrives filed the bytes and threw the market data away, and none of them was rare: a BORROWER attaching the data file (that door had no appraisal detection at all); a STAFFER filing it anywhere except the appraisal condition's XML slot (the auto-import is gated on the slot matching `/xml/i` AND the condition being `rtl_cond_appraisaldocs`); a document SLOTTED AFTERWARDS (that route only ran an UPDATE); an appraisal import that FAILED for its own reasons; a vendor EMAILING the report back on an order (`order-inbox`), which is how it most often arrives; a chat attachment; a closing-chain email.
    · **`src/lib/research/xml-catch.js` IS THE ONE CHOKEPOINT.** Every door hands it the bytes; it decides whether they are an appraisal and files them through the SAME `xml-import.importXml` a hand upload uses, so a report is filed identically whichever door it came through — a search cannot be trusted if half the market was filed one way and half another. **IT NEVER TOUCHES THE LOAN FILE** (no `appraisals` row, condition, finding, notification or value — which is exactly why it can safely run on every upload while the appraisal DESK stays as gated as it was: deciding a file's official appraisal is a judgement, filing a comparable sale is not), never throws, never runs on the request path, and never guesses — "is this an appraisal?" reuses the parser's OWN `detectMismo`, so an Encompass iLAD loan-application export is ignored in SILENCE (no ledger row) while a UAD 3.6 report we cannot read yet is RECORDED as held.
    · **IT MUST NOT RACE THE DESK.** On a successful `runAppraisalImport` the catch is SKIPPED — the desk feeds the warehouse itself, richer, with the photographs. Firing both on the same bytes double-files every property in the report on any report carrying neither an effective date nor a named appraiser, because such a report has no fingerprint and BOTH de-dup guards stand down by design; nothing heals it afterwards. A FAILED desk import still falls through to the catch, which is what closes that gap.
    · **A BORROWER'S UPLOAD IS `untrusted`.** Its property facts land in full — a comparable sale that happened, happened — but it may FILL and never REWRITE the shared, every-staff-user appraiser roster (`ingest.upsertAppraiser`'s `fillOnly`). Before this, one attached document could replace a real appraiser's company, phone and email for the whole firm. `research_imports.uploaded_by` references `staff_users`, so a borrower id is never passed and is verified anyway — a foreign-key violation there would lose the whole report.
    · **PREVIOUS AND FUTURE.** `xml-sweep.js` walks the back book at boot (200/pass), self-draining via `documents.research_xml_checked_at` — stamped whatever the verdict, because "we looked, it was a rent roll" is an answer — and DELIBERATELY NOT stamped when the READ ITSELF failed, so one storage outage never drains a real report out of the sweep. `research_xml_attempts` (db/463) is the other half of that rule: without it the queue is oldest-first and a handful of permanently dead documents hold the front forever (storage's temp-dir fallback guarantees a supply of them). Off with `RESEARCH_XML_SWEEP_DISABLED=1`.
    · `research_imports.source` records HOW each report arrived and is shown on the import list, because "did the reports on our loan files come in?" is the question this was built to answer. It is COALESCEd on conflict — the first door to file a report is the one that brought it in. Tests `scripts/test-research-xml-catch-pure.js` + `test-research-xml-catch-db.js` (both in `npm test`; one section per door, each proven to FAIL with its wiring reverted). When you add a new way for a document to enter the system, call `fireCatch` with the bytes — never a second copy of the sniff.
  - **Access: EVERY staff user, no per-file scoping** (owner-directed) — a deliberate departure from the loan-file rule, defensible because the data is addresses, property characteristics and recorded sale prices, with no borrower name, loan amount, contact detail or document. `/api/research` applies `requireStaff` as a whole router. Photo bytes are served only for a document actually linked in `property_photos`, so it never becomes a "download any document by id" hole. Tests: `scripts/test-research-property-key.js`, `test-research-valuation.js` (both pure) and `test-research-db.js` (real DB + real HTTP), all in `npm test`. Rebuild `web/v2/portal` after touching any of the five screens.
- **Draw management runs on the Sitewire integration (owner-directed 2026-07-19; supersedes the old "sandboxed / separate portal" note).** The full construction-draw system is built IN this repo on top of the Sitewire API (`src/sitewire/*` — the unified `rollup.js` [draws↔SOW↔budget], `reallocation.js` [net-zero/undrawn-only change requests], advisory `risk.js` red-flag engine, `monitor.js` [portfolio early-warning: stale/overdrawn/behind-pace/past-maturity/wire-overdue], `money.js` [retainage split + lien-waiver release gate] — routes `/api/sitewire` [staff] + `/api/borrower` draw routes [`borrower-draws.js`] + public token accept [`draw-findings-public.js`], worker `src/sync/sitewire-sync.js`, schema `db/131`–`db/135`, exports via shared `src/lib/xlsx.js` [reallocation / audit-trail / GL / draw-packet], UI `app-v2` DrawsPanel [rollup+ledger+retainage+waivers+risk+findings+activity+packet]/BorrowerDraws/StaffDrawRules/DrawAccept/StaffDraws, design + full error spec `docs/SITEWIRE-DRAW-MANAGEMENT-RESEARCH.md` [§16 = the never-guess error-handling specification]). Sitewire is the system of record for borrower draw submission + inspection + photos (mostly READ to us as `lender_owner`); PILOT is the push engine + the draw desk + the money ledger. NON-NEGOTIABLE rules, never regress:
  1. **Off by default, staged.** `SITEWIRE_ENABLED` master switch (reads/reconcile) + a SEPARATE `SITEWIRE_OUTBOUND_ENABLED` write gate + `SITEWIRE_DRYRUN` (log the exact body, send nothing). The 3-header token (`SITEWIRE_ACCESS_TOKEN`/`_CLIENT`/`_UID`) comes from Render env ONLY — never committed.
  2. **Only-ours rule = GO-FORWARD ONLY (owner-directed 2026-07-20, hardened; supersedes the short-lived "human-confirmed LINK/adopt" idea).** PILOT creates, tracks, reconciles, follows, and delivers draw findings for ONLY the properties IT pushed to Sitewire (`sitewire_property_links`, `matched_by='created'`) from the go-live date (`SITEWIRE_GO_LIVE_DATE`, default `2026-07-20`) forward. PILOT NEVER adopts, links, mirrors, follows, or overwrites a pre-existing hand-entered Sitewire property — not automatically and not by any UI action. There is NO `'linked'` matched_by and NO adopt path (the owner was not confident in adoption; a `matched_by='linked'` row would be a latent trap and must not be reintroduced). Loan number is a COLLISION key only: on a collision PILOT PARKS (`sitewire_loan_already_in_sitewire`, never duplicates, never adopts) and the file's draw section shows an "already in Sitewire — PILOT is not following it" banner. The ONLY way to bring a pre-existing property under PILOT management is for a human to DELETE it in Sitewire and push a fresh copy from the file (a warned re-push that creates a brand-new `matched_by='created'` property). Every read/reconcile/portfolio path filters `matched_by='created'`; a property with no `'created'` link row is structurally invisible. A pushed ('created') property is stamped live-in-PILOT (`pushed_at`, surfaced as `managed_since`).
     - **Error handling is GO-FORWARD too (owner-directed 2026-07-20).** A file enters the sync-review ERROR QUEUE (and the bidirectional reconcile/findings/drift workflow) ONLY once PILOT has pushed it (a live created property — `orchestrator.isManaged`). Everything that can fail BEFORE that (`SITEWIRE_BIRTH_REASONS`: no SOW, budget mismatch, unmatched partner, incomplete address, the loan-already-in-Sitewire collision, the unit/type advisories, …) is recorded ON THE FILE (`sitewire_property_links.raw.setup_status`) and shown inline in its OWN draw section — NEVER as a global review row or email — so an old funded file nobody pushed can't clutter the queue. `park()` routes any birth reason on a not-yet-managed file to `recordSetupStatus()` instead of the queue; `setup_status` is cleared on a successful push, after which normal error handling takes over. The rollup returns `preexisting` / `setup_status` for the draw-section banners. `db/164` auto-closed the pre-existing birth-phase rows and seeded the collision banner. Reviews on a MANAGED file (post-push workflow) are untouched.
  3. **Born on the click OR on the coordinator's Start (owner-directed 2026-07-19).** The integration is born for a file on `funded` + EITHER the borrower's "Request a draw" click (`borrower.js` enqueues `push_file`) OR the DRAW COORDINATOR pressing "Start the draw process" (`POST /api/sitewire/files/:id/start-draw` — the coordinator now comes FIRST, before the borrower). Never on `funded` alone. Both paths stamp a durable birth record (`applications.draw_setup_requested_at` for the borrower click; `sitewire_property_links.draw_setup_started_at`/`_by` for the coordinator Start), and the worker's `backfillStrandedBirthsOnce` catches EITHER when the switch flips on (so starting while `SITEWIRE_ENABLED` is off never strands the file).
  4. **Per-unit crosswalk.** Our one SOW line with per-unit columns EXPLODES into one Sitewire job item per unit (`src/sitewire/mapper.js explodeSow`, deterministic `"Unit N - <name>"`), bound by the Sitewire-assigned id in `sitewire_job_item_links` (Sitewire has no external-id field). Reverse roll-up maps pulled draw requests back to the single SOW line. Σ exploded == frozen `rehab_budget` to the cent (G-RECON) or the push is BLOCKED + parked.
     - **G-UNITS = PHYSICAL building units, never a hard block (owner-directed 2026-07-20 — "use physical building units").** The unit count PUSHED to Sitewire (`propertyFields.total_units`) is the PHYSICAL building count = `Math.max(1, fileUnits, sowUnits)` (the larger of the file's `applications.units` and the SOW's `state.units`). A file-vs-SOW unit disagreement is NOT an error and NEVER parks the push (the old `sitewire_units_mismatch` hard-park is retired) — a 4-family where the borrower works only the exterior + one unit legitimately has a SOW built for fewer/other unit sections. The physical count is always ≥ every per-unit budget/media line the explosion references (those use the SOW's unit count), so Sitewire can never carry a "Unit N" line for a unit the property doesn't have; units with no work simply have no budget lines. A disagreement raises only a NON-BLOCKING advisory (`sitewire_units_note`, deduped `dedupe:'units'`) so staff can fix a stale file count; the push proceeds and **G-RECON (Σ exploded == frozen budget to the cent) is the real gate**. Surfaced on the coordinator's Start-draw preview (`GET /files/:id/draw-setup` → `units:{file,sow,physical,disagree}`, shown in `DrawsPanel.jsx` StartDrawCard). `db/160` auto-closes any pre-existing `sitewire_units_mismatch` open review rows so previously-stranded files push on retry. Covered by `test-sitewire-lifecycle.js` (decouple case) + `test-sitewire-draw-setup.js` (route `units` block).
  5. **Never guess.** Every write goes through the guarded client/orchestrator (never a raw call from a route), is journaled to `sitewire_write_log`, no-op/echo-suppressed, volume-circuit-broken, and read-after-write verified; anything ambiguous (unmatched capital partner, geocode fail, budget mismatch, bind failure, unknown inbound line, 422) parks a `sync_review_queue` row (`field_key='sitewire'`) — never silently applied or dropped. Money is integer cents; `net_release = approved − fee` (per-partner fee: virtual $299 default / physical per partner). draw_events come back UNSORTED — sort by `occurred_at`.
  6. **Draw-Coordinator persona** (`draw_coordinator` role + `manage_draws` capability, default holder Lisa Katz = Sitewire user 16146, mapped by email in `staff_users.sitewire_user_id`). Change-request + findings-accept/dispute workflows (research doc §13/§14) reuse `change_requests` / condition reopen / SharePoint Version-N / the inbound-email reply routing — the borrower's accept is captured in PILOT (never in Sitewire). The legacy email-only "Request a draw" button still fires (LO + processor + borrower + draws@yscapgroup.com) and now ALSO enqueues the Sitewire push.
  7. **Coordinator "Start the draw process" + inspection-method policy (owner-directed 2026-07-19).** After funding, the coordinator's DrawsPanel `StartDrawCard` shows the whole to-be-pushed setup — prereq checklist, matched capital partner, resolved inspection method + fee, required inspector/CP-approval — and ONE button (`POST /files/:id/start-draw`) that pushes property + construction budget + Scope of Work + fees and reads them back (guarded orchestrator, park-on-error). `GET /files/:id/draw-setup` is the read-only preview behind it. The inspection policy is the admin rules engine (`sitewire_inspection_rules`, StaffDrawRules screen, `platform_setup`): per capital partner + optional program, `inspection_method` = the AUTO method, `allow_virtual`/`allow_physical` = which methods are ALLOWED (both = the coordinator may SWITCH per file), and `fee_cents_virtual`/`_physical` = the fee for each. `POST /rules` never lets a rule forbid its own default method. `orchestrator.resolveInspection(link, rule)` resolves the effective method = the coordinator's per-file choice (`sitewire_property_links.inspection_method`) ?? the rule default, validated against the allow flags (a forbidden stored method falls back to an allowed one — never a disallowed push), with the matching fee (a null physical fee falls back to the virtual fee, never $0). `start-draw` 422s a coordinator method the rule forbids — never guessed. (`db/143`.) The SOW→Sitewire explosion guarantees every job-item NAME is unique (`mapper.uniquifyNames` + cross-category taxonomy qualification, e.g. "Tile (Flooring)" vs "Tile (Baths)") so bind-by-name is never ambiguous; a line already drawn against is never renamed (Sitewire locks it); an unmapped property/construction type is left blank + raises an advisory review (never guessed).
  8. **Smart-link note buyers to Sitewire + coordinator-editable fee (owner-directed 2026-07-19).** Our free-text note-buyer label (`applications.lender`, e.g. "Fidelis"/"Blue Lake") rarely matches Sitewire's directory name exactly ("Fidelis Investments LLC"/"Blue Lake Capital"). `sitewire_partner_links` (`db/151`, PK `label_norm` = lowercased + non-alphanumerics stripped) holds the durable, human-CONFIRMED map; `orchestrator.resolveCapitalPartnerId` consults it FIRST (a link binds; `sitewire_id` NULL = an explicit "no Sitewire partner"/handled-externally), then an exact directory match, then a suffix-tolerant CANDIDATE that is NEVER auto-bound (never-guess — a fuzzy match only SUGGESTS; a human confirms via the StaffDrawRules "Match note buyers to Sitewire" table → `POST /partner-links`, which validates the id exists in the synced directory). `db/151` seeds the three owner-confirmed links (fidelis→19, bluelake→41, corrfirst→27 — real ids on lender 236) with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` so a later admin re-link always wins. Separately, the coordinator may set a per-file DRAW FEE on the Start-draw screen: `sitewire_property_links.fee_cents_override` (`db/152`, integer cents 0..$100k) overrides the rule fee for that file only; `start-draw` clears the override when the entered fee equals the chosen method's rule fee, and `resolveInspection` falls back to the rule fee on any null/negative/non-finite override (a bad value can never zero the pushed `processing_fee_cents`). The allow-flags still gate which METHOD the coordinator may pick; the fee itself is a free amount they own per file.
  9. **Durable inspector media + PILOT-branded reports (owner-directed 2026-07-20; Draw Management phase 2).** Sitewire hands us inspection photos/videos + the per-draw PDF as PUBLIC, PRE-SIGNED, EXPIRING URLs. (2a) `src/sitewire/media-archive.js` pulls them into PILOT's OWN storage (`storage.save`) recorded in `draw_media` (`db/170`, UNIQUE `sitewire_draw_id`+`source_key`=sha256(url)) — NOT `documents` rows (per-photo would flood SharePoint). Best-effort + idempotent; SSRF-guarded fetch (`assertPublicHttps` + `isPrivateIp` every hop, manual redirects, per-file + run-total caps). Photos are NOT mirrored. (2b) `src/sitewire/draw-report.js` builds PILOT-branded PDFs from the persisted findings (`draw_finding_lines`) + the durable `draw_media` bytes via jsPDF `doc.addImage` (JPEG + well-formed PNG only, format from MAGIC BYTES not content_type; a bad buffer is skipped, never thrown) — three shapes: per-draw, whole-project, and a borrower-safe copy (every capital-partner name scrubbed via `borrower-safe.scrubText`, photo GPS removed; the per-draw processing fee IS shown to the borrower per the 2026-08-03 rule below — only our project-wide fee INCOME is staff-only). The pure builder `buildDrawReport` unit-tests with no DB; the DB side (`loadReportMeta`/`attachPhotoBytes`/`storeDrawReport`) is lazy-required so the builder never touches PG. The REPORT (not the photos) is stored as a `documents` row (`doc_kind='draw_inspection_report'`, `source_type='system'`; staff copy `visibility='staff_only'`, borrower copy `visibility='borrower'`) — idempotent by a version-hashed filename (`db/171` partial unique index + 23505 backstop, mirrors esign `storeSignedDocument`); an unchanged draw reuses the row, a change mints a fresh one and supersedes the prior of the SAME visibility (the borrower copy never supersedes the staff copy). Routes: `GET /files/:id/draws/:drawId/report?mode=staff|borrower` + `GET /files/:id/report` (whole-project) — `manage_draws` + `canSeeFile` + (per-draw) IDOR draw-owns-file; a file with no draws 404s (never an empty PDF). Reports only ever reflect DURABLE (archived) photos, so they never break on an expired link. (2c) **Auto-deliver on findings delivery (enhancement 2026-07-20).** The `load → attach photos → build → store+supersede → cache-by-version` sequence is now ONE shared helper `drawReport.buildOrGetReportDoc(appId,{sitewireDrawId,scope,mode})` used by both the report route and the deliver path. When staff deliver findings (`POST /files/:id/findings/:drawId/deliver`), `drawReport.autoDeliverArtifacts(appId,drawId)` runs after the notifications: it archives the (expiring) media FIRST (so the version hash + report embed it) then pre-builds BOTH the staff + borrower reports — so the durable copy + both branded PDFs exist the instant findings land, never dependent on a later manual "archive" click (before this, a report built pre-archive had ZERO photos). Every step is best-effort + independently caught: it can NEVER block, reverse, or un-notify the delivery. Off-switch `DRAW_AUTODELIVER_ENABLED=0`. Tests: `scripts/test-sitewire-media-archive.js`, `scripts/test-sitewire-draw-report.js`, `scripts/test-sitewire-auto-deliver.js` (all in `npm test`).
  10. **Draw-project LIFECYCLE — finish the draw process / mark paid off / re-open (owner-directed 2026-07-20; Draw Management #82).** The coordinator closes a project out from the desk: `sitewire_property_links.lifecycle_state` (`db/174`: `active`|`finished`|`paid_off` + `lifecycle_at`/`_by` + `lifecycle_synced`). `orchestrator.setPropertyLifecycle(appId, state, staffId)` records the PILOT-side state (PILOT is the source of record for the desk + ledger, so it works with Sitewire off) and, when writes are on, DEACTIVATES the Sitewire property (`client.updateProperty(id, {inactive:true})` for finished/paid_off; `false` to re-open) so no further borrower draws can be submitted — through the same guarded path as every write: `circuitCheck` + `updateProperty` + read-after-write (`getProperty` re-confirms `inactive` persisted, else PARK `sitewire_lifecycle_verify_failed`) + `journal` + park-on-non-retryable / rethrow-on-retryable. GO-FORWARD ONLY — only a MANAGED file (`matched_by='created'` + a live property) can be closed out (`not_managed` otherwise); idempotent (same state = no-op) EXCEPT a state recorded while writes were off (`lifecycle_synced=false`) re-drives the deactivate once writing is on — so the "no further draws" guarantee actually holds. A worker `backfillUnsyncedLifecycleOnce` (mirrors `backfillStrandedBirthsOnce`) auto-syncs any `lifecycle_synced=false` managed link when the switch flips on. Route `POST /files/:id/lifecycle {state}` (`manage_draws` + `canSeeFile`). The dashboard (`StaffDraws` — the coordinator/admin active-draws view that already exists) surfaces the state per file (`/draws` + `/portfolio` return `lifecycle_state`), adds "Active projects" / "Finished / paid off" filters, and a finished/paid-off project is EXCLUDED from the stale/behind-pace/overdue monitor (it's intentionally done). Per-file control lives in `DrawsPanel` `LifecycleControl` (confirm before firing). Tests: `scripts/test-sitewire-lifecycle-actions.js` (DB-gated; writes-off skip, writes-on sync + read-after-write verify-fail park + 422 park + retryable rethrow + guards, in `npm test`).
  11. **Borrower draw experience — closed dispute loop + own report (owner-directed 2026-07-20; Draw Management #80).** Most of the borrower draw experience already existed (`BorrowerDraws` in `Application.jsx`: see each draw, accept, or dispute a line with photo evidence via `dispute_media`; findings-delivered + release notifications). #80 closed the remaining gaps: (a) **closed dispute loop** — when staff decide the LAST open disputed line (`POST /files/:id/findings/:findingId/lines/:lineId/decide`, finding → `resolved`), the borrower is told the OUTCOME via a designed, borrower-safe `draw_dispute_resolved` notification (per-line "Approved — now $X" / "Reviewed — kept at $Y", only amounts they already see; new type registered in `notify.js` KICKER_OF/CATEGORY_OF/BORROWER_MAJOR_EMAIL) — previously staff decided silently. (b) **the borrower's OWN branded report** — `GET /api/borrower/draws/:appId/report?drawId=` (`borrower-draws.js`, own-file + per-draw IDOR, mode HARD-FORCED to `'borrower'` so a borrower can never get the staff copy) reuses the phase-2b `draw-report` builder + `storeDrawReport` (visibility='borrower'); surfaced as a "Download report (PDF)" button in `BorrowerDraws`. Tests: `scripts/test-sitewire-borrower-draw.js` (notify-map registration + borrower-safe report [partner scrubbed, GPS stripped, no fee/net] + the closed-loop notification row, in `npm test`).
  12. **THE DRAW COORDINATOR IS NEVER OUT OF THE LOOP — a DocuSign viewer on the wire form, and a BCC on every borrower draw email (owner-directed 2026-07-28: "the draw coordinator assigned to a file should be always added as a viewer on the DocuSign package that goes out for the wire request form … receiving notifications when it's being signed", and "on every email that is going out to the borrower about the draw process the draw coordinator and the loan officer should always be looped into that email").** Both halves route through ONE definition of "the file's draw coordinator", `src/lib/draw-recipients.js` — PILOT has no `applications.draw_coordinator_id` (`application_assignees` models only loan_officer / processor, db/103), so `drawCoordinatorsForFile(appId)` reads the assignment off what the coordinator actually DID: (1) `sitewire_property_links.draw_setup_started_by` (whoever pressed "Start the draw process" — written fill-only, so it is the durable per-file record), then (2) a LIVE `workflow_items` hand-off routed `to_role='draw_coordinator'` with a resolved `to_staff_id` — keyed on the ROLE, not on a submission-type list, so a hand-off type added later is covered for free. Deactivated staff are excluded, and a file with nobody assigned falls back to the whole active desk, so a draw message is never uncovered. `coordinatorsOrDesk` is the single place that fallback lives.
      · **The email half is a CHOKEPOINT, not fifteen call sites.** `notify.js` `_borrowerBcc` (the ONE place a borrower email's BCC is composed — the two former copies were folded into it) appends `drawLoopInBcc(appId)` = the file's coordinator(s) + the draws@ desk + the file's loan officer(s) whenever `categoryOf(opts.type) === 'draws'`. Keyed on the notification's own CATEGORY — which `CATEGORY_OF` already assigns to every draw type — so this is true for the draw emails that exist today AND every one added later; the class where a new draw email quietly ships without the desk on it cannot recur. Deliberately NOT gated on `cfg.ccLoanOfficerOnBorrowerEmail`: that switch governs routine borrower mail, and the owner's rule for draws is unconditional ("always"). ADDITIVE — a caller's own `bcc`/`bccExtra` still rides — and it rides the PRIMARY borrower only (`_skipOfficerBcc`), so nobody gets one copy per borrower. Best-effort: a lookup failure leaves the base list untouched and never stops the borrower's email. A NON-draw email is byte-identical to before.
      · **The DocuSign half is scoped to the wire form.** `esign/orchestrate.loadCcViewers(db, appId, purpose)` adds `drawEnvelopeViewers(appId)` (coordinator(s)/desk + the draws@ inbox) as carbon copies when `purpose === 'draw_request'`. A DocuSign CC is a real recipient — DocuSign notifies them as the envelope is sent/viewed/signed and delivers the executed copy + Certificate of Completion, which is exactly what was asked for. NOT added to an origination package (term sheet / Heter Iska): the coordinator has no part in those, and copying them there would put servicing staff on the borrower's loan documents.
      · **ONE draw email deliberately carries NO staff BCC, and it must stay that way:** PILOT's "your wire form is ready to sign" (`esign/notify-signers.js`, `drawWireReadyToSign`) embeds a MAGIC LINK that signs the borrower IN AS THEM straight into the DocuSign ceremony — BCC'ing staff would hand a staffer the ability to execute the wire form in the borrower's legal identity. The coordinator is looped into that exact step the correct way instead: as the DocuSign VIEWER above. Do not "fix" this by adding a BCC.
      · Test `scripts/test-draw-loop-in-db.js` (in `npm test`): the resolver's precedence + deactivated/closed-hand-off/garbage-id cases, all six borrower draw types looped in through the LIVE send path, the non-draw control, the switch-off "always" case, the additive-bccExtra case, one-copy-per-coordinator on a co-borrower file, a real `buildDefinition` on a `draw_request` envelope, the origination package staying clean, and the magic-link no-BCC guard. The email and DocuSign halves were each proven to FAIL with their wiring reverted.
- **ORDER ATTORNEY CLOSING PREP + the per-closing EMAIL CHAIN (owner-directed 2026-07-28; db/359).** A THIRD order on the Orders desk beside Title and Insurance, and the first address family in the app whose job is to capture a chain we do NOT own. It automates a step `db/005` has described by hand since day one — `rtl_p5_atty` ("Attorney email sent: 'File ready for closing prep'", hint "TeamAG@privatelenderlaw.com — attach term sheet, contract (+assignment), LLC docs, insurance invoice, ID") and `rtl_p5_titleinfo` ("Title contact GIVEN to attorney — not CC'd", hint "Attorney opens their own chain with title"). Both conditions are honoured literally and both are nudged to `'received'` (never `satisfied`) when the order sends.
  1. **THE ADDRESS IS THE HOOK, NOT THE REPLY — this is the whole feature.** Every other reply-to here assumes the outside party REPLIES (`file+<appId>@` fans out to the team; `title+/insurance+<appId>@` files a vendor's returned documents onto that order). A closing attorney does not: they take the request and open a BRAND-NEW chain, their own subject, their own recipient list (title, settlement agent, realtor, borrower's counsel, our team), and work the closing there — invisible to the file under the reply model. So the closing-prep email PRINTS a unique address, `closing+<token>@<CHAT_REPLY_DOMAIN>`, and asks them in plain words to keep it on that chain. `file-inbox.recipientsFromEvent` already reads To + Cc + Bcc + envelope recipients, so a merely-CC'd address is enough. The token is a RANDOM 20-hex (80-bit) value, not the application id, for three reasons: it is printed in a body an outsider retypes (so it must be short), it must not publish an internal identifier, and it is rotatable. LOWERCASE HEX deliberately — the `chat+` family is base64url and therefore case-SENSITIVE, which has already caused one live regression (`file-inbox.chatKeyFromRecipients`); hex can be lowercased wholesale like `file+`.
  2. **THE THREAD KEY IS STORED, NOT DERIVED — and that is why `email-log` needed a new argument.** `threadKeyFor` keys a thread on (file, normalized subject), which is exactly wrong here: the attorney's subject is unknown and may change mid-chain. `closing_threads.thread_key` (`<appId>:closing#<token>`, the `#` making a collision with a subject-derived key impossible) is stable and stored, and BOTH `captureOutbound(send, {threadKey})` and `captureInbound({threadKey})` now accept it. Everything else keeps the subject-derived key and is byte-identical. `captureInbound` also finally stores `attachments` + `cc_emails`/`to_emails` — an inbound message used to record NO sign that documents came with it, and on a chain we don't own that is the only record of who else is on it.
  3. **OUR LATER MESSAGES LAND INSIDE THAT CONVERSATION, and never twice.** `closing-thread.sendOnThread` is the ONE chokepoint for putting anything on a chain. Threading is belt-and-suspenders because no single mechanism survives every provider: (a) real RFC headers — we mint our own `Message-ID` per send and reference the chain's root + tip (`In-Reply-To`/`References`), which required a `headers` pass-through in `email/resend.js` (sanitized, CRLF-injection-proof) and `email/graph.js` (Graph accepts ONLY `X-` headers, so it carries `X-Pilot-Closing-Thread` and relies on the subject); (b) the SAME subject, `Re:`-prefixed once — what Gmail and Outlook actually fall back to when a provider rewrites Message-ID. Idempotency is CLAIM-THEN-ACT: the row goes into `closing_thread_messages` first under the partial unique index on `(thread_id, dedupe_key)`, so losing that race means somebody already sent it; a claim that never reached the provider is RELEASED so a retry can claim it again. A `dedupe_key` of NULL is a deliberate human re-send and is always allowed.
  4. **THE THREE AUTOMATIC UPDATES.** `closing-prep.announce()` puts them on the chain and is SILENT (never an error) when the file has no chain — closing prep was never ordered, so there is no attorney to email. **Executed term sheet** ← `esign/webhook.js notifyTerminal`, guarded on `purpose='term_sheet_package' AND !is_test AND application_id`; that is the only correct hook: it runs solely for the WINNER of the terminal claim (`${map.col} IS NULL RETURNING`), so a redelivered webhook racing a poller tick cannot double-send; `handleCompletion` has already filed the signed PDF so there is something to attach; and a counter-signed package reaches `completed` only after the lender's admin signs, so `completed` genuinely means ALL parties. Keyed on the ENVELOPE, so a re-issued and re-executed term sheet is a real new event. It attaches the document THIS envelope produced (via `esign_envelope_docs.completed_document_id`), never "the newest signed term sheet on the file". **New expected closing date** ← fired from EVERY door that can move it (the closing-date route, the workflow closing hand-off, the ClickUp inbound pull) — calling it from several places is deliberate and safe, because the dedupe key and not the caller is what guarantees one email. **THE OWNER'S RULE (owner-directed 2026-07-28): the date is announced ONLY once closing prep has been ORDERED, and from then on EVERY real change is announced.** *"If the estimated closing date is switched again and again BEFORE the closing was set on the closing workflow then it should not get a notification; when you put it on the closing workflow then it should get a notification on the estimated closing date, and going forward after that the notifications should fire."* A date shopped around during underwriting is not news to an attorney who has not been engaged — and the ORDER EMAIL itself is what first tells them the date (`markCarriedByOrder` claims it as `carried`, so the order is never followed seconds later by a separate "the date is now …"). Two mechanisms, both required: **`announce()` refuses without a live `file_orders` row** (`orderIsLive` — order_type `attorney`, status not `not_ordered`/`cancelled`, FAILS CLOSED), because a `closing_threads` row is NOT proof anyone was engaged (`sendOnThread` opens the chain BEFORE it sends, so a failed order leaves one behind, and CANCELLING deliberately keeps it); and **the dedupe key carries the announcement's ORDINAL** (`closing_date:<n>:<prev>-><new>`) so a date that oscillates keeps being announced — keying on `<from>-><to>` alone survived exactly one round trip (A→B→A→B regenerated the first key and went silent, leaving counsel holding a stale date). "Nothing changed" is decided by comparing to what we last SAID, never by the key. **Clear to close** ← `notifyStatusTransition` (the shared announcement for both portal doors) AND `inbound-ctc-confirm.confirmCtc` separately, because the ClickUp-originated CTC deliberately bypasses `notifyStatusTransition`. A BACKSTOP sweep (`notification-digests.closingChainCatchupOnce`, business hours) covers what no door announces — `product-registration` can introduce a file's FIRST closing date through a COALESCE, and a door added next year will not know about any of this. Adding a fourth update is one entry in `AUTO_EVENTS`, one `event_kind` in db/359's CHECK, and one `announce()` call.
  5. **WHAT GOES IN THE EMAIL, and the two things that must NEVER.** Documents come from `tpr-export.selectTprDocuments` — the repo's ONE chokepoint for "every current document connected to this file" — so the package inherits the recursive owning-entity walk, the borrower AND co-borrower profile documents, the current/non-rejected/non-chat filters and the expired-good-standing rule for free; `closing-prep.GROUPS` then buckets it in ATTACH PRIORITY order (term sheet → contract → assignment → entity docs → insurance → photo ID) by TEMPLATE CODE (stable across relabelling) plus `doc_kind` for documents on no condition (a profile driver's licence, a generated term sheet, a vendor's returned binder). The insurance binder/invoice are matched by SUBSTRING on a lowercased `slot_label`, never equality — two writers produce two spellings ("Insurance binder" from the condition UI, "Binder" from the Orders desk) — and an order-return document nobody has labelled yet counts as arrived-unlabelled, not missing. **The HETER ISKA can never leave**: `isFrozenOut` is a SIXTH guard on top of the TPR selector's five, and it needs TWO tests because a doc_kind is snake_case and `_` is a word character, so a `\b` regex finds no boundary inside `heter_iska_signed` — kinds are matched exactly, the word-boundary regex covers the human filename/label. **The INSURANCE CONTACT is never included** — not as a recipient, not in the body — and BOTH types are excluded (`insurance_agent` AND `flood_insurance`), in the SQL, matching every other insurance gate in the app. The TITLE contact is the opposite: it goes IN THE BODY and is never a Cc, which is `rtl_p5_titleinfo`'s rule. The body also carries the deal in words — how many borrowers, the vesting entity, and on an assignment the underlying contract price, the fee AND the effective price the loan is sized on (display only; no frozen engine number is computed here). A blocker set refuses to send until the file is REGISTERED and has a term sheet: the owner's rule is that the attorney cannot draft without terms.
  6. **NOTHING IS EVER SILENTLY DROPPED.** `buildAttachments` fills a per-provider byte budget (`CLOSING_ATTACH_BUDGET_MB` 20; Graph gets 2.5 because it rejects inline attachments past ~3 MB) in priority order, and everything that does not fit — or cannot be read, or is empty, or has no stored copy — comes back in `skipped` WITH A REASON, which is printed in the email, returned to the sender and shown on the card BEFORE they send. A closing attorney quietly one document short is the exact failure this feature exists to prevent.
  7. **INBOUND: the chain's documents get their own package.** `closing-inbox.saveChainDocs` files each attachment as `doc_kind='closing_correspondence'` with `checklist_item_id = NULL` — the owner's "the closing folder of the file, not the closing package, a separate document package". It is deliberately distinct from the post-closing `closing_package` item (the final executed set) and from the closer's `closing_hud_final`/`closing_pkg_signed` conditions (specific documents a human signs off); nothing here clears a condition. SharePoint mirrors it to `Closing/Correspondence`, and it is EXCLUDED from `TPR_DOC_SELECT` — running correspondence and drafts are not investor-delivery documents. Same integrity contract as the Orders desk: `decodeUploadBase64`, stored sha256, magic-byte sniff, and a persisted per-thread `__closing_<token>` marker in `app_results` so a webhook redelivery minutes later cannot double-file. `retrieveAttachmentsSafe` is now memoized per delivery (`attachmentsOnce`) — three consumers wanted the bytes and a closing package was being downloaded three times.
  8. **A reply typed in the closing inbox goes to the ATTORNEY.** `POST /emails/reply` with `scope:'closing'` sends on the chain to the recipients PILOT itself sent to (never from the request body). Without that branch the reply box there is a trap: the default fan-out includes the BORROWER, so answering the closing attorney would have sent lender-to-counsel correspondence — pricing, the whole entity file — to the borrower, and the attorney would never have received it.
  10. **AUDIT PASS (same day, before merge) — five confirmed defects, each now with a regression test.** (a) **A failed send used to kill an automatic update permanently.** The dedupe key IS the "already sent" proof, and a `status='error'` row kept it — so one two-second provider blip meant the attorney was never told the term sheet was executed, from any door, and the backstop could not help either. `settleMessage` now RELEASES the key on `'error'` (`dedupe_key = CASE WHEN status='error' THEN NULL END`) while keeping the row for the audit trail, AND `closingChainCatchupOnce` grew an executed-term-sheet arm that re-drives `esign/webhook.announceExecutedTermSheet` (now exported) for a completed `term_sheet_package` on a chain with no `sent`/`carried` announcement — reusing that function rather than a second copy that could attach the wrong signed document. (b) **THE HETER ISKA LEAKED, in BOTH this package and the investor TPR export.** `\b(iska|heter)\b` needs a non-word character on both sides, so `HeterIska_Signed.pdf` and `HETERISKA.PDF` matched NOTHING and shipped. `FROZEN_NAME_RE` (and the mirrored `TPR_DOC_SELECT` predicate) is now two branches: `heter[\s_.-]*iska` adjacent in any casing with NO boundary requirement (nothing else in a loan file spells that), plus a STANDALONE `iska`/`heter` where the boundary is deliberately KEPT so a "Siska Ave" or an "Iskander" is never silently dropped — verified both directions in JS and in Postgres. Keep the two in step. (c) **Placing the order used to be followed by a phantom "the expected closing date is NOW …"** on essentially every file, because the order email prints the date but nothing claimed it. `markCarriedByOrder` now claims — as `status='carried'`, which holds the key without pretending an email went out — the closing date the order stated, `clear_to_close` on a file already there, and `executed_term_sheet:<envelope>` when the package already contained the executed copy. `lastRecipients` reads only `'sent'`, so a carried row is never a recipient source, and the card labels it "included in the request above". (d) **`POST /emails/reply {scope:'closing'}` FAILED OPEN**: with no chain it fell through to the default fan-out, which includes the BORROWER — the exact outcome the branch exists to prevent. It now refuses with `not_ordered`. (e) **The insurance exclusion keyed only on the mutable directory type**; a vendor retype or a merge walked it past a filter that never read the per-file link's own copy. It now takes the stricter of the two. Also: `cleanEmailList` unwraps a pasted `Name <addr>` (the brackets would have made Graph reject the whole send) and refuses mail-structure punctuation; the attachment budgets parse through one `mbBytes` helper (a typo'd value silently became `NaN`, and `total + len > NaN` is always false — which turned the budget OFF rather than falling back); a $0 effective price is omitted rather than printed to counsel; and `attachmentsOnce` no longer memoizes an EMPTY retrieval, so a transient failure is retried by the next consumer instead of shared by all three.
  11. **ROUND 6 — the six items round 5 left open (db/363, db/364).** Each was reproduced on a real Postgres first; `scripts/test-closing-chain-hardening.js` (in `npm test`) pins all six and every assertion was proven to FAIL on the previous code.
      · **A TRANSIENT FAILURE NO LONGER LOSES A CLOSING DOCUMENT — and the fix is the dedupe, not the marker.** `file-inbox` wrote the `__closing_<token>` marker unconditionally, so a storage or DB blip on attachment 2 of 3 lost that document permanently: the chain read as handled and every redelivery skipped the block. Withholding the marker had been tried and was WORSE, for a reason worth keeping: dedupe went through `doc-dedup.recentDuplicateDocId`, whose window is **120 SECONDS**, so a redelivery minutes later (Resend retries up to 8 times, with backoff) re-filed everything — two missing documents became ten duplicated ones. The real defect was that `saveChainDocs` had always COMPUTED and STORED a `sha256` and never once QUERIED it. `closing-inbox.alreadyFiled` now dedupes on (file, kind, **content hash**, filename) against a CURRENT row with **no window**, so a retry is idempotent however much later it arrives — which is what finally makes withholding the marker safe. `failed` is split into **`failedPermanent`** (undecodable base64, an empty attachment, a file that no longer exists — retrying yields the identical result) and **`failedTransient`** (storage/DB threw); the marker is withheld ONLY for the latter. The retrieval-cap shortfall is deliberately PERMANENT: `retrieveAttachmentsSafe` caps DETERMINISTICALLY, so a 12-document package yields the SAME first 10 every time and blocking on it would block the marker forever — a human asks counsel to resend, which is the only thing that can retrieve it. The team notice now only says "ask the attorney again" for what a retry CANNOT recover; a transient shortfall gets an in-app "being retried automatically" note instead of a false instruction to chase counsel. Different bytes under the same filename still file normally — a revised draft must never be swallowed by the guard that stops redeliveries.
      · **INBOUND MAIL HAS A SENDER VERDICT (db/364 `email_messages.sender_auth`).** The webhook verified RESEND's Svix signature — that the DELIVERY is from our provider — and nothing verified the SENDER. `closing+<token>@` is designed to be BROADCAST to title, the settlement agent, the realtor and counsel, and anything arriving on it is filed automatically, so a spoofed From carrying wiring instructions would look exactly like the real thing. `file-inbox.senderAuth` reads the MTA's own `Authentication-Results` (falling back to a standalone `Received-SPF`) into `{spf,dkim,dmarc,verdict}`: DMARC is the aligned verdict and WINS when present (a list rewrite failing DKIM is not a forgery), otherwise either SPF or DKIM passing is the bar — requiring both would mark most legitimate mail suspicious and train people to ignore the flag. **NO headers is `unknown`, and `unknown` may never render as a pass.** **DELIBERATELY NOT A GATE**: legitimate mail relayed through a list or an assistant's rule fails SPF routinely, and refusing it would lose real closing documents — the expensive direction. It is surfaced instead, in the ALWAYS-VISIBLE part of `ClosingPrepCard` (never inside the collapsed history), because the only moment the warning is worth anything is BEFORE somebody opens the attachment and wires money.
      · **A STALE CLOSING DATE CAN NO LONGER BE THE LAST THING COUNSEL HEARS.** `lastClosingDateAnnouncement` is read outside any lock, so two date changes landing together both saw the same previous announcement, minted DIFFERENT keys (their target days differ, so the partial unique index cannot collapse them) and both sent — in whichever order they happened to finish. `announce()` now re-reads the file's CURRENT date immediately before the send (`currentClosingDay`, the same COALESCE the sweep uses) and stands down as `superseded` when it no longer matches. No lock needed: announce is always called after the write it announces has committed, so the loser sees the winner's date. An unreadable date is NOT a stand-down — a DB blip must never silently drop a real announcement.
      · **ONE MESSAGE, TWO CLOSINGS, TWO RECORDS (db/363).** `uq_email_msgs_inbound` made an inbound message unique GLOBALLY by `inbound_id`, so a reply-all carrying two of our closing addresses recorded on `closingRefs[0]` only — the second file's team watched its closing counter tick up with nothing to read, which is worse than silence. The true identity is "this message, AS RECORDED ON THIS FILE", so the index is now `(inbound_id, COALESCE(application_id::text,''))` — **COALESCE, not a bare column**, or two NULLs would be DISTINCT and an unmatched message would insert a fresh row on every redelivery. `file-inbox` computes `captureTargets` ONCE (one per closing ref, each with its OWN pinned thread key) and all THREE capture sites loop it — including the auto-reply refinement, which had been passing `applicationIds[0]` while the row was filed under a closing ref, harmless only because the old index made it an update-by-inbound_id no-op.
      · **AN ATTORNEY ORDER NOW LEAVES THE ORDERS DESK.** Nothing ever wrote `completed`, so every deal PILOT ever closed still sat there looking like outstanding work. `closing-prep.retireClosedOrdersOnce` (run from the digest dispatcher) moves an `ordered`/`documents_in` attorney order to **`completed`** on a file whose deal is over, and `GET /orders` now hides `completed` alongside `cancelled`. **`completed`, NEVER `cancelled`** — the two are not interchangeable: `attorneyEngaged` treats `cancelled` as an explicit STAND-DOWN, so using it would shut the Follow-up and Email-Center reply doors on every funded file, which is precisely the round-5 regression. `completed` sits outside `('not_ordered','cancelled')`, so a human can still write to counsel after funding (the recorded mortgage, the final title policy, a payoff letter) while `mayAnnounce` stops the automatic updates.
      · **THE BACKSTOP SWEEP CAN NO LONGER SILT UP.** `announce()` refuses a funded/declined/withdrawn file, so those rows could never gain a `sent` message and never left the executed-term-sheet result set; ordered oldest-first under `LIMIT 50`, a handful of long-closed deals permanently crowded out the live file whose announcement had failed — the one thing that query exists to recover. Both arms now filter on **`closingPrep.DEAD_DEAL_SQL`**, and that list is ONE definition (`DEAD_DEAL_STATUSES`, with the SQL literal BUILT from the array) shared by the live gate and the sweep, because any drift between them re-arms the starvation. The cap also logs when it is reached — no silent caps.
  9. Also in this pass: `settlement_agent` is a real `contact_type` (the owner asked for the settlement agent's details and no type meant it — added to all THREE list copies: `routes/staff.js`, `routes/borrower.js`, `FileContacts.jsx`); and `routes/borrower.js`'s contact link used `ON CONFLICT (application_id, contact_type)`, a key `db/078` DROPPED, so the borrower's title/insurance contact form raised Postgres 42P10 on every submission — corrected to the `(application_id, service_contact_id)` spec the staff route uses. Tests `scripts/test-closing-prep-pure.js` + `scripts/test-closing-prep-db.js` (both in `npm test`; the DB one caught a phantom `a.registered_program` column inside a swallowing catch — that column is a Condition-Center RULE field, not a column on `applications`). Rebuild `web/v2/portal` after touching the card.
- **THE TERM SHEET THAT GOES OUT ON DOCUSIGN IS THE FINAL ONE — it may never print "INITIAL TERM SHEET — NOT FINAL" (owner-directed 2026-08-02: "the term sheet going out from the actual loan file from the DocuSign section, after everything is ready and we're sending out the official term sheet, still says not final / initial only. That term sheet should be the final term sheet, because that is what is going to everybody through DocuSign after you finished all the required steps"). db/404.**
  · **ROOT CAUSE — the wording is PRINTED INTO the PDF, and it was decided at the wrong moment.** `web/v2/tools/termsheet.js` draws the provenance badge, the header title ("Final Term Sheet" vs "Preliminary Term Sheet"), the "INITIAL TERM SHEET — NOT FINAL" line and the per-page identity footer from ONE flag, `provFinal`, which the portal host passes in at GENERATION time (`window.TS_PROVENANCE`). The staff pricing route computed that flag from **`esignSendGate(...).ready`** — and `ready` includes the `rtl_p1_product` (Product & pricing) CONDITION SIGN-OFF, which is the step that FOLLOWS the registration, while the PDF is captured DURING the registration. So the flag was false essentially every time: every sheet on every file was stamped initial, the DocuSign package attaches the stored `term_sheet` document verbatim, and the sheet the borrower signed always said it was not final. **THE CLASS: never compute a flag that is baked into an artifact from a condition that can only become true after the artifact is made.**
  · **ONE definition of "final" — `src/lib/esign/term-sheet-stamp.js`**, consumed by the studio's stamp (the pricing route), the register response and the DocuSign send guard, so the three can never disagree. Split like gate.js/gate-disposition.js: `stampFromDisposition(dispo)` is PURE (unit-tested with no DB) and `termSheetStamp(appId,{db})` is the thin reader on top; db + gate are required LAZILY so the rule and its wording constants load with no database in reach. The rule: **every send-gate requirement must be met EXCEPT the P&P sign-off** (`IGNORED_AT_STAMP`, exactly one code — widening that set would let a sheet call itself final on an unfinished file). An APPROVED send-before-CTC exception counts exactly as it does for the send (`dispo.outstanding[].waived`), so a file cleared to send by exception issues a FINAL sheet instead of being stuck on an initial one. Fails CLOSED — an unreadable gate stamps INITIAL, the honest wording.
  · **NOTHING UNSAFE ISSUES BECAUSE OF THAT ONE OMISSION.** `gate.js` still enforces the P&P sign-off in FULL for the actual send — the stamp relaxes the WORDING, never the AUTHORIZATION (pinned by a test that asserts the gate refuses at the exact moment the stamp says final).
  · **The stamp is decided AFTER the registration lands.** `POST /pricing/register` returns `termSheetFinal` computed post-persist (so it knows this registration's own staleness and pending-approval state, not the stale answer the panel fetched before), and `ProductStudioPanel.register()` now **captures the PDF AFTER the register**, calling the new imperative `TermSheetStudio.setProvenance(kind)` first so the sheet it is about to export carries the right stamp in the same tick. The studio panel also SAYS which sheet registering will produce, and names what is still outstanding (`termSheetFinalBlockers`), before the officer clicks.
  · **`documents.term_sheet_final` (db/404) records what the bytes PRINT — a description of the file, never an authorization.** Both upload doors (staff + borrower) take the generator's reported stamp (`b.termSheetFinal === true`, so only an explicit true counts) and persist it; `doc-dedup.recentDuplicateDocId` gained a term-sheet-only `term_sheet_final IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` clause so a FINAL sheet is never collapsed onto an initial one within the 120s window (that would leave the file holding a sheet whose face says NOT FINAL while the send read it as settled). NULL = unknown (every pre-db/404 document) and is treated as NOT final — truthful, because those were all generated under the rule that printed the initial wording.
  · **[SUPERSEDED 2026-08-06 by the "THE SENDER GENERATES THE FINAL" rule below — the refusal described here NO LONGER EXISTS and must not be re-added.]** `orchestrate.js` used to REFUSE to mail a term sheet not recorded as FINAL, permanently, with "re-register in Products & Pricing" as the one-step fix. That refusal was the DEAD END the owner hit twice: re-registering produced another INITIAL (Products & Pricing has only ever stamped the preview copy), so the message named a fix that could not work and the file could not send at all. The stamp itself still decides the WORDING of the studio's preview copy — everything else in this rule stands — but **nothing in the send path may block on it.**
  · **The panel says it BEFORE anyone presses Send** — `tracking.fileEsign` returns `termSheet` from `termSheetStampBlock`, whose document query MIRRORS `orchestrate.latestDocument` so the panel and the send can never judge different sheets; `EsignFileSection` renders it as a blocker with an "Open Products & Pricing" button, the same shape as the Encompass gate, and disables the term-sheet Send + Re-issue with the reason. Best-effort: a read failure reports no block (the send still refuses).
  · Tests `scripts/test-term-sheet-final-stamp.js` (pure — the whole truth table, incl. the exception cases) and `scripts/test-term-sheet-final-stamp-db.js` (real Postgres — the stamp against real conditions, the send refusing initial AND legacy sheets, the panel agreeing with the send, the stamp-aware dedupe), both in `npm test`. Same pass: `scripts/esign-it-fixture.sql` had gone stale (missing `expected_closing`, `rehab_type` and the registration-issuability columns), so `test-esign-orchestrate.js` had been dying before its first assertion — repaired, and it now covers this path end-to-end. Rebuild `web/v2/portal` after touching the panels.
- **THERE IS ONE TERM SHEET AND IT IS THE STUDIO'S SIX-PAGER — every surface, every stage; only the STAMP differs (owner-directed 2026-08-14). THIS REVERSES the 2026-08-06 "the sender generates the final" rule below it, and the short server-side renderer that rule introduced is DELETED.** The owner, on finding a three-page document in a signed DocuSign package: *"I have no idea why, where we're sending the final term sheet package for DocuSign, it's generating a whole different version … My intent was to make the final stamp. When we click Generate Final Term Sheet to Send to DocuSign, it generates the same exact six-pager term sheet, with a final term sheet stamp."* And, choosing the shape: *"we just need to make sure you're using the same system that you're generating every term sheet with. You're also using it to generate the final, and it should not be the short version for the final. The only difference should be that it should have the final stamped instead of the active file stamp."* And on scope: *"If it's on an active file, if it's initial, if it's just on our website, everything should have the same version."*
  · **WHAT THE SIX-PAGER IS.** Page 1 the structure (provenance stamp, parties, hero, loan structure + payments, leverage + cash-to-close, how the loan is built, eligibility snapshot); page 2 key dates + additional loan terms + terms & conditions 1–4; page 3 the full disclosures & conditions; page 4 acceptance & signatures; page 5 the pricing ladder; page 6 Inputs & Loan Derivation. It is drawn by the **Term Sheet Studio** (`web/v2/tools/termsheet.js` `exportPdf` + `drawDerivationPage`), in the BROWSER, because that is where the form fields and the frozen engines it reads both live.
  · **WHY THE SHORT ONE EXISTED, and it is the lesson.** The 2026-08-06 fix was solving a REAL dead end (below), and it fixed it — but the only way to build a sheet on the SERVER was to write a new renderer, and that renderer drew a different, much shorter document. **A fix to the SEND PATH silently replaced the DOCUMENT, and nothing failed:** the sheet generated, the anchors landed, DocuSign sent it, borrowers signed it. It went out on real files for eight days and surfaced only when a human compared two signed PDFs. THE CLASS: when a fix changes WHERE an artifact is produced, it has changed WHAT is produced — the new producer must be proven to emit the same artifact, or the change is a rewrite wearing a bug fix's clothes.
  · **THE REAL DEAD END, and how it is fixed WITHOUT a second document.** The INITIAL/FINAL wording is PRINTED INTO the PDF as it is drawn, so a FINAL sheet can only come from re-drawing it. The 2026-08-02 rule made the sender REFUSE a sheet not recorded FINAL and told staff to *"re-register in Products & Pricing"* — but registering stamps FINAL only when the file is ALREADY ready to issue, so on an unready file re-registering produced another INITIAL and the same refusal returned with the same advice. **A remedy that cannot produce the state the refusal demands is a dead end.** The answer was already built on 2026-08-04 (#1020) and was simply not pointed at: `ProductStudioPanel.finalizeTermSheet()` re-opens the studio on the SAME registered scenario, calls `setProvenance('file_final')`, captures the six-pager and attaches it — no re-pricing, no re-register side effects — and `EsignFileSection`'s Send button runs it as `finalizeAndSend`. So: the refusal stays, and `term-sheet-stamp.REGENERATE_MESSAGE` now names THAT BUTTON. Three ways forward and no dead end — finalize-and-send, finalize-now, or the super-admin override (db/469). **Never restore the "re-register the product" wording**; it is what produced the loop.
  · **THE STAMP IS THE ONLY DIFFERENCE.** `PROV_COPY.file_final` prints "FINAL TERM SHEET / Issued for signature from the loan file"; `PROV_COPY.file` prints "ACTIVE LOAN FILE" + "INITIAL TERM SHEET — NOT FINAL". Products & Pricing stamps `file_final` when the register response says the file is ready (`resp.termSheetFinal`) and `file` otherwise — so a ready file's registration attaches the FINAL sheet directly and the Send button has nothing left to do.
  · **WIRING, so nothing can drift back.** `orchestrate.PACKAGES.term_sheet_package` carries the term sheet as a STORED document (`freshnessCheck: true`) — deliberately **NOT** `generate: true`; `docgen.BUILDERS` has **no** `term_sheet` entry and must never gain one; `esign/term-sheet-pdf.js` and `orchestrate.buildTermSheetView` are DELETED; `tracking.termSheetStampBlock` reports the real stamp again (hard-coding `{final:true, block:false, canFinalize:false}` is what left the finalize buttons in the bundle but permanently unreachable). Guarded by `scripts/test-one-term-sheet.js` (in `npm test`), which fails the build if a server-side term-sheet renderer or a `term_sheet` builder comes back.
  · **THE BORROWER'S EMAIL WAS A THIRD LEAK.** The 2026-08-12 "attach the term sheet to the terms-are-ready email" work (#1146) had only the short renderer to reach for, so borrowers were emailed the three-pager too. `terms-notify.js` now attaches the STORED six-pager and never renders one — and only when the stored sheet belongs to the registration being announced (`documents.created_at >= product_registrations.created_at`), because the studio uploads just AFTER the register call returns, so the newest stored sheet can still be the PREVIOUS registration's. This email exists because a headline number moved, so mailing the pre-change sheet would hand the borrower the very figures we changed; with nothing fresh to attach it sends without one, exactly as it did before 2026-08-12.
  · **A SERVER-SIDE RENDERER IS NOT FORBIDDEN — a SECOND DOCUMENT is.** If one is ever genuinely needed (a headless send with no browser in reach), it must reproduce all six pages and be proven against the studio's output page by page, not written fresh from the registered quote because that was the reachable data.

- **A VALUE WE COMPOSE FOR AN EXTERNAL API IS CAPPED AT THAT API'S LIMIT — AT THE CHOKEPOINT (owner-reported 2026-07-27: "Send failed. DocuSign 400 … Length exceeds the maximum of 100 characters for 'emailSubject'").** DocuSign caps `emailSubject` at **100 characters** and does NOT truncate — it rejects the ENTIRE envelope create with a 400, so the borrower never receives the documents at all. ROOT CAUSE: the subject is assembled by plain string concatenation and nothing in the codebase knew the limit existed. The template spends ~60 characters before the address is even appended (a 24–37-char package wording + `Loan #` + a 14-char YS loan number), leaving barely **37** for the property — which the 2026-07-21 change had started appending so a counter-signer can tell which property they're signing. So the address was NOT unusually long: `129 Carlisle St, Wilkes-Barre, PA 18702` is an ordinary address, and the subject came to **102**. ANY address over ~37 characters failed, on all three packages (term sheet, Heter Iska, draw request), for every file since 2026-07-21. TWO LAYERS, never remove either: **(1) the structural backstop** — `docusign.js` `capEmailSubject()` (surrogate-safe, `EMAIL_SUBJECT_MAX=100`) is applied inside `buildEnvelopeDefinition`, which EVERY envelope in the system is built by, so no caller present or future can post an over-long subject; **(2) the smart fit** — `esign/orchestrate.js` `composeSubject()` keeps the package wording + loan number WHOLE (what the signer and the admin counter-signer identify the request by) and shortens only the ADDRESS via `fitAddress()`, which drops the least-identifying end first (zip → state → city, matching the canonical "street, city, ST zip" shape from `address.canonicalOneLine`) so it degrades to "129 Carlisle St, Wilkes-Barre, PA" instead of a mid-word chop; an ellipsis clip is the last resort only. A subject that already fits is byte-identical to before. Test `scripts/test-esign-subject.js` (in `npm test`) pins the reported case + every package × a battery of real addresses ≤100 with the loan number intact. **THE CLASS, for every future integration:** when you compose a value from file/user data and hand it to an external API field that has a documented length limit, cap it where the request is BUILT (the one chokepoint), not at each call site — and prefer shortening the variable part intelligently over blind truncation. Never add a new subject/name/label sent to a third party without checking its limit first (DocuSign: `emailSubject` 100, document `name` 100, `emailBlurb` 10000).
- **SharePoint one-way sync — NEVER delete; never touch human-curated content (owner-directed, 2026-07-12; design finalized 2026-07-13; HARD RULE).** Every document saved on the server is mirrored automatically into the team site at `Pipeline Drive/<Officer>/<Borrower>/<Address>/YS portal syncing/<Condition>/` (`src/lib/sharepoint.js` Graph client, `src/lib/sharepoint-map.js` fuzzy folder resolver, `src/lib/sharepoint-backup.js` reconciler; state in `sharepoint_folder_cache`/`sharepoint_condition_state`, per-doc stamps on `documents`; gated by `SHAREPOINT_BACKUP_ENABLED` + `MS_*` creds).
  - **The `<Condition>` folder is the SAME clean TPR-export category — not the long condition label (owner-directed 2026-07-30: "the sync by Pilot folder should look like the TPR export").** `sharepoint-backup.categoryPathFor` used to file a condition doc under its raw checklist LABEL ("Assets & bank statements verified — met", "Assignment letter (if the contract is assigned)", …). It now routes every condition/loose doc through `tpr-export.categoryFor` — the SAME categorizer that names the TPR package's folders — so the mirror reads "Bank Statements", "Contract & Assignment", "Appraisal", "Credit Report", "EMD", "Insurance", "TITLE", "ID", "Scope of Work", "Other Documents".
