The STRUCTURAL homes are unchanged (LLC nests under the LLC name; REO/<property>; Term Sheet Unsigned/Signed; photo ID → "Photo ID"; TPR Exports; Draw Reports; Closing [+ Correspondence]; Chat Attachments). GOING-FORWARD only for the live mirror (a new doc lands in the clean folder; `ensureChildFolder` is exact-match so old long-named folders are never renamed by the mirror — no-rename policy holds). PREVIOUS files: the DRY-RUN repair `scripts/sharepoint-recategorize-existing.js` renames the automation's OWN condition folders to the clean names via `sp.renameOwnItem` (createdByThisApp + in-mirror ancestry + sibling-collision-skip + eTag — two old folders → one clean name are SKIPPED not merged; nothing moved/deleted; run with `--apply`); the EMD slice of that repair ALSO runs automatically as the one-shot boot pass `src/lib/sharepoint-emd-refolder.js` (owner-directed 2026-07-31 — see the EMD rule in the note-buyer section). Never re-inline a category map — reuse `tpr-export.categoryFor` so the mirror and the TPR package can never drift. Test `scripts/test-sharepoint-category.js` (pure, in `npm test`). The rules, in order of severity:
  1. **NEVER delete or recycle anything, anywhere in SharePoint — with ONE owner-sanctioned exception (amended 2026-07-16).** `sharepoint.remove()` is a permanent throwing no-op and no general Graph `DELETE` may exist. The single exception: `sharepoint.deleteReplacedCorruptMirror()` may delete a mirror copy the integrity audit DIAGNOSED as corrupt, ONLY after its verified good "(fixed copy)" replacement is uploaded and re-verified live, ONLY inside a Pilot-created sync tree, behind seven mandatory guards (kill switch `SHAREPOINT_DELETE_REPLACED_CORRUPT=0`, DB-ownership ref, replacement-first size verification, same-size-as-diagnosed, expected-parent, Pilot-leaf ancestry walk, If-Match eTag pinning — full text in docs/SHAREPOINT-POLICY.md). Never relax a guard, never reuse the function for "cleanup"/dedupe/any other purpose, never add another delete path. Everything else: deletion stays a manual, human-only action in the SharePoint UI.
  2. **Never overwrite/replace an existing file** — all uploads use `conflictBehavior:'fail'` and uniquify on conflict.
  3. **Moves/renames are forbidden with ONE owner-approved exception:** the reconciler may move **its own previously-uploaded mirror copies** (verified against `documents.sharepoint_backup_ref` AND an expected-parent check in `sp.moveOwnItem`) between folders **it created inside a `YS portal syncing` folder**. Today the ONE sanctioned mover is the SHELF sweep `sharepoint-backup.refileRow` (see the three-shelf rule below); the old supersede-driven Version-N shuffle it replaced is retired. Nothing human-created or human-moved is ever touched (the expected-parent check makes a human-moved file unmovable by us).
  4. **One-way**: documents flow portal → SharePoint only. Folder NAMES are read for fuzzy matching (borrower middle-name tolerant; address matching requires house-number equality + EXACT suffix-normalized pre-comma street tokens, so stage folders like "Open loan" and different streets like "Oak Street Extension" can never match); document bytes are never read back from SharePoint. No confident match → CREATE a folder, never guess into someone else's. **Every folder the automation creates at the officer/borrower/address level is named `<name>, YS portal sync`** (owner-directed 2026-07-13) so staff can tell API-created folders from human ones — matched folders are never renamed, and the marker is stripped during matching. Layout specials: LLC docs nest under the LLC's NAME with `Formation Documents`/`EIN Letter`/`Operating Agreement` subfolders (a LAYERED owning entity is its own llcs row, so it gets its own named folder the same way); photo IDs always file at the borrower PROFILE level; track-record docs file into `REO/<project address>` — one folder per line item, ALWAYS at the borrower profile level (owner-directed 2026-07-13; even when the upload came through a per-file request condition, `scopeKeyFor` routes track-record docs to the borrower scope so there is ONE REO tree per borrower); term sheets live in `Term Sheet/Unsigned` (the `Signed` sibling arrives with the DocuSign integration).
  5. The app permission is `Sites.ReadWrite.All` (owner-approved 2026-07-13 — owner declined the `Sites.Selected` downgrade); because that scope technically permits deletes, the no-delete guarantee is enforced **in code and by this rule**, audited on every change that touches a Graph call path. Auth is dual: certificate (`MS_CLIENT_CERT_PEM`) preferred, client-secret fallback.
  The binding policy statement lives in `docs/SHAREPOINT-POLICY.md`; design + verification history in `docs/SHAREPOINT-INTEGRATION-RESEARCH.md`.
- **THE MIRROR STILL SAVES EVERYTHING — but the category folder holds only what somebody ACCEPTED (owner-directed 2026-08-03, right after db/424 stopped un-accepted documents leaving the building: "yes do the SharePoint folder too … even though it gets rejected, we should always still save copies in folders. So we should have a folder with all old versions and old old versions to be placed in the old version folder"). db/425.** Three shelves inside every category folder, and the layout is the owner's own pick: `<Category>/` = accepted + current (what a human opens the folder to see, and the same set the export ships); `<Category>/Waiting for review/` = nobody has decided on it yet; `<Category>/Old Versions/Version N/` = rejected AND superseded, grouped per batch. **NOTHING IS DELETED — a rejected document is MOVED, never removed**; the no-delete policy above is untouched and the whole re-filing path contains no delete of any kind (guarded by a source assertion in the pure test).
  · **ONE rule decides the shelf — `src/lib/sharepoint-shelf.js` `shelfFor(row)`**, used by the mirror AND the sweep so they can never disagree. ORDER MATTERS: `is_current=false` is checked FIRST, so an ACCEPTED document a newer one replaced is an OLD VERSION, not the live copy. It FAILS CLOSED — an unrecognised review state is shelved, never left on the live shelf — and an absent `is_current` reads as current so a NULL never buries a live document.
  · **A document is mirrored within SECONDS of upload, long before anyone reviews it, so its shelf is not knowable at mirror time.** It is filed on "Waiting for review" and MOVED when the verdict lands, by the `refileOnce`/`refileRow` sweep on the mirror's own cadence. `documents.sharepoint_shelf` records where the copy actually IS, so the sweep finds the handful of changed documents with ONE indexed query instead of asking Graph about everything ever mirrored. NULL = mirrored before db/425, and it deliberately means "work it out and file it", never "leave alone" — that is how the back book gets tidied, a few documents per pass.
  · **The sweep selects through the SHARED `ENRICH_SELECT`, and that is load-bearing.** It must resolve `stateKeyFor(row, scopeKeyFor(row))` to the SAME key the mirror recorded the category folder under, and that key runs through `categoryFor` — which reads the condition label, the template code, the LLC name and the track-record address. A hand-written SELECT that omits any of them categorises the document differently, the state lookup misses, and every affected row is skipped as `no_category_folder` FOREVER (that is exactly what the first cut did, silently, and it is invisible because a skip is not logged). Pinned by a source guard in `test-sharepoint-shelf-pure.js`.
  · **The Version counter is bumped ONCE per stream per pass**, so a whole supersede batch lands in ONE `Version N` folder — never one folder per document (the "Version 47" explosion this file already fought once). A LATER supersede is its own batch and gets the next number, which is what "old versions and old old versions" means when you open the folder.
  · **A HUMAN'S ARRANGEMENT WINS, PERMANENTLY.** The move goes through `sp.moveOwnItem` pinned to `expectedParentId`, so a copy somebody dragged or renamed is unmovable by us; that refusal (404 / 412 / itemNotFound / "moveOwnItem refused") stamps the shelf we WANTED plus a plain reason on the row, so the sweep stops re-asking Graph about it every pass. Any OTHER error is rethrown — it is transient and belongs to the next pass.
  · A document PILOT itself generates or orders is born `accepted` (db/424), so it files straight into the category folder with no detour. Tests `scripts/test-sharepoint-shelf-pure.js` + `scripts/test-sharepoint-shelf-db.js` (both in `npm test`; the DB one drives a real mirror + sweep against an in-memory SharePoint and asserts zero deletes). When you add anything that decides WHERE a mirrored copy lives, ask `sharepoint-shelf` — never re-inline a review test or a folder name.
- **SharePoint mirror INTEGRITY + anti-churn layer (owner-directed 2026-07-15, the corrupted-files / "Version 47" incident) — never regress any of these:**
  1. **Every upload decode goes through `lib/upload-bytes.decodeUploadBase64()`** — never a bare `Buffer.from(x,'base64')`. Node's lenient decoder silently SKIPS invalid characters, so a `data:`-URL prefix shifts the base64 alignment and garbles EVERY byte of the stored file (verified: `%PDF` at no offset) — that garbage then mirrors to SharePoint as "a corrupted document that won't open." The chokepoint strips a `data:` prefix, accepts url-safe/wrapped base64, and REJECTS (400) anything else. Any new upload endpoint must use it.
  2. **Every mirror upload is integrity-VERIFIED**: `sharepoint.uploadNew` fails any upload whose committed driveItem is missing id/size or whose size differs from the bytes sent; the reconciler additionally compares Microsoft's QuickXorHash (implemented in `sharepoint.quickXorHash`, self-calibrating against Graph on fresh uploads — one calibration failure falls back to size-only, never mass-flags). A failed check = a failed row = retry; a bad item is never recorded as a document's mirror. **EXCEPTION — Office formats (xlsx/docx/pptx…, `sharepoint.isOfficeFormat`): SharePoint REWRITES these seconds after upload ("property promotion" — size AND hash drift). Root cause of the 2026-07-16 stuck-xlsx queue: NEVER byte-compare an Office file after upload.** Their integrity is proven once from the PUT response (size drift there = promotion, warn-only); post-upload identity comes from PROVENANCE (`createdByThisApp` + exact name + our folder — the adopt path), and the verify audit stamps them ok without size/hash comparison. Never "simplify" Office handling back to byte equality.
  3. **The corrupted-mirror audit + re-sync** (`sharepoint-backup.verifyOnce/drainVerify`, boot + every `SHAREPOINT_VERIFY_POLL_SEC` [6h default], admin: `POST /api/admin/sharepoint/verify`, health: `GET /api/admin/sharepoint/health`): compares LOCAL bytes vs the mirrored item's METADATA (size/hashes — bytes are never downloaded back; one-way holds). Corrupt mirror → the good copy re-uploads into the SAME folder named `<name> (fixed copy)<ext>` and the doc re-points to it; the corrupt item is NEVER deleted (no-delete policy) — audit-logged for manual cleanup. `source-suspect` (magic-byte sniff says the bytes were garbage BEFORE upload, e.g. an HTML e-sign page saved as .pdf) → sync-review row telling the LO to request a fresh copy — re-mirroring can't fix those. `local-missing` (portal bytes gone) → review row, mirror untouched (it may be the only copy).
  4. **Autosaved snapshot streams NEVER version-shuffle** (the Version-47 root fix). Regen kinds (`track_record_html`, `tpr_export`, `*_export` — system-regenerable artifacts, `isRegenKind()`): same-session autosaves COALESCE onto one documents row (`track-record-snapshot.js`, 10-min window, only while un-mirrored); rows superseded before mirroring settle WITHOUT uploading (`settleSupersededSnapshots`); survivors get a long settle window (`SHAREPOINT_SNAPSHOT_SETTLE_SEC`, 10 min default) and land in the category folder ROOT — no Version-N folders for autosaves, ever. HUMAN uploads keep the owner's full Version-N history flow.
  5. **Duplicates are structurally suppressed**: byte-identical same-name same-scope docs share one mirror copy (sha256 dedup); an upload-succeeded-but-unrecorded retry ADOPTS the existing identical item (size+hash match) instead of minting a `(id)` duplicate; cross-process drains are serialized by DB leases (`sync_locks` — deploy overlap / scale-out can't double-upload); the boot attempt-reset is time-guarded (30 min) so deploy bursts don't re-burn Graph retries.
  6. `documents.sha256` / `sharepoint_item_size` / `sharepoint_verified_at` / `sharepoint_integrity` / `sharepoint_skipped_reason` (db/115) record the audit trail; `sharepoint_skipped_reason` distinguishes deliberately-not-uploaded (superseded snapshot, duplicate bytes) from mirrored. Tests: `scripts/test-sharepoint-integrity.js` (chokepoint, quickXor vs spec, regen classification — no DB needed).
  7. **Every SQL predicate on `d.doc_kind` MUST be NULL-safe (`COALESCE(d.doc_kind,'')`) — a bare column is a three-valued-logic trap (root cause of the recurring "(not yet attempted)" alert, fixed 2026-07-21).** `doc_kind IS NULL` for an ORDINARY upload (insurance PDF, invoice, most human docs — the column is only set for `photo_id`/`term_sheet`/`track_record_html`/`*_export`/etc.). `REGEN_KIND_SQL` compared a bare `d.doc_kind`, so for a NULL kind the whole expression was SQL **NULL, not FALSE**; fed into the drain + stray-net regen-skip guard `NOT (REGEN_KIND_SQL AND is_current=false)`, a doc with `doc_kind NULL` **and** `is_current=false` (an ordinary upload that got SUPERSEDED on its slot before the 4s-debounced drain caught it) evaluated to `NOT(NULL)=NULL` → the row was silently FILTERED OUT of BOTH `pendingBatch` and `neverAttemptedStrays`, yet `stuckDocuments`/`reconciliation` (no regen guard) still counted it → it sat "(not yet attempted)" for the 6h→12h window until the escalation force-attempted it, and RECURRED for every newly-superseded ordinary upload. The fix wraps every `d.doc_kind` in `REGEN_KIND_SQL` (`src/lib/sharepoint-backup.js`) in `COALESCE(d.doc_kind,'')` — one definition, propagates to all six consumers — matching `NEVER_MIRROR_SQL` and the JS `isRegenKind()` (which already null-guards via `String(k||'')`). Belt-and-suspenders: `stuckEscalateHours()` now escalates at the SLO threshold (not 2×), so any future strand self-heals WHEN the alert fires, not 6h later. Regression test `scripts/test-sp-regen-null-guard-db.js` (DB-gated; proven to fail on the bare-column version). When adding ANY selector/settle predicate that references `doc_kind` (or any nullable column) inside a negated/`AND`ed guard, COALESCE it — a bare nullable column in `NOT(... AND ...)` silently drops rows.
  8. **The mirror is PROVIDER-AGNOSTIC — never re-add a `storage_provider='local'` filter (owner-reported 2026-08: the `[sp-sync] WARNING … STORAGE_PROVIDER='s3' … will not pick up documents` alarm).** When S3 shipped as a real storage provider (`STORAGE_PROVIDER=s3` → the dual-read `s3`/`local` composite in `src/lib/storage.js`), every SharePoint-mirror SELECTION path still filtered `COALESCE(storage_provider,'local')='local'` — a placeholder from when `s3` was a stub — so every S3 document was STRANDED: never queued by `pendingBatch`, never claimed by the FSM `claimBatch`, never verified, never counted as normal backlog, only a one-shot stray attempt that then CARDED it "cannot read". The byte-read was ALWAYS provider-agnostic (`storage.read` → dual-read), so the gap was purely selection. Fix: the `'local'` filters are removed from `pendingBatch`, `sp-mirror-queue.claimableWhere` (kept in lock-step so `shadowCompare` parity holds), `verifyBatch`, `stuckDocuments`, and the reconciliation `pending`/`oldest_pending_secs` counts; bytes are read from the doc's OWN provider via `providerForRow(row)` (a `'local'`/NULL doc reads straight from disk — no wasted S3 GET — and `probe()` is then the RIGHT provider's health, so a transiently-unmounted local disk still RETRIES instead of parking permanent). The stray-net's non-local special-casing is gone (a failed attempt is surfaced by the ordinary permanent-card / stuck-doc path); `nonlocal_pending` stays as an INFORMATIONAL provider breakdown only (dropped from `needs_attention`); `classifyMirrorError` now maps an S3 404 → permanent-missing and 403 → permanent-creds; and the startup warning fires ONLY for a provider the storage layer genuinely can't read (the not-yet-implemented `sharepoint` stub), never for `s3`. **The integrity-audit `verifyRow` applies the SAME transient-vs-missing triage as `mirrorRowInner` (audit follow-up):** it raises the sticky `local-missing` / "the mirror may be the only surviving copy — do not delete it" verdict ONLY when the storage is healthy AND the error is a genuine missing-object signal (`SOURCE_MISSING_RE` = ENOENT / S3 404 / NoSuchKey); a transient S3 5xx/timeout (the dual-read wrapper only falls back to local on 404/403, so a 5xx propagates straight to the verify read), a transiently-unmounted local disk, or an S3 403 access-denied instead stamp `verify-error` (re-check next rotation, no false alarm) — otherwise a routine S3 blip during the 6-hourly verify would false-flag the whole mirrored corpus "only copy left" and flip the mirror unhealthy. Test `scripts/test-sp-verify-transient-db.js`. `local`-mode deployments are byte-identical (every doc is `local`, so removing the filter changes nothing). Tests: `test-sp-strays-slo-db.js` + `test-sp-mirror-queue-db.js` now assert an s3 doc is selected/claimed and mirrors through the normal path; `test-sharepoint-integrity.js` covers the `explainExclusion` change. NEVER reintroduce a provider filter into a mirror selector — "which providers can be mirrored" is decided by what `storage.read` supports, not by a hard-coded `='local'`.
  9. **THE AUDIT LOOKS FOR THE COPY BEFORE IT DECLARES IT DELETED — and an "item-missing" verdict stays in the rotation (owner-reported 2026-08-20, file YSCAP258134738 / ASHER SALAMON: "Assignment_Asher_Salamon_734_Dennis_Pl v2 JM CLEAN - signed.pdf — its mirror copy is no longer in SharePoint").** The mirror held exactly ONE handle on its SharePoint copy — the driveItem id in `documents.sharepoint_backup_ref` — and `verifyRow` treated a single Graph 404 on it as PROOF the copy had been deleted. A driveItem id survives a rename and an in-drive move; it does NOT survive a delete-then-restore that mints a new id, a re-upload, or a drag through an Explorer/OneDrive-synced folder (a create+delete server-side). In every one of those the document is sitting in the drive and the audit reported it gone.
     · **THE STAMP WAS WRITTEN AND NEVER READ.** `uploadAndRecord` has always stamped `PilotDocumentId` onto every mirrored item, commented "so the link survives ANY human rename/move" (roadmap R1) — and a repo-wide grep found `PilotDocumentId` written in exactly one place and read in NONE. The durable link was built and then not used; the audit verified by the fragile handle instead. `sp.findByPilotDocumentId` is that missing read (a read-only list query of our own library — the same class as `listChildren`, no bytes ever downloaded), and `ensurePilotColumns` now INDEXES `PilotDocumentId` too: that lookup IS the "future id-based lookup query" the `indexed:` line was written for, and it was indexing the wrong one of the two id columns.
     · **`relocateMirror` IS THE LADDER, cheapest first, and a hit re-points the row and CONTINUES the verify** — so the normal size/hash comparison still has to pass and a relocation is never taken as a clean bill of health: (1) **its own folder, its own name** (the restored / re-uploaded copy back where it belongs with a fresh id), accepted ONLY if Graph's `createdBy` says our app made it, so a human's same-named file is never claimed as ours; (2) **the `PilotDocumentId` stamp, drive-wide** — the exact stamp match IS the proof of ownership, so no further test is needed. The mirrored NAME is recovered from the recorded `sharepoint_web_url` (`mirrorNameFromWebUrl`, pure) because the mirrored name is DERIVED — echo-stripped, sanitized, possibly trimmed or uniquified — so it is NOT `documents.filename` and cannot be re-derived without re-running that whole pipeline. `itemId` is re-bound after a relocation so the corrupt-mirror audit entry names the item that exists, never the dead id. No hit → the old behaviour, unchanged.
     · **AN `item-missing` VERDICT IS RE-CHECKED TOMORROW, NOT IN 30 DAYS.** `stampVerdict` backdated only `verify-error`; `item-missing` was stamped `now()`, so `verifyBatch`'s `verified_at < now() - 30 days` predicate exiled it for a MONTH — and the ONE thing that puts the copy back is a person (a recycle-bin restore, which keeps the driveItem id, or a re-upload). So a copy restored an hour later stayed "missing" for thirty days with the loan officer's card open the whole time, because `sync-review-recheck` can only close that card on a good integrity verdict and only this pass writes one. `RECHECK_SOON_RE` now covers both. The cost is one metadata read per affected document per rotation, and the population is by definition tiny (it makes the mirror report unhealthy); `queueReview` dedupes per document and a dismissal sticks, so nothing re-notifies.
     · **THE EMAIL TELLS THE TRUTH ABOUT WHETHER PILOT IS RETRYING.** `sharepointDocEmail` said "PILOT keeps retrying on its own" on EVERY mirror card. For the four verdicts the integrity audit writes (`item-missing` / `local-missing` / `source-suspect` / `malware-flagged`) that is FALSE and provably so: `stampVerdict` leaves `sharepoint_backed_up_at` SET, and every selector that feeds the mirror — `pendingBatch`, `neverAttemptedStrays`, `stuckDocuments`, `forceAttemptDoc` — requires it NULL, so the document is in NO work queue and sits exactly as it is until a human clicks Retry. That sentence is what decides whether an officer opens the screen today or leaves it to the automation, so it is now per-verdict (`SP_DOC_PARKED`, keyed on the card's own `raw_value.kind`); an upload failure genuinely IS retried and its copy is unchanged, and an unreadable/absent `raw_value` falls back to the retrying wording (never a false "stopped").
     · **DELIBERATELY NOT AUTO-RE-MIRRORED.** A human deleting a copy is their prerogative (the standing rule in the verify-verdict block above), and re-uploading over a deliberate deletion would fight them in a loop. The fix is to LOOK before crying wolf and to keep looking — never to overrule the person. Tests `scripts/test-sp-item-missing-relocate-db.js` (in `npm test`; the re-find by folder+name and by stamp, a human's same-named file refused, the honest missing verdict, the backdating with a "checked just now" CONTROL, and a restored copy healing itself + auto-closing the officer's card) + the truthful-copy section of `test-sharepoint-doc-lo-email.js`; three mutations of the production code were each proven to fail them. When you add another way to find a mirror copy, put it in `relocateMirror`'s ladder — never a second re-find, and never widen the provenance guard.
  10. **A CONTAINER'S PAYLOAD MAY NEVER IMPERSONATE THE CONTAINER — an ANCHORED signature outranks a scan-anywhere heuristic, always; and the fix advice must name an action the reader can actually take (owner-reported 2026-08-20, file YSCAP258134701 / Dovid Turim: "TPR_YSCAP258134701_2026-07-21.zip — the FILE ITSELF appears corrupted (content is pdf, not zip) … request a fresh copy from whoever uploaded it", and "we're getting this lately a lot").** Two defects, one card.
      · **ROOT CAUSE — REPRODUCED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS CHANGED, not reasoned about.** `upload-bytes.sniffKind` ran its tolerant "%PDF anywhere in the first 1KB" scan BEFORE the anchored `PK` ZIP signature. `lib/zip.js` is STORE-only ON PURPOSE — its members are already-compressed PDFs, so compressing them again buys nothing — which means the first member's raw `%PDF` sits about SEVENTY bytes into the package, comfortably inside that 1KB window. Measured on the real writer's output: the bytes start `PK\x03\x04`, are a valid zip, and `%PDF` first appears at offset 73; `expectedKind` said `zip` and `sniffKind` said `pdf`. So the integrity audit reported a perfectly good package PILOT had just built ITSELF as corrupted — on essentially EVERY TPR export whose first document is a PDF, which is what "we're getting this lately a lot" was. **THE CLASS: a heuristic that scans a WINDOW can always be satisfied by a container's PAYLOAD, so it may never be judged before a signature anchored at byte 0.** The tolerance itself is correct and is KEPT (the PDF spec allows a preamble and real readers scan for the header) — it is DEMOTED below every anchored signature, never removed, and HTML moved up with it for the same reason: an e-sign error page saved as `.pdf` that happens to mention `%PDF` in its body is exactly the accident the detector exists to catch, and it used to slip through as a PDF.
      · **WHO MADE THE FILE DECIDES WHAT A PERSON CAN DO ABOUT IT.** "Request a fresh copy from whoever uploaded it" is unactionable nonsense on a document PILOT generated itself — a TPR export, a track-record snapshot, a draw report: NOBODY uploaded it, so nobody can re-send it. `verifyRow` now reads `row.source_type === 'system' || isRegenKind(row.doc_kind)` and says "re-run the export" instead; the card carries `pilotMade` in its `raw_value` so `sync-review.sharepointDocEmail` can say the same thing to the loan officer, and `source-suspect:pilot` joins `SP_DOC_PARKED` so the email is honest about PILOT not retrying on its own (the 2026-08-20 parked-verdict rule above). Advice a reader cannot act on is a defect, not cosmetics.
      · **PREVIOUS AND FUTURE — `src/lib/sp-source-suspect-recheck.js`.** The sniffer is fixed going forward; every document already stamped `source-suspect` under the old rule would keep its false verdict and its open officer card forever, because `verifyBatch` only re-reads a row whose stamp is older than the 30-day window. The one-shot boot pass CLEARS `sharepoint_verified_at` on those rows and **RE-JUDGES NOTHING ITSELF** — that is the whole design: clearing the stamp puts the row at the head of the audit's own queue (its selector orders NULLS FIRST) and the audit re-decides it with every guard it already has, remote comparison included. Re-reading the bytes here would create a SECOND place that judges content, and the one that drifts is the one that leaks. A row that really IS damaged is simply re-stamped, and `queueReview` dedupes on the open card so nobody is re-emailed about a card they already have (and a dismissal still sticks). Marker-guarded exactly like `sharepoint-emd-refolder` (clearing unconditionally on every boot would loop: clear → audit stamps → clear again), it stamps the marker only when the batch was NOT full so a truncated pass is finished by the next boot rather than declared done, and it never throws — a boot pass may not break boot. Off with `SP_SOURCE_SUSPECT_RECHECK_DISABLED=1`.
      · Tests `scripts/test-content-sniff-order-pure.js` (which builds its fixture with the REAL `zip()` writer, deliberately NOT a hand-rolled `PK…%PDF` buffer — the bug lived in the interaction between our own STORE-only layout and the sniffer, so a fake buffer could pass while the real thing still failed) and `scripts/test-sp-source-suspect-db.js` (real Postgres: the reported export judged healthy with no card raised, a genuinely damaged upload still caught and still told to ask its uploader, a damaged PILOT-generated file told to re-run the export on both the card and the email, and the one-shot re-check clearing a stale verdict exactly once). Both in `npm test`; four mutations of the production code were each proven to fail them. **The DB suite stubs Graph to a HEALTHY mirror of each row's own bytes, never to a 404** — a 404 diverts `verifyRow` into its item-missing branch, which raises its OWN card and OVERWRITES the verdict, silently masking the very check under test (a first cut of that suite did exactly this).
  13. **THERE IS NOT ONE "APPROVED" — THERE ARE FIVE; A LINE'S PHOTOS ARE PROVEN FROM EVERY PLACE WE HOLD THEM; AND OUR DRAW FEE IS KNOWN BEFORE THE WIRE IS RECORDED (owner-reported 2026-08-03, file YSCAP258134591 / 195-197 Parrish St draw #1: a fully inspected, fully photographed $25,000 roof draw whose report led with "APPROVED FOR RELEASE $0", whose four roof lines were each flagged "no inspection photos attached", whose $299 fee was nowhere, and whose Excel packet still showed the whole $25,000 as remaining).** Five root causes, all the same shape — one field asked a question it does not answer.
      · **THE APPROVAL LADDER IS `src/sitewire/approval.js`, and it is the ONLY place that decides what "approved" means.** Everything used to read `sitewire_draws.total_approved_cents`, which Sitewire fills only on the FINAL lender approval — so from the moment the inspector finishes until we press Final approve it is 0, and the report, the packet, the desk, both draw lists and the totals row all answered ZERO on a draw whose own line items said $6,250 each. The real ladder is: borrower requests → **inspector pre-approves** → borrower accepts → capital partner reviews → **we finally approve and release, net of our fee**. `drawMoney()` returns `inspector_approved_cents` / `final_approved_cents` / `not_approved_cents` / `fee_cents` / `net_release_cents` / `approval_stage`, and `approved_cents` is the INSPECTOR's figure until final approval lands. **Source precedence, never guessed:** the live request mirror → the delivered-findings snapshot (`draw_finding_lines`) → the draw total; a NULL approved amount is "the inspector has not answered this line yet", never a confident $0, so an un-inspected draw reports `has_inspector_amounts:false` and reads "inspecting". `final_approved_cents` stays 0 until the last rung and is the ONLY number that may ever be described as released. **Never re-read `total_approved_cents` directly on a display surface** — route it through this module, or two screens will disagree about one draw again.
      · **INSPECTION EVIDENCE IS A UNION, NOT A COUNTER — `src/sitewire/inspection-evidence.js`.** The risk engine asked exactly ONE source: `sitewire_draw_requests.inspection_count`, mirrored in `reconcile.reconcileOne` from `r.inspections.length` on `GET /draws/:id`. **That payload does not carry the inspections array — only `GET /requests/:id` does** (the SAME shape gap that left `job_item.name` null, fixed 2026-07-22). So `Array.isArray(undefined)` was false, every request mirrored 0, and the stored 0 re-wrote itself on every poll — while the photos arrived perfectly through `persistDrawFindings` into `draw_finding_lines.photo_count` and were archived into `draw_media`, which is exactly why the report printed them. Evidence is now the union of (1) the mirror, (2) the delivered findings, (3) the archived media; `HEAL_SQL` pushes the truth back onto the mirror **GREATEST-only** after every reconcile and every findings delivery; and reconcile now binds `null` (not 0) when Sitewire is silent, so an absent field can never lower a real count. **THE CLASS: any "is there evidence?" test that trusts a single mirrored counter reports "none" the moment that payload omits the field — read every durable record, and never let an absent value overwrite a known one.** A line whose every money request is verified also drops `front_loading` from `medium` to `low` with wording that says the photos verify it.
      · **OUR DRAW FEE IS PROJECTED, NOT WAITED FOR.** The fee reached the desk only through `draw_disbursements`, written when a human records the wire — so every report and packet before that showed a $0 fee and a net release equal to the gross. It is settled at draw-setup time (partner/method rule + per-file override), so `rollup.projectedFee(db, appId)` resolves it through **the same `orchestrator.resolveRule` + `resolveInspection` the Start-draw screen uses** (never a second copy of that math), marks it `fee_projected` until a release makes it final, and `rollup.fees` tracks our income for the project separately (`charged_cents` vs `projected_cents`). Best-effort — an unreadable file simply shows no projection.
      · **AN INSPECTED LINE FINDS ITS BUDGET LINE BY `sow_line_key`, NEVER BY NAME.** Our Scope of Work keeps ONE "Roof" line; Sitewire keeps one job item per unit ("Unit 1 - Roof"), so the report's name match could never succeed — the budget row read "—" for requested/approved while four unbudgeted "Unit N - Roof" rows dangled underneath. The Excel packet already joined on the crosswalk key, which is why the spreadsheet got that column right and the PDF did not.
      · **A DRAW IN FLIGHT IS SPOKEN FOR** (owner: *"we should treat a draw that is not fully approved, even if it's halfway approved, as if it is approved… we can still decline it, and everything goes back to fully available"*). The rollup gains `committed` (= drawn + approved_pending), `available` and `pct_committed`, and every HUMAN-READ surface shows **available** — the report, the packet, the desk tiles, the borrower's own draw eligibility, and `portal-draws.composerLines` (whose per-line remaining counted only `d.status='approved'` draws, so a borrower could compose a second request against money a draw in flight had already claimed). **`remaining` / `pct_complete` deliberately KEEP their released-only meaning**: the risk engine measures a pending draw against the line as it stood BEFORE that draw, so folding the draw in would make every draw flag itself as busting its own line. Nothing is stored — these are live sums, so an amend or a decline frees the money by itself.
      · **AMEND AND REOPEN MUST SURVIVE THE FINAL APPROVAL** (owner: *"we're missing the amend button, which is available in Sitewire after the final approval"*). `app-v2` hid the whole action row once a draw reached `approved` — the one state amend exists for — and sent no note, which the route has required since audit B-10, so every click 400'd even on an open draw. Both actions now render post-approval and prompt for the reason that is journaled.
      · **THE BORROWER SEES THE DRAW FEE (owner-directed 2026-08-03: "yes add the fee to the borrower report too") — SUPERSEDES the earlier "lender fee/net stripped" rule for THIS fee only.** It comes out of the borrower's own approved amount and decides what actually wires, so their report shows `Approved on this draw − draw processing fee = Amount wired to you` plus a plain explanation, worded from the ONE definition `approval.netExplanation(m, {borrower:true})` so their copy and ours can never describe the same deduction differently. **What stays hidden is unchanged and is a DIFFERENT thing:** the capital-partner / note-buyer name (still scrubbed), photo GPS (still stripped), and **our fee income ACROSS the project** — the "Our draw fees on this project" band, `rollup.fees`, and the lender-only per-draw fields on `GET /api/borrower/draws/:appId/rollup` (`fee_cents`, `fee_kind`, `fee_projected`, `net_release_cents`, `retainage_held_cents`, `net_explanation`, `released`, `release_date`). That strip is a hand-written destructure, so `test-draw-approval-ladder.js` section F asserts every lender-only key is named there — **add a new one and name it in the strip in the same commit.**
      · **THE BORROWER SEES THE RELEASED AMOUNT ON THEIR SCREEN, not only in the PDF (owner-directed 2026-08-03: "yes add the released amount to the borrower screen too").** Their report had shown the fee and the net since the earlier fee ruling while the SCREEN still said only "the inspector approved $25,000" — so the two surfaces disagreed about the one number they care about most. The per-draw money now rides through the borrower rollup (`fee_cents`, `fee_projected`, `net_release_cents`, `retainage_held_cents`, `released`, `release_date`), and `BorrowerDraws` renders it as a **You receive** column on the draws table and a headline figure on the results card, keyed to the draw by `sitewire_draw_id` off the SAME rollup the PDF is built from. **Two things are still stripped and they are a different kind of secret:** `fee_kind` (which inspection tier priced the draw — our fee SCHEDULE, not their money) and the rollup's `net_explanation`, which is the STAFF sentence; the route replaces it with `APPROVAL.netExplanation(d, {borrower:true})` so the screen, the PDF and the email phrase one deduction one way. `rollup.fees` — our income ACROSS the project — stays deleted and always will. **Section F now guards BOTH directions** (a lender-only key must stay stripped AND an entitled figure must NOT be), and `test-draw-dispute-evidence.js` asserts the LIVE payload over real HTTP, because a source-grep cannot prove the route actually answers with the money; each was proven to fail when reverted.
      · **THE RELEASE IS A HEADLINE NUMBER, NOT A ROW (owner-directed 2026-08-03: "enhance our own report and the borrower's report to include and pick a number for a released amount — how much is going to be released after this draw, which includes the fees netted out").** "Approved" and "what actually moves" are two different questions, and the second is the one everybody opens the PDF to answer. `moneyHero` carries BOTH side by side — the approval on the left, **the release on the right**, each a headline figure, with the deduction named under the release so the gap between them is never a mystery ("after the $299 draw fee"). Wording is state-aware: `TO BE RELEASED` → `RELEASED` once a release is recorded, and the "(standard draw fee for this file)" caveat disappears when the fee stops being a projection. The panel now SIZES ITSELF to its wrapped meaning text (a fixed height used to let a long one spill past its own background). A whole-project report has no hero, so `releaseSummary()` answers the same question in its own band (released so far / still to be released / — staff only — the fees netted out of those releases). **The findings EMAIL quotes the same figure from the same rollup the report is built from**, so the email and its own attachment can never disagree; its hero label was also corrected from "Approved for release" (which described the gross) to "Approved by the inspector".
      Tests: `scripts/test-draw-approval-ladder.js` (pure) + `scripts/test-draw-packet-db.js` (real Postgres — the heal, the rollup, the fee resolution and the Excel packet's own numbers, which is why `buildDrawPacket` was lifted out of the route into `src/sitewire/draw-packet.js`), both in `npm test`; every assertion reproduces something that was wrong on the reported draw.
  14. **THE DRAW GOES TO THE INVESTOR ONCE THE BORROWER AGREES — and HOW it is funded decides what the email asks for (owner-directed 2026-08-03; db/454).** The last rung of the ladder above, which PILOT never modelled: after the borrower accepts the inspector's findings the draw goes to the NOTE BUYER, who puts up the money. `src/sitewire/investor-delivery.js` is the PURE half (the money split, the mode rule, the wording, the recipient rule) and `investor-delivery-send.js` the IO half (contacts, attachments, the send, the record).
      · **TWO FUNDING MODES, and `reimbursement` is the DEFAULT.** *reimbursement* — WE wire the net to the borrower and the investor reimburses us the FULL approved amount; our draw fee never moves separately, it simply stays with us out of the money we already netted ("the investor just looks on the entire amount, they reimburse us the entire amount, and we only released a net to the borrower, so we stay with the fee in our bank account"). *investor_direct* — the investor wires the net to the borrower and our fee to us. **In BOTH modes the investor funds the same total (the approved amount); only WHO is paid differs**, which is why `investorMoney` derives `to_us = approved − to_borrower` rather than re-adding the fee — the three figures can never fail to reconcile. The mode resolves per-draw (`draw_findings.funding_mode`) → per-file (`sitewire_property_links.investor_funding_mode`) → the system default, and an unrecognised stored value FALLS THROUGH rather than taking effect (a typo must never redirect a wire). Both columns are on OUR tables, never the mirrored `sitewire_draws`, so a Sitewire reconcile can never overwrite a coordinator's choice.
      · **THE EMAIL ALWAYS STATES THE THREE FIGURES** the owner named — what was approved, what reaches the borrower, what comes to us — in both modes. Only the ASK differs ("reimburse us $X" vs "release $Y to the borrower and $Z to us"). A $0 fee is stated in words rather than printed as a `$0.00` line. It is rendered by the ONE shared `email/template.js` (same brand, same deliverability posture as every other YS email), sent FROM the draw desk with the coordinator's own sign-off, and deliberately carries no exclamation marks or urgency phrasing — pinned by a test, because this lands in an outside partner's inbox and must not read as spam.
      · **THE BORROWER IS NEVER A RECIPIENT — of any kind.** `composeRecipients` applies `dropBorrower` to EVERY list (To and Cc), so a borrower address arriving by any route — typed into the investor contacts by mistake, or a borrower who is also a staff user — is removed rather than trusted. Our people ride as **Cc, not Bcc**, deliberately: the investor's reply must reach the whole team. The coordinator, the loan officer and `draws@yscapgroup.com` are always copied, resolved through the existing `draw-recipients.js` (never a second copy of that lookup).
      · **CONTACTS ARE PER NOTE BUYER, and the KEY is the reason `investorKeyFor` exists.** `normNoteBuyer` is deliberately EXACT across this codebase (loosening it would let a look-alike name export another buyer's data tape), so the owner's real label "Fidelis Investors LLC" normalizes to `fidelisinvestorsllc`, NOT `fidelis` — a contact list keyed on that normalizer alone would never be found on a real file. Rather than loosen the shared normalizer, `investorKeyFor` reuses the shared PREFIX helper the repo already blessed for this buyer (`isFidelisNoteBuyer`, db/337). **A future buyer needing the same treatment gets a helper in `field-registry` — never a fuzzy match invented in the delivery module.** A buyer with nothing saved BLOCKS its own delivery with a plain "add the contacts" message; PILOT never guesses an investor address. Seeded with the owner's three Fidelis contacts, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` so a later edit always wins.
      · **THE BORROWER MAY AGREE OUTSIDE THE PORTAL.** A coordinator records a verbal/emailed approval through `POST …/findings/:id/mark-accepted`, which runs the SAME `status='accepted'` transition their own Accept button does — only `accepted_via='staff'` plus the recorded note and staff id distinguish it, so "who said the borrower agreed?" is answerable years later. A note of ≥8 characters is REQUIRED. Only `accepted` and `resolved` (a dispute worked out) count as agreement; `delivered` and `disputed` block the send.
      · **NOTHING IS EVER SILENTLY DROPPED.** The four attachments (the inspector's own report — Sitewire's per-draw PDF *and/or* the physical inspector's TrustPoint paperwork, resolved on ITS OWN draw number because the two systems number draws independently; our branded report; the Excel draw packet; the borrower's signed wire instructions) are fitted to a per-provider byte budget IN PRIORITY ORDER, and anything that cannot be attached comes back in `skipped` WITH A REASON — shown on the desk before the send, printed in the email, and stored on the delivery record. Every send is recorded in `draw_investor_deliveries` (a re-send is a NEW row — the investor genuinely received two emails), and the blockers are re-checked AT SEND TIME, never trusted from the preview the screen fetched earlier.
      · **A `render()` RETURN VALUE IS AN OBJECT, NOT A STRING** — `template.render` returns `{subject, html, text}`, and the first cut passed the whole object to `sendMail` as the HTML body. Every unit test still passed, because the `none` email provider accepts anything: a malformed body only shows up at the real provider. So `test-investor-delivery-db.js` section I **stubs the mailer and asserts on the WIRE PAYLOAD** (the body is a string, nothing rendered as `[object Object]`, the From/Reply-To are the draw desk, the borrower is absent, every attachment carries base64 content). **When you add any new outbound email, assert on the captured payload — a passing send against the noop provider proves nothing.** Tests `scripts/test-investor-delivery-pure.js` + `scripts/test-investor-delivery-db.js`, both in `npm test`.
  15. **A DRAW EMAIL RANKS ITS MONEY, CARRIES THE DRAW'S OWN FACTS, AND GOES OUT ONCE WITH EVERYBODY VISIBLY ON IT (owner-directed 2026-08-03).** The owner, quoting a live notification — *"Draw #2 for 825 Bishop St was approved: $33,450 of $50,000 requested"* — asked for three separate things, and each names a distinct defect.
      · **THE NUMBERS WERE PROSE.** Four quantities in one sentence at one size: nothing stands out, and the reader must parse a sentence to learn the one thing they opened the mail for. The ask is a HIERARCHY — *"a BIG amount of how much is going to be released, and a smaller amount how much was approved, and a small amount also how much was requested, and a small amount of the difference"* — and it is not decorative: the amount that MOVES answers "what happens to me", and approved / requested / held-back are the arithmetic behind it. `src/lib/email/draw-email.js` `drawFigures` is that block, and **WHICH figure is the headline follows the STAGE, never the status column**: money moving (final approved / released) → the RELEASE leads; the inspector has proposed and we are asking the borrower to confirm → the APPROVED amount leads, because leading with a release would announce money nobody has authorised to move on an email whose whole purpose is to ask permission; nothing approved yet → the REQUEST leads, because a $0 release headline is a lie by layout. A figure that is zero, or that would merely repeat the headline, is OMITTED — a "Not approved: $0" row invites a question with no answer.
      · **THE FACTS WERE THE WRONG FACTS.** Every draw email carried the file's identity block — purchase price, ARV, loan amount, program. True, and irrelevant: nobody reading a draw notice is asking what the property will be worth after the rehab. `drawFacts` replaces it with the draw's own — the processing fee, the inspection / approval / expected-release DATES, and the owner's *"rest remaining from the rehab budget"* — plus a budget meter. It shows **AVAILABLE, not `remaining`**, because available counts a draw in flight as spoken for (the owner's own rule); showing `remaining` would tell a borrower they can still draw money that this very email is about. Any row whose value is unknown is dropped — an email that says "Draw fee —" teaches the reader our numbers are unreliable.
      · **THE WORDING DID NOT MATCH THE STAGE.** "Approved" described the inspector's PROPOSAL *and* our FINAL approval — one of which asks the borrower to confirm and the other of which means money is moving. `stageCopy` keeps them apart for every rung, in both voices; the borrower voice never names the capital-partner step (the frozen borrower-safe rule).
      · **THE MONEY IS NEVER RECOMPUTED HERE.** `draw-email.js` is PURE and chooses only which figures to show and how big; `src/sitewire/draw-email-blocks.js` is the IO half and takes every number from `rollup.loadRollup()` — the same call behind the desk, the borrower's screen, the branded PDF and the Excel packet, whose per-draw entries come from `approval.drawMoney()`, the ONE source of per-draw money (rule 13). So an email can never quote a figure that disagrees with the report attached to it. **A TrustPoint draw is converted through the SAME `drawMoney`** (`moneyFromTrustpoint`), never matched by id against the Sitewire rollup: `trustpoint_draws.tp_draw_id` and `sitewire_draws.sitewire_draw_id` are DIFFERENT ID SPACES, and passing one for the other would either find nothing or — far worse — find another draw carrying that number and put its money in the email. The link column is `trustpoint_draws.sitewire_draw_id`.
      · **ONE EMAIL, EVERYBODY ON IT — `notify.notifyAppThread`.** The owner: *"the system is just sending out like three separate emails of the same thing … our team is getting it separately, the borrower is getting it separately … everybody should be looped in ONE email so we can then keep responding … so we can see who else is looped in. It gives more clarity."* **THE DUPLICATE WAS STRUCTURAL**, not a mistake at any call site: a draw event called `notifyAppBorrowers` (borrower email, team hidden on Bcc) AND `notifyAppStaff` (one email EACH to every assignee), so the coordinator received the same sentence twice in two unrelated threads and a reply to either reached nobody else. **A Bcc was the wrong tool and not as a matter of taste**: it is invisible, so a reply — theirs or ours — reaches nobody, the conversation splits into as many private threads as there were recipients, and it could never be the team's ONLY copy because nobody can tell they were on it. That is precisely why the separate staff blast existed. So the draw loop-in is now a **VISIBLE Cc** (`_borrowerCc`, keyed on the notification's own CATEGORY exactly as the Bcc rule was, so every draw email present and future gets it free), and the staff fan-out still runs — for the in-app rows, the per-staffer mute settings and the "was anybody on this file?" answer — with `inAppOnly` suppressing its now-redundant email. The Bcc is filtered against the Cc, or the loan officer (who is in the loop-in AND is the monitoring Bcc) receives it twice: the duplicate re-introduced one layer down.
      · **THE PIXEL AND THE Cc CANNOT COEXIST, AND THE Cc WINS.** The open-tracking pixel is keyed on the BORROWER's notification, and the existing Bcc case avoids forging an open by SPLITTING the send — which is exactly what a visible Cc must never do, because two messages are two threads and reply-all on either reaches half the people. So a Cc'd send carries **no pixel at all**. That costs an open statistic; keeping it would cost either the thread or the truth of "the borrower opened this" (any Cc'd staffer's mail client or security scanner loading the image forges it). A reading nobody can trust is worse than none.
      · **AN EVENT NEVER GOES DARK, and the obvious check is the wrong one.** `notifyBorrower` returns the in-app row's id whether or not an email followed, so its return value is NOT proof of a send: a borrower with no address on file, or a parked file, would report "reached", silence the team's copy, and the event would go out to NOBODY. `notifyAppThread` therefore checks directly whether the file has a mailable borrower, and re-enables the staff email when it does not. It fails toward SENDING.
      · **THE STAFF FAN-OUT IS NOT SIMPLY DROPPED**: a staffer on the file but outside the draw loop-in (an assistant, a processor) would otherwise lose the event entirely. They keep their in-app row — which is where routine team notifications already live (the 2026-07-20 in-app-only rule).
      · Tests `scripts/test-draw-email-pure.js` (the composer's whole truth table, the borrower-safe voice, and the template's back-compatibility byte-for-byte) and `scripts/test-draw-email-db.js` (real Postgres + a STUBBED MAILER asserting the WIRE PAYLOAD — one send, the borrower in To, the coordinator/officer/desk in a visible Cc, nothing on Bcc, no pixel, the in-app rows still written, and the no-mailable-borrower fallback). Both guards were proven to FAIL when reverted: restoring the Bcc reproduces the owner's report exactly (**2 emails, nobody visibly copied**), and trusting the return value drops the fallback. `test-draw-loop-in-db.js` (the 2026-07-28 always-looped-in requirement) is UNCHANGED in intent and now collects the Cc as well as the Bcc — the requirement did not move, the header carrying it did. **When you add a draw email, use `notifyAppThread` + `drawEmailBlocks` — never a bare `notifyAppBorrowers`+`notifyAppStaff` pair, and never a second money query.**
      · **EVERY REMAINING DRAW EMAIL WAS CONVERTED (owner-directed, "finish the remaining draw emails"), and WHICH helper each one uses is a judgement with a rule behind it.** `notifyAppThread` is for an event the team must also RECORD and that has no staff notice of its own — the release (`routes/sitewire.js` `/disbursements`) and the retainage release, matching the TrustPoint twin in `mirror.js`. Everything else stays `notifyAppBorrowers`, deliberately: the draw loop-in Cc is applied at the notify chokepoint for EVERY 'draws' notification, so the team is already visibly on the one email, and `notifyAppThread` would add a staff in-app row nobody needs. That is right where a DIFFERENT staff message already went (the portal request's coordinator task — an action, not a receipt), where the staffer reading it is the one who just caused it (cancel, the Trinity decision, a dispute decision), and for the findings REMINDER, where an in-app row every few hours would bury the desk feed. **The rule the pair-ban is really about is one EMAIL per event, not one helper.**
      · **A PORTAL DRAW REQUEST HAS ITS OWN MONEY SOURCE, AND IT IS STILL `drawMoney`.** A §5B request does not exist in Sitewire until close-out, so there is no rollup entry to read — `draw-email-blocks.moneyFromPortalRequest` converts its own row, exactly as `moneyFromTrustpoint` does. It is deliberately NOT passed as status `approved`: `drawMoney` reads that as OUR final approval, which would make the composer lead with a "to be released" headline and — with no fee resolved on this path — print "no draw fee on this release", a claim we cannot make because the close-out nets the file's fee later. `pending_capital_partner` is the honest reading: a decision recorded, no release yet, so the APPROVED amount leads and no wire amount is promised. Pinned by section F of the pure suite.
      · **A CANCELLATION GETS NO FIGURE BAND, ON PURPOSE.** Nothing was approved and nothing moved, so a 40px headline would give the amount a weight it does not have; the facts box saying the construction budget is untouched is the actual answer. Same discipline in the findings reminder, which carries a draw's figures ONLY when exactly ONE result is waiting — with two, a single draw's numbers would headline one and misrepresent the other.
      · **THE TEAM'S OWN DRAW EMAILS ARE IN SCOPE TOO** ("the emails that are going out to our team and the emails everywhere"). `catalog.trustpointImport` — the coordinator's enter-this-in-TrustPoint notice — had its total in a meta row indistinguishable from the forty line items stacked under it; the total is now the headline and the lines are a real table, with the count it could not show NAMED rather than silently truncated. Tests: sections F/G of `test-draw-email-pure.js` and section E of `test-draw-email-db.js` (the portal-request row and the recorded-release ledger read against the real schema — the same swallowing-catch column class as `wire_due_at`).
  16. **A DOCUMENT THE BORROWER EMAILS TO SITEWIRE REACHES THE PILOT DRAW ON ITS OWN — and every supporting document is previewable + reviewable on the draw card (owner-directed 2026-08-10: "CCF_000016.pdf — From inbound email, Draw 1" sat in Sitewire's Documents tab and never reached the draw or the investor; "anything like this should be included and attached to the draw as invoices and additional proof … I want to be able to preview it regularly"). db/517.**
      · **`src/sitewire/property-doc-ingest.js` is the pull**, run at the TAIL of every `reconcileOne` on the property payload the reconcile already fetched (no extra Sitewire call, AFTER the draws loop so the mirror rows exist). ONLY a document whose `draw_id` maps to a mirrored draw ON THIS FILE is pulled — that one filter puts the proof on the right draw AND excludes PILOT's own pushed property documents (appraisal/SOW, which carry no draw); a property-level document is not lost (the coordinator's Documents card lists everything Sitewire holds, live). It files through `drawAttachments.attach` — never a second door — so it inherits the byte sniff, HEIC conversion, EXIF strip, duplicate guards, SharePoint mirror and investor delivery.
      · **BORN PENDING, as a borrower upload** (someone outside the company emailed it in; db/424 — nothing un-accepted leaves the building). The accept/reject lives ON the draw card (`POST …/attachments/:attId/review`, ordinary db/013 review fields) and the delivery preview names anything still waiting. The desk gets an in-app cue (`draw_docs_pulled`, category `draws`, in STAFF_INAPP_TYPES).
      · **NEVER TWICE, NEVER A RE-DOWNLOAD STORM.** Each Sitewire document's durable identity is `draw_attachments.source_key` (`sw:<draw>:<created_at>:<filename-off-the-redirect-URL>`, unique per file — db/517); bytes already on the file under any name (sha256) are recorded as done without a second copy; a PERMANENT refusal (wrong type, oversize) is remembered in `sitewire_property_links.raw.doc_ingest_skips` (bounded ledger) while a TRANSIENT failure deliberately retries next pass. The download is host-allowlisted (`orchestrator.safeSitewireDocUrl`) through the media archive's SSRF-guarded `fetchBinary`. Best-effort end to end — can never fail the reconcile.
      · **The preview** (`AttachmentPreview` in `DrawsPanel.jsx`) renders the image/PDF from an authed blob in place — an `<img>`/`<iframe>` cannot carry the Bearer token, so never wire a bare URL there; a type the browser cannot render degrades to Download, never a broken frame.
      · **The amount doctrine's LAST GAP is closed here too**: an approved amount that moves in Sitewire AFTER findings were delivered (and only while the finding is still `delivered`/undecided) raises a re-deliver cue to the team — PILOT never re-emails the borrower on its own; a released draw keeps the existing release-drift park. Fires once per change by construction (the mirror already advanced).
      · Test `scripts/test-draw-doc-ingest-db.js` (in `npm test`; the pull, the dedupe, the refusal ledger, the review door over real HTTP incl. IDOR, the re-deliver cue — three mutations proven to fail it). When Sitewire grows a new place documents appear, feed it through `ingestForProperty`'s candidates — never a second pull path.
  17. **AN iPHONE PHOTO (HEIC) CONVERTS TO JPEG AT THE DOOR — one chokepoint, and a failed conversion NEVER loses the photo (owner-directed 2026-08-10: "the photos are in HEIC format, the iPhone format … we need to add our site to be able to read this format and also a lot of other formats").** No browser renders HEIC, no email client previews it, and jsPDF (the branded reports) embeds only JPEG/PNG — so a HEIC stored verbatim is a photo nobody can open anywhere it matters. **`src/lib/heic.js` is the ONE conversion** (`isHeic` by ISO-BMFF brand — video brands deliberately do NOT match; `maybeConvert` NEVER throws and returns the ORIGINAL bytes on any failure, because losing evidence to gain a preview is backwards; `heic-convert` — pure JS + WASM, lazy-required so a missing module degrades to store-the-original). Wired at the three draw doors: `draw-attachments.attach` (which then re-hashes the STORED bytes — a sha256 describing bytes we did not store breaks every content-dedupe that reads it), `media-archive.archiveDrawMedia` (convert BEFORE hash/store so rotated-URL dedupe stays stable), and `borrower-draws.normalizeDisputeMedia`. **The WASM decode blocks the event loop ~1–2s per photo** — the boot backfill (`media-archive.backfillHeicMediaOnce`, forward-only cursor in `sync_runtime_state`, one full sweep then self-terminates, originals never deleted, re-point pinned to the ref it read) paces itself 1.5s between conversions so a backlog can never freeze a health probe; keep that pacing on any future bulk conversion. HEIC stays OUT of serve-document's INLINE_SAFE (browsers cannot render it — an unconverted survivor downloads instead). When another upload door needs iPhone photos readable, call `heic.maybeConvert` — never a second decoder, and never a conversion that can drop the original.
  18. **EVERY PHOTO REACHES THE REPORT — the report embeds a page-sized copy, and the full-quality original is never touched (owner-directed 2026-08-13: "It should include all the photos"; and, on the delivery email, "even if it's not attached, it shouldn't say 'Hey, this was not attached' — it makes it unprofessional"). db/540.**
      · **ROOT CAUSE — THE BUDGET WAS SPENT ON PIXELS THE PAGE THROWS AWAY.** jsPDF's `addImage` copies JPEG bytes in VERBATIM (its `compression` option is a no-op on JPEG), so a ~3.5 MB phone photo cost 3.5 MB of `EMBED_BYTE_BUDGET` — 60 MB ran out after about FIFTEEN of the ~100 an inspection carries. And those pixels were never rendered: the photo grid cell is **118pt wide (1.64in)** and the largest cell in the document is 160pt, so a 4,032px photo is embedded at ~2,460 DPI into a box that shows ~492px at print-quality 300 DPI. **~98.5% of every photo's data was discarded by the renderer, by area, on every build.** So this is not a quality trade — it is declining to carry what cannot be drawn.
      · **THE SECOND HALF OF THE BUG WAS SILENCE.** `attachPhotoBytes` hit the budget and `break`-ed, so the surplus photos were removed from `l.photos` before the builder ever saw them — while the report's "N additional photo(s) are saved in PILOT" note counts only what the BUILDER skipped. Photos therefore vanished with **no note, no count, nothing**. The loader now COUNTS them and passes `photosOmitted` into `buildDrawReport`, which seeds its own counter with it, so every omitted photo is accounted for on the page (the repo's standing no-silent-caps rule).
      · **`src/lib/image-fit.js` `fitJpeg` IS THE ONE DEFINITION**, and its six rules exist because breaking any of them damages evidence: never throws (every failure returns the ORIGINAL buffer — a draw photo is the proof the money moved against); JPEG only (`jpeg-js` cannot decode PNG and this repo has no PNG decoder — `lib/appraisal/photos.js` has an ENCODER only); never upscales and returns an already-correct photo **byte-for-byte** (re-encoding only adds a second generation of loss, and the identity check is what proves it did not happen); never returns a BIGGER buffer (a crushed source re-encoded at high quality genuinely grows — reproduced, not theorised); the source is bounded from the SOF header BEFORE the decode (an OOM kill is not catchable); and the output's magic bytes are re-read rather than assumed. It REUSES `photos.downscale` (now exported) — never a second box filter, or two surfaces would disagree about what a shrunk photo looks like.
      · **IT IS CACHED, AND THAT IS FORCED BY THE CODEC, NOT A PREFERENCE.** `jpeg-js` is pure JavaScript (the no-native-dependency rule) and decoding one 12-megapixel photo takes **~3 SECONDS, blocking the event loop** — a 100-photo report would be ~5 minutes and an 80-photo archive run would freeze the server for four. So the archive path is UNCHANGED and `media-archive.buildDisplayMediaOnce` builds the copy once, in the background, one photo at a time with a real pause between them (the `backfillHeicMediaOnce` discipline). **Never call `fitJpeg` from a request handler in a loop.**
      · **`draw_media.display_ref` SITS BESIDE `storage_ref`, WHICH IS NEVER REPLACED OR DELETED.** The reports PREFER the display copy and **fall back to the original** — which is what makes it safe to ship mid-drain and safe when a display blob goes missing: a photo with no display copy appears exactly as it does today. Failing to build one costs nothing but size.
      · **THE QUEUE CANNOT WEDGE AND CANNOT STARVE.** `display_checked_at IS NULL` is the work list and stamping it is the drain, so new photos and the back book flow through ONE path. A row whose bytes cannot be READ is deliberately not stamped done — one storage blip must not condemn a draw's photos — but it is retried only `MAX_DISPLAY_READ_TRIES` times (the count rides in `display_skip_reason` as `unreadable:N`) and then drops out, because rows are taken id-ascending and a few permanently-missing blobs would otherwise sit at the head forever and starve every real photo behind them. It stays UNSTAMPED, so a deliberate re-sweep is one UPDATE. `more` means "a FULL batch that made real progress", so a batch of nothing-but-unreadable backs off to the idle interval instead of spinning every minute.
      · **THE VERSION HASH HAD TO LEARN ABOUT IT.** Building a display copy changes neither the photo COUNT nor `archived_at`, so without the display state in `reportVersion` a report cached while the photos were still full-size would be served forever and would never pick up the ones that now fit. `EMBED_BYTE_BUDGET` stays at 60 MB deliberately — it is a memory guard on the build, not an editorial limit, and it now holds ~230 display-sized photos.
      · **THE DELIVERY EMAIL NAMES WHAT IT ENCLOSES, NOT WHAT IT FAILED TO ATTACH.** It used to append *"Not attached: PILOT draw report (too large to attach to one email — send it separately). Let us know and we will send it over."* — an apology for our own plumbing, to a capital partner, ending by asking THEM to chase US. **Nothing is lost internally, which is what makes the quieter email honest rather than a cover-up:** every skipped item and its reason is still returned to the caller, still shown on the delivery card BEFORE anyone presses send, and still written to `draw_investor_deliveries.skipped`.
      · Tests `scripts/test-image-fit-pure.js` (the six rules, a 400-input fuzz with no exception, and a deterministic never-bigger fixture) + `scripts/test-draw-photo-fit-db.js` (real Postgres + real storage: the worker, the untouched original read back byte-for-byte, the retry cap, the report's preference AND its fallback, the omitted count reaching the page, and the version hash) — both in `npm test`, and nine mutations of the production code were each proven to fail them. When you add another surface that embeds a photo into a document, take the display copy and fall back to the original — never re-inline a resize, and never drop a photo to save bytes.
  19. **THE DELIVERY EMAIL REDRAWS THE REPORT AT EMAIL SIZE, AND THAT COPY IS NEVER FILED (owner-directed 2026-08-13: "while you click the Deliver to Investor, it should take all the photos and redraw a new report on a much more compressed version that should definitely fit in all the email versions"). db/541.**
      · **WHY A SECOND SIZE AT ALL.** Item 18 gave every photo a page-sized display copy, so every photo now reaches the report — but that report is ~25 MB on a 100-photo draw. Right for the copy we keep; still unmailable: Gmail refuses over **25 MB RECEIVED**, Outlook over 35, and email encoding inflates a file by about a third on the way out. The compact rendition is ~700px / ~50 KB a photo, so the same 100-photo report is **~5 MB** and clears every mailbox with room to spare. 700px is still ~1.4x what the 118pt photo cell shows at 300 DPI — a smaller FILE, not a worse-looking page.
      · **BOTH RENDITIONS COME OUT OF ONE DECODE — `imageFit.fitJpegSizes(buf, specs)`.** The decode is the entire cost (~3s for 12 MP, blocking the event loop) and each extra resample+encode is ~0.2s, so a second pass over the same photo would DOUBLE the only expensive part of the job. Measured: 4.0s for both against ~7s for two separate calls. Every spec is judged INDEPENDENTLY — a photo can be too small to need the display rendition while still being worth shrinking for email, and one spec's `already_small` must never be read as the other's. `fitJpeg` is now a one-spec call through the same code and is unchanged in behaviour.
      · **THE EMAILED REPORT IS BUILT IN MEMORY AND IS NEVER A `documents` ROW — `drawReport.buildReportBytes`.** Three traps make a stored second report the wrong answer, and none of them fails loudly: (1) the stored report is found by a VERSION-HASHED FILENAME computed from the DRAW'S DATA, which says nothing about photo size — so a compact rendering computes the IDENTICAL filename, `buildOrGetReportDoc` hands back the full-quality one instead, and the email goes out oversized again with nothing explaining why; (2) fold the rendition into the hash to tell them apart and the compact copy then SUPERSEDES the full-quality one (`is_current=false`), dropping the good report off every screen and package that reads it and shelving it into SharePoint's "Old Versions"; (3) a separate `doc_kind` falls OUT of the regeneration rules that stop the Version-N folder churn and INTO the investor TPR package, where a low-resolution copy does not belong. Built, attached, sent, recorded — never filed.
      · **THE FALLBACK CHAIN IS DELIBERATE: compact → display → original.** The background worker is paced, so a freshly archived draw can be ahead of it; sending a LARGER report is a far better failure than sending none. `attachPhotoBytes(sections, {rendition})` defaults to `'display'` — **the copy on the file must never silently become the small one.**
      · **db/541's RESET MUST TERMINATE, and the guard that makes it terminate looks redundant.** db/540 and db/541 ship together, so in production no row can be stamped by a display-only worker — but a database that already ran db/540 alone holds rows stamped done with no email copy, and those photos would silently never gain one, so db/541 un-stamps exactly those. **Every migration re-runs on every boot**, so the real danger is the opposite: the worker always writes EITHER `compact_ref` OR `compact_skip_reason`, and it is the `compact_skip_reason IS NULL` test that stops a photo whose compact rendition legitimately came back "no saving" from being un-stamped and re-decoded (~3s of blocked event loop) on every single deploy, forever. Do not "simplify" it away — it is pinned by a test that runs the REAL migration file twice.
      · **WHAT WENT OUT IS RECORDED PER ITEM** (`draw_investor_deliveries.attachments[].rendition`), because the compact copy is never filed and this row is the only lasting answer to "what did that investor actually receive?". **WHERE OUR OWN TEAM READS WHAT DID *NOT* GO** moved with the email wording: `skipped` is returned on the send response, stored on that row, and now SELECTed into the desk's delivery history. It is deliberately NOT known BEFORE a send — `deliveryPreview` does not gather the attachments, because that would build the report and the Excel packet on every page load. Warning beforehand is a real cost and would be a new, deliberate decision.
      · Tests: the multi-size section of `test-image-fit-pure.js` and sections C2/D2/F of `test-draw-photo-fit-db.js` (the email rendition preferred, both fallbacks, the default unchanged, the migration converging, and a source guard that the delivery never calls the STORING path) — six further mutations of the production code were each proven to fail them. When you need another rendition, add a spec to `fitJpegSizes` — never a second decode, and never a stored second report.
  20. **TRINITY — THE PHYSICAL INSPECTION WE ORDER OURSELVES, AND THE AUTOPILOT IS DELIBERATELY OFF (owner-directed 2026-08-14; db/552; the verified API research + probe log is `docs/TRINITY-INSPECTION-API-RESEARCH.md`).** The owner: *"We need to set up a workflow for physical inspections that are not with Bluelake … whenever the inspection/draw requests get submitted by the borrower, the order should automatically be placed with Trinity as the physical inspector company."* And, on the boundary: *"Don't mess up sitewire integration for any virtuals. Don't touch that. Don't touch the trust point integration that we already have for Bluelake … just build a brand new path."*
      · **THERE ARE NOW THREE DRAW PIPELINES AND A FILE IS ONLY EVER LIVE ON ONE** (`src/sitewire/routing.js` `platformOf`): `sitewire` = VIRTUAL, PILOT approves, **findings auto-deliver to the borrower (the tested autopilot — do not touch)**; `trustpoint` = Blue Lake, their administrator approves, mirrored; **`trinity` = physical, non-Blue-Lake, ordered by us over the Trinity API, and NOTHING is delivered automatically.**
      · **THE AUTOPILOT LINE IS THE SHARPEST RULE IN THIS BUILD.** *"For this physical inspection from Trinity, we should get back the Trinity report. Our system should update with the exact figures that the inspector of Trinity approved, and then we should manually click on 'Deliver to the borrower'."* So `ingest.syncOrder` reads Trinity, fills in our figures, archives the report and photos, and **cues the DESK — never the borrower** (`notifyDeskReady`, an IS NULL-guarded claim so two pollers cannot cue twice). The one action that reaches a borrower is `POST /api/trinity/files/:appId/orders/:id/deliver`, which runs the EXISTING `portal-draws.approveTrinityRequest` — so the borrower's accept/dispute experience is byte-for-byte what it already is, and only the inspector's name changed. **Pinned by section G of `test-trinity-order-db.js`: after a completed report the draw request is still `submitted`, its `approved_cents` is still NULL, and the borrower has ZERO notifications.** Never add an auto-deliver here.
      · **THE PRODUCT IS FORM 19 ("Blank General Purpose Line Item Draw") BECAUSE OF ITS DATA MODEL, not its label** — it is the DOLLAR-based draw, the only shape whose line items carry `itemCost`, `amountRequested` and **`previousPercentCompleted`**. That last field is how the HISTORICAL DRAWS travel (*"if the first two draws were virtual, they need to know how much money was drawn already … and how much money is still available for each and every line item"*): `previousPct = committed/budgeted × 100`, 4 decimals, **clamped to 0–100** (an over-drawn line would otherwise send >100 and Trinity refuses the whole order). Remaining-per-line is carried implicitly (`itemCost × (1 − previousPct/100)`) AND explicitly as an .xlsx, because the percentage drives their system while the spreadsheet is what an inspector reads. The WHOLE budget is sent, not only the requested lines; `isRequested` marks this draw's.
      · **WHAT COMES BACK IS STRUCTURED DATA, NOT A SCRAPED PDF.** `GET /forms/19/orders/{id}/budget` gives per-line `percentCompleted` + **`remarks`** (the inspector's own per-line note — the owner's *"read the notes of the inspector"* and *"why it was not approved"*). `approved(line) = (percentCompleted − previousPercentCompleted)/100 × itemCost`, reconciled to Trinity's own total with the residual absorbed by the largest line so **Σ lines === total to the cent, always** — and `mapper.readResults` **REFUSES** (a plain-language `blocked_reason` for the desk, no number written) when a line moves backwards or the total disagrees beyond rounding. Never soften that into a guess: it decides what a borrower is paid.
      · **FIVE API RULES, EVERY ONE PROVEN BY PROBING THE LIVE SANDBOX, each fatal if missed:** auth is **form-urlencoded** (JSON → 415); a document's `data` must be a **full data URI** (raw base64 → 400); `groupId` validates the **file EXTENSION** (a .csv into group 2 → 400, so the budget goes as .xlsx); **`customerKey` is an exactly-once key** (409 "An order already exist with this CustomerKey" — which is why a lost response can never create a second order: the retry RESOLVES the 409 via OData instead of re-posting); and **cancel is a REQUEST, not an act** (200 with the status unchanged), so we record that we asked and wait for status 14. Trinity's file URLs are **pre-signed and expire (~50 min)**, so the report and every photo are archived into our own storage on sight — never store a Trinity URL and expect it to work later.
      · **THE WEBHOOK IS A NUDGE, NEVER A FACT.** Trinity's deliveries carry NO signature and NO shared secret (their docs: notifications that "only provide the IDs, event type, and a short description"). So `/api/public/trinity-webhook/:token` authenticates on a secret PATH token, believes NOTHING in the body, and every fact is re-read with an authenticated GET. **The poll is the correctness machinery** (open orders only, oldest-first) and the webhook only makes it prompt.
      · **THE SEAM INTO THE SHARED CODE IS THREE LINES, EACH GUARDED SO THE OTHER TWO PIPELINES CANNOT CHANGE:** `reconcile.js` calls `trinity/intake.maybeOrderFromSitewire` beside the existing TrustPoint hook (it returns immediately unless `platform === 'trinity'`), and `portal-draws.createRequest` calls `orderForPortalRequest` inside its existing `if (!isTp)` branch. **The claim is our OWN unique `customer_key`** (`swd-<drawId>` / `pdr-<requestId>`), never a column on a Sitewire table — so ordering twice is structurally impossible and no Sitewire schema is touched. The PILOT report reuses `draw-report.buildDrawReport` + `attachPhotoBytes` as PURE LIBRARY FUNCTIONS (plain data in, PDF out) so a Trinity draw and a Sitewire draw can never render as two different documents.
      · **A PARTIAL UNIQUE INDEX NEEDS ITS `WHERE` IN THE `ON CONFLICT`** — `uq_tio_customer_key`, `uq_tol_order_key` and `uq_toc_trinity_id` are all partial, and Postgres cannot infer a partial index without repeating the predicate (42P10). This shipped wrong three times in one build and was invisible because the write sat behind a `.catch(() => {})`; the crosswalk insert is now deliberately UNCAUGHT, because it is what ties Trinity's answer back to our job items. Tests `scripts/test-trinity-mapper-pure.js` + `scripts/test-trinity-order-db.js` + `scripts/test-trinity-eligibility-pure.js` + `scripts/test-trinity-writeback-db.js` + `scripts/test-trinity-desk-route-db.js`, all in `npm test`. When you add a Trinity surface, route money through `mapper.readResults` and never deliver anything to a borrower without a human pressing a button.
      · **THE THIRD DOOR — the coordinator orders it herself (owner-directed 2026-08-16: *"It should automatically start ordering the reports whenever a physical inspection comes in. We should also have the option to order it on our end in the draw center. When we are ordering manually, it should also send over all the information that we set up."*).** `POST /api/trinity/files/:appId/orders` → `intake.orderManually` (`manage_draws`), with `intake.orderOptions` as the read behind the picker. It exists because the two automatic doors mint the order record as a SIDE EFFECT of a borrower submitting a draw, so when an automatic order stood down for any ordinary reason (switch off, credentials not in, Trinity unreachable, a draw that predates the program) there was nothing to press — the desk's button only ever RE-DROVE a record that already existed. **It changes nothing about WHAT is sent, by construction:** it resolves the SAME record the automatic door would, keyed identically (`swd-<drawId>` / `pdr-<requestId>` — the key IS the claim, so a manual click and a later automatic pass can never make two orders for one draw), and hands it to the SAME `order.placeOrder`. It REQUIRES a draw: form 19 is a line-item draw and `buildOrderPayload` refuses a payload with no line requesting an amount, so a standalone inspection has nothing to inspect against — compose the draw request first, which orders it on its own. It refuses a file that is not Trinity's through the same shared `eligibility` rule (a virtual file is Sitewire's inspector, a Blue Lake file is TrustPoint's; two inspectors on one draw is the thing that must never happen).
