      · **THREE DEFECTS THE A-TO-Z PASS FOUND, each invisible to every test that existed, and each now guarded.** (1) **THE WHOLE DESK WAS UNREACHABLE**: `src/routes/trinity.js` mounted `requireStaff` WITHOUT `requireAuth`. There is no global `authenticate` in `server.js` — every other staff router mounts both — so `req.actor` was never populated and every Trinity route answered `403 "staff only"`, to everyone, for every file, forever. Nothing noticed because every test called the modules directly and none went through Express; `test-trinity-desk-route-db.js` is the first thing here to speak HTTP. **A router that mounts only `requireStaff`/`requireBorrower`/`requireTpo` is asking about an actor nobody resolved — always mount `requireAuth` first.** (2) **THE SITEWIRE AUTO-ORDER DOOR WAS STILL DEAD**: the eligibility RULE was fixed on 2026-08-14 and the CALLER was not — `reconcile.js` passed only `platform`, so `method` arrived undefined, rule 1 read "not physical", and the rule answered no on every draw ever submitted, silently (the call is fire-and-forget and its refusal is a return value nobody reads). **A correct rule fed the wrong context is still a dead door** — pinned by a SOURCE assertion in `test-trinity-eligibility-pure.js` §F, because no unit test of the rule can see its caller. (3) **A DRAW COUNTED AS ITS OWN HISTORY**: `budgetLines` summed every approved request on the file including the draw being inspected, so a draw already approved on our side when the inspection was ordered was reported to the inspector BOTH as money already released AND as the amount being requested — "this line is 50% done, now release the other 50%" on a line where nothing was paid. `placeOrder` now excludes it by id (`excludeDrawId` / `excludePortalRequestId`); every other reader still sees that money as released.

- **THE MIRROR SCOREBOARD SHOWS WHY, NOT JUST HOW MANY — and a copy a human moved is IN SharePoint (owner-reported 2026-08-09: "2,655 Total documents / 1,755 In SharePoint … how can something like this be? We need to fix this. Why are not all documents in SharePoint?").** Nothing was broken: 0 waiting, 0 stuck, and the 900-document gap was EXACTLY the deliberately-not-mirrored pile (1,755 + 900 = 2,655 to the document). What was broken was the SCREEN — the gap was explained by ONE grey line of small print naming two example reasons ("e.g. Heter Iska, appraisal photos") for a third of the entire library, so a correct number read as 900 missing documents. **THE CLASS: a number a person cannot reconcile is a defect even when it is right. When a total splits into buckets, show every bucket — an "e.g." on a third of the population is not an explanation.**
  · **`SKIP_BUCKETS` in `sharepoint-backup.js` is ONE table that both WRITES the skip reasons and GROUPS them**, so a new reason gets its own line on the scoreboard for free and the wording can never drift from the grouping. The reasons this file writes are named constants (`SKIP_REASON_SUPERSEDED` / `_HEAL`, `SKIP_REASON_LEAD`, `duplicateBytesReason`, `SKIP_HUMAN_PLACED_PREFIX`); two of them are written elsewhere (`lib/esign/webhook.js`'s self-test doc) and are matched by PREFIX with the writer named in a comment. Matching is by prefix where the text carries a variable (a document id, a folder name) so `duplicate bytes — … document 4172` and the two different superseded wordings each group correctly.
  · **AN UNRECOGNISED REASON SURFACES AS `other`, IN AMBER — never folded into a friendly label.** A skip nothing recognises is precisely what a person needs to see; rounding it into "kept in PILOT only" would hide the next real bug behind a reassuring sentence.
  · **A COPY A HUMAN MOVED CARRIES A SKIP REASON *AND* A MIRROR REF, AND IT IS IN SHAREPOINT.** `refileRow` stamps `sharepoint_skipped_reason` when a person has moved or renamed our own copy (their arrangement wins, permanently) — on a row that already has `sharepoint_backup_ref`. So the raw `skipped` count is NOT the not-mirrored pile: every scoreboard bucket is scoped to `sharepoint_backup_ref IS NULL` (`SKIP_NOT_MIRRORED_SQL`), or the screen would report a document as missing while it sits in the team site. The legacy `skipped` field is unchanged for its existing consumers; the screen reads the new `skipped_not_mirrored`.
  · **`unaccounted` is the belt to that suspender** — a document with bytes in NONE of the four buckets. Normally 0; a never-mirror kind sits there for the seconds between upload and the settle pass. Without it the totals could silently fail to add up, which is the whole complaint. All of it is FILTERs on the reconciliation's existing single scan — no second query.
  · **[REVERSED 2026-08-12 — appraisal photos are a never-mirror KIND AGAIN and are also EXCLUDED from the TPR export. The owner now wants ONLY the appraisal PDF + XML in SharePoint and in the TPR export; the photos stay in PILOT + the off-site backup. `appraisal_photo` is back in `NEVER_MIRROR_REASON` (sharepoint-backup.js, reason = LEGACY_SKIP_APPRAISAL_PHOTO so the scoreboard's `appraisal_photos` bucket still recognises it), added to the `tpr-export.js` doc_kind `NOT IN` exclusion, and `backfillAppraisalPhotoMirrorOnce` short-circuits. NO-DELETE still holds — photos already mirrored by the 2026-08-09 pass keep their `sharepoint_backup_ref` and stay in SharePoint until a human removes them there. Tests re-pinned: `test-appraisal-photo-mirror-db` (rewritten to the not-mirrored behavior), `test-sharepoint-e2e-db` + `test-sp-strays-slo-db` (photo assertions flipped). Do NOT re-add photos to the mirror without fresh owner direction. The 2026-08-09 write-up below is retained for history.]** APPRAISAL PHOTOS ARE MIRRORED NOW, INTO THEIR OWN FOLDER — appraisal_photo left the never-mirror set (owner-directed 2026-08-09, immediately after the scoreboard above let them SEE the skipped pile). Shown the full list of what the mirror deliberately skips and asked which of it to change, the owner picked the photos, *"in their own subfolder"*. They had been excluded because ~24 images per file flood the category folder sitting next to the actual loan documents; `categoryPathFor` → `[tprCategoryOf(row), 'Appraisal photos']` answers that without costing the pictures — the parent is DERIVED, never spelled, so the photos always sit beside the report even if the category is renamed. **That one line also hands them their own VERSION STREAM for free**: `categoryFor` is the JOINED path and `stateKeyFor` slugs it, so a re-import superseding 24 photos can never bump the appraisal REPORT's Version-N counter — two folders must be two streams. `heter_iska_signed` is now the ONLY never-mirror kind and its policy is unchanged.
    · **PREVIOUS AND FUTURE — `backfillAppraisalPhotoMirrorOnce` (booted in server.js), and it MIRRORS FIRST, STAMPS AFTER.** Removing the kind from the never-mirror set only reaches future imports: every photo already in PILOT was SETTLED (`backed_up_at` stamped + a skip reason) precisely so it would leave the pending population, and a settled row is invisible to the drain forever. **The obvious implementation is the trap** — clearing the settle stamps so the normal drain picks the rows up sets `backed_up_at = NULL` on documents created WEEKS ago, and the backlog SLO measures `min(created_at)` across exactly that population, so the first boot would report an oldest-pending age of weeks, breach the SLO, flip the mirror unhealthy and email every admin over a change the owner asked for. Calling `mirrorRow` directly on a still-settled row uploads it and lets `uploadAndRecord` overwrite the stamps on SUCCESS, so a failure leaves the row exactly as settled as it found it. It deliberately does NOT call `recordFailure` (that path re-arms a row as pending — the same false alarm). **Pending / stuck / oldest-pending never move because of this pass, even during a total Graph outage** — pinned by the test. Bounded + self-draining; `SHAREPOINT_PHOTO_BACKFILL_DISABLED=1`, `SHAREPOINT_PHOTO_BACKFILL_FILES`.
    · **ONLY THE CURRENT SET IS COPIED.** A re-imported appraisal retires its predecessor's photos (`is_current=false`); copying those too would put two contradictory photo sets of one property in the team site, so a superseded set is re-stamped with a reason saying exactly that and stays out. Nothing is deleted — the bytes stay in PILOT. The scoreboard bucket keeps matching BOTH that reason and the LEGACY never-mirror string (still on every pre-change row), so no photo falls into `other`.
    · **GRAPHICS RIDE ALONG DELIBERATELY.** The extracted set includes the form's own artwork (sketches, maps, and the appraiser's signature — the 2026-08-02 hero bug). They are NOT filtered out, because `classifyImage` is documented as conservative in ONE direction only — a misjudged photograph is moved to the end of the gallery, never dropped — so using it to EXCLUDE would risk losing a real photo, which is the expensive direction. Mirroring everything cannot lose a picture.
    · Three suites asserted the old policy and were updated to assert what is now TRUE rather than having the coverage dropped: `test-sharepoint-e2e-db` (its never-mirror settle guard now rides the Heter Iska, plus a new inverse assertion that a photo IS mirrored), `test-sp-strays-slo-db` (a photo that genuinely cannot be copied must now appear as STUCK — silently exempting it again would hide a real failure behind a retired policy), and the `unaccounted` guard in the scoreboard suite. Test `scripts/test-appraisal-photo-mirror-db.js` (in `npm test`).
  · Test `scripts/test-sharepoint-scoreboard-db.js` (in `npm test`, real Postgres — a pure test cannot prove a SQL partition) asserts the buckets partition the table with nothing double-counted and nothing left over, that `in SharePoint + not-copied-on-purpose + waiting = total`, that the breakdown SUMS to the pile, that the human-moved copy counts as mirrored, and that an unknown reason reaches the report as `other`. It imports the predicates from `backup._scoreboardSql` rather than retyping them — a test that re-typed the SQL would prove nothing about the query that actually runs.
- **Dates are calendar strings, ClickUp writes are journaled + guarded, and suspicious changes go to REVIEW — never silently applied (owner-directed after the 2026-07-15 DOB incident; full research in `docs/CLICKUP-DATE-INCIDENT.md`). NEVER regress any of these:**
  1. **A date-only value is a `'YYYY-MM-DD'` string end-to-end.** Never a JS `Date`/epoch mid-pipeline: the pg type parser (`src/db.js` OID 1082) returns strings; the frontend renders through `app-v2/src/lib/dates.js` (`fmtDay`/`dayInputValue`), NEVER `new Date('YYYY-MM-DD')`/`toLocaleDateString` on a date-only value.
  2. **ClickUp date writes go ONLY through `transforms.dateOnlyToClickUpEpoch()`** — 4 AM `America/New_York` (ClickUp's own storage convention for this team; an epoch at UTC midnight gets re-dayed by ClickUp to the PREVIOUS NY day — that was the DOB corruption). It refuses out-of-range years and throws rather than emit an epoch its own pull would read as a different day. Never call `toEpochMs` on a write path.
  3. **Every outbound ClickUp field write is journaled** (`clickup_write_log`, db/107, before + after, SSN/card masked) and **no-op suppressed** (the task is read first; equivalent fields are skipped). Any new write path must journal the same way.
     - **The no-op equivalence check MUST canonicalize per field type — a value that is EQUAL after trivial normalization is never an "overwrite" (owner-reported 2026-07-20, Shloimy Friedman; same class as the address/phone fixes before it).** `mapper.fieldValueEquivalent` is the chokepoint the no-op suppression (`orchestrator.js`) runs BEFORE the PII overwrite shield, so a field it calls equal never reaches the shield and never queues a `pii_overwrite_blocked` review. It already normalized date/number/dropdown/location(coords+formatted)/phone(last-10-digits); **email and SSN were missing and caused false reviews** — a case-only email (`Shloimy6125@…` vs `shloimy6125@…`) and a PILOT SSN pushed as 9 bare digits vs ClickUp's dashed `123-45-4776` (both mask identically as `✱✱✱-✱✱-4776`). Now email fields (`EMAIL_FIELD_IDS`: borrower/second-borrower/loan-officer/processor/underwriter) compare **case+whitespace-insensitive**, and `borrowerSSN` compares **digits-only** (9-digit). Every OTHER identity-comparison surface already canonicalizes (inbound heal, `sync-autoresolve`); this closed the last gap. `db/195` auto-closes the already-queued FALSE email/SSN `pii_overwrite_blocked` rows that are provably equal (email case-insensitive; SSN identical mask = same last-4 formatting), leaving genuine conflicts open. When adding ANY new identity/PII field to the sync, give it a canonical comparison here too. Tests: `scripts/test-sync-review-pii-normalize.js` + the email/SSN cases in `scripts/test-clickup-write-guards.js`.
     - **PILOT presents/pushes the SSN in the REAL dashed format `XXX-XX-XXXX` (owner-directed 2026-07-20).** SSNs are STORED as 9 bare digits (encrypted — the encryption layer consumes digits; never store dashes, it would break `ssn_last4`/`maskSSN`/dedup), but every place we PRESENT one now dashes it via `fields.formatSsn`: the ClickUp push (`mapper.buildTaskFields`, `borrowerSSN`) and the staff reveal (`GET /borrowers/:id/ssn`). Because the no-op equivalence compares SSNs digits-only (above), pushing the dashed value NEVER conflicts with a dash-less ClickUp value — it just writes a well-formatted SSN (and only when the field is blank/changed; an equal dash-less value stays suppressed, so we never churn ClickUp's existing formatting). Inbound reads sanitize back to digits (`ssnForStorage`→`sanitizeSsnDigits`), and `pii-guard` scrubs BOTH dashed (`SSN_SEP`) and bare (`SSN_BARE`) forms, so the new format is still redacted in logs/borrower-facing text.
  4. **Suspicious changes stop at the `sync_review_queue`** (db/108; staff UI `/internal/sync-reviews`; endpoints in `src/routes/staff.js`): an automated ±1-day DOB move (the corruption signature), an inbound out-of-range year (mid-typing / 2-digit "26" — auto-pivoted proposal via `pivotSuspectYear`), an inbound DOB that disagrees with the portal. Approve applies through the normal audited path; nothing suspicious is EVER silently applied or silently dropped. The sync stays fully bidirectional for everything else.
  5. **Wipes are structurally impossible in both directions** — the push skips empty values (a portal blank can never clear ClickUp), the pull writes through COALESCE (a ClickUp blank can never clear the portal), and there is no delete anywhere in the sync. Never weaken these.
  6. **Every mid-typing artifact is blocked at three layers**: draft-commit date inputs (no save-per-keystroke), server-side strict `YYYY-MM-DD` + 1900–2100 validation on every date write path, and the push chokepoint (2). When adding any new date field or write path, wire ALL three.
  7. Data repair tool: `scripts/clickup-date-restore.js` (dry-run default, `--apply` verifies each write by re-read + journals). Inbound/outbound field changes are audited (`clickup_pull_field_change` / write journal) — the cross-system API history must never go dark again.
  8. **A typed 2-digit year RESOLVES to the real year, system-wide (owner-directed 2026-07-15).** Every date entry point routes through `lib/fields.normalizeTypedDate(v, kind)` (which wraps `sanitizeDateOnly`): a user typing "26" into a date input (browser sends `0026-…`) lands as 2026 for closings/application/acquisition/track-record dates and as the adult-making century for DOBs — never as literal year 0026, and never a different date than ClickUp shows. Unresolvable years (0203, 9999) are rejected, not guessed. Any NEW date field must use this helper, not a bare regex.
- **ClickUp write-safety hard stops + duplicate-task lifecycle (owner-directed 2026-07-15; policy in `docs/CLICKUP-DATA-SAFETY.md`, layered ON TOP of the date rules above — never regress either):**
  1. **The HTTP chokepoint refuses destruction structurally** (`src/clickup/client.js`): `setField` throws on any empty / null-bearing / NaN-bearing value (`guardNoFieldClearing` — JSON silently turns those into field-clearing nulls); `updateTask` is a STATUS-ONLY allowlist (`guardTaskUpdatePayload` — the sync never renames a task or touches descriptions); task DELETEs stay hard-blocked. No caller can bypass these, present or future.
  2. **A scoped push can never CREATE a task** (`orchestrator.pushApplication`) — an unlinked file's edit can't spawn a near-duplicate; creation is reserved for `createForNewFile` and the admin repush. Scoped (queue) pushes also FAIL CLOSED when the pre-write read fails (the queue retries); only the human-watched full repush proceeds warn-only.
  3. **PII overwrite shield**: a FULL repush may FILL a blank ClickUp identity field (name / SSN / email / cell / home address) but never REWRITE a differing one — blocked writes are journaled and queued to `sync_review_queue` (`pii_overwrite_blocked`; approval re-pushes scoped via `resolveOnly` keys `ssn`/`current_address`/etc.). Deliberate edits still flow: they arrive as scoped pushes of exactly that field.
  3b. **A DOB CHANGE is ALWAYS a human decision — no automated path may apply one it can't PROVE (owner-directed 2026-07-15, after the Shaindel Schwimmer restore-script rewrite).** Every DOB-conflict site (inbound heal, outbound push gate, restore script) consults ONE decision function — `src/lib/sync-autoresolve.js` `decideDob()` — which auto-settles only the PROVABLE verdicts (same day in different storage forms; an implausible value losing to a plausible adult DOB; a typed 2-digit-year ClickUp artifact beating a `clickup_backfill`-provenance portal value), applying the canonical day to BOTH systems journaled (`adoptDobEverywhere`). Everything else queues to `sync_review_queue` and only applies via review resolution. A blank DOB may be filled. Every DOB entry/fill point uses `lib/fields.sanitizeDob` — real calendar date AND adult plausibility (age 18–120). DOB reviews dedupe per BORROWER, not per task. Never re-widen any of this. TWO deliberate-human channels write through the gate (2026-07-15 night, Shalom Elbaum): (a) HUMAN-EDIT-WINS inbound — a ClickUp DOB that CHANGED since the task's last snapshot (evidence of an edit at the source) and is a plausible adult DOB is adopted to both systems (`source='clickup_human_edit'`), closing stale reviews, instead of parking a human's explicit fix in review; (b) a staff-typed DOB from the portal (file screen or borrower profile) rides `enqueueClickupPush(..., {humanEditKeys:['date_of_birth']})` or `adoptDobEverywhere(source='staff_edit')` — the outbound gate honors `opts.humanEditKeys` for exactly that field. Both channels stay journaled + breaker-limited; never extend humanEditKeys to automated callers.
  3c. **The sync review is TWO-SIDED and the loan officer owns it (owner-directed 2026-07-15 evening).** Every review row records `clickup_value` AND `portal_value` (db/110); resolution picks a WINNER (`POST /sync-reviews/:id/resolve {winner:'clickup'|'portal'}`) whose CURRENT value is re-read live and applied to BOTH systems (dates / DOB / SSN / status; SSNs are never stored in the queue — masked display only). Every new row emails + in-app notifies the file's loan officer (`sync-review.js notifyLoanOfficer`, `notified_at` dedupe) with a deep link to `/internal/sync-reviews`, and LOs can resolve rows on their own files. New reviewable field kinds must add: a queue producer with both side values, an `applyReviewWinner` applier, FIELD_LABELS/REASON_COPY entries in `SyncReviews.jsx`, and tests.
  4. **Outbound volume circuit breaker**: more than `CLICKUP_MAX_FIELD_WRITES_10MIN` (default 300) field writes in a rolling 10 minutes opens the circuit — every further push throws (queue retries later) and the opening is audited (`outbound_circuit_open`). A runaway loop or mass-clobber bug stops hard instead of continuing.
  5. **Duplicated tasks land as their OWN file, then the task is re-stamped.** "Duplicate a task to start a new deal" is the documented workflow: the copied Portal-File-ID stamp is ignored (stale-copy detection), and creation DEFERS (`match_status='duplicate_pending'`, visible in the Control Center manual-review queue) while the duplicate still shows the source deal's address — so a same-address TWIN file (carrying the old deal's data) can never materialize. The officer's address edit re-triggers ingest, the file is created from the CLEANED task, and the `portal_stamp` scoped push re-points the task's stamp/link at its own new file (also healed portfolio-wide by the boot reconcile pass). A GENUINE same-address second deal is unblocked deliberately via the admin force-create endpoint (`POST /api/admin/clickup/manual-review/task/:taskId/force-create`).
  5b. **A COPIED YS loan number is a stale duplicate-workflow artifact, and a stuck task is never silent (root-caused 2026-07-15, Asher Salamon / 734 Dennis Pl).** Every unique key a ClickUp duplicate inherits (the Portal-File-ID stamp AND the YS loan number) must be treated as a possible stale copy, never as an identity claim: a loan number bound to ANOTHER live task of the SAME borrower is ignored for matching and NEVER imported onto the new file (`copiedLoanNumber` in `ingest.findExistingApp`/`linkOrCreateApplication`; a `copied_loan_number_needs_assignment` review row tells the LO to set the real number in ClickUp, which fills via COALESCE and auto-closes the row). A number shared by a DIFFERENT borrower stays `ambiguous`. And no materialization gate may fail silently: every `ambiguous`/`duplicate_pending` ingest queues a visible `file_link` review row (auto-closed on materialization), and the boot one-shot `retryStuckTasksOnce` re-drives the stuck backlog (oldest-first, ≤200/boot, 400ms-paced, only when inbound creation is ON) through the current resolver on every deploy so a root-cause fix heals the backlog, not just tasks that get a new webhook. When adding any new globally-unique matcher key, handle its copied-by-duplication form the same way.
  5d. **EVERY stuck sync state is a review row WITH OPTIONS (owner-directed 2026-07-15 night).** File-level stuck states — ambiguous task, deferred duplicate, task-deleted orphan, dead-lettered push, unlinked file past the recovery window — each queue a `sync_review_queue` row offering concrete resolution ACTIONS (`src/lib/sync-file-review.js` `REASON_ACTIONS` + `POST /sync-reviews/:id/resolve-file`): create-the-file (force), link-to-candidate, retry-push, archive/keep-orphan, create-task. Actions run the sync's own guarded machinery (never bypass a write guard), are audited, and auto-close on natural recovery; a reviewer's dismiss STICKS (`suppressIfRejected` — re-producing passes must not respawn it); rows for a file with no task dedupe via the synthetic `task_id='app:<uuid>'` key. When adding a NEW stuck state: producer + `REASON_ACTIONS` entry + action applier + UI copy/buttons in `SyncReviews.jsx` + `scripts/test-sync-file-review.js` coverage (it drift-checks UI copy against the server contract).
  5c. **A CLEARED ClickUp DOB vacates the conflict — it never clears a real portal DOB, but it is never ignored either (Leifer, 2026-07-15).** The inbound heal's blank-DOB branch: portal DOB plausible → auto-close the borrower's open DOB reviews ("PILOT keeps X"); portal DOB IMPOSSIBLE (fails `sanitizeDob`) → wipe-don't-guess (NULL the portal copy, audited `dob_wipe_dont_guess`) so provable garbage is not preserved just because ClickUp went blank.
- **Merging: on ANY conflict, BOTH enhancements must survive (owner-directed, 2026-07-15).** Never resolve a merge/rebase conflict by picking a side wholesale, and never merge with unexamined conflicts. Read both versions until you understand what each change is FOR, then compose them so every fix/feature from both sides remains intact (the 2026-07-15 write-safety work was hand-layered onto the parallel date-incident fixes this way — that is the standard). If two changes genuinely conflict in intent, stop and ask the owner rather than silently dropping one. After any conflicted merge, re-run the affected test suites and re-audit the composed result.
- **Encompass is READ-ONLY, FROZEN (owner-directed, 2026-07-22; HARD RULE; POST allowlist extended 2026-07-22 for pipeline SEARCH — read-shaped only).** **ELEVATED 2026-08-14 to the HARDEST rule, on top of all rules, for BOTH products and every program (owner: "make this the strongest enforcement … it should only be one-way … nobody should ever guess … block anyone from writing anything to Encompass unless you have written authorization").** The single source of authorized writes is now the **pad `docs/ENCOMPASS-WRITE-AUTHORIZATIONS.md`**, enforced across the WHOLE repo (RTL + LT) by the CI gate **`scripts/check-encompass-readonly.js`** (in `npm test`): any Encompass write not listed in the pad fails the build, the read-only clients are proven still read-only, and no raw Encompass fetch can bypass a guard. Today the pad lists exactly ONE write — flood ordering (`src/encompass/flood-order.js`, RTL only); Long-Term has NO write path. **No agent may ever GUESS a write** — only the specific endpoint/field the owner authorized in writing, super-admin-gated + audited. The gate is the CODE half; the owner also directed the CREDENTIAL half — set the Encompass API user's ICE persona to READ-ONLY so a write is refused by Encompass itself (see the pad). Do NOT weaken/bypass the gate or edit it to add a write — add a pad entry with the owner's written authorization. PILOT ↔ Encompass (ICE Mortgage Technology / Ellie Mae LOS) is a **one-way, read-only** connection. PILOT NEVER MUTATES anything in Encompass — NEVER PATCHes a loan, NEVER creates a loan, NEVER advances a milestone, NEVER updates a field, NEVER uploads to eFolder, NEVER deletes anything, NEVER "replaces" anything in Encompass. The Encompass mapping (`docs/ENCOMPASS-DATA-MAPPING.md`) is pull-only — every row is `←ENC`. Enforced STRUCTURALLY in `src/lib/integrations/encompass.js` (a module-level `_fetchGuarded` refuses any HTTP method other than GET against `/encompass/*`, with a **hard-coded POST allowlist of exactly two READ-SHAPED endpoints** that return data without mutating anything: `POST /oauth2/v1/token` (OAuth auth) and `POST /encompass/v3/loanPipeline` (pipeline search — the only way to find loans by loan number, since Encompass exposes this as POST because the search filter goes in a JSON body). The module exports only `{name, configured, ping, apiGet, pipelineSearch, READ_ONLY}` — no `apiPost`/`apiPut`/`apiPatch`/`apiDelete`/`updateLoan`/`createLoan`/`patchLoan` ever. Verified on every commit by `scripts/test-encompass-readonly.js` (in `npm test`), which asserts the export surface, that the POST allowlist has EXACTLY two entries, and that every other non-GET is refused. **A THIRD read-shaped POST was added 2026-07-26 with the owner's explicit sign-off** ("Go ahead with all the fixes", answering a direct request to allow reading fields BY FIELD NUMBER): `POST /encompass/v3/loans/{guid}/fieldReader` — hand it a list of field ids, it returns their values. It RETURNS data and mutates nothing. It is matched by the NARROW `_isFieldReaderPath` predicate (must start `/encompass/v3/loans/` and end `/fieldReader`, one GUID segment, no query) rather than the exact-path Set, because the loan GUID varies. It exists for CORRECTNESS, not convenience: the SAME field number lives at a DIFFERENT JSON path from loan to loan — 1859 sat at `closingDocument.finalVestingDescription` on one loan and was ABSENT on 117 Brook (the LLC name was in `borrowerUnparsedName1`), and 388's real value (1.000) appears at NO stable path, while the path we had been reading held a different fee entirely (2) — exactly the owner-reported 1%-vs-2%. `reader.js` fetches all registry field ids on every per-file pull and stashes them as `loan._fieldValues`; `flattenLoan` seeds the extract from those FIRST so an authoritative number always beats a guessed path, and a fieldReader failure degrades to the old path-based read. **The fieldReader RESPONSE SHAPE is not stable and MUST be normalized (fixed live 2026-07-27, loan YSCAP258134629 / 117 Brook — the LLC still read blank and origination still read 2%).** VERIFIED LIVE against the tenant: `POST /encompass/v3/loans/{guid}/fieldReader` returns an OBJECT map `{ "388":"1.000", "1859":"MW TRADING LLC" }`, while `/encompass/v1/...` (and ICE's own SDK contract `List<LoanFieldDataContract>`) returns an ARRAY of `{ fieldId, value }` pairs. The reader had accepted ONLY the object and DISCARDED an array as `{}`, so `_fieldValues` could silently blank out. `client.readFields` now routes every response through the pure `encompass-field-map.fieldReaderToMap()` (handles object map AND array), and on a batch failure it RECURSIVELY SPLITS the id list and merges what succeeds — because v3 FAILS THE WHOLE call with HTTP 400 if ANY one field id is invalid/unpermitted (ICE 24.2), so one bad id must not blank all 40. The FALLBACK loanPaths were also corrected from the live loan JSON: 1859 adds `closingDocument.borrowerUnparsedName1` / `uldd.fannieTrustName` (where the vesting name actually lives on 117 Brook — `finalVestingDescription` was absent), and the WRONG `closingCost.gfe2010.loanOriginationPercentage` (a DIFFERENT fee that reads 2 where field 388 is 1) was REMOVED from 388 so a fieldReader-down degrade reads an honest "no data", never a confident wrong 2%. Finally, because the panel reads the STORED snapshot, `reconcile.computeFindings` SELF-HEALS a copy that predates this wiring: when `encompass_extra` lacks `_fieldValues` it does ONE best-effort read-by-number (guarded by a field-364 same-loan check), merges + persists (via a jsonb merge of just the `_fieldValues` key, so a concurrent fresher pull is never clobbered) — so every PREVIOUS file becomes correct on its next view without a manual re-pull, not just new pulls. The heal is GATED to the panel read paths (`computeFindings(..., {heal:true})` from the three `GET .../encompass/{findings,status,raw}` routes) — NEVER the tape/issuance gates, which loop over up to 1000 files and must not each fire a live Encompass call; and `client.readFields` splits its id list ONLY on an invalid-field HTTP 400 (never on a network/timeout/auth error), so a bulk export during an Encompass outage can never fan out into a request storm. All read-only: no new POST endpoint, no widened predicate, no export-surface change. **Do NOT add a FOURTH POST endpoint, and do NOT widen `_isFieldReaderPath`, without owner sign-off in the owner's own words** — these three are the spec, not a template. Do NOT add a write helper to that module; do NOT relax `assertReadOnlyPath`; do NOT delete the `READ_ONLY = true` sentinel; do NOT reintroduce a `→ENC` or `⇄` row into the mapping doc; do NOT enqueue a `sync_queue` row with `target='encompass'` (the enum value stays reserved but forever unused for pushes). The `sync_review_queue`, `pii_overwrite_blocked` shield, DOB `decideDob` gate, and the volume circuit breaker have NO reason to apply on the Encompass side because there IS no Encompass write path. To reopen this decision (adding a real WRITE) the owner must say so in their own words — a general "make it better" is NOT authorization.
  - **The COMPARISON is by MEANING — two matching corrections (owner-directed 2026-07-27); the term-sheet SEND gate is UNCHANGED.** (1) **Rehab type — Cosmetic ≡ Light.** The value map collapses `cosmetic` / `cosmetic rehab` onto the SAME `'light'` token as `light rehab` / `moderate` (owner: "cosmetic and light rehab is the same technically, they can call it however they want"), so a Cosmetic file against Encompass "Light Rehab" MATCHES instead of the old "Doesn't match" (the 2026-07-26 "Cosmetic is its own bucket" mapping is superseded). (2) **Capital provider (note buyer) — corporate-form + name tolerant.** `mapValue` strips a trailing corporate form (LLC / L.L.C. / Inc / Corp / Co / Ltd) + stray punctuation before the value-map lookup (a SECONDARY lookup that only ever ADDS a match — the primary exact lookup still wins, so no other enum can be affected), and `capital_provider` carries `nameFallback` so a buyer NOT in the map still compares by normalized entity NAME when BOTH sides carry one. So "Fidelis Investors LLC" ≡ "Fidelis Investors" MATCHES (it wrongly read "No data to compare" while plainly showing data), an unmapped buyer with the same name matches, a genuinely different buyer mismatches, and **"no data to compare" is reserved for a genuinely EMPTY side** — never a value that is present. Helpers `stripCorpForm` / `normPartnerName` in `encompass-field-map.js` (in `_internals`); the other enums keep their strict "unmapped → not comparable" behavior (only `nameFallback` entries get the name compare). Tests: the rehab/capital cases in `test-encompass-field-map-pure.js`. **The term-sheet DocuSign SEND gate is DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED** — it stays `issuanceGate` in `staff.js` `esign/send` (an Encompass mismatch blocks the regular send; an ADMIN may override with a recorded reason; the separate `esign-before-ctc` super-admin exception flow — the "escalate for exception" button — covers the appraisal / P&P / closing prerequisites and shows exactly what's missing). Do NOT replace that with a no-override hard block — a 2026-07-27 attempt to do so was REVERTED at the owner's explicit direction ("I didn't tell you to remove the escalate-for-exception feature"); the escalation path must always remain.
  - **THE MASTER SWITCH — `ENCOMPASS_ENABLED` turns the WHOLE Encompass connection off, in one place, for BOTH products (owner-directed 2026-08-23: *"I want you to set in the credential something and give me what I can set, like Encompass enabled, and I can click zero or one, whatever, to make sure that the system disables all the Encompass credentials"*, and *"create a switch that I can, any time, switch to turn the entire integration on. For now, leave it on"*). Full operator page: `docs/ENCOMPASS-MASTER-SWITCH.md`.**
    · **THE VALUE, and blank-is-ON is the SAFETY PROPERTY.** unset / blank → ON; `0` / `false` / `no` / `off` (any casing, stray spaces tolerated) → OFF; anything else → ON. It is **ON today and ON by default**, because Encompass is live in production and shipping it default-off would disconnect the tenant on the next deploy — asserted on its own rather than left implied by the value table. Read at CALL time, never captured at boot.
    · **ALL THREE CLIENTS, AND NOTHING REACHES THE WIRE.** `configured()` returns false on the RTL read-only client, the flood WRITE client and the Long-Term client, however complete the credentials are — and each one's own `_fetchGuarded` refuses BEFORE a request is built, so not even the login is attempted. Downstream that means the Long-Term worker's passes cost nothing, the "Pull everything from Encompass" button answers with the reason instead of pretending to start, and the API-Health card says it is switched off. **Nothing is deleted, rotated or rewritten** — the credentials stay exactly where they are and the connection is alive again the moment the switch is removed. It sits ABOVE the flood switches (`ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_*`), which are untouched.
    · **THE REASON MUST TELL THE TWO STATES APART.** "Add the credentials" is useless advice on a tenant whose credentials are sitting right there and were switched off on purpose, so every screen that explains a stopped connection reads the SAME switched-off sentence from ONE place (`OFF_REASON`) and keeps its own separate "the credentials are not set" wording — the admin Encompass routes, the per-file reader stamp, the appraisal-XML catcher, the four Long-Term sync passes, the LT status route and the pull button. **Each call site asks the PURE switch module directly, never a method on the Encompass client** — the test suites replace those client modules wholesale in `require.cache` and a stub carries only the methods its own test needs, so a call site depending on a new client method throws on every stubbed test (caught in review, before merge, on exactly this change). The switch modules are pure and are never stubbed.
    · **THE RULE IS WRITTEN TWICE AND PINNED BY A TEST.** Long-Term may not import RTL code, so `src/lib/integrations/encompass-enabled.js` (RTL) and `src/longterm/encompass/enabled.js` (LT) each carry it — both PURE, no requires — and `scripts/test-lt-encompass-kill-switch-pure.js` runs BOTH over the same battery and fails the build the moment they disagree (a value read as off on one side and on on the other would leave half the system logging in while somebody believed Encompass was off). **Change one, change the other.** The RTL half is `scripts/test-encompass-kill-switch-pure.js`; both are in `npm test`, need no database, and TWELVE mutations of the production code were each proven to fail them with a green control either side. HONEST NOTE, measured: the READ-ONLY client's own fetch guard is a BACKSTOP that never bites on a reachable route today (every exported path calls `ensure()` first, which already refuses) — it is kept for the route added later that forgets to ask; the Long-Term and flood guards are not redundant and are proven to bite.
  - **FLOOD ORDERING IS THE ONE OWNER-AUTHORIZED ENCOMPASS WRITE — flood only, nothing else (owner-directed 2026-07-30, in the owner's own words: "only be able to order flood right now and not anything extra").** Staff click "Order flood certificate" on the flood condition (`rtl_cond_flood`) → PILOT orders a **Life-of-Loan flood determination from ICE Mortgage Technology's OWN flood service** through the Encompass API → the certificate PDF is filed onto that condition and the flood-zone determination is recorded → a proven flood zone auto-attaches the flood-insurance condition. This is the FIRST and ONLY write into Encompass; everything else in the integration stays read-only/frozen.
    · **ISOLATED, NOT a widening of the read-only module.** The write lives in a SEPARATE guarded client `src/encompass/flood-order.js` (its own `_fetchGuarded` whose allowlist passes ONLY the OAuth token + the flood place/status/resource calls; every other method+path throws before the wire — proven by `scripts/test-encompass-flood-order-pure.js`) + the orchestration `src/encompass/flood-desk.js` (DB + conditions + PDF filing). `src/lib/integrations/encompass.js` is UNTOUCHED and still refuses every write; `scripts/test-encompass-readonly.js` stays green. Do NOT add flood (or any) write to `encompass.js`; do NOT widen its 3-endpoint POST allowlist. To add ANY OTHER Encompass write, the owner must authorize it in their own words — flood is the spec, not a template.
    · **OFF by default, staged, dry-run-first.** `ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_ENABLED` (master: reads + the poll worker) + a SEPARATE `ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_OUTBOUND_ENABLED` (actually place an order) + `ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_DRYRUN` (build + log the body, send nothing) — all default OFF, flipped live on the API-Health page via `switches.js`. Credentials default to the tenant's existing Encompass creds; a dedicated flood-authorized API user can be dropped into the `ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_*` overrides. A volume circuit breaker + a one-outstanding-order-per-file unique index guard runaway/double orders.
    · **THE EXACT ICE FLOOD CONTRACT STILL NEEDS LIVE CONFIRMATION.** ICE's own flood (Enhanced Flood Report / Life-of-Loan) ships on Encompass Partner Connect (loan-scoped Service Orders — the default framework), but the exact endpoint, the tenant's flood service/product id, and where the SFHA flag / zone / PDF sit in the response are confined to ONE descriptor (`FLOOD_SERVICE` in flood-order.js), all env-overridable. Verify against the LIVE tenant before enabling outbound: turn on + dry-run, confirm the body, place ONE real test order, then enable outbound.
    · **THE v3 serviceOrders BODY REQUIRES `serviceSetupId` (confirmed live 2026-07-30: the first real order returned "SOO-1125: serviceSetupId is required").** The place-order body is the documented EPC shape `{ serviceSetupId, reason, request: { type, options }, scope? }` (NOT the old flat `{ type, productName, options }`). `serviceSetupId` is the TENANT'S configured flood service id (identifies which vendor/service to order — the Encompass admin has it); with the right serviceSetupId the Life-of-Loan product is encoded by the setup, so `request.options` defaults to `{}`. It comes from **`ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_SERVICE_SETUP_ID`** (Render env, never committed — a tenant config value). Until it is set, ordering refuses BEFORE the wire (`serviceReady()` gate → `FLOOD_NO_SERVICE_SETUP` / the desk's `not_configured` message "needs your Encompass flood service id"), so a guaranteed 400 never reaches ICE. Every remaining body field is env-overridable so the exact contract can be dialed in without a redeploy: `ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_REASON` (default "Manually Requested"), `ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_REQUEST_TYPE` (default "Flood"), `ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_SCOPE` (optional), `ENCOMPASS_FLOOD_OPTIONS_JSON` (optional raw JSON for request.options) — a dry-run prints the exact body to verify first.
    · **THE FLOOD-ZONE SIGNAL + the flood-insurance condition.** `in_flood_zone` (`conditions/engine.js loadRuleContext`) now reads the newest COMPLETED `encompass_flood_orders` row (SFHA flag / A*V* zone) in ADDITION to the current appraisal — so a proven flood zone from EITHER source drives it. `db/378` adds the staff-only, rule-driven `rtl_cond_flood_insurance` condition (auto_apply='rules' on `in_flood_zone is_true`), so the engine attaches it automatically when in a flood zone (and retracts it untouched when not). `db/377` = the `encompass_flood_orders` table. The button self-hides until the feature is on; a file with no `ys_loan_number` shows "add a loan number first" (the loan number is the Encompass link key). Route `POST /api/staff/applications/:id/order-flood` (any staff, file-scoped); poll worker `startFloodPoller` in `encompass-sync.js`. Tests: `scripts/test-encompass-flood-order-pure.js` + `scripts/test-encompass-flood-db.js` (both in `npm test`). The PDF is filed as `doc_kind='flood_determination'`, staff-only, on the flood condition through the normal upload chokepoint (so it mirrors to SharePoint).
- **ELEMENTIX IS TWO PLANES BEHIND TWO DOORS, AND CONTACT DETAIL MAY NEVER REACH A LENDING DECISION (owner-directed 2026-08-18; db/588 + db/589; the shipped architecture is `docs/ELEMENTIX-CRM-BUILD.md`, the design behind it `docs/ELEMENTIX-CRM-PLAN.md`, the measured connector `docs/ELEMENTIX-RESEARCH.md` + `docs/research/elementix/*`).** RTL only — `leads` (db/008) is an RTL table and everything here sits on it.
  1. **TWO CLOSED TOOL SETS, TWO DOORS, AND `lookups.js` WAS NOT TOUCHED.** `src/lib/elementix/lookups.js` is the UNDERWRITING plane (does this borrower's claimed track record exist in the recorded deeds?) and `submit_contact_enrichment` is permanently absent from its allowlist. `src/lib/elementix/crm-tools.js` is the CRM plane (an officer looking somebody up to telephone them), reached only through `/api/elementix`. That is the FCRA line `docs/research/elementix/04-connector-verification.md` draws — *"a contact detail may not appear in an underwriting decision"* — and it is STRUCTURAL rather than a convention: widening the underwriting allowlist would put the paid tool one typo away from a verification sweep. **Never add a contact tool to `lookups.js`, and never read `elementix_contacts` from an underwriting path.** What the two planes DO share is deliberate: the transport (`src/elementix/client.js` — rate bucket, money cap, ledger, never-throws) and the request PREFLIGHT, because a malformed request is answered `-32602` AND still spends a slot of the office's shared 1,000/hour.
  2. **ONE CONNECTION FOR EVERYBODY — `ELEMENTIX_SEAT_MODEL` defaults to `company` (owner-directed: "everybody should never need to auth again on their own; the super-admin auth that exists already should lead the integration for everybody").** THE CONSEQUENCE IS THE THING TO REMEMBER: Elementix stamps every unlock with the EMAIL OF THE LOGIN THAT MADE IT, so under a shared connection `unlockedBy` identifies a **seat**, not a **person**. For work done in Elementix's own screens that email is the only answer there is, and `elementix_users` maps it to an officer. For a trace made INSIDE PILOT it is the shared login, and mapping it would hand every PILOT-originated unlock to one person — a duplicate lead in the wrong pipeline with a notification claiming it. So **`backfill.pilotOwnerOf` asks PILOT's own record first** and, where PILOT already completed the trace, the import refreshes the detail and LEAVES THE HISTORY ALONE (it never rewrites the reason the officer typed or the record that a credit was spent). `matchUsers` auto-links only an **internal, ACTIVE** staffer.
  3. **THE MONEY: `submit_contact_enrichment` is the ONLY paid tool, behind four guards, in this order.** A named officer + a typed reason ≥4 characters (the library refuses a blank; the route refuses one too short to read back a month later); **we never re-buy what we already hold** (asked from OUR OWN database first — `lookups.isUnlocked` fails CLOSED, right for showing a phone number and exactly backwards for spending money); **never twice at once** (a charged `pending` trace inside `PENDING_HOLD_HOURS`=48 answers "already running"); and the monthly cap counted from `elementix_calls`, **failing CLOSED when the count cannot be read**. THE ORDER INSIDE `client.callTool` IS LOAD-BEARING AND IT IS NOT "GATES FIRST": the paid REFUSALS come first (a rule about what the CALLER asked for must not depend on a switch, a URL or a stored token, or a configuration state could be arranged such that a sweep spends credits), while the LEDGER WRITE sits below EVERY remaining refusal and still above the wire — **including the OAuth gate, which is the one that was missed**. It first ran with the refusals (with Elementix off, rate-limited or in dry run that wrote a paid row and THEN returned "switched off"), and then above `oauth.accessToken` — so an expired connection answered `not_connected` AFTER a credit had been recorded, on every retry, until the month's cap refused real work. **The test is not "can this still refuse for a configuration reason" but "can this still refuse AT ALL": if any line below the ledger write can return without reaching `post()`, the ledger write is in the wrong place.** Nothing that failed to reach the wire may be billed against the HOURLY cap either — `wentOut` lists every such reason, and `dry_run` plus the four OAuth reasons had to join it (a dry run was costing ~240 phantom calls an hour and would have throttled the underwriting desk on calls nobody made). **A DRY RUN IS A REFUSAL, NOT AN ANSWER** (`{ok:false, reason:'dry_run'}`) — answering `{ok:true,data:null}` makes `rowsOf` read "nobody by that name" and tells an officer their contact is on its way while nothing was sent.
  4. **TWO LOOPS, AND ONLY THE BULK ONE IS BEHIND A SWITCH.** LIST+WORK (creating leads out of work done in Elementix's own screens) are behind `ELEMENTIX_CRM_SYNC_ENABLED`, which is **ON** (owner-directed 2026-08-19 "set up auto pull leads") and is a real switch on the API Health page **read at CALL time** — read once at boot, a flip did nothing until the next deploy because the timers were never armed. The officer is notified about a **fresh** unlock only (the vendor's own `unlockedAt`, default 7 days): the same import carries the ~1,000-contact backlog, and notifying on that buries the one notice that is news. One unworkable row can never stall the queue — every failure is stamped against its own row and retired after three tries, or a throwing row returns at the head of the next batch forever. **SETTLE is not**: `crm.skipTrace` waits ~6 seconds for the vendor's enrichment job, a slower job is recorded `pending` AND THE CREDIT IS ALREADY SPENT, so `drainPendingSkipTraces` is the second half of a click a human already made — without it the contact never arrives, no lead is made, nobody is notified and the money is gone. It is FREE and gives up after 48 hours. Neither loop can reach the paid tool, asserted from the SOURCE in `test-elementix-backfill-db.js` (for the settle path, by reading the two `crm.js` functions it touches — a whole-file grep would be wrong, since `crm.js` legitimately buys contacts).
  5. **A TOOL'S PUBLISHED DESCRIPTION IS A SUMMARY, NOT A SCHEMA.** The catalogue names no "list our users" tool, and the first reading of it concluded the history was unpullable and the owner needed a CSV export. The owner said dig deeper and was right: `get_contact_status` returns undocumented `unlockedBy`/`unlockedAt`, and `list_people({unlockStatus:'unlocked'})` puts that email on EVERY row — the whole history in two calls (1,041 contacts, 13 logins, none missing). Shapes that were MEASURED and must not be re-guessed are listed in the build doc; the sharp ones: `get_person` → `{person:{…}}` singular; `get_person_lender_network` rows sit under **`lenderConnections`** (seventy lenders read as ZERO before it was captured); `get_contact_info` returns the job **two levels deep**; the page parameter is **`perPage`** (`limit` is ignored silently, default page 5); `nextPage` means "the page was full", never "there is more". Captured envelopes live in `scripts/fixtures/elementix-shapes.json` — call the tool, record the response, and only then remove it from `UNVERIFIED_SHAPE`. **A section never read, or that failed, is NEVER rendered as a confident zero.**
  6. **THE DOOR IS INTERNAL-ONLY, THE RECORD IS SCOPED, AND THE DESK IS RATE-LIMITED.** Every internal staff role may use it (the owner wants every officer skip tracing from their own login, so a capability gate nobody holds would kill it); an EXTERNAL staff row — a TPO broker, who must be `is_external=true` because a broker is the loan officer on their own firm's files — is refused first thing. Getting through the door is NOT permission to touch any record by id: `/link`, `/for/:kind/:id`, the profile READ, the profile BUILD and the alias merge all route through the shared `permissions.visibleLeadSql` / `visibleBorrowerSql` (moved there from `routes/staff.js` so a second door can ask without re-inlining a scope). **"SEEN" MEANS SEEN BY YOU.** The profile doors first accepted a bare `elementix_persons` header row as proof — and a header row is written for EVERY person anybody ever looks up, so that opened every profile on the plane to every officer and let anybody spend forty of the organisation's calls building one. A relationship is a lead or a borrower the officer can already see, or their own skip trace; the screen links BEFORE it builds, so the ordinary flow never needed the header branch. **AND THE COST CHECK CARRIES THE PRICE, NEVER THE NUMBERS**: `/people/:id/contact` is deliberately UNSCOPED (an officer must be able to ask what the next click costs about somebody they have not attached to anything yet), which is exactly why it may only answer counts — it was returning the stored row whole, so any internal officer could read another officer's borrower's phone numbers two hops from a name, with no audit row behind it. When a route must stay open, narrow what it SAYS, not who may ask. The desk is also THROTTLED PER OFFICER (`keyedRateLimit` on `req.actor.id`, 120/min, mounted after `requireAuth`), because one "Refresh data" click walks eight paged sections into ~40 outbound calls out of an allowance the WHOLE ORGANISATION shares — keyed on the PERSON, never the IP, since the office is behind one address and a per-IP bucket would read a busy afternoon as abuse.
  7. **REFUSED AT THE DOOR AS A COMPLIANCE MATTER:** `FORBIDDEN_ARGS` blocks `gender` and `hasHispanicName`. The vendor offers them as "inferred gender based on first name" and "surname is likely Hispanic/Spanish"; narrowing a marketing list by an inferred protected characteristic is what ECOA and the Fair Housing Act exist to prohibit, and PILOT is a lender. It FAILS the request rather than dropping the argument, because a filter quietly removed makes a screen show results that do not match what it asked for.
  8. **EVERY VENDOR PAYLOAD INTO `jsonb` GOES THROUGH `crmTools.vendorJsonb`.** Two failures, both AFTER the money is spent: a NUL byte (22P05 — and `nul-strip` never sees a vendor answer, which arrives over `fetch`, not `express.json`; the strip must be on the OBJECT via `fields.jsonbText`, never on the serialized string), and SIZE (capping by slicing serialized JSON yields a document Postgres rejects outright — an over-large payload is REPLACED by a marker that says so).
  8. **THE ADMIN CRM DESK IS A FILTER, NEVER A SECOND BOARD (owner-directed 2026-08-19: "make admin can see everybody all crm in admin crm screen — set up to switch view and jump from one officer full crm screen from each and everybody").** `/internal/crm` (behind `manage_team`) lists every ACTIVE INTERNAL officer with their CRM numbers — leads, leads from Elementix, contacts unlocked, credits spent this calendar month — from ONE statement (four CTEs LEFT-JOINed onto the roster, never a query per officer), with a company row AND an unassigned row so the arithmetic reconciles, and an officer who has done nothing appearing at a MEASURED zero rather than being absent (a missing row reads as "this person does not exist"). Opening one mounts **the existing leads board** with an `officerId` prop — never a copy: two lead boards drift, and the one that drifts is the one an admin reads somebody's numbers off. **`officerId` IS ANDED ONTO `visibleLeadSql`, NEVER IN PLACE OF IT** — for an admin it narrows the company to one officer, and for a plain loan officer `officer_id = <somebody else>` AND `(officer_id = me OR unassigned)` can never both hold, so they get an EMPTY list rather than another officer's desk. It is uuid-validated before Postgres (`leads.officer_id` is uuid; a malformed value raises 22P02 and turns the whole list into a 500 that reads as "you have no leads"). **AND IT SITS ABOVE THE SCOPE SNAPSHOT, unlike the three origin filters** — that placement is load-bearing and was found only by RENDERING it: the facet counts are taken over the snapshot, so with the filter below it the "From Elementix" tile on one officer's page printed the WHOLE COMPANY's count under her name while every test passed. Whose desk this is belongs to the scope; which origin group is selected does not.

  9. **THE ELEMENTIX HISTORY LANDS ON THE BORROWER'S OWN PROFILE, AND THE TRACK-RECORD SEARCH IS THE PROFILE BUILDER'S OWN DEEP SEARCH (owner-directed 2026-08-19: "the profile builder pulls in all the properties, all the mortgages, everything… the track record search only populated one file from 10-20 properties… do the same kind of deep search… all the entities should be added to his personal profile… all the properties added on the track record automatically. Not as verified, of course… and backdate this for the profiles we already have"; db/597).** `src/lib/elementix/deep-history.js` is the ONE definition.
     · **WHY THE OLD SEARCH FOUND SO LITTLE — three structural reasons, all closed:** it resolved the person BY NAME per state (refusing ambiguity a human link had already settled), it read ONE vendor page per list, and it searched only the companies already typed on the profile. When `borrowers.elementix_person_id` is set, `importer.runSearch` now pulls the person's whole history BY ID — deeds + mortgages + the company roster, paged exactly as the profile builder pages (250 × 4, same call budget, constants pinned by the pure test), across the human-CONFIRMED cross-state family — through `lookups.js` (the underwriting plane's own door; `get_person_entities` joined its closed set as a FREE tool). The name-based person search is skipped when the linked pull ran, and companies this import put on the profile are not re-searched one by one (their records came through the person pull) — the allowance is spent once, not per company.
     · **EVERYTHING IMPORTS, NOTHING IS GUESSED, NOTHING IMPORTS AS VERIFIED.** The history goes through the importer's OWN gates — `stageOne` (dedupe key, durable declines, match banding) then the importer's own verbs — so: a DECLINED property is never re-raised; an EXACT match fills blanks on the existing line while a VERIFIED line is LEFT FOR A HUMAN (the auto path never passes `confirmReopen`); a NEAR match always waits (auto-merging one is how a duplicate line is born); a new line lands `pending` (db/485) with the deal type the records STATE (bought+sold = flip) or LEFT BLANK plus a note — `importNew`'s `allowUnstatedDealType` is reachable ONLY from the auto path (the HTTP decide route never passes it; grep-guarded), so no human door can create a typeless line. A blank-type line counts toward nothing until a person answers.
     · **ENTITIES NEED A STATED ROLE AND ARE HELD OUT OF THE MONEY.** A company lands only when the vendor states the person's role (isPrincipal / sosOfficer / elementixSigner — absence and explicit false are NOT roles), through `promoteEntityName` (junk guard + strict promotionMatch, reuse-never-duplicate), capped (`ELEMENTIX_IMPORT_MAX_ENTITIES`, default 300, remainder REPORTED) — and a row this import CREATES carries the db/400 adoption stamp (`adopted_source='elementix'`) so `entityDocsPendingNames` keeps its bank balances OUT of provable liquidity until a document lands or it is verified. A human's own entity is never stamped. Formation state fills in (fill-only, created rows only), which is also what makes the next records search able to use the exact matcher.
     · **THE FCRA LINE HOLDS.** The deep import reads ONLY the deeds/mortgages/entities sections and the persons/aliases headers — never `elementix_contacts`, never the overview section (it carries a mailing address), never a CRM-plane tool — asserted on the SOURCE by the pure suite.
     · **THREE DOORS, ONE MIDDLE.** The track-record search (live pull), the profile BUILD route + the borrower /link route (cache import, best-effort — never fails the build or the link, and a borrower with no cached profile gains NOTHING, not even a search row), and the back-book sweep `backfillOnce` in `elementix-crm-sync` (cache-only — ZERO vendor calls, so deliberately NOT gated on the Elementix switch; its own call-time switch `ELEMENTIX_HISTORY_IMPORT_ENABLED`, default ON; bounded, self-draining via the db/597 stamps, a row retired after 3 failed attempts with `imported_at` deliberately left NULL — stamping a failure "imported" would be a false record). Every cache import writes a provenance `track_record_searches` row (`query.source='elementix_profile_import'`) so the queue's "last search" says where the lines came from.
     · **TWO PRE-EXISTING DEFECTS FIXED AT THEIR CHOKEPOINTS, both measured before fixing:** (a) `stageOne` fed `pilot_address_same_place` the vendor's one-line STRING, which the SQL comparer reads as `''` (`jsonb_typeof <> 'object'`, db/415/497) — so `match_confidence='exact'` was UNREACHABLE from every vendor candidate ever staged and every real match banded 'near'; it now passes `ADDR.parseToAddressObject(...)`, the same shape the import stores. (b) `importNew` promoted a personal-name deal's `entity_name` into a "company" NAMED AFTER THE BORROWER (a person-named `llcs` row with document slots nobody can fill); it now refuses the borrower's own name via `namesMatchLoose` — the same rule `stageOne` applies for the match basis — and the line simply imports with no entity link, which is what owned-personally looks like.
     · **THE `personKeyed` FLAG WIDENS EXACTLY ONE THING.** Rows off the person's own id may carry `isGrantee:false` meaning "the PERSON is not the grantee" while the deed conveyed to THEIR company — so `candidatesFrom` consults the strict name test even on an explicit false ONLY when `research.personKeyed === true`. The ENTITY branch is byte-identical (the York, PA rule), pinned by the pure test in both directions.
  10. **THE BORROWER SEARCHES THEIR OWN RECORDS, EVERY LINE OPENS ITS OWN STORY, AND WHAT CAME FROM THE RECORDS SAYS SO (owner-directed 2026-08-19: "also on the borrower side, they can click the search button on themselves and import their entire track record … on each and every thing of the track record, we should be able to click 'See more information' … we need to design a very modern stamp, which would be an Elementix verify stamp … when you're exporting the Excel track record or the PDF … it should have a stamp which says 'Verified to Elementix'").** No schema change — every part of this is derived or reuses an existing writer.
      · **THE BORROWER'S DOOR IS A SEPARATE MODULE ON PURPOSE — `src/lib/track-record/self-search.js`.** It runs the SAME `importer.runSearch` the staff button runs, so a borrower's click can never import anything a staffer's click could not; it is separate so `borrower-confirm.js` stays vendor-free (its own guard test greps THAT file for vendor requires, and a shared door would have broken it). **A BORROWER-RUN SEARCH NEVER SEARCHES A BARE PERSONAL NAME** (`personalNameSearch:false` → the importer's `nameSearchAllowed`): a same-name stranger's portfolio would land on a public screen, which is the whole reason the staging table exists. The linked person-id deep pull is UNAFFECTED — that identity was confirmed by a human — and so are the entity searches; the suppressed search is RECORDED as a `personal_name_not_searched` skip, never dropped silently. **THE BUDGET IS BOUNDED THREE WAYS AND TWO ARE DURABLE**, because an in-memory counter is reset by a deploy: a per-borrower `keyedRateLimit` at the route (never per-IP — the office is one address), a 15-minute cooldown on ANY search row for that borrower (staff searches included; the engine is idempotent, so a re-run minutes later can only re-spend calls to re-find the same records), and a 10-per-30-days ceiling counted from `query->>'requestedBy'='borrower'` so a staff search never spends the borrower's allowance. Every sentence the borrower reads is composed SERVER-SIDE (`borrowerSummary`): counts in plain words, the vendor NEVER named to a borrower, and "found nothing" never worded as "you have no history".
      · **"SEE MORE INFORMATION" REUSES THE ONE READ — `src/lib/track-record/line-details.js`.** It calls `verify-run.runVerify`, the SAME pull and the SAME 90-day cache the "Check the records" button uses, so the story, the pillar verdicts and the stamp all come from ONE read of the county records: a fresh click is a cached read at zero vendor cost and `refresh:true` is the force that button already had. The fill comes from the importer's OWN `candidatesFrom` mapping — never a second reading of a deed, so a figure this door fills is byte-for-byte what the import queue would have staged. **THE APPLY IS FILL-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION**: `col = COALESCE(col, $n)`, so a value anybody typed always wins and a concurrent edit between the read and the write simply wins too — a property of the statement, not a check somebody can forget (a behaviour test cannot stage that race, so the shape is pinned at the SOURCE by the pure suite). A DIFFERING recorded value is SHOWN and never offered. A VERIFIED line refuses a material fill with `409 code='would_reopen'` until asked a second time, mirroring the importer's merge verb, because db/485 honestly re-opens the review — `seller_name` is deliberately `material:false` and matches db/485's own list.
      · **THE STAMP IS DERIVED IN SQL AND NEVER STORED — `src/lib/track-record/records-stamp.js` is the ONE definition.** Three writers already record the provenance (the importer's `origin='public_records'`, the candidate decisions, verify-run's pillar observations), so a fourth writer keeping a denormalized column in step would drift from them and a derived CASE cannot: a re-run that stops proving downgrades **verified → sourced** with no writer anywhere. **TWO HONEST STATES, NEVER COLLAPSED:** `verified` = a `track_record_pillars` row with `pillar IN ('ownership','exit') AND auto_source='elementix' AND auto_verdict='proved'`; `sourced` = the importer's origin OR an elementix candidate joined on `imported_track_record_id + status='imported'` / `match_track_record_id + status='merged'` (a DECLINED candidate proves nothing about a line and must never count). **NEITHER IS `track_records.is_verified`** — that is the human's experience verification, a third thing this module never reads, and recency is date arithmetic (`auto_source='derived'`) so it is never a records match. The ENTITY flavour reads the db/400 adoption stamp (`llcs.adopted_source='elementix'`) and has ONE state on purpose: an entity's document verification is a human judgement about its papers.
      · **STATE IS NEVER CARRIED BY COLOUR ALONE** (glyph shape AND word both differ, so the chip survives grayscale and colour-blindness — WCAG 1.4.1), and every colour is an explicit dark on white per the hard rule, never an `--ink*` token. **Screen chips say "public records"** (what the claim is about); **the EXPORTS say "Verified to Elementix"**, the owner's exact words — Elementix is a records VENDOR, not a capital partner, so naming it breaks no scrub rule. The PDF stamp is a FLAT block (never angled), and the ✓/○ glyphs are deliberately absent from the jsPDF block (WinAnsi cannot render them).
      · **ONE DEFINITION, THREE MIRRORS, ONE GUARD.** `app-v2/src/components/track-record/RecordsStamp.jsx`, `web/v2/tools/track-record-portal.js` and `web/v2/tools/track-record.js` cannot require server code (the `lib/payoff.js` arrangement), so each carries a mirror — and `scripts/test-records-stamp-pure.js` reads ALL FOUR FILES and fails the moment any pair drifts, with the summary fragments DERIVED from live `summaryLine` output rather than retyped, so changing the server wording moves the fragments and a stale mirror fails. **Never re-inline the wording or a review test** — ask `records-stamp` (`stampSelect` / `exportCellText` / `summaryLine` / `ENTITY_STAMP_SQL`).
      · **AN UNSTAMPED EXPORT IS BYTE-IDENTICAL TO BEFORE.** The 'Public records' column and the stamp block appear ONLY when a row actually carries a stamp — an always-blank column on an investor package is noise, and the whole back book exports exactly as it did. Surfaces carrying it: the ledger + line detail + all four entity screens, BOTH saved-copy builders (`html-copy.js` and the bridge's `snapshotHtml` — **the two must stay in step**, as that file's own comment says), the tool's own xlsx + PDF, and the investor package's xlsx + PDF (`tpr-export.js` → `track-record-export.js`).
      · **THE FULL SCREEN IS THE LIVE WORKSPACE, NOT THE STATIC COPY.** `StaffApplication`'s "Open full screen" navigates to `/internal/track-record?borrower=`; the legacy grid survives behind a "Spreadsheet editor" button (still the ONLY bulk-edit / xlsx-import editor, so nothing was removed before something replaced it). The records-pull workbench also mounts on that workspace when it is narrowed to one borrower — the SAME shared `StaffPropertyWorkbench` the loan file and the profile mount, never a copy.
      Tests `scripts/test-records-stamp-pure.js` + `scripts/test-records-stamp-db.js` (both in `npm test`); nine mutations of the production code were each proven to fail them, and the suite caught a real wording bug (bad pluralization in two borrower sentences) before merge.
  Twelve suites in `npm test` (`test-elementix-{oauth-pure,callback-public,lookups-pure,request-contracts-pure,crm-db,profile-db,crm-routes-db,backfill-db,crm-desk-db,lead-source-db,deep-history-pure,deep-history-db}` plus `test-elementix-profile-filters-pure`); every guard added across the audit passes was mutation-proven (the deep import's seven mutations included). When you add a surface here, put contact detail behind `/api/elementix`, scope it per record, and never let it be read from an underwriting path — and when you add another way Elementix data reaches a borrower's real profile, route it through `deep-history.runImport`, never a second staging or entity path.
- **TABLE FUNDING — which note buyers may fund that way, and why a table-funded loan never gets a purchase advice date (owner-directed 2026-08-09).** The owner: *"most of the properties that have Fidelis as a note buyer should be defaulted to table funding, which we have on the closing side … look on the channel in Encompass cx.tablefunder — if that says table funding, then it is not going to have a PA date … for Fidelis, you can allow that to be table funding, and for RCN or ROC or TempleView, you can also allow them to be table funding. Any file that is Blue Lake or emcap or corrfirst it should never be table funding. It should always be direct RTL / delegate or direct RTL / w tpr — it needs to follow this rule in order to pass Encompass Sync … for the other note buyers … you don't need to care about this field."* **RTL only** — `applications` IS the RTL table; the long-term side has its own `lt_*` tables and does not appear anywhere in this.
  · **NOTHING NEW WAS INVENTED — both halves already existed and had never been joined up.** `closing_workflow.table_funded` has meant "sold at the closing table" since 2026-07-26 (derived from the closer's warehouse pick, `closing.tableFundedFor`, and already the fork that decides whether a file enters the purchasing desk). And **`CX.TABLEFUNDER` was already being pulled on every Encompass sync** — as `ref_table_funder`, `compare:'reference'`, `our:'none'`: read, displayed, compared against nothing. The owner's rule gives that field a job, so it is promoted to a real compared registry row (`funding_channel`) whose OUR side is the warehouse pick.
  · **THE NAMES ARE THE TENANT'S, NOT THE OWNER'S MESSAGE**, which was an explicit instruction (*"when I say name, look for the correct research for the correct spelling of the nodes"*). `src/lib/funding-channel.js` keys on `VALUE_MAPS.capitalProvider` — the table whose key list was read LIVE off the Encompass capital-provider dropdown on 2026-07-26 — so **"Core First" is CorrFirst, "ROC" is Roc Capital, "TempleView" is Temple View Capital**, and the rule can never disagree with the Encompass panel's own capital-provider row about who a file belongs to. MAY table fund: `fidelis`, `rcn`, `roccapital`, `templeview`. NEVER: `bluelake`, `emcap`, `corrfirst`. Everyone else: no rule at all. Adding a real new spelling is one line in THAT shared table, never a second list here.
  · **THE TWO DIRECT VALUES SHARE ONE TOKEN, and that is a correctness requirement.** Our side of the comparison is a WAREHOUSE PICK, which cannot tell delegated from TPR because PILOT never records which. Giving them separate tokens made every correctly-configured direct file read as a MISMATCH on a distinction our side is structurally incapable of holding — caught before it shipped, and pinned by test B12. The question the row asks is binary: sold at the table, or still to be sold. Encompass's exact wording is never lost (the panel shows the raw value beside the normalized one). The value map also recognizes a plain **Y/N flag**, because the registry has described this field as a "table funder FLAG" since it was written while the owner describes it as a channel — both readings mean the same thing about the loan, and neither can be confirmed from here (`verified:false` says so).
  · **THE REGISTRY ROW IS REFERENCE-ONLY, AND THE TWO REAL QUESTIONS ARE ROWS WE CONTROL** (`reconcile.compareFundingChannel`). It was built as a compared enum first, and that shipped a hazard caught in review: `summarize()` counts "no data to compare" as NOT PASSING, and this field's tenant values are **unverified**, so on any FUNDED file whose `CX.TABLEFUNDER` held a spelling the value map does not carry (a table funder's NAME, say) our side would be present, theirs unmappable, and **the term sheet AND the data-tape export blocked — with the only remedy being a code change nobody at the desk can make.** That is the dead-end class this file warns about elsewhere ("a gate whose own remedy cannot produce the state the gate demands"). So the registry row is `compare:'reference'` (displayed, never counted), and two synthetic rows ask the real questions, each firing ONLY on a value PILOT can positively read: **`funding_channel_rule` (BLOCK)** — the owner's hard rule — and **`funding_channel_agreement` (ADVISORY)** — our closer's warehouse pick disagreeing with Encompass about how the loan funded. An unreadable channel, an unpicked warehouse, an unknown buyer and a buyer with no rule all produce NO row at all, so nothing unverified can ever gate. The recorded way past a genuine violation is the same super-admin field exception every other row on that panel has — `requestException` looks the field up in the COMPUTED set, so a non-registry row works there for free (proven, not assumed: test F6–F9). OUR note buyer decides whose rule applies, Encompass's copy only as a fallback — otherwise the sync would apply a DIFFERENT buyer's rule than the 5% SOW contingency, the bank-statement months and the data-tape gate are applying to the same file.
  · **THE PA DATE IS ENCOMPASS FIELD 2370** (owner-supplied). `ENCOMPASS_PA_DATE_FIELD_ID` stays as an OVERRIDE — setting it blank switches the field off, which is the fastest way to stop a wrong id 400-ing the whole read batch. Adding it to the registry auto-includes it in `allFieldIds()` → the fieldReader → `_fieldValues` → `release-party.syncPurchaseAdviceDate`.
  · **TABLE FUNDED IS SOLD, CHECKED BEFORE THE PA DATE** (`release-party.soldStatus`). A table-funded loan is sold AT the table, so no purchase advice is ever coming and its absence proves nothing — checking it first is what stops "no PA date" reading as "not sold" and warning the coordinator, and chasing the closer, on every Fidelis deal forever. EITHER signal alone is enough (our warehouse OR Encompass); requiring both would make a data-entry gap on either side generate exactly those false alarms, and the two disagreeing is already surfaced as the `funding_channel` row. `soldVia` reports WHICH, so the desk says "Table funded — sold at the closing table" rather than leaving a coordinator wondering where the date is.
  · **~~THE DEFAULT IS NOW "TREAT IT AS SOLD"~~ — SUPERSEDED 2026-08-13 (see "NOT SOLD YET → WE RELEASE THE NET" below): an unsold loan is now RELEASED BY US and the warning became a badge with a coordinator override. The rest of this bullet is kept as the history of why the wording moved.** (owner: *"default should be like it was sold already, but it should give you a warning that it doesn't have a PA date if you're sure you want to do investor delivery"*), so the warning leads with permission to carry on and offers releasing it ourselves SECOND. It still refuses nothing and writes nothing. The title states the FACT ("No purchase advice date on this file") rather than the old conclusion ("This loan isn't sold yet"), which is too strong now that a table-funded loan is correctly read as sold without one.
  · **FIDELIS DEFAULTS TO TABLE FUNDING AS A SUGGESTION, NEVER A WRITE** (`closing.warehouseSuggestionFor`, offered on the Funding card while the warehouse is still unset). The owner said plainly that a few Fidelis deals are not table funded, and auto-writing it would silently mark those files sold. It is computed from the note buyer rather than stored, so a file whose buyer changes gets the right suggestion with nothing having to notice, and it disappears the moment anyone picks a line.
  · **THE 30-DAY CHASE** — `notification-digests.purchaseAdviceMissingOnce` (super admins + the file's `closer_id`; `PURCHASE_ADVICE_CHASE_DAYS`, default 30). Super admins only and **never the shared `NOTIFY_ADMINS` inbox** — that list is hand-typed ADDRESSES with no role attached, the one list that could carry somebody the owner did not name; every admin still SEES it in PILOT. Three independent things must say the loan is still to be sold before anyone is told: our warehouse is not the Table Funding line, Encompass's channel does not say table funding either, **and neither of the TWO places a purchase advice can be recorded has one** — the Encompass-read `applications.purchase_advice_date` AND the closer's own hand-entered `purchasing_advice` row (missing the second would chase files where a human had already recorded it). Self-gating ≤ once/file/week. **ANSWERED 2026-08-09** — *"yes chase them, blue lake emcap corrfirst — only stuff that needs to be sold to get this reminder. Fidelis is on a case-by-case basis, most of Fidelis is table funding. All the other RCN, [Roc], and Temple View are also table funded, so we don't need a reminder. Only these few are real sold."* So the chase now asks TWO questions and both must pass: is this FILE still to be sold (the table-funded checks), and does this BUYER's business get sold after closing at all (`funding-channel.chaseMissingPurchaseAdvice`)? `rcn` / `roccapital` / `templeview` answer NO and are never chased, even on a file our closing desk did not mark table funded — the exclusion is about the buyer, which is precisely why the file-level check cannot express it. **Fidelis is deliberately NOT on that list**: that is the owner's "case-by-case", and its table-funded files are already silenced by the file check, so only the few that genuinely have to be sold are chased. An **unrecognised or absent** buyer IS chased — the owner named who needs no reminder, and going quiet on a funded loan nobody has classified would be a gap that appears the day a new note buyer is added and that nobody would notice. (The owner's "Rack" is Roc Capital — the same speech-to-text slip as "Core First" for CorrFirst; read through the shared table, never off the message.)
  · **THE WARNING AT THE DELIVERY IS A CHECK, NOT A STOP** — `investor-delivery-send.deliveryPreview` returns `sold_warning` alongside, and deliberately OUTSIDE, `blockers`, so `can_send` is decided by the blockers alone. Pinned the non-tautological way: giving the same file a purchase advice date must leave the blockers **byte-identical** (comparing `can_send` against `blockers.length === 0` would still hold if the warning became a blocker, so that assertion would never have bitten — the mutation was run and the byte-identical check is what caught it). A table-funded file gets no warning at all.
  · Tests `scripts/test-funding-channel-pure.js` (129 checks — the buyer spellings, the channel readings, and a long section on the rule DECLINING to judge, because the expensive failure on a BLOCK-gated row is a FALSE violation) and `scripts/test-purchase-advice-chase-db.js` (45 checks against a real database — both new queries' columns proven to exist, since a phantom column inside their catch blocks would report a confident, wrong "nothing to do" forever, plus the exception escape hatch and the never-blocks guarantee). Eleven mutations of the production code were each proven to fail them. When you add a surface that asks how a loan funded, route it through `funding-channel` — never a second copy of the buyer list or the channel spellings.
- **THE INVESTOR'S CUT OF OUR DRAW FEE — the money ledger ONLY (owner-directed 2026-08-13).** The owner: *"out of our $299 fee, our $250 fee, or our $499 fee, certain investors charge off the fee for the release, and their fee gets netted from RV … any file that is funding with CorrFirst that is already sold to CorrFirst, the investor fee on the ledger should automatically fill out as $95 … our net fee should automatically deduct the $95, and our final fee is only $204. If it's not sold yet, then we get all of the entire fee.
