The hard rules should be set: CorrFirst $95, Blue Lake $250 … by Blue Lake our net fee is 0 because they charge the entire thing … Don't change anything from the front end that a borrower sees or that a staff member sees. The only change we need to make is to the actual ledger, which is the fee: how much of the fee is actually going to be deposited in our bank account?"*
  · **IT SPLITS ONE FEE, IT NEVER CHANGES ONE.** `draw_disbursements.fee_cents` keeps its exact meaning and value — the fee charged out of the borrower's approved draw, which is what the term sheet, the borrower's screen, every draw email and the investor delivery already print. The new columns (db/542) answer the SECOND question about that same fee: `investor_fee_cents` (what the note buyer keeps for handling the release), `net_fee_cents` (**GENERATED ALWAYS AS `GREATEST(0, fee_cents - investor_fee_cents)` STORED**, so the deposit can never be typed into disagreeing with the ledger it sits in) and `investor_fee_key` (who kept it). A CHECK keeps the cut inside the fee. The borrower's net (`computeRelease`) does not take the cut as an input at all — proven, not asserted, by recording the same draw with and without one.
  · **THE RATES LIVE IN ONE PLACE** — `INVESTOR_DRAW_FEE_CENTS` in `src/sitewire/investor-fee.js` (pure): `corrfirst: 9500`, `bluelake: 25000`. OWNER-SET NUMBERS — changing one changes booked income on every future release for that buyer, so it takes the owner saying so; adding a buyer is one line there and nothing else. The buyer is recognised through the SHARED capital-provider table (`funding-channel.toBuyerKey`), so "Core First" ≡ "CorrFirst" here for the same reason it is everywhere else.
  · **IT ONLY APPLIES ONCE THE LOAN IS SOLD TO THEM, AND IT NEVER GUESSES.** Sold is `release-party.soldStatus` — the same three-valued answer (sold / not_sold / unknown) the draw desk's "who releases the money" card shows, so the ledger and that card can never disagree. Only a positive **sold** fills the box in; not-sold and unknown fill in **$0** (we keep the whole fee) and say so in words, naming the buyer's rate so a coordinator who knows the file is sold applies it with one press. Filling it in on a maybe would quietly under-report our own income on every file PILOT cannot read.
  · **THE BOX IS EDITABLE, AND BOTH WRITERS SHARE ONE DEFAULT.** `POST /api/sitewire/disbursements` takes an optional `investor_fee_cents`: absent = the rule, typed = the typed figure, validated like every other money field (NaN/negative → 400; a cut bigger than our fee → 422 and nothing recorded). The automatic investor-released writer (`auto-release.js`) calls the SAME `defaultInvestorFeeCents`, so a release PILOT books itself and one a coordinator types can never book different income — and there the **receivable is the NET fee**: a buyer who keeps $95 of our $299 sends us $204 and owes us $204, so a Blue Lake draw owes us nothing and stays `fee_status='n_a'` (exactly how the observed TrustPoint rows have always been stamped).
  · **SURFACES: THE LEDGER CARD AND THE ACCOUNTING EXPORT, NOTHING ELSE.** The "Investor fee $" box beside "Our fee $", an "Our fee … keeps … Deposited to us" line under the untouched "Borrower nets", two ledger columns, the "Our fees" tile's sub-line, `rollup.fees.investor_cents` / `net_cents` (deleted for borrowers by `borrowerSafeRollup` with the rest of `fees`), and two GL-export columns. Every one of them is hidden on a file with no such deal, so those files read exactly as they did before. **No borrower or broker surface changed, and nothing about the draw itself changed.**
  · Tests `scripts/test-investor-draw-fee-pure.js` (40 checks — the two rates, the owner's worked $299 → $95 → $204 example, "not sold = the entire fee", the caps and refusals, and the borrower-money-never-moves invariant) and `scripts/test-investor-draw-fee-db.js` (36 checks against a real database THROUGH THE REAL ROUTE — the auto-fill, the edit in both directions, the refusals recording nothing, Blue Lake banking nothing, the generated deposit refusing a hand-written value, and the desk carrying the rule). Both in `npm test`.
- **THE SOLD SIGNAL WAS SLOW AND HALF-BLIND — the bug behind "why did we need to click Refresh?" (owner-reported 2026-08-13).** A file with a purchase advice date in Encompass (field 2370, 07/31) still read as NOT SOLD on the draw desk more than two weeks later, so the ledger offered no investor fee until somebody pressed *Refresh PA date*. TWO independent causes, both fixed:
  · **PILOT DISAGREED WITH ITSELF.** `release-party.soldStatus` read only `applications.purchase_advice_date` (the Encompass column), while our OWN purchasing desk records the same advice — date and usually the advice document — in `purchasing_advice.advice_date`, and the 30-day "no purchase advice" chase has always accepted EITHER. The sold signal now reads all three sources — table funding, Encompass's field, and our own desk's record — in that order, each with its own `soldVia` so the card says which one answered. This alone fixes every file whose advice our team has already logged, retroactively and with no backfill: it is read live, not stored.
  · **THE ENCOMPASS RE-READ IS A ROUND-ROBIN.** `sync/encompass-sync.js` pulls ONE file every 15 minutes ordered by staleness across every non-declined file with a loan number — so a given file's turn comes round once every (files ÷ ~96) days, and NOTHING re-read a specific file on demand except the manual button. Now: (1) `release-party.refreshSoldSignal` re-reads **the purchase advice date alone** — one field by number, not the whole loan — when the draw desk opens a file that still reads as not sold, so the coordinator gets the right answer on the FIRST look; and (2) the poll gained a **priority lane** — a funded file with a live draw project and no advice yet goes first, everything else keeps its previous order behind it.
  · **EVERY GUARD ON THE SELF-REFRESH IS DELIBERATE:** only when not already sold; only with a CACHED loan GUID (it never triggers a pipeline search — that is the round-robin's expensive path); throttled per file via `sitewire_property_links.sold_check_at` (db/544, `DRAW_SOLD_RECHECK_MINUTES`, default 30) **stamped BEFORE the call** so a failing read cannot retry on every page load; its own timeout so a slow Encompass never holds up a screen; read-only into our own column; and it never throws. The Encompass client is INJECTABLE (like `db`), so the whole path — throttle, one-field read, landing — is proven against a stub with no network and no credentials.
- **INVESTOR DRAW FEES ARE ADMIN SETTINGS NOW, not code (owner-directed 2026-08-13: "every investor that we add to our system should automatically come up with their option to set how much their fee is. It should already preset the rules that we set together").** `investor_draw_fees` (db/545) keyed by the SHARED capital-provider token, seeded with CorrFirst $95 / Blue Lake $250, edited on the admin Draw Rules screen ("What each investor keeps from our draw fee"), which lists **every note buyer the system knows about** — add an investor and their box is simply there. `GET/PATCH /api/sitewire/investor-fees` (read: `manage_draws`; write: `platform_setup`, journaled). A configured **0** means "keeps nothing" and is honoured; **clearing** returns the buyer to the built-in rate. A rate over $5,000 is refused as a decimal slip rather than silently zeroing our income for that buyer.
  · **ITS OWN TABLE, DELIBERATELY** — not a column on `sitewire_inspection_rules`, whose rows are keyed to a Sitewire capital partner and drive the PUSH (a rule whose partner does not resolve is rejected by design, audit B-9). A draw-fee rate has nothing to do with pushing a property, and coupling them would have made somebody create a routing rule just to record "CorrFirst keeps $95".
  · **THE BUILT-IN RATES REMAIN THE FALLBACK,** and `loadConfiguredRates` answers `{}` on an unreadable table — so an empty or broken table still books CorrFirst at $95 rather than silently reporting income we never received. All three write paths (the hand-recorded release, the desk's default, the automatic investor-released writer) read the same table.
- **THE RELEASE CARD SHOWS THE ARITHMETIC, NOT A SENTENCE (owner-directed 2026-08-13: "it should be nicer and more visible to understand everything").** Two columns side by side, each ending in the number that actually moves: **The borrower** (approved − our fee − retainage → *Wired to the borrower*) and **Our fee** (charged − what the investor keeps → *Reaching our bank*, or *Stays with us out of the wire* on a we-release draw, where nothing arrives separately). A "Reaching our bank" tile joins the project totals whenever an investor keeps anything. Every one of these is hidden on a file with no such deal.
- **THE POST-PURCHASE HAND-OFF — Encompass says it sold, PILOT finishes the purchase (owner-directed 2026-08-13).** The owner: *"the purchase advice date you enter manually in PILOT needs to match the purchase advice date in Encompass … and Encompass needs to have a purchase advice date filled in in order for you to be able to mark purchase completed. And when the system realizes that Encompass has a purchase advice date filled out, it should email the post-purchase people … but once one of the two is done, the draw coordinator should be able to continue as usual — it's sold, fine. PILOT should still have outstanding tasks for the post-purchaser to take care of."*
  · **TWO CLOCKS THAT DELIBERATELY DO NOT WAIT FOR EACH OTHER, and that is the whole design.** The DRAW side moves the moment EITHER source says sold (Encompass's date, our own purchasing record, or table funding) — the coordinator is never held up by paperwork on another desk. The PURCHASING side stays **outstanding** until the post-purchase team finishes it here. Pinned by test 6a/6b: the same file reads `sold` on the draw desk while its purchase is still outstanding.
  · **THE GATE ON "MARK PURCHASE COMPLETE"** — `post-purchase.adviceGate` (PURE): Encompass must hold a date, PILOT must hold one, and they must be the **same calendar day**. Both sides go through `release-party.paDateOf`, the same parser the sold signal uses, so a stored timestamp, an ISO date and a typed US date compare as the days they are. Each refusal names WHICH fact is missing and prints **both** dates — a gate that only says "no" is a dead end, and the person reading it is the one who can fix it. Only on the way to complete: moving a file BACK to outstanding is never gated, because that is how a mistake is corrected.
  · **A SUPER-ADMIN WAY THROUGH, with a typed reason, audited** (`purchasing_complete_override`). Encompass is read-only to PILOT, so without it a file whose date is wrong THERE could never be closed out HERE — exactly the "gate whose own remedy the user cannot perform" this file warns about elsewhere. Refused without `override:true`, refused for a non-super-admin, refused with a reason under 8 characters.
  · **THE EMAIL FIRES FROM THE ONE PLACE THE DATE LANDS** — `release-party.syncPurchaseAdviceDate`, which all three read paths (the poll worker, the draw desk's self-refresh, the manual button) go through, so the hand-off cannot depend on which of them noticed. `applications.purchase_advice_notified_at` (db/546) makes it **once only**, claimed with an `IS NULL`-guarded UPDATE so two simultaneous reads cannot both announce. It stays quiet when the date was cleared rather than set, and when the purchase is ALREADY complete — asking for work that is done is noise.
  · **THE WORK IS LEFT WHERE IT IS SEEN, not only in an inbox:** an outstanding purchasing task ("Upload the purchase advice, enter the purchase advice date, and mark the purchase complete"), added only if an identical open one is not already there.
  · **WHO IS TOLD IS A LIST, NOT A CONSTANT** — `post_purchase_notify` (db/546), **seeded by NAME against the real staff roster** (Malky Katz, Chaya Gruber; active internal staff only) because an id copied from one database is meaningless in another and a wrong one would silently email nobody. Read by the purchasing desk, edited by an admin on that screen (`GET/POST /api/staff/purchasing/notify-list`), and the card says plainly in red when the list is EMPTY — the honest failure, since an empty list means nobody is told.
  · Test `scripts/test-post-purchase-db.js` (42 checks, in `npm test`): every gate case through the REAL route including the two date shapes agreeing, the override and its two refusals plus its audit row, the announcement being once-only / skipped on a finished purchase / leaving exactly one task, the fire-from-the-shared-landing-point path, the two-clocks invariant, and the recipient list being readable by the desk but editable only by an admin.
- **NOT SOLD YET → WE RELEASE THE NET, AND THERE IS NO INVESTOR FEE — plus the draw desk's way past it (owner-directed 2026-08-13; SUPERSEDES the advisory "treat it as sold" default of 2026-08-09).** The owner: *"If Encompass has a PA date already, then it should always proceed with the setting of the file — if the investor releases directly, or if we release and we get reimbursed. If it's not yet sold, then it should always be set up that we release the net amount … every file that is not sold yet should have a badge: 'this file was not sold yet' — but the draw coordinator can click Change Setting and process the draw, imagine if it was sold already. In case anything goes wrong she should have this ability, which should give her a double warning … if it's not sold yet it should not fill out the investor fee, because the investor is not charging it yet — they're not reimbursing it and they're not releasing it. They're going to buy the loan with the draw released already, so it's not going to be an extra charge."*
  · **THE SOLD SIGNAL NOW DECIDES, IT NO LONGER COMMENTS.** `release-party.enforcedMode` (pure): sold → the settings ladder stands, whichever way it points; not sold / cannot tell → **`reimbursement` (we release)**, whatever the file, the capital provider or the company default say. `manual` is deliberately left alone — PILOT never witnessed that money and must not claim to know. `describe()` therefore returns the EFFECTIVE `mode` plus `configuredMode` / `forcedByNotSold`, so the desk shows the coordinator's own choice AND why it is on hold, and every consumer (the money ledger's `release_party`, `auto-release`'s `autoLedger`, the draw checklist, the investor-delivery email's money split) reads one answer. An unsold file therefore can never auto-write an investor-released ledger row, and can never book a fee receivable nobody owes.
  · **THE INVESTOR FEE IS ABSENT, NOT ZERO.** `investor-fee.describe()` returns `offer:false` on an unsold loan, the ledger does not render the box at all, and `POST /disbursements` **422s** a cut typed against one ("they aren't charging a fee on this draw — this loan hasn't been sold to them yet"). Before the sale we wire the borrower the net out of our own money, so there is no remittance for anybody to deduct from and **nothing is deposited to us at all** — the release is simply smaller by our fee. The ledger says exactly that on a we-release draw ("Our fee $299.00 stays out of this wire — we release the net, so there is nothing to collect afterwards") and only says "coming to us" when the investor is the one wiring.
  · **THE BADGE + THE DOUBLE WARNING** — `notSoldBadge` (which kept the old `warning` export name so every screen and the delivery preview keep rendering it) states what is happening and carries the action. `POST /files/:id/treat-as-sold` (`manage_draws` — the DRAW COORDINATOR, deliberately, not super-admin only) stores `treat_as_sold_at/by/note` on the draw project (db/543); the screen asks **twice** (what it changes about the money, then "are you sure the loan is sold?") and the route refuses anything without `confirm:true`. Turning it back OFF needs no confirmation — it only returns the file to what Encompass says. Every flip is journaled.
  · **IT NEVER REWRITES THE LOAN.** `applications.purchase_advice_date` stays Encompass's, read-only, untouched: `soldStatus` keeps answering the FACT, `effectiveSold` answers "what do we process this draw as", and the two are reported side by side (`sold` / `soldEffective` / `treatedAsSold` / `soldVia:'coordinator'`) so a desk, an audit or a future engineer can always tell a sale from a decision to proceed as if. The override can only move the answer TOWARDS sold — a loan Encompass says IS sold cannot be un-sold by a click. Guard `test-draw-plumbing-unmoved` 4f–4i pins the module to exactly two writes (the PA date, and the override's own three columns) and to never writing the funding mode.
  · Tests: `test-draw-release-party-pure.js` (102 — sections D/E rewritten for the new rule), `test-draw-release-party-db.js` (77 — the same file sold, then unsold, then processed as sold, and back), `test-investor-draw-fee-pure.js` (46) and `test-investor-draw-fee-db.js` (56 — through the real routes, including the 422 and the whole override round trip). `test-funding-channel-pure` F21/F22 and `test-purchase-advice-chase-db` B6/G2 were re-pointed at the new rule; G2b additionally pins that the investor email's money split follows it, so the email can never contradict the desk.
- **Investor-Specific Guideline (ISG) overlay + the grounded AI stack around it (built 2026-07-23/24; ADVISORY only — never blocks/clears, never touches a frozen number).** The note-buyer guideline desk reads a file against its NOTE BUYER's own condition guidelines and surfaces ONLY what that buyer would be unhappy about. Pieces, all best-effort and NULL-safe:
  1. **The desk** — `src/lib/underwriting/investor-guidelines/desk.js` `runInvestorGuidelineDesk(appId, db)` → `assess()` produces per-condition `verdicts` (VERDICT.SATISFIED='satisfied' / OUTSTANDING / CONFLICTS / DEFERRED) plus an `unhappy[]` overlay (flag `coverage_gap` = the buyer requires it but there is NO condition on the file, or `conflict` = a value breaks their rule; `construction_feasibility` gap escalates to fatal via `gapSeverity`). Route `GET /api/underwriting/:appId/investor-guidelines` (staff-only); UI `app-v2/src/components/InvestorGuidelinesPanel.jsx` leads with a happy/not-happy headline and shows only the `unhappy[]` items, everything-checked collapsed. Note-buyer names are fine on this STAFF surface only.
  2. **`desk-sync.js`** mirrors `desk.unhappy[]` into advisory `ai_suggestions` (coverage_gap→kind:'condition'/attach_condition, fatal for construction_feasibility; conflict→kind:'finding' fatal) via the file-view auto-sync `step()`. Feasibility condition `rtl_cond_feasibility` (`db/285`) is the real template a heavy-rehab/ground-up file must carry; Blue Lake cond 200 maps to it (`bluelake-rtl-spec.js`, propagated by the seed's `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`).
  3. **Loan File Primer** (`src/lib/underwriting/loan-primer.js`) = the canonical AI grounding block. `groundingBlock(appId, db)` unions `conditions/engine.loadRuleContext` + `underwriting/whole-loan-context.buildWholeLoanContext` + `underwriting/twin.factsForFile`; `PRIMER_TEXT` encodes the money-field distinctions (purchase_price vs as_is vs arv vs loan_amount vs cost basis vs effective/recognized price), claimed-vs-verified experience, program-vs-registered_program, and note-buyer secrecy. Missing-vs-zero discipline: `money(0)='$0'`, `money(null)='(missing)'`. `borrowerFacing` scrubs the note buyer. It grounds every GPT call so the model never confuses a loan number.
  4. **The grounded AI satisfaction-quality verifier** (`investor-guidelines/ai-guideline-verify.js`) — for the file's SATISFIED note-buyer conditions, asks the grounded GPT brain whether the CLEARED evidence actually meets that investor's EXACT rule; records ONE advisory warning finding (`source:'investor_guideline_ai'`, dedupeKey `isg-ai-verify:<cond_no|itemId|name>`) only when `meets===false`. OFF unless `azureOpenai.available()`; per-file cost-capped (`costMeter.allowSpend`); hard-bounded (≤12 checks); NEVER throws. Route `POST /api/underwriting/:appId/investor-guidelines/ai-verify` (staff-only, returns immediately + runs the bounded checks in `setImmediate`) + `api.aiVerifyInvestorGuidelines(appId)` still EXIST but are no longer wired to a button — the standalone "AI-check the met guidelines" button was REMOVED in the #240 UI unification (see item 8) because the deterministic review now runs in the ONE whole-loan run; this GPT depth layer can be re-triggered from a route if a future surface needs it.
  5. **False-clear guards** (all advisory): `wrong-condition.js` DOC_TYPE_TO_CONDITION_CODES uses the REAL template codes (insurance/LLC/contract were previously dark → dead map); `cure-signoff-advisory.js` warns when a sign-off flips a condition satisfied on a WEAK cure proof (not_satisfied/partial/unable) — AND (owner-directed 2026-07-24, "verify WHY each sign-off cleared") when a DOCUMENT-required condition (`item_kind='document'`) is signed off with NO cure proof AND NO document on the item at all (`noEvidenceWarning` → `cure_signoff_no_evidence`, "cleared with nothing"; only the `document` kind, fail-SAFE on a count error so it never false-warns), wired into the staff.js sign-off route; `reasonability.js` `assignment_fields_missing` + `purchase_exceeds_arv` checks (pure data-integrity, `num(null)` returns null not 0). Blue Lake / Gold 5% SOW contingency is carried as a real signal via `rehab-budget.sowContingencyPct(payload)`.
  Pure tests in `npm test`: test-loan-primer-pure, test-investor-guideline-desk-sync-pure, test-cure-signoff-advisory-pure, test-sow-contingency-pct-pure, test-ai-guideline-verify-pure, test-underwriting-reasonability. When extending ISG: keep it advisory (records `ai_suggestions`, blocks/clears nothing), grounded on the primer, env+cost-gated for any GPT call, and never leak a note-buyer name to a borrower surface.
  8. **The investor-guideline review is folded INTO the ONE whole-loan run — not a second AI pass (owner-directed 2026-07-24: "one run, one cost, all findings in one place, categorized investor-specific").** `src/lib/underwriting/investor-guideline-review.js` `review(bag)` is a PURE deterministic rule TABLE (the owner's examples generalized: Blue Lake escalations — NY loan / assignment / loan >$1.5MM / rehab >as-is or >$250k / ground-up deposit >$1MM / cash-out >$250k / conversion / mid-construction; transferred appraisal — Blue Lake not-eligible, CorrFirst needs a transfer letter; CorrFirst comps-not-close; FICO-file≠credit; claimed>verified experience; stale exit; flood zone; price>value post-appraisal). Each rule's `when(x)` returns true(fire)/false(satisfied)/**null(insufficient data → NEVER fabricate)**. `run.js` `assembleRun` builds the input bag from `ctx.values` (note_buyer, property_state, loan_amount, is_assignment, rehab_budget, as_is_value, arv, purchase_price, fico) merged over `i.investorInputs` (the desk-sourced signals the canonical context can't hold — imported credit FICO, appraisal transferred/rural/comps/mid-construction, verified-vs-claimed experience, flood zone, ground-up/cash-out/conversion), and pushes `review(bag)` findings into the SAME `findings[]` before `decision.decide` → they land in the ONE deduped registry, `category:'investor_guideline'`, `source:'investor_guideline'`. Advisory: a fatal ISG finding flags CTC/funding (`blocks_term_sheet:false`; `summarize()` still flips the aggregate on any fatal) as a super-admin-overridable HARD WARNING via the R6.18 issuance backstop — never a hard block, never a frozen number. `whole-loan-context` now carries `property_state` + `note_buyer` (=`applications.lender`, STAFF-ONLY) so the review reads them from the canonical context. Missing signal → no finding, so it's silent until each upstream signal is wired (go-forward: appraisal desk → appraisal.*, credit import → fico_credit, experience → claimed/verified/stale, flood → in_flood_zone). Tests `test-run-investor-guideline-pure.js` + `test-investor-guideline-review-pure.js`. **UI unified (#240, 2026-07-24):** the standalone top-level "Investor guidelines" file section was RETIRED — `InvestorGuidelinesPanel` now renders as a SUBSECTION inside "Document review & PILOT findings" (`sec-underwriting`, the one AI document reviewer), and its separate "AI-check the met guidelines" button (a second AI pass) was removed. Investor-guideline findings carry sources `investor_guideline`/`investor_guideline_desk`/`investor_guideline_ai`, labeled "Investor-specific guideline" in the one `UnderwritingPanel` finding list. One review, one place, no separate AI section. Rebuild `web/v2/portal` after touching these.
  7. **A note-buyer guideline has a DISPOSITION — not every rule is a "post a condition" (owner-directed 2026-07-24; the overlay was turning back-office reminders into coverage gaps).** A rule with an empty trigger (`T.rural={}`, `T.non_arms_length={}`) fail-opens to "always applies," so the overlay was surfacing email / non-arms-length / rural / appraisal-transfer / occupancy as "no condition on the file → post one." The fix: each spec row carries a `disposition` (`desk.js` `dispositionOf(cond)` — explicit `cond.disposition` wins, else CONSERVATIVELY inferred: domain `non_arms_length`→concern, `rural`→appraisal; `clears_by` `internal_verification`→file_data, `system_field_check`→system; default **document**, so a genuine document gap is NEVER silenced by inference), and `assess()` routes by it: **document** → coverage gap if the mapped PILOT condition is missing (today's behavior + the many-to-one collapse); **file_data** (email `data_field`) → surface ONLY a KNOWN-EMPTY slot as `info_missing`, never a condition; **appraisal** (rural/transferred, `concern_field`) → SILENT until `appraisal_present`, then a concern signal surfaces `appraisal_review`; **closing_package** (occupancy) → SILENT until `closing_package_present`; **concern** (non-arms-length, ALL buyers, `concern_field`) → NEVER proactive, only with a concern signal + the explanation; **system** → an auto-eval rule, conflicts still surface via the CONFLICTS path. Governing discipline: a non-document rule whose triggering signal is ABSENT is SILENT (never fabricated), so these stop showing even before every upstream signal (borrower email, appraisal findings, non-arms-length concern from the fraud/assignment-chain) is wired — that signal-wiring is the go-forward stage. Design `docs/ISG-GUIDELINE-DISPOSITION.md`; test `test-investor-guideline-disposition-pure.js`. When adding a note-buyer rule, set its disposition (and `data_field`/`concern_field`) — do NOT let a back-office rule default into a document gap.
- **The whole-loan run (R6.14) is now ACTIVATED in production — it was built but had NO trigger, so it never ran (owner-directed 2026-07-24; ADVISORY, super-admin-overridable, fails OPEN).** `src/lib/underwriting/run.js` `runWholeLoan` / `assembleRun` (and the R6.15 debounce decider `run-trigger.js` `decideTrigger`) were fully built, exported, and tested but had **zero production callers** — so `underwriting_runs` was never written, the run cockpit / "why this decision" / findings-export always showed "no run", the folded-in **investor-guideline `review()` rule table never fired live**, and the R6.18 **issuance backstop on CTC/funding always failed open** (it reads the latest run, and there was none). The activation is a thin, guarded auto-trigger — NOT a rewrite of the run:
  1. **`run.js` `maybeRunWholeLoan(appId, db, trigger)`** — the ONE guarded entry point every live surface uses. It is **deduped** (`runWholeLoan(..., {skipIfUnchanged:true})` → if a CURRENT non-superseded run already captured this exact `source_hash`, it returns that run instead of writing a churn row; it still builds the context to compute the hash, but only PERSISTS when a real input moved), **never throws** (a failed run must never break a page load or a status change), and **kill-switchable without a deploy** via `WHOLE_LOAN_RUN_DISABLED=1`.
  2. **Wired at two points, both best-effort:** (a) the file-view auto-sync `setImmediate` block in `src/routes/underwriting.js` (its OWN connection, after the `step()` loop) so viewing a file keeps its run fresh → the cockpit / ISG escalations / verdict populate; (b) inside `issuance-backstop.backstopForRun` BEFORE `resolveFromLatestRun`, so a CTC/funding/term-sheet issuance decision always reflects the CURRENT file (this is what makes the R6.18 backstop actually function).
  3. **Still advisory + safe by construction:** every gate built on the run is a super-admin-overridable HARD WARNING that fails OPEN on no-run/error (governing "AI never blocks" rule); the run only READS and records to the immutable run tables, and touches NO frozen pricing number. So activation can never hard-block or mutate anything the engine owns. When adding a new material recompute surface, call `maybeRunWholeLoan` (never `runWholeLoan` directly on a hot path) so it stays deduped + guarded. NOTE: this makes the folded-in ISG rule-table review LIVE, so #232 (per-condition data-source wiring — feeding `investorInputs`) lights up rules 6–18; rules 1–5 (NY / assignment / loan>$1.5MM / rehab) fire from `ctx.values` today.
- **#232 — per-condition data-source wiring for the ISG rule table (`run.js` `gatherInvestorInputs`).** Now that the whole-loan run is live, `runWholeLoan` GATHERS the note-buyer-review signals the canonical context can't hold, straight from the file's own data, and merges them into `investorInputs` (an explicit caller value still wins). NEVER-FABRICATE is the whole discipline: a signal it can't PROVE is OMITTED (its rule's `when` returns null → silent), each read is its own try/catch, and the function NEVER throws. Wired so far (each zero-risk-of-false-fire): **`fico_credit`** ← the borrower-tied HIGHER-OF-TWO report-backed `credit_reports.middle_score` (the pricing convention — `credit/index.js` fileCredit — tied to each borrower via `borrower_id`, emitted ONLY once EVERY borrower on the file is pulled, else omitted; lights `isg_fico_mismatch` — an EXACT priced-vs-credit mismatch, both present). #260 FIX: the old query took the single latest pull with NO borrower filter, so on a CO-BORROWER file the newest pull could be the OTHER borrower's score → the FATAL 'restructure at X' false-fired on ordinary two-borrower files; **`appraisal_present`** ← a current `appraisals` row (gates `isg_price_value_over_requirement` so it only judges price-vs-as-is AFTER the appraisal is in); **`effective_purchase_price`** (#261) ← the canonical recognized/sized price (`whole-loan-context` `effective_purchase_price` — the assignment `recognizedPrice`, else null on a straight purchase) so `isg_price_value_over_requirement` compares the RECOGNIZED (loan-sized) price to value, not the gross contract price. #261 FIX: the FATAL fired on gross `purchase_price > as_is_value`; on an assignment the gross price includes a wholesaler fee the loan isn't sized on (may be the borrower's own non-financed funds), so a deal whose recognized price is comfortably within value FALSE-FIRED the FATAL. Now uses the SAME `effective_purchase_price ?? purchase_price` recognized-price convention as `run.js` sizingInputs and `appraisal-underwriter.js`; a straight purchase (no effective price) falls back to the gross price → unchanged; **`in_flood_zone`** (#250) ← the current appraisal's flood determination, mirroring PILOT's own already-blessed flood definition (db/207): set **true ONLY when PROVEN** in a Special Flood Hazard Area — `appraisals.fema_flood_sfha = true` OR a FEMA zone (`fema_flood_zone`) OR the appraiser's stated zone (`flood_zone`) starting `A`/`V`; a concrete NOT-in-SFHA determination (SFHA explicitly false, or a stated non-A/V zone like "X") → **false** (harmless — the FATAL `isg_flood_zone_needs_insurance` rule fires only on `=== true`); NOT-checked/all-NULL/no appraisal → **OMITTED** (never guessed either way, so the FATAL rule can't false-fire on an unknown). Go-forward signals (each still needs its own careful never-false-fire wiring): **`claimed_exp` + `verified_exp`** (#251) ← claimed = `requested_exp_flips+holds+ground` (the frozen claim-of-record; `reo` is a legacy counter NOT part of the experience math — `src/lib/experience.js` `requestedFromApp`), verified = `experience.countBorrowersExperience(fileBorrowerIds, db, {verifiedOnly:true}).total` (co-borrower-summed VERIFIED track-record deals in the frozen 3-year exit window — REUSES `RECENT_EXIT_SQL`, never re-derived) — feeds the FATAL `isg_experience_claimed_over_verified`. NEVER-FALSE-FIRE: emit the pair ONLY when there IS a claim (sum > 0); no claim → both OMITTED (moot, never a fabricated 0); a real verified 0 (claimed but nothing verified yet) DOES surface — exactly the note-buyer rule; any query error → OMITTED. **`has_stale_exit`** (#252) ← a track-record deal with a KNOWN exit date OLDER than 36 months (REUSES `experience.EXIT_DATE_SQL` — flip=sale, hold=lease/refi — with its window inverted), gated on a real claim (claim > 0) so a file that doesn't rely on experience is never nagged; feeds the WARNING `isg_experience_stale_exit`. true=a stale exit exists; false=checked, none stale; OMITTED on error / no claim (a deal with no exit date is NOT stale). **`is_cash_out` + `cash_out_proceeds`** (#253) ← is_cash_out from `applications.refinance_economic_type` (the structure underwriter's ECONOMIC classification, independent of label — db/267): 'cash_out'→true, 'rate_term'→false, NULL→OMIT; cash_out_proceeds = `verified_cash_out` ?? `estimated_cash_out` (prefers verified; the estimate is an acceptable basis for an ESCALATION-to-review, never a decline). Feeds the Blue Lake escalation `isg_bl_cashout_over_250k` (fires only on is_cash_out && proceeds>$250k). Read from the SAME applications row as the experience block. **`appraisal_rural`** (#254, WIRED) ← `appraisals.nbhd_location_type` (parser-extracted from MISMO PropertyNeighborhoodLocationType, enum Urban/Suburban/Rural — extract.js) read from the SAME current-appraisal row as the flood signals: 'Rural'→true, 'Urban'/'Suburban'→false, NULL/no-appraisal→OMIT; feeds the all-buyers FATAL escalation `isg_rural_property`. **`appraisal_mid_construction`** (#255, WIRED) ← `appraisals.condition_of_appraisal === 'SubjectToCompletion'` (the precise parser-extracted UAD value for construction-not-complete; SubjectToRepairs/Inspection/AsIs → false; NULL/no-appraisal → OMIT) feeds the Blue Lake FATAL escalation `isg_bl_mid_construction`. **`appraisal_transferred`** (WIRED 2026-07-27) ← `appraisals.lender_name` (the MISMO ordering-lender name, populated by `appraisal/import.js`): a lender name that is present AND does NOT contain the "yscapital" token (matched via `field-registry.normNoteBuyer`, so "YS Capital Group"/"…LLC" all read as ours) → **true** (a genuine transfer from another lender); a present YS-Capital lender → **false**; a BLANK lender / no appraisal → **OMIT** (never guessed). "Ours" is matched generously precisely because `isg_bl_transferred_appraisal` is a FATAL for Blue Lake — a false positive on our own appraisal is the expensive direction. Feeds (owner 2026-07-27, the transfer split): **Blue Lake → `isg_bl_transferred_appraisal` (BIG FATAL — Blue Lake does NOT accept a transferred appraisal even WITH a transfer letter; the only fix is a new appraisal in our name)**, and **EVERY OTHER note buyer → `isg_transferred_appraisal_letter` (audience `all`, WARNING advisory — post a condition to request the transfer letter; silent once `appraisal_transfer_letter === true`)**. Wired in BOTH `run.js gatherInvestorInputs` and the ISG desk (`desk.js`, concern_field `appraisal_transferred`). STILL DARK: appraisal **comps-close** — NOT extracted by the appraisal parser (no such field), so `isg_cf_comps_not_close` stays silent until real comp-distance extraction is added; **ground_up_deposit** (isg_bl_groundup_deposit_over_1m) and **is_conversion** (isg_bl_property_conversion) have NO source column in applications — wire only once a real field exists. Re-run freshness (#257, FIXED): the gathered investor signals are now FOLDED INTO the run's `source_hash` (`buildSourceVersions.investorSignals` — sorted `key=value` over the present signals), so a change to ANY of them (a new credit pull, a flood/rural/mid-construction determination added to an existing appraisal, a track-record line getting verified, a cash-out classification) changes `source_hash` and the deduped auto-trigger (`maybeRunWholeLoan` `skipIfUnchanged`) writes a FRESH run instead of returning the stale one — so the wired findings actually refresh live. (Previously `source_hash` was context/registration-only, so a signal-only change silently returned the stale run.) Tests: `scripts/test-gather-investor-inputs-pure.js` (mock-db, never-fabricate + error-omit) AND `scripts/test-gather-investor-inputs-db.js` (DB-gated — inserts real `credit_reports` + `appraisals` rows with the FEMA flood columns so a phantom-column drift like the #248 `b.full_name` class is caught; a pure/mock test never could). When wiring a new signal here, prove it can't FALSE-fire its rule (a fatal ISG finding is an advisory hard-warning on CTC/funding), omit-don't-guess when the source is absent, and add a DB test whenever it reads a raw column. **Integration capstone (#256)** `scripts/test-run-investor-signals-e2e-db.js` proves the WHOLE chain, not just the gather: a file whose current appraisal is a FEMA SFHA + Rural neighborhood, run through the full `runWholeLoan`, actually PERSISTS `isg_flood_zone_needs_insurance` + `isg_rural_property` rows in `underwriting_run_findings` (gather → assembleRun → review → persistRun). When wiring a new signal, prefer extending this integration test so a gather-to-finding regression can't slip through. **Note-buyer keying (#262, HARDEN):** `buyerMatches` keys the file's note buyer with the SHARED `field-registry.normNoteBuyer` (the SAME normalizer the conditions engine uses for `ctx.note_buyer` and the ISG desk uses to match `investors.label_norm`), not a private duplicate — so the review keys a note buyer exactly the way the rest of the ISG stack does, and any real future alias (a longer dropdown label) is added in that ONE shared place so every consumer agrees at once. `normNoteBuyer` is pure (field-registry has no requires) so the review stays PURE/never-throws. Deliberately NOT doing speculative suffix-fuzzy matching (e.g. "Blue Lake Capital"→bluelake): on a FATAL buyer-escalation rule an over-match is a real false-fire, and matching only in the review would DESYNC it from the desk — a genuinely suffixed production label is an owner-verifiable change to the shared normalizer, not a guess. Guarded by a keying-contract test (`test-investor-guideline-review-pure.js` — canonical labels in any casing/spacing match their audience; a different buyer and a blank buyer never trigger a Blue Lake escalation).
- **The whole-loan RUN was silently returning null on every call — buildWholeLoanContext had 3 phantom columns (#248; the DB test that finally caught it).** No test ever ran `buildWholeLoanContext` against a real DB (the others are pure/`assembleContext`-only), so three wrong column names sat undetected — and because the run (activated #682) wraps the call in a swallowing catch, EVERY run just returned null. The new DB-gated test `scripts/test-whole-loan-context-appraisal-db.js` is the first to exercise it and surfaced all three: (1) the application query selected `b.full_name` from **borrowers**, which has `first_name`/`last_name` (that column lives on `staff_users`) — this threw on EVERY call, so the run was fully dark despite #682; fixed to `NULLIF(TRIM(COALESCE(b.first_name,'')||' '||COALESCE(b.last_name,'')),'')` (the repo's standard borrower-name composition). (2)+(3) The appraisal was silently DARK too: `whole-loan-context.js` loaded the current appraisal with `WHERE is_current = true ORDER BY created_at` — NEITHER column exists on the `appraisals` table (the current-row flag is `superseded = false`, the timestamp is `imported_at`; db/137/188). The query threw on every call and a swallowing `try/catch` nulled the appraisal, so the canonical context's **appraisal-sourced `as_is_value`/`arv` candidates were never populated** (they only ever came from the registration/application). Fixed the query to `superseded = false ORDER BY imported_at DESC` (+ select `imported_at`) and mapped the appraisal version stamp (`apprVer`) to `imported_at` (falling back to `created_at` for a synthetic test object). This makes the appraisal a real `confidence:'high'` candidate for `as_is_value`/`arv` — it can never override the `definite`/`governing` registration value (source-priority), touches no frozen number, and a genuine appraisal-vs-registration disagreement now surfaces as a (correct, advisory) discrepancy. **A pure test can't catch a wrong-column bug (it mocks `db.query`), so this ships with a DB-gated test** `scripts/test-whole-loan-context-appraisal-db.js` (inserts a real appraisal row, asserts the value surfaces, and that a `superseded` appraisal is excluded). LESSON: every raw column referenced in a swallowed-catch query is unverified until a DB test exercises it — when adding an appraisal query, use `superseded = false` + `imported_at` (grep the repo's other appraisal queries for the pattern), never `is_current`/`created_at`. **#259 (another instance, found by an adversarial bug-hunt):** `whole-loan-context.js` read the subject state from `app.property_state` — a column that DOES NOT EXIST on `applications` (state lives in the `property_address` jsonb). So `ctx.values.property_state` was ALWAYS null and the Blue Lake FATAL New-York-escalation rule `isg_bl_ny_loan` could NEVER fire. Fixed to `fieldRegistry.normState(app.property_address.state)` (reuse the conditions engine's normalizer — codes pass, full names map, unknown → null so the rule stays silent, never false-fires). Guarded by a real-DB assertion in `test-whole-loan-context-appraisal-db.js` (inserts `property_address:{state:'NY'}`, asserts `ctx.values.property_state==='NY'`) — the pure test had MASKED the bug by fabricating a `property_state` field on its fixture. Lesson reinforced: a pure test that sets a field directly on a fixture object proves nothing about whether that field exists in the real schema.
- **Sessions are LONG and sliding, sign-out is PER DEVICE, and a 401 is the ONLY thing that may ever sign someone out (owner-reported 2026-07-26: "staff keep getting logged out automatically / signed out right after signing in"). Never regress any of the four layers:**
  1. **Length + slide.** `cfg.accessTtlSec` is a 30-day IDLE window (`ACCESS_TTL_SEC`), and `authenticate()` re-issues the token via `X-Refresh-Token` once it is older than `cfg.sessionRefreshAfterSec` (12h, `SESSION_REFRESH_AFTER_SEC`) — capped at half the token's life so a deliberately short TTL still slides. It used to wait for the HALF-LIFE, so a token was renewed only after days. Anyone who signs in at least monthly can never be aged out by the clock. The refresh **carries the `sid` over** — a refreshed token is the SAME session, or per-device revocation would break.
  2. **Per-device sign-out.** Every access token carries a random `sid`; `POST /auth/logout` revokes only that sid (`revoked_sessions`, db/321). It used to bump the GLOBAL `token_version`, so signing out on a phone silently killed the desktop — reported to the user as the catch-all "your session expired". `token_version` remains the account-wide hammer for the events that deserve it (password change/reset, admin deactivation, `{everywhere:true}`). A legacy token with no `sid` still authenticates and falls back to the global bump, so a deploy never signs the company out. The revocation check rides inside the SAME auth query and **fails OPEN** (a missing table degrades per-device sign-out; it must never 503 the portal).
  3. **THE CHOKEPOINT — after `authenticate()` proves the session, `res.status` is wrapped so no downstream handler can answer 401 (401 → 502).** The SPA reads ANY 401 as "your session died" and wipes the token (which lives in `localStorage`, so it drops every tab on the device). Dozens of routes relay an upstream vendor's HTTP status verbatim (`res.status(e.status)`) — Sitewire, TrustPoint, ClickUp, credit — so a rotated vendor token used to sign the STAFFER out of PILOT. Three modules had worked around it individually (`sync-file-review.js`, `clickup/relink.js`, `sync-autoresolve.js` each document the trap with an `expose` flag); the wrapper kills the whole class at one point, for every route present and future. Do NOT remove it, and do NOT "fix" a route by re-relaying a 401.
  4. **Every session rejection names its reason, and the SPA confirms before signing anyone out.** `sessionDenied()` answers `{error, code, session:'invalid'}` with `code` ∈ `bad_token | mfa_incomplete | wrong_token_kind | session_revoked | signed_out | account_deactivated`, and logs one line — "why was I signed out" is answerable from the logs, not guessed. Client side (`app-v2/src/lib/api.js`, mirrored in the frozen V1 `app/src/lib/api.js`): a 401 no longer signs anyone out on its own — `handle401` probes `/auth/me` once (single-flight, plain `fetch` so it can't recurse) and clears the token ONLY if that also 401s, then shows the SERVER's wording ("This account has been turned off") instead of the generic expiry notice.
  Also closed in the same pass: `completeMfa` for staff had **no `is_active` check** (the password path filters `is_active=true`), so a deactivated staffer with 2FA could finish signing in, receive a valid token, and be bounced by `authenticate()` on the very first API call — the exact "I sign in and I'm instantly signed out, every time" report. It now refuses at the door with `account_deactivated`. **Operational note: if `/api/health` ever shows `jwtStable:false`, `JWT_SECRET` is unset in the environment and every restart/deploy invalidates every session in existence — that alone reproduces this complaint and no code change can fix it.** Test `scripts/test-auth-session.js` (in `npm test`, no DB needed).
- **THERE IS ONE CLIENT SIGN-IN AND ONE STAFF SIGN-IN — a borrower's HELPER signs in on the client screen and the SERVER works out which credential they typed (owner-directed 2026-08-09: "it has a separate sign-in URL, and it has a separate link for the helper … they should just sign in from the same screen that the borrowers are signing in … their credentials should know that they're only a helper and give them the correct helper credentials", then, asked: "you don't even need to mention on the login page that there is such a thing as a helper. There is only staff login and borrower's login").**
  1. **THE SEPARATE DOOR IS GONE, AND THE LOGIN SCREEN NEVER MENTIONS HELPERS.** `/assistant/login` and its screen are retired (the route redirects to `/login` so an old bookmark still works); the "Helper sign in" link is off the client login footer, which now names only **Verify email** and **Staff sign in**. Do NOT re-add a helper link, a helper screen, or helper-explaining copy to that page — a helper is a CLIENT and types their own email and password like everybody else.
  2. **`tryAssistantCredentials` in `src/auth/index.js` is the ONE definition of "are these helper credentials"**, built exactly like `tryStaffCredentials` because a helper is simply the THIRD credential store (`borrower_assistants`, never `borrower_auth` — see `src/lib/borrower-assistant.js`): a compensating hash so the fallback cannot be used to enumerate who was invited to help on somebody's loan, a wrong password counted against **that** account's own lockout (or the cross-surface fallback becomes an unthrottled way to guess passwords — the same audit finding `tryStaffCredentials` carries), and a real borrower token carrying the assistant envelope, so the PII strip and the sign block ride along untouched. `answerCrossSurfaceLogin` tries STAFF first (their console is their primary identity) then HELPER, and is called on BOTH failure paths of `POST /auth/borrower/login`.
  3. **THE TRAILING COMPENSATING HASH ON THE NO-ACCOUNT PATH IS LOAD-BEARING — do not "simplify" it away as redundant.** It looks redundant next to the hashes inside the two cross-surface tries, and it is not: it is what holds that path's hash count EQUAL to the "borrower exists, wrong password" path, which spends one on its own verify. Remove it and "this email is registered" becomes tellable from "it is not" by response time alone.
  4. **`POST /auth/assistant/login` is KEPT and DELEGATES** to the same function — old bookmarks, saved password-manager entries and any in-flight integration still work and behave IDENTICALLY to the client login. Never re-implement the credential check there: a second copy is how two doors drift apart in lockout counting and in what the token carries.
  5. **THE STAFF LOGIN IS DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE.** It cross-tries BORROWER credentials and does NOT try helper ones — a helper is a client and is told to use the client login, so the internal console door stays scoped to the two identities that belong there. Widening it is an owner call.
  6. **THE CSS ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS A CLASS, NOT A ONE-OFF (owner: "the CSS is terribly messed up").** `app-v2/src/styles.css` styles `.input,select,textarea` — the **CLASS**, not the `input` **TAG**. So a bare `<select>`/`<textarea>` is styled while a bare `<input>` falls back to the browser default: a narrow box with the wrong font, border and colours on a white PILOT card. On the retired screen the password box came from `PasswordInput` (which carries the class) while its `<input type="email">` one line above did not — which is exactly what the owner was looking at. Grepping the class turned up **the same bug on `AcceptTerms.jsx`**, the public page a new borrower lands on from an emailed term sheet, on all 8 of its text fields beside a `<select>` that WAS styled. **Every form control on a public screen carries `className="input"` (or an explicit inline style that draws its own box).**
  7. **`scripts/test-auth-screens-pure.js` (in `npm test`) is the structural guard** over every public/pre-auth screen: no undressed control, no separate helper sign-in anywhere, the old-bookmark redirect intact, and the server wiring still in place. It strips COMMENTS before every "must not appear" assertion — the code that removed the separate sign-in necessarily NAMES it in a comment, and a guard that read comments would fail on the very fix it protects and then get "fixed" by deleting the explanation. Keep `PUBLIC_SCREENS` in step with the public `<Route>`s in `App.jsx`. All 9 mutations of it were proven to fail; the end-to-end proof (a helper signing in on `/auth/borrower/login` and that session being scoped to the borrower, still PII-stripped and still refused signing) is in `scripts/test-borrower-assistant-db.js`, whose 6 core assertions were proven to fail with the wiring reverted.
- **A BORROWER'S NAME IS FOUR FIELDS PLUS ONE BIG ONE — and `full_name` is what everything reads (owner-directed 2026-07-27; design `docs/NAME-SPLIT-RESEARCH.md`, db/345 + db/346).** PILOT stored a person as first_name + last_name only, and every one-line name from ClickUp was cut with `lastIndexOf(' ')` — so a middle name silently became part of the FIRST name ("Issac Michael" / "Grunzweig") and a generational SUFFIX became the LAST name ("John Michael Smith" / "Jr"). Encompass models the same person as three separate fields (`applications[].{party}.firstName / .middleName / .lastName` + `.suffixToName`), which is also what MISMO and every closing document want — so the middle name was literally documented as "(no column) — finding only" in `lib/integrations/encompass-field-map.js`, and `reconcile.compareIdentity` joined first+last on BOTH sides, meaning a file whose Encompass copy is correctly split read as a NAME MISMATCH against our merged first name. That row is `GATE.BLOCK`, so it HELD TERM SHEETS on files where nothing was wrong.
  1. **The columns.** `middle_name` (optional — NULL means the person has none), `name_suffix` (Jr./III/MD, kept OUT of last_name so the surname compares cleanly), `name_review_needed` + `name_review_reason` (a PROMPT, never a gate — nothing on a file ever waits for it), `name_split_checked_at` (so the boot repair is resumable). **`full_name` (db/346) is a POSTGRES GENERATED COLUMN** built from the four parts — not a trigger, not application code — so the one big field CANNOT drift from the pieces on any write path, present or future. To change a name you write the PARTS; the API splits a typed full name for you. It is never NULL (blank → `''`), so compare it as `NULLIF(x.full_name,'')`.
  2. **ONE splitter: `src/lib/person-name.js`** (pure — no DB, no network). The owner's rule is absolute: **two words split in half**, at full confidence, never a review. Three or more words is a judgement call, so every split reports a CONFIDENCE and flags the uncertain ones. Deliberate conservatism you must not "simplify": a bare trailing `V`/`X` is NOT a suffix (it is far more often an initial standing in for a surname, and mis-reading it DELETES the surname); surname particles are STRONG (`van`, `von`, `della`, `dos`, `du`, `ter` — always attach) vs WEAK (`de`, `del`, `la`, `al`, **`ben`** — real given names too, so they only attach when written LOWERCASE, and the split is flagged); `Mac`/`Mc` are deliberately absent; a blank/placeholder name raises NO prompt (the sync mints `Unknown Unknown` shadows in volume). `compareNames` is middle-name TOLERANT — first + surname must agree, a suffix present on BOTH sides must agree (a father and his son are different people), a middle name that one side omits or abbreviates is the same person — and it re-splits a LEGACY merged `first_name` on the fly so no comparison ever depends on the backfill having run.
  3. **Previous files: `src/lib/name-heal.js` at boot**, JavaScript and NOT a migration on purpose — a PL/pgSQL twin of the splitter would drift from the live one (the `pilot_term_norm` / `pilot_property_type_norm` class). THE INVARIANT: a repair only ever REARRANGES a name, never restates it — it rebuilds the whole line before and after and refuses any row whose words changed (a leading courtesy title is the one permitted removal). Never touches a row that already has a middle name or a placeholder; re-checks the values it read inside the UPDATE; stamps every row it looks at; never throws.
  4. **ClickUp: one line in, one line out, NO CHURN.** Inbound splits the single `Borrower Name` field into all four columns and carries the confidence as `_nameSplit` so `ingest.healBorrowerFields` can raise the prompt (fill-only, and only on a profile nobody has looked at). Outbound writes `full_name` back into that one field. **`mapper.fieldValueEquivalent` treats two names describing the SAME person as equivalent for the name fields** — without it, the first repush after the split would rewrite every card (or be blocked by the PII overwrite shield and queue a pointless review) for every borrower with a middle name. The tolerance is DROPPED when the push carries `humanEditKeys` naming a name column or is an approved review — the two ways a human says "write this" — so a deliberate correction still lands. The task↔file **identity graph** (`clickup/identity.js`) deliberately keeps comparing first + last: it matches EXACTLY against identities recorded on earlier syncs, and adding the middle name would silently weaken every stored match on the duplicate-a-task workflow it exists to protect.
  5. **Encompass stays READ-ONLY.** `IDENTITY_MAP` gains real `middle_name`/`name_suffix` entries; `compareIdentity` compares by MEANING through `compareNames`; `enrich.verifyMiddleName` FILLS a blank middle name from Encompass's own field, agrees silently (clearing the prompt — Encompass independently confirmed our split), or queues a review. It NEVER overwrites, never touches the first/last name, and there is still no write path to Encompass. `scripts/test-encompass-profile-enrich.js` asserts every `UPDATE borrowers` in enrich is fill-only — read it before adding a fourth.
  6. **The one big field is still what EVERYTHING reads.** Every SQL `first_name || ' ' || last_name` is now `NULLIF(x.full_name,'')`; every JS join goes through `person-name.displayName(row)` (server) / `lib/personName.fullNameOf(row)` (app-v2), both of which prefer `full_name` and FALL BACK to joining the parts — so a query that has not been updated behaves exactly as before. **When you add a surface that shows a name, call those helpers — never re-inline a concatenation.** Every form (loan application, borrower profile, staff profile, both co-borrower forms, intake, registration) takes an optional middle name, and the staff profile shows ONE big Full name box with First / Middle / Last / Suffix underneath that stay in step as you type. A locked borrower change-requests a middle-name edit through the loan team like any other identity field. SharePoint is deliberately untouched (`borrowerMatches` was already middle-tolerant, so existing folders still match and new ones are simply shorter — which helps the 259-char path ceiling). Tests: `scripts/test-person-name.js` (pure) + `scripts/test-name-split-db.js` (real DB + real HTTP), both in `npm test`.
- **OFF-SITE BACKUP — pg_dump, encrypted here, into a vault that is not Render; and a WEEKLY DRILL that actually restores it (owner-directed 2026-08-02: "if the database restarts we lose all our data… a real backup that can be used to restore… and if any tables are added they should automatically be added to the backup"). Full research + vendor decision + the RESTORE RUNBOOK: `docs/DATABASE-BACKUP-AND-RESTORE.md`. db/409.**
  1. **THE GAP WAS NEVER "a restart loses data" — it was that every copy lived inside the Render account.** Managed Postgres does not lose data on a restart, and Render runs continuous point-in-time recovery (3 days on a Hobby workspace, 7 on Pro) — which is the FIRST thing to reach for on an ordinary mistake and must stay switched on. What it cannot survive is losing the account itself (billing, a compromised login, a deletion noticed in three weeks), and it exports nowhere we control. So the new layer is a copy at a DIFFERENT company, encrypted with a key that vendor never sees. **THE VENDOR IS CLOUDFLARE R2 (owner-directed 2026-08-02), which is where the live documents already are** — the owner chose one vendor over two, accepting that a total loss of the Cloudflare *account* would take both copies. That decision is only safe because of two things, and NEITHER may be quietly undone: the backups live in their OWN bucket, and they use their OWN API token scoped to that bucket alone (never the `S3_*` documents credential) — the realistic threat is a leaked credential, not Cloudflare failing, and a documents key that cannot address the backup bucket cannot erase the backups. **R2 has no per-object Object Lock**, so `BACKUP_S3_OBJECT_LOCK_MODE` is BLANK and immutability comes from a 35-day **bucket lock rule set in Cloudflare** — keep it equal to `BACKUP_KEEP_DAILY_DAYS` so the prune only ever deletes objects whose lock has expired (a refused delete is reported, never fatal), and remember `vault.probe()` reports the lock as `unknown` on R2, which is NOT the same as "protected". The upgrade back to two vendors needs no code: add an AWS/B2 bucket as the SECOND vault (`BACKUP_S3_SECONDARY_*`).
  2. **`pg_dump` IS THE ANSWER TO "new tables automatically".** It takes no table list — it asks the database what exists and dumps all of it (tables, views, indexes, sequences, constraints, triggers, functions, in dependency order). There is deliberately NO list of tables anywhere in this system to keep up to date, and there must never be one: the moment a backup names its tables, the next migration silently falls outside it. The INVENTORY taken beside the dump (`src/lib/backup/inventory.js`) is NOT a filter — it is the RECEIPT, counted from the SAME snapshot the dump reads (`pg_export_snapshot()` → `pg_dump --snapshot=`), which is what lets the drill compare a restore to the row instead of to a tolerance.
  3. **A BACKUP IS NOT A BACKUP UNTIL IT HAS BEEN RESTORED.** `scripts/backup-verify.js` runs weekly: downloads a real backup, decrypts it, RESTORES it into a scratch database, and compares tables/rows/sequences/views/indexes/triggers/constraints against the receipt. It emails either way — the one "all good" worth reading. Two guards stand in front of its target because it wipes it: never the live database, and never a database whose name does not look disposable (`src/lib/backup/targets.js` `isSameDatabase` / `looksLikeScratch` — ONE definition, shared by the backup, restore and verify tools, because a second copy that drifts is how a drill wipes the wrong thing).
  4. **ENCRYPTED BEFORE IT LEAVES THE PROCESS** (`src/lib/backup/cipher.js`): AES-256-GCM, a per-backup HKDF-derived data key with a random salt (so one master key encrypting a thousand nightly dumps never reuses a key/IV pair), the header fed in as additional authenticated data, and the tag at the END so TRUNCATION IS DETECTED. A flipped bit, a half download, an edited header and the wrong key all fail LOUDLY — never as partial data that looks restorable. NO NEW DEPENDENCIES (Node `crypto` only, the same rule as `storage-s3.js`, whose `signV4` the vault client REUSES rather than re-implementing).
  5. **THE THREE THINGS THAT MUST COME BACK TOGETHER.** The database; the DOCUMENTS (they live in object storage and the DB only holds a reference — restore the database alone and every loan file has dead links, so `src/lib/backup/documents.js` copies them incrementally into the same vault under the same key, keys kept verbatim so a restore puts each file back exactly where the database already points); and **`SSN_ENCRYPTION_KEY`**, without which a perfect database restore leaves every SSN permanently unreadable. That key + `JWT_SECRET` + `BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` belong in the password manager, not only in the Render dashboard. Say this out loud in any future backup/DR work — it is the failure that looks like success.
  6. **FAILURE POSTURE.** A partial multipart upload is ABORTED, never left as a half object (an abandoned upload is invisible in a listing and still billed); a dump that exits non-zero is never uploaded; every upload is read back and size-checked before anything is called a success; the scratch file (the whole company under one key) is 0600 and deleted in a `finally`; the retention prune runs only AFTER the new backup is stored, never deletes the newest backup, and deletes NOTHING if the plan would leave fewer than `BACKUP_KEEP_MINIMUM` (a half-read listing must never read as "nearly empty, prune it"). Every run lands in `backup_runs` and a failure EMAILS a human — a backup system that fails quietly is worse than none, because it is believed. `/api/health` carries `backup:{protected,lastBackupAt,lastVerifiedAt}`.
  7. **INERT UNTIL CONFIGURED.** Nothing in the request path ever loads these modules; with no key and no bucket the job refuses politely and the app is unaffected. It runs as its own Render CRON services (`ys-capital-backup` nightly, `ys-capital-backup-verify` weekly) on **`Dockerfile.backup`** — Docker specifically because `pg_dump` must be at a major version >= the server's and the native Node image does not guarantee one; `--preflight` refuses on a mismatch and names the version to install. **When the database is upgraded, bump `PG_MAJOR` in that Dockerfile.** Tests `scripts/test-backup-cipher.js`, `test-backup-retention.js`, `test-backup-vault-pure.js` (all pure, all in `npm test`). When adding a new stateful store to this system, add it to the vault — and never add a table list.
- **THE TRACK RECORD REVIEWS ITSELF, AND PILOT NEVER MERGES TWO LOOK-ALIKE ADDRESSES ON ITS OWN (owner-directed 2026-08-02; db/418).** The owner, twice: *"merging files with similar address — NEVER before human review … the system should always need a human to confirm, never do this risky stuff itself"*, and *"we should NOT send it to the regular manual review queue. We should have findings ON the track record of stuff that was not done correctly … you should not be able to clear and sign off on the experience condition till you clear those findings. If you find our SUBJECT PROPERTY on the track record that should be a finding to be cleared … give a few options over there what to do."*
  1. **`src/lib/track-record-findings.js` is the ONE place that decides a track-record problem, and the ONLY producer of `track_record_findings`.** Two codes today: **`duplicate_line`** (two lines that look like one property, so a deal is counted twice toward experience — options *merge* / *they are two different properties* / *dismiss*) and **`subject_property_on_record`** (the property THIS loan is buying, listed as a deal already done — options *take the line off* / *the borrower really did own it before* / *dismiss*). The OPTIONS come from the server (`actionsFor`), never a list retyped in a screen, so a new code gets its buttons for free.
  2. **NOT the sync review queue.** That queue answers "PILOT and another system disagree about a field": keyed on a ClickUp task, on an admin screen nobody opens while underwriting, and resolving a row there cannot gate a condition. A track-record problem is our OWN data, judged by the person working the file, and it MUST be able to hold a condition — so it follows the `appraisal_findings` shape (db/137) instead. Borrower-scoped: a duplicate is wrong on every file the person has, so `application_id` is **NULL** for `duplicate_line` and set only for `subject_property_on_record`. Stamping a duplicate with the triggering file hides it from their other files, and `openForFile` filters on exactly that column.
  3. **THE GATE.** `signOffGate`'s `isExp` branch (`rtl_p3_reo` / tool_key `track_record`) refuses while ANY finding is open, BEFORE the claimed-experience check — a duplicated line is wrong whether or not this deal is priced on experience. It FAILS OPEN on a read error: a database blip must never make a condition permanently unsignable.
  4. **DETECTION IS BY PAIR, NEVER BY GROUP.** `address.sameAddress` is deliberately NOT transitive — a bare row matches every unit in a building (`5 Main St` ≈ `Apt 1` and ≈ `Apt 2`, while `Apt 1` ≉ `Apt 2`) and a house-number RANGE matches every number it spans (`27-29` ⊇ `27` and ⊇ `29`, while `27` ≉ `29`). Grouping transitively therefore puts genuinely different properties in one group; the first cut of the heal pass did exactly that and would have DELETED a real second condo unit. Every pair reported is one the address check confirmed directly, so the two addresses on the card are always the two the action acts on. **Never re-introduce group-based merging.**
  5. **A DECIDED FINDING STAYS DECIDED**, and **a run only retires what it actually looked for.** The detector re-runs on every file view, so a reviewer's "these really are two different houses" must never come back (the trap `finding_decisions`/db/333 closed for the AI desks). And a run that never evaluated a code must not close it: the boot pass passes no subject address, so retiring everything absent from its results silently resolved every `subject_property_on_record` in the book on every deploy — and one level down, syncing file A retired file B's. A finding whose problem genuinely went away DOES close itself, so it can never strand the experience condition with nothing to click.
  6. **Nothing is removed unattributed.** `track-record-heal.mergeTrackRecordPair` and `track-record-findings.removeLine` both demand a staff id, refuse to destroy VERIFIED evidence, refuse a merge whose figures disagree, and re-point (merge) or detach (remove) documents rather than let `documents.track_record_id`'s CASCADE take them. The boot pass now only RE-KEYS and delegates — it detects nothing and deletes nothing. Tests `scripts/test-track-record-findings-db.js`, `test-track-record-dedupe-pure.js`, `test-track-record-dedupe-db.js` (all in `npm test`).
- **A TRACK-RECORD EDIT TOUCHES ONLY WHAT THE REQUEST SAID — and the review reset belongs to db/485, never restated in a route (2026-08-09 A-to-Z audit).** Both PUT doors (staff `PUT /track-records/:id`, borrower same path) built their UPDATE from `trackRecordCols(b)` — the FULL column shape — so a partial body NULLED every absent figure (sale price, dates, entity name), silently reset an absent dealType to `'flip'`, and the borrower door's `llc_id=$3` cleared the entity link on any body omitting `llcId`. Worse, the entered stamp (`trackRecordEnteredCols`) force-wrote `is_verified=false` + `'pending'` on EVERY save, so a notes-only edit or a byte-identical autosave echo destroyed a reviewer's verification that db/485 — whose own tail says "no material column moved … left completely alone" — would have preserved. **`trackRecordSentOnly(cols, b)` (routes/borrower.js, shared by BOTH doors) is the guard**: drops any column whose body key was not sent (exception: `ownedPersonally` still clears the entity — the flag IS the statement about it), drops `is_verified`/`verification_status` on edits entirely (db/485 is the one judge of a review reset, firing on real material change from every writer), and carries the who-typed-this stamp only when the edit spoke about the deal's substance (`TRK_MATERIAL_COLS` = db/485's own list). The CREATE doors are untouched — entering a line is pending review (the 2026-08-03 rule), and their ON CONFLICT retry still resets. Same pass: `importer.compareCandidate` compares the address by PLACE via `address.sameAddress` (two spellings of one house rendered as a CONFLICT on the very candidate the matcher matched THROUGH that address), agreeing rows say "They agree." instead of "Neither holds anything.", and `matchExisting`/`compareCandidate` read the candidate's entity link from `proposed_llc_id` (candidates have no `llc_id` column, so the one fill that carries Check A over to ownership was silently skipped forever). Borrower-side: `borrower-confirm.SAFE_FIELDS` is ENFORCED (`pickSafe` over every candidate payload — the header claimed the projection was built from it while `dealType` had already been added past it), and `ConfirmFoundProperties` keeps what is on screen when a refresh fails instead of blanking the section mid-answer. Tests `scripts/test-track-record-partial-edit-db.js` (in `npm test`; the verification-survives case was proven to FAIL pre-fix) + the compare/llc cases exercised by `test-track-record-importer-db.js`. When you add a field to the track-record doors, add its body key to `TRK_COL_SENT_KEY` in the same commit — an unmapped column silently reverts to write-always.
- **THE TRACK RECORD CENTER IS ONE ARRANGEMENT, EVERYWHERE — and the SAVED COPY IS BUILT ON THE SERVER (mega-workspace phases B–F, owner-directed 2026-08-09: "combining the file's three track-record sections into the one real workspace … everything visible, nicely designed").** The loan file's `sec-track` section and the borrower profile's Track record tab render the SAME shared components, two lenses of one center; the borrower's downloadable saved copy no longer depends on anyone opening the client tool. The rules:
  1. **`app-v2/src/components/track-record/ExperienceHeader.jsx` + `RecordLedger.jsx` are THE arrangement** — the experience math (Claimed / Verified + the tool's own tier ladder / Still-needed) and the record as a grouped ledger (Fix & flip / Fix & hold / Ground-up) with the **REO band**: every non-counting line stating WHY (the REO-is-the-residual-list rule made visible). Every figure and reason comes off `src/lib/track-record-todo.js` — the header NEVER recomputes gate math, and the REO reasons are the endpoint's own LINE_TODO codes (`exit_expired`/`future_exit`/`no_exit`), degrading to verified=counting when the todo is unreachable (with a claim on file and no answer yet the Still-needed box shows "—", never a false "met"). **Lenses**: the FILE lens passes the three per-line file actions (they mint conditions on that file) + the doc-review handlers; the PROFILE lens (`lens="borrower"`, `StaffBorrowerDetail.jsx`) omits them — an absent handler renders no button — and passes `extraActions` (check-the-records / verify / revoke) + `onOpenEntity`. When you add a track-record surface, mount THESE components — never a third arrangement.
  2. **THE TO-DO PAYLOAD AGREES WITH THE SIGN-OFF GATE, in both directions.** `trackRecordTodo` asks `registeredExperienceNeed` BEFORE the claim gate (the same 2026-08-09 need-first ordering `signOffGate` got — a claim zeroed under a live registration surfaces the registered need, never "no experience required"), and `ok` turns on the SHORTFALL alone — being UNREGISTERED is informational since the 2026-08-06 gate rule (Products & Pricing's own condition demands the registration; the screen's banner says the condition CAN be signed off). Pinned by section 5 of `scripts/test-experience-gate-need-db.js`. Never re-add a `!registered` term to `ok`, and never gate the experience block on the claim alone.
  3. **THE SAVED COPY IS REBUILT SERVER-SIDE ON EVERY WRITE PATH — `src/lib/track-record/html-copy.js`, hooked at the ONE chokepoint `events.publishTrackRecordUpdate`** (the same call every write path already makes for the #112 live refresh, so the copy inherits that coverage forever — a staff PUT, a verify, an importer decision, a merge heal all refresh it with no per-door wiring). Per-borrower TRAILING debounce (`TRACK_RECORD_COPY_DEBOUNCE_MS`, 4s), fire-and-forget (may never fail or slow a write), kill switch `TRACK_RECORD_SERVER_COPY_DISABLED=1`. It stores through `track-record-snapshot.saveSnapshot` (coalesce + supersede + SharePoint kick), mirrors the client copy's established document (same sections/chips/footer; its call-to-action links the LIVE portal record, not a stale seed), and is BORROWER-SAFE by construction — deal facts only, everything escaped, never lo_notes/internal notes/a note-buyer name. The borrower's own tool sheet still posts client copies (byte-untouched, constraint A13) — both writers coalesce onto one row, latest wins. Test `scripts/test-track-record-html-copy-db.js` (in `npm test`). When you add a track-record write path, call `publishTrackRecordUpdate` (you already must, for the live refresh) — never wire the copy separately.
  4. **THE STAFF-SIDE LEGACY IFRAMES ARE RETIRED (phase E): no embedded `track-record.html` in the loan file, and no condition-row iframe modal** — the experience condition's "Open the track record" jumps to the center (`goToSection('sec-track')`), and the panel's "Open full screen" ToolModal is the ONE remaining staff editor until the center edits natively. Do NOT re-add an embedded mount; the `?internal=1` bridge support and the borrower's `TrackRecordScreen` iframe stay untouched (frozen V1 + A13 depend on them).
  5. **Phase F surfaces**: the workspace has its own full-screen route `/internal/track-record` (`?borrower=<id>` narrows to one person — the profile links there) beside the Approvals tab mount; the KEYBOARD MAP (`?` overlay, J/K, 1/2/3 focus a check, C/X/D on the focused check) fires ONLY actions the server's own `next`/`other` offer — a keystroke is never a way around a rule the buttons respect; and the search sheet shows the BUDGET METER (`GET /api/staff/elementix/usage` → ledger-counted calls this hour + paid credits this month against `cfg.elementix` caps) — read-only, unknown reads "unknown" never zero, the refusing caps stay in `callTool`. **Three more from the same audit's system sweep:** (1) **a DECLINE is durable by PLACE** — the dedupe key's `doc:` form was minted from whichever deed row the vendor returned first, so a declined house came back under a sibling key on the next differently-paged search; the key is now the SORTED joined id set, and `stageOne` also asks the ADDRESS (`trackRecordKey`) about prior `declined`/`staged` rows when the key finds nothing (imports/merges deliberately do NOT suppress by address — a genuinely new deal on a once-owned house is real and the matcher bands it). (2) **the deed's counterparty is KEPT** — `candidatesFrom` stores the acquisition seller / exit buyer on `raw.counterparty`/`raw.buyer_name` and `importNew` writes `track_records.seller_name` (db/499's column had NO writer, so the related-party control was starving; `counterparty.js` graph wiring into verify-run remains a recorded go-forward item). (3) **an expired snooze is back in the queue** — `loadQueue`'s SQL returned it but the JS buckets filed `status='snoozed'` NOWHERE. Test `scripts/test-track-record-audit-fixes-db.js` (in `npm test`).
- **"INDIVIDUAL — NO ENTITY" IS OFFERED ON ALL THREE APPLICATION DOORS (owner-directed 2026-08-02).** The rule, the Gold refusal (`vesting-program-rule`), the affidavit condition (db/417) and the display shipped first, but the CHOICE existed only as a staff waiver INSIDE an already-created file — so every applicant had to claim an entity and a staffer undid it afterwards. **`fields.vestsIndividually(body)` is the ONE reading**, used by the staff create, the borrower's direct create AND its draft submit (the path the application form actually uses — wiring only the first leaves the real door broken), and the public intake. It **defaults to an ENTITY**: reading a blank or garbled answer as "individual" would silently drop the LLC condition off the file. **A NAMED ENTITY WINS** over the radio, mirroring `vestsInIndividual` (llc_id wins), so the flag and the entity can never contradict each other. The public marketing form had ASKED since it shipped and even printed the answer on its review summary while never SENDING it — the same collected-then-discarded class as the refinance fields. When adding any column to one of these INSERTs, add its value in the SAME position: the borrower door has already been bitten by a shifted bind (an interest-reserve amount landing in `payoff_lender`), and `test-vesting-doors.js` asserts every `INSERT INTO applications` runs `$1..$N` with no gap.
- **Never hardcode or commit secrets; never store a secret shared in chat (owner-directed, 2026-07-12).** All secrets come from Render environment variables (`src/config.js`), never from source. Do NOT write an Azure client secret, Render API key, ClickUp token, DB URL, or any other credential into a tracked file, a doc, a commit message, a PR body, or a code comment. `.env` is git-ignored and stays that way. A credential pasted into a chat/transcript is considered **compromised** and must be **rotated** before use — do not "save it to Render" or wire it in as-is; have the owner set the rotated value in the Render dashboard (or via the Render API using a key the owner just rotated). The audit gate below applies to any secret-handling change.
- **Two borrowers, ONE credit report — merged / separate / one-borrower imports (owner-directed 2026-07-27).** A joint ("merged") credit report is ONE document covering BOTH borrowers, and it used to be unimportable: `parse.js` de-duped scores per bureau across the WHOLE document, so the second borrower's three scores were silently dropped and the "middle" score was the median of six numbers belonging to two different people; the upload path forced exactly one borrower (`selBorrowers.length === 1`), so the screen simply refused the file. Now:
  1. **`parse.js` SEGMENTS a merged document per borrower** (`parsed.borrowers[]`, `parsed.isMerged`).
