Ownership of each score/tradeline/inquiry/record is established three ways, in order of certainty and never guessed: STRUCTURE (the item sits inside that borrower's own subtree — MISMO 3.x `PARTY/ROLES/ROLE/BORROWER`), EXPLICIT LINK (`_BorrowerID`/`BorrowerID` naming the borrower's `_ID` — MISMO 2.x), then a 3.x `RELATIONSHIP xlink:from/to` arc. Unclaimed TRADELINES read as JOINT (they appear on both borrowers flagged `sharedAcrossBorrowers`); an unclaimed SCORE is given to NOBODY (`mergedUnattributedScores`) — an unlabelled score could price the wrong borrower's deal. The per-bureau de-dupe now runs INSIDE each borrower. A single-borrower document is byte-for-byte unchanged (an empty `<BORROWER>` placeholder is not a second borrower).
  2. **`match.js` ties the report's people to the FILE's borrowers** in strength order — `ssn` (last-4, proof) → `name` (first+last) → `lastName` (unique remaining) → `order` (one each left, no name contradiction; `verified:false`). Contradicting last names are NEVER paired. An `order` match still can't move a FICO on its own: store.js's existing SSN check governs the write-back.
  3. **`index.js` imports three ways.** MERGED = one file → a `credit_reports` row EACH (own slice, own middle score, own FICO), the one physical PDF/XML filed ONCE and shared by both rows (`store.storeImport({reuseDocs})`, filenames `credit-report-merged.*`). SEPARATE (`files:[{borrowerId,xml,pdfBase64}]`) = a file per borrower in one action. ONE = a single borrower, the other keeping their own condition. Merged is AUTO-DETECTED (`merged:true` forces it, `merged:false` refuses it); a merged file on a one-borrower file, or covering nobody on the file, is REFUSED with a plain reason rather than filed against the wrong person.
  4. **Imported TOGETHER → ONE condition.** Both reports land on the file-level `rtl_cond_credit` and a leftover empty co-borrower condition is cleared (`coConditionClosed`) — the file stopped showing the same credit condition twice. A one-borrower import still splits out the co-borrower's own condition (`coConditionOpened`), unchanged.
  5. **A reissue reference belongs to the BORROWER it was issued for** (`reissueReportIds:{borrowerId:ref}`, per-borrower boxes in the modal, each pre-filled from THAT borrower's own last report). It used to be hard-wired to the primary, so reissuing the co-borrower alone dropped the typed reference and failed with "a reissue needs the reference number" — the co-borrower could not be imported independently at all.
  6. **A JOINT LIVE response is sliced to the borrower who was pulled** (reissuing a joint reference returns both people) — storing it flat put the other borrower's score on them. An empty live response now names the document's top element (`<html>`, `<ERROR>`) so a gateway/error page is obvious.
  7. **`fileCredit(appId,{scope})` + `GET …/credit?scope=co|primary`** narrow the section to ONE borrower, and `<CreditCondition fieldKey>` uses it — the co-borrower's own condition shows THEIR report/history/last attempt instead of repeating the whole file's credit section. Un-ticking a borrower no longer half-fades and resizes their card (it recedes at full contrast with a "Not in this import" tag; the note under the cards sits in a fixed slot so the screen doesn't jump).
  Tests: `scripts/test-credit-merged-pure.js` (2.x/3.x segmentation, joint tradelines, unattributed scores, matching) + `scripts/test-credit-merged-import-db.js` (merged split, one filed document, one condition, separate files, per-borrower reissue against a stubbed provider, joint live response sliced) — both in `npm test`. Rebuild `web/v2/portal` after touching the panel.
- **A JOINT credit order is ONE request with ONE reference number — and "how many BORROWER elements" is NOT "how many people" (owner-directed 2026-07-27, with the Xactus SupplementX 3.4 Postman collection as the source of truth).** Two things, both learned from real vendor artifacts rather than guessed:
  1. **The Xactus/MISMO 3.4 joint request** ("Test Joint - CRx — Wanna & Needa House" vs "Test Individual - CRx") differs from the individual one in EXACTLY three places: one borrower `PARTY`/`ROLE xlink:label="Borrower01"` becomes TWO (`Borrower01`+`Borrower02`); the single `RELATIONSHIP` arc (`CreditRequestData001` → the borrower role, arcrole `CREDIT_REQUEST_DATA_IsAssociatedWith_ROLE`) becomes ONE PER BORROWER; and `CreditRequestType` flips `Individual`→`Joint`. There is still ONE `CREDIT_REQUEST_DATA` and ONE `CreditReportIdentifier` — **which is why a joint report carries a SINGLE reference covering both borrowers, and two borrowers does NOT have to mean two reference numbers.** `provider.buildRequestBody({borrowers:[…]})` builds it (`borrower` stays the one-borrower form and is byte-identical to what it always sent); `provider.pull({borrowers})` orders it. Both shapes are offered per import — the modal's "How to order for N borrowers" toggle picks **One joint order** (one reference box, `joint:true` + `jointReissueReportId`) or **Separate orders** (a reference box each, `reissueReportIds`), defaulting to joint when the file already has a joint reference (`preview.jointReissueReportId` = the last reference shared by 2+ borrowers). `importJointLive` sends the one request, splits the joint response per borrower, files the one document once, and lands both rows on the one condition. Contract test `scripts/test-credit-joint-request-pure.js` pins our request against the vendor sample; DB coverage in `test-credit-merged-import-db.js` (§5: one request for both, refuse-before-sending on incomplete PII, and the same file ordered the other way).
  2. **A REAL vendor report repeats ONE borrower many times** — the live Xactus 3.4 file (report 91768164) carried FOUR `BORROWER` elements for Patrick Kamara: one per bureau under `CREDIT_FILES/CREDIT_FILE/PARTY` (including the alias "KAMARA PATRICK", name reversed) plus the deal-level `PARTIES/PARTY` — while `CREDIT_SCORES` sits at the CREDIT_RESPONSE level, outside every party subtree. The first cut of the merged-report work counted BORROWER elements, so it read that as four people, could attribute the scores to none of them, and **blanked a report that plainly showed 576/573/561 — the file's middle score went from 573 to "no score" in production.** Two structural guards now make that class impossible: `samePerson()` folds repeated records of one human into ONE segment (SSN last-4 decides when both carry one — two different last-4s are two different people; otherwise the name, compared order-insensitively so "KAMARA PATRICK" ≡ "PATRICK KAMARA"), and **`splittable`** — a document is only read per-borrower when at least one segment actually OWNS a score. Several people with NOBODY's scores labelled falls back to the whole-document read (never a blanked headline score) and sets `mergedAmbiguous` + `mergedBorrowerNames`, which the importer turns into "import each borrower's own report instead". A single-borrower document is byte-for-byte what it always was. Guarded by the `REPEATED` / `TWO_REAL` / `AMBIGUOUS` fixtures in `test-credit-merged-pure.js` (each reproduces the live shape and fails on the pre-fix code). **LESSON for any future per-party splitting: count PEOPLE (identity), never ELEMENTS, and never let a smarter reading produce LESS data than the plain one — fall back instead.** In the same pass the MISMO 3.x identity fallback landed: 3.x keeps the name/SSN on the PARTY, not on `BORROWER`, so `parsed.borrower` now falls back to the canonical deal-level party (it had always been null on 3.4 files, which silently disabled the SSN check that guards the FICO write-back).
  3. **`.crx-recip-card.off` collided with a GLOBAL `.off{display:inline-flex;white-space:nowrap}` utility** (the officer chip, `styles.css` line ~1729): an un-ticked borrower card became an inline-flex, shrink-wrapped, no-wrap box — its info grid collapsed to 63px and spilled ~230px to the right, scrolling the whole modal sideways (owner-reported, twice). Renamed to `.crx-off`; the un-ticked state also moved off the name line into the eyebrow so a long name plus two tags can't widen the card. **Namespace every state modifier on a namespaced component (`crx-*`) — a bare `.off`/`.on`/`.un` will collide with the global utilities.** Verified by rendering the real markup against the real stylesheet in headless Chromium and asserting `scrollWidth - clientWidth === 0`, not by eye.
